2026-08-22: Add palette-index color bindings
(SetColors=name=i:N)

Let a color setting track a live palette index instead of a
frozen RGB value, addressing issue 12991. OSC 1337
SetColors=<name>=i:N binds the color to palette index N
(0-15 follow the loaded preset and dark mode; 16-255 are the
fixed cube and grayscale ramp), so a badge or tab color can
follow the preset rather than a hardcoded color.

Implementation reuses the existing expression-binding
system:

- Expose the live palette as a colors.* variable scope
  (colors.ansi.<name>, colors.indexed[N], and named colors)
  in iTermColorScopeVariables, updated from the color map.
- SetColors=<name>=i:N writes a KEY_BINDINGS entry so the
standard
  binding observer applies and re-applies the color
  reactively.
- Alpha: an optional @A suffix (badge only) and a new
  iterm2.with_alpha(color:, alpha:) built-in, honored only
  by colors that render with alpha (badge and cursor guide).
- Ownership: palette bindings are tagged {expression, owner}
in
  KEY_BINDINGS so a concrete override or reset clears only
  the bindings this feature created, never a user-authored
  one, without inspecting expression text.
- Override/reset/baseline handling so a concrete SetColors,
an OSC
  110/111 reset, a UI color pick, or a tab clear defeats the
  binding, and a later reset restores the Edit-Session
  value.

Adds ModernTests/iTermColorScopeVariablesTests and release
notes in docs/notes-3.7.1.txt.

2026-08-22: Password manager: unified Edit panel, in-place
editing, 1Password secure add/edit

- Add supportsInPlaceEdit / editInPlace / canEditPassword
capabilities to the
  data source protocol. Route renames through an in-place
  update when the data source supports it, falling back to
  delete-then-re-add otherwise.
- Replace the password-only editor with one Edit panel,
always enabled when a
  row is selected, that edits name, user name, and password.
  The password prefills asynchronously so the sheet never
  blocks on a slow backend. Remove the now-redundant Edit
  Account Name / Edit User Name context menu items.
- 1Password: create and edit via secure stdin JSON templates
(op item create /
  op item edit), so a chosen password never appears on the
  command line; drop autogeneratedPasswordsOnly.
- Select the newest installed op and enforce a 2.23 minimum
(older CLIs
  silently ignore piped edits). Fix a main-thread exec()

  plain cached Process.

The .permissions client-local action carried the raw session

2026-08-20: Cache lastRemoteHost to avoid a full
interval-tree walk per prompt

-lastRemoteHost was an uncached reverse-enumerator scan of
the whole interval tree, run on every OSC 133 prompt via
addMarksForPathsInRange:.  On a local session (no
VT100RemoteHost marks) it scanned every mark and found
nothing on every prompt. Cache the result, including the nil
("no host") case.

Only adding or re-homing a VT100RemoteHost mark can change
the answer, so the cache survives the prompt/screen-mark
churn of a redrawing TUI. It is invalidated at every path
that adds or moves a host mark: setRemoteHost: (add),
moveNotesOnScreenFrom:/removeNotesOnScreenFrom: (alt-screen
swap), addSavedIntervalTreeObjects:baseLine: (unfold and
porthole restore), and arrangement restore. A removed cached
mark is detected on the read path by its niled entry. The
read-only snapshot recomputes each sync, so it stays correct
without copying the cache across the doppelganger boundary.

Adds RemoteHostCacheTests covering the add, alt-screen-swap,
and fold/unfold invalidation paths; each fails if its
invalidation hook is removed.

Related to issue 12992.

2026-08-20: Skip the lastCommandMark cache invalidation for
unrelated mark removals

willRemoveScreenMarksFromIntervalTree nilled the
lastCommandMark cache for every removed mark. Since
-lastCommandMark is consulted on every OSC 133 prompt (via
assignCurrentCommandEndDate) and a redrawing TUI removes
marks constantly, that turned each prompt into a full
reverseLimitEnumerator walk of the interval tree.

Only invalidate when the removed mark is actually the cached
last-command mark; removing any other mark cannot change
which command mark is last. Test against the raw cache (new
-cachedLastCommandMark accessor) instead of -lastCommandMark
so the check itself does not trigger the walk.

This is one of several per-prompt tree walks; the runaway
memory it fed is bounded separately by the per-token
autoreleasepool (6b5a68f41). Related to issue 12992.

2026-08-20: Bound token-execution memory with a per-token
autoreleasepool

Executing a token can create many autoreleased temporaries.
OSC 133 handling, for example, looks up marks
(lastRemoteHost, lastPromptMark, lastCommandMark) by walking
the interval tree with reverseLimitEnumerator, which
allocates an NSMutableArray per step. execute() drains a
whole batch of queued token arrays in a single GCD block, so
under a firehose (a TUI that clears and repaints every
frame) one call processes tens of thousands of tokens before
its autorelease pool is drained. Those temporaries pile up
into multiple GB of live NSMutableArray and CFString.

Scope an autoreleasepool to each token so the temporaries
drain immediately instead of accumulating across the batch.

Verified on a release-style build driving a full-screen
repaint stream: the process grew to 7.2 GB in about 80
seconds without this change and stayed flat near 400 MB with
it, under the same workload.

Fixes the runaway memory growth in issue 12992.

(BOOL, default

  also pop the consent modal; the automatic (periodic) check
  is gated on this preference.

  pipeline now threads the outcome through to every terminal

Both controls grey out with the other AI settings when AI is

2026-08-19: Fix Tahoe tab bar overflow menu dropping the
first hidden tab

The overflow chevron is a pull-down NSPopUpButton, which
uses the menu item at index 0 as its (hidden) label instead
of showing it in the popped menu. The code inserted a
throwaway placeholder at index 0 to compensate, but commit
30d27df18 wrapped that insert in an "if (@available(macOS
26))" branch that skipped it on the false assumption that
the Tahoe overflow button was not a pull-down. It still is,
so on macOS 26 the first overflowed tab was swallowed as the
button's label: with one hidden tab the chevron opened an
empty menu, and with more only the first was missing.

Gate the placeholder on the button's actual pullsDown state
rather than the OS version, so it is restored on macOS 26
and self-corrects if a non-pull-down button is ever used.

2026-08-18: Fix blob-replay truncation live test's token
budget

The test lowered AITokenLimit to 8000 to force truncation,
but a terminal-linked chat registers ~25 tool schemas
costing ~5,500 tokens, which maxTokens subtracts from the
budget alongside the conversation.  The system prompt plus
one padded round then pushed even turn 1 over 8000, so every
turn was rejected pre-send with requestTooLarge and no blob
was ever stored (blobCount 0 != 6).

Raise AITokenLimit to 16000 so the tool-schema floor plus a
round fits, and raise AIResponseTokenLimit to 7000 so it
clears that floor too: truncate() budgets maxTotalTokens -
maxResponseTokens and counts only the conversation, not the
tool schemas, so unless maxResponseTokens exceeds the tool
floor the request is rejected before truncation can
activate. With both raised, turn 1 fits, later rounds cross
the truncate threshold, and the oldest round's blob is
dropped from the final request as the test asserts.

2026-08-18: Skip retired claude-opus-4-1 in the live AI
harness

Anthropic retired Opus 4.1 now that Opus 4.8 ships; every
request for claude-opus-4-1 returns HTTP 404 for all keys,
so each live test that iterates the Anthropic model list
failed on that one entry. Add it to unreachableForNewKeys,
the existing skip set that already carries retired Google
models, so vendor sweeps pass.

2026-08-18: Fix AI chat request bugs on the Responses API
and harden tests

Three related defects surfaced from real OpenAI chat
sessions:

1. Tool schema strict-mode: scroll_wheel and both
register_watch
   variants listed a property in `properties` but not in
   `required`, which OpenAI's strict function schemas reject
   with HTTP 400. The reflection generator (JSONSchema.for:)
   had the same latent flaw for optional fields. Optionals
   are now expressed as nullable-and-required by
   construction on both the hand-authored and generated
   paths.

2. Response parsing crash:
UnkeyedDecodingContainer.decode([Any]) had no
   branch for JSON null, so a null inside an array never
   advanced the container and recursed until the stack
   overflowed. Now consumes null, descends nested arrays via
   nestedUnkeyedContainer, and throws on an unrecognized
   element instead of spinning.

3. Dropped user turns: the volatile per-turn context (the
orchestration
   <workgroups> snapshot, the terminal/screen block) was
   folded into the message list as a trailing user message,
   then the Responses builder's previousResponseID suffix(1)
   truncation kept that context and dropped the user's
   actual newest message. Every turn after the first reached
   the model as bare context and it replied as if nothing
   was asked. The volatile context is now passed separately
   and appended after truncation.

Tests: - New hermetic guards: tool schemas validated at the
serialized wire
  layer (both device-paired states); the [Any] null-array
  decoder; and the Responses previousResponseID +
  volatile-context interaction.
- Live harness: a per-vendor tool-schema acceptance sweep
and a
  per-vendor volatile-context multi-turn guard (OpenAI
  exercises the previousResponseID delta path). AILiveDriver
  now threads previousResponseID and extra tool
  declarations.

2026-08-18: Companion: drop ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption to
fix App Store upload

With the flag set to true, App Store Connect's uploader
expected a matching ITSEncryptionExportComplianceCode. None
exists (the ASC export-compliance flow resolved to "no
action needed" once France was excluded), so the upload
failed with an export-compliance mismatch. Removing the key
lets ASC's per-version compliance answers govern, which
unblocks the upload.

2026-08-18: Add a placeholder AI key that returns a canned
response

Setting the AI API key to "placeholder" makes
AITermController short-circuit completions and return a
fixed demonstration reply instead of contacting any provider
(mirroring the Apple Intelligence no-wire path), and makes
the AI connection test report success. This lets App Review
exercise the AI chat, including through the iOS companion
app, with no real key and no cost. Benign in production: it
only triggers on the literal key "placeholder".

2026-08-18: Fix Companion keyboard ^C sending a literal "c"

The synthesized key event carried the unmodified letter in
NSEvent.characters, so the injected ^C reached the mapper's
post-Cocoa data path (which emits event.characters verbatim)
and sent "c" instead of 0x03. This showed up under apps
using the modern CSI-u mapper and in the standard mapper's
regular path.  Put the real control byte in .characters,
matching a hardware Ctrl+<key>, and add a post-Cocoa
regression test that the old pre-Cocoa test helper masked.

2026-08-18: Companion: let the phone opt into push
notifications

Previously the phone requested notification permission only
when the Mac's alerts toggle was on
(wantsNotificationPermission in the handshake), so a paired
user had no phone-side way to enable notifications and
canNotify stayed false with no signal.

After a fresh pairing, iTerm2 Buddy now shows a soft prompt
asking whether to receive notifications; Enable runs the
existing permission flow (iOS prompt, register for remote
notifications, report status to the Mac). Settings also
gains a Notifications control, shown only while paired, that
reflects the current authorization and can turn
notifications on or route to iOS Settings when denied.

2026-08-17: Fix sparkle pointer

2026-08-17: Recognize localhost by any of the machine's
names

Dropping a file on a local terminal offered to upload it
over scp when the machine answered to more than one name
(for example .local versus a DHCP-assigned name), because
localhost detection compared the reported host against a
single gethostname() for exact equality.

Enumerate the machine's actual names (Unix name, Bonjour
LocalHostName and its .local form, and the DHCP FQDN) and
match a reported host against them exactly. Freeze the
verdict at report time, since a machine's names drift over
time (mDNS can renumber the .local host), and remember names
confirmed local so an earlier name stays recognized after
the live names change. Matching is exact only, with no
short-label or bare-"localhost" leniency, so a genuine
remote reached over plain ssh that reports "localhost",
"localhost.localdomain", or a name sharing only this
machine's first DNS label is not misclassified as local.

Apply the same detection to the file:// URL scp decision,
the git-poller and status-bar isLocalhost fallbacks, and the
legacy Unknown-locality fallback on VT100RemoteHost.

2026-08-16: Companion: on-device (Whisper) dictation mic in
the session composer

Add a Whisper mic button to the composer bar, alongside Send
and Close, for dictating without the system keyboard
(on-device, private, no cloud).

The live-transcript-span logic (each pass replaces the last,
leaving typed text intact) lives on the composer text view.
The dictation lifecycle lives in the canvas coordinator,
which owns a token in the shared DictationController, drives
start/finish/cancel, streams the running transcript into the
composer via an Observation re-arm loop, and presents the
enable/download and error alerts. The accessory bar only
renders the mic state (idle / recording with a level pulse /
spinner while the model loads) and reports taps.

Send finalizes any in-flight dictation so its tail is
included; Close, keyboard dismissal, session end, and
teardown all cancel it. Presenting the enable-dictation
alert no longer closes the composer: it stays open behind
the alert and the keyboard returns when the alert is
dismissed.

2026-08-16: Companion: local composer for session input,
opened by button or dictation

Add a Composer to the live session view: a local
UITextView-backed editor above the keyboard for writing or
editing input, then sending it to the session all at once
(no per-keystroke round trip, and dictation gets reviewed
before it runs).

The session keyboard first responder becomes a hidden
UITextView (not a bare UIKeyInput) so the system dictation
key engages at all. That one view serves two modes:
pass-through (invisible, forwards each keystroke to the
session) and composer (visible, accumulates a local draft).

Entry points: - The compose button in the keyboard accessory
opens it instantly.  - Tapping the system mic opens it on
the tap, via the
  UIKeyboardDidBeginDictation notification, before the first
  word is recognized; a textViewDidChange fallback opens it
  once dictation delivers text if that notification ever
  stops firing.

Multi-character inserts in pass-through are treated as
dictation's opening chunk (typed input is always a single
grapheme), so nothing dictated leaks to the shell. Send
types the draft with no trailing newline; Close keeps the
draft.

2026-08-15: Tab groups: collapsible groups with animated
collapse/expand

Named, colored, contiguous runs of tabs headed by a group
"chip" that collapses to hide its members and expands to
reveal them, across all tab styles (Tahoe, Yosemite,
Minimal, Dark) and both orientations.

- Collapse/expand is animated: horizontal bars interpolate
cell widths,
  vertical bars interpolate row slots, laying out every
  frame so scroll, chip size, cross-axis, and contiguity
  stay correct. A chip cell being inserted or removed runs
  through the same animator, so a new chip is positioned
  correctly throughout and its width tweens in lockstep with
  the neighbors instead of snapping into place at the end.
  Expanding a chip tweens it gradually from its wide
  collapsed width to its narrow settled width as the members
  grow out of it (pure growth, no snap).
- Group membership and the collapsed/name/color state ride
each member
  tab (no separate registry), so they survive reorder, drag
  between windows, tear-off, duplicate, and
  window-arrangement restore.
- Invariants: a group's members stay contiguous (the pinned
prefix wins
  when a group would straddle the pinned boundary, leaving
  it as up to two runs) and the active tab is never inside a
  collapsed group.  Auto-expand enforcement is deferred
  during batch operations (duplicate / move / tear-off /
  reorder / restore / drag) via a single nestable,
  exception-safe suppression, and settled once,
  model-driven, when the operation completes.
- Pinning or unpinning a grouped tab acts on the whole group
as a block
  so it never straddles the pinned boundary, and contiguity
  is enforced once after the block settles.
- Progress bars are hidden during the slide (they are not
repositioned
  per frame, so they would otherwise jump). Group chips and
  outlines are not drawn under the window buttons on a
  scrolled Minimal bar, and a tab with active progress
  inside a collapsed group falls back to its inline progress
  bar.

Includes the pure ordering/contiguity logic
(iTermTabGroupOrdering, unit-tested) and release notes in
docs/notes-3.7.txt.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: review fixes, drag drop feedback,
cleanup

Correctness fixes from the origin/master...HEAD review: -
Capture the color picker's target (tab vs group) when the
  picker opens, fixing an MRC use-after-free of
  _tabGroupIDForColorPicker and a stale panel silently
  retargeting a group.
- Keep a group's last member in its group on a no-move drop
  (its own gid-carrying slot now reports the group), and
  fire didDrop only when position or membership actually
  changes.
- Skip chip cells in the overflow menu (a chip became a
blank
  item selecting a nil tab), lastVisibleTab,
  numberOfVisibleTabs, and the accessibility tab lists.
- Honor fit-to-content widths when a group exists instead of
  snapping every tab to uniform widths.
- Gate the scrollable-bar trailing clip outset on chips
being
  present so groupless bars keep the exact viewport clip.
- Enforce pinned-prefix and group-contiguity invariants
  through one canonical order (iTermTabGroupOrdering) used
  by applyTabOrder: and the reorder repair: pinning a tab is
  no longer undone, group drops clamp to the pinned zone,
  and a wrapped Move Tab Left/Right clamps instead of
  no-opping.
- Stop cloning group identity across windows: a single
member
  moved or dropped elsewhere ungroups (only whole-group chip
  drags and tear-offs carry the group).
- Report an honest create-tab API status when tab_index is
  refused or clamped.
- Fix the duplicate join slot at a group's trailing edge and
  the NSNotFound drop anchor snapping a dropped group to the
  front of the bar.
- Push group membership to cells by tab view item, not by
  position; mid-drag the dragged tab's cell is absent, which
  shifted every later tab's group id (a one-tab group
  vanished into its neighbor while dragging).
- Use the live mouse location in the tab bar's drag
handlers;
  AppKit's throttled draggingLocation interleaves stale
  points and flapped the drop target.

Drop feedback while dragging into another window's bar: -
Run the drag animation timer in the tracking run-loop mode;
  it starved during a live drag and slots never opened.
- Distribute drop slots on entry keyed on drag participation
  so a stale placeholder cannot suppress setup.
- Shrink a full (stretch-to-fit) bar's tabs from snapshotted
  base widths so the slot carves a visible gap.
- Auto-scroll a scrollable bar, both directions, to reveal
  the targeted slot.
- Size the slot to the dragged unit's on-drop extent in the
  destination, not its size in the source bar.
- Keep the group outline around the dragged member's own
slot
  so it shrinks with the slot instead of snapping down.

Policy: dragging a one-tab group's last tab out dissolves
the group; in-place drops and chip drags keep it.

Architecture and cleanup: drop context now flows through
tabView:didDropTabViewItem:inTabBar:joiningGroupWithID:
(droppedInsideGroupID side channel removed); one shared run
enumerator feeds both styles' group decorations; deleted the
dead PSMTabGroupChipView instance side, unused drag helpers,
and an uncalled getter; consolidated the duplicated luma
math in NSColor+PSM; cached chip text measurements; batched
the membership push to one normalize per update; resolved
terminalForTabBar: via the delegate; gated expensive RLog
dumps; added always-on drag telemetry (animTick, calc,
dragScroll) for field debugging; release notes.

New tests: PSMTabGroupEnumeratorTests,
PSMTabGroupDropResolutionTests, iTermTabGroupOrderingTests,
plus live drag coverage in PSMTabGroupDragTests (timer mode,
slot sizing, squeeze, auto-scroll, stale-slot entry).

2026-08-13: Tab groups: stop clipping the last group's
outline in a scrollable bar

When tabs overflow and the scrollable tab bar is on, the
viewport clip ends exactly at the last tab, but a group's
enclosing pill extends a couple points past its tabs, so the
last group's outline was cut off flat on the right.  Expose
the run outset from the tab style and widen the trailing
viewport clip by it (into the add-button margin, which has
room).

2026-08-13: Adjust the tab group color palette

Swap systemTeal for a custom teal (#50afbc) and drop
systemYellow.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: refuse dropping a group onto a
session pane

Dropping a whole group onto a SessionView split off just its
first member into a split pane, which is never what you
want. Stamp a marker on the drag pasteboard for whole-group
drags and have SessionView read it off the NSDraggingInfo
(entered/updated/prepare/perform) to reject them, rather
than reaching into the shared drag assistant. Single-tab
drops are unaffected.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: fix crash drawing dividers when all
cells are placeholders

drawTabBar entered the divider loop whenever any cell
intersecting the clip rect was drawable, but mid-drag the
clip rect can cover only zero-width placeholders that the
divider filter drops, leaving an empty list.  0..<(count -
1) then formed 0..<(-1) and trapped with 'Range requires
lowerBound <= upperBound'. Guard on count > 1.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: stop clipping the group outline in
the drag image

The floating drag image captured only runFrame + 1pt, but
the enclosing group outline is outset ~2pt past the member
pills on the right, so the right edge of the outline was cut
off. Widen the capture to include it.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: don't offer in-group drop slots
while dragging a group

A whole-group drag is never allowed to land inside another
group, but the front-of-group and end-of-group join slots
(single-tab affordances) were still inserted, so approaching
another group's last tab opened a slot. Skip both when a
group is being dragged.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: ungroup a single tab torn off into a
new window

Dragging one grouped tab out of the tab bar creates a new
window, but the tab kept its group id, leaving a lone
one-tab group in the new window. Only a whole-group tear-off
should stay grouped, so clear the tab's group membership
when it becomes a new window on its own.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: drop slots for dragging a tab into
and around a group

Build up the drop-slot behavior for dragging a single tab
near a group: an "end of group" slot that joins the group as
its last member, a matching "after group" slot just outside
it, and "before group" slots, each reachable by approaching
from either direction. Keep the group outline drawn over an
open in-group slot, keep the chip anchored when the group's
first member is dragged, keep before/after-group slots
available when dragging a group's first or last member, and
re-resolve membership when a member is dragged out of its
group.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: Group Color menu item reusing the
tab color picker

Add a "Group Color" item to the chip context menu using the
same ColorsMenuItemView swatch control as Tab Color.
Preset/reset swatches set the color on every member of the
group (the color rides the tabs). The "more colors" cell
opens the same custom/system picker: a new
tabGroupIDForColorPicker steers the picker's central
-applyTabColor: to the group instead of a single tab, with
the popover anchored to the group's first member. Building
the tab color menu clears that id so a later tab pick isn't
misrouted.

2026-08-13: Tahoe: 2pt inter-tab spacing so outlines don't
touch

At 1pt of cell spacing, adjacent selected tabs' outline
strokes met (each reaches ~0.5pt past its pill) and the
group run's enclosing outline overlapped the neighboring
tab. Use 2pt so there's ~1pt of clear gap between adjacent
outlines, matching the system tab bar, and the group outline
sits flush with the gap instead of crossing into the next
tab.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: don't offer another group's interior
as a group drop target

Dragging a whole group could target a slot inside another
group, which would nest/mix groups. After the drag target is
chosen, if it falls strictly inside another group's run
(between its chip and last member), snap it to that group's
near edge (before the chip or after the last member, per the
mouse). Edge slots and non-group slots are unchanged.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: draw dividers between tabs within a
group (Tahoe)

The Tahoe style suppressed the inter-tab divider whenever
either neighbor was grouped, so a group's members ran
together with no visible boundaries. Draw a divider between
two members of the same group (it sits inside the run, clear
of the enclosing outline) and only skip it at a group edge,
where the identifiers differ and a divider would collide
with the outline.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: sensible chip tooltip and singular
close confirmation

- Hovering a group chip showed the per-tab tooltip built
from a nil session
  ("Name:/Profile:/Command:"), because a chip has no tab
  view item. Show the group's name instead (or no tooltip
  when it is unnamed).
- The batch close confirmation said "(1 tabs)"; use "1 tab"
for the singular.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: Close Group closes pinned members
too

Close Group skipped pinned tabs, so a group made entirely of
pinned tabs had nothing left to close and the menu item did
nothing. Skipping pinned is right for Close Other Tabs /
Close Tabs to the Right (don't nuke pinned tabs outside the
group) but wrong for Close Group, whose members are the
group. Parameterize the batch close with skippingPinned: NO
for Close Group, YES for the others.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: fix crash from duplicating/dragging
a pinned group

Two problems around pinned tabs and groups:

1. Duplicating a group inherited the pinned flag
(arrangementForDuplication
   encodes it), so a copy landed as a pinned tab at the END
   of the bar -- a mix of pinned and unpinned members
   stranded outside the pinned zone, which is an invalid
   state. -duplicateTabGroup: now unpins the copies so a
   duplicated group is a normal, contiguous, unpinned group.

2. Starting a group drag on a non-contiguous group crashed:
   -distributePlaceholdersInTabBar:withDraggedGroupMembers:chip:
   assumed the members were a contiguous strided run (cB -
   cA == 2*(count-1)) and ran the removal loop off the end
   of the cell array (removeObjectsInRange beyond bounds). A
   mix of pinned and unpinned members isn't contiguous (the
   layout segregates pinned tabs to the front), so guard the
   run and bail instead of corrupting the cell list.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: resize the chip immediately after a
rename

The chip's width is derived from the group name, but
-renameTabGroupWithID: only updated colors, so the chip kept
its old width until some later relayout (e.g. switching away
from and back to the app). Re-derive and lay out the chips
synchronously after renaming so it resizes to the new name
right away.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: right-click chip context menu

Right-clicking a group chip now shows a group menu. The tab
bar's -menuForEvent: falls back to -chipForPoint: (chips are
invisible to -cellForPoint:) and asks the delegate via a new
optional -tabView:menuForTabGroup:. PseudoTerminal builds it
with:

- Rename Group            (prompt; fans the name out to
every member) - Duplicate Group          (duplicates each
member's sessions into a new
			    "<name> copy" group of the same
			    color)
- Move Group to New Window (reuses the drag tear-off) - Save
Group as Window Arrangement  (named arrangement of the
group's tabs) - Remove All Tabs from Group
(ungroups, keeping the tabs) - Close Group / Close Other
Tabs / Close Tabs to the Right
			    (one batch confirmation, then
			    close; skips pinned)

Also trim whitespace/newlines in the group-name prompt: tab
labels carry a trailing newline that was seeding the field
(and the saved name) with it.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: adopt the group's name/color when a
tab joins by drag

With the definition carried per member tab, a tab that
changed group membership by drag kept its old name/color:
resolveDroppedTabGroupMembership set the new tabGroupID but
not the denormalized name/color. Dragging a tab from group
G1 into G2 left it with id=G2 but name/color=G1, and if it
became G2's first tab the whole chip derived G1's
definition.

Add -reconcileTabGroupDefinitionForTab:, which copies
name/color from an existing member of the tab's group (or
clears them when it belongs to none), and call it wherever a
tab's tabGroupID changes: drag resolution, add-to-
existing-group, and remove-from-group.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: single source of truth on the tabs;
persist name/color; naming UI

Group state lived in two places: membership on
PTYTab.tabGroupID (persisted in the tab arrangement) and the
name/color in a per-window iTermTabGroupRegistry that was
never persisted. So restored groups kept their membership
but came back with empty names and default colors, and the
two stores could desync.

Move the whole definition onto the member tabs and delete
the registry:

- PTYTab gains tabGroupName and tabGroupColor, encoded in
the tab arrangement
  (color via the KEY_TAB_COLOR dictionary encoding). The
  definition now travels and persists with the tabs, so
  restoration works with no window-arrangement wiring and
  cross-window drag no longer needs to copy a definition.
- PseudoTerminal is the PSMTabGroupDataSource itself:
-tabGroupWithIdentifier:
  derives name/color from the first live tab carrying that
  id. updateTabGroups just pushes membership; there is
  nothing to prune (a group with no member tab simply has no
  source and vanishes).
- Deleted iTermTabGroupRegistry; iTermTabGroup is now a
plain value type.
  moveTabGroup:/tearOffTabGroup: dropped their group
  argument and the ensureGroup: copy.
- Group identity remains the tabGroupID (a UUID); names are
not assumed unique.
  Adding a tab to an existing group copies the definition
  from a member; rename fans the new name out to every
  member.

Naming UI: "New Group" now prompts for a name at creation
(NSAlert + text field), and a "Rename Group" context-menu
item renames the group. Removed the obsolete
registry/arrangement unit tests.

2026-08-13: Rework tab-group drag onto the single-tab drag
machinery

Group drag was a bespoke mouse-tracking loop plus a
hand-rolled reservation-gap preview, separate from how tabs
drag. It re-ran the stretch layout for the source hole
(instead of the fixed-width placeholder slide a tab drag
does) and kept glitching. Make a group a first-class block
inside PSMTabDragAssistant so it rides the exact same
placeholder + calculateDragAnimation + NSDraggingSession
path as a single tab; the hole, fixed-width slide,
drag-away-removes, cross-window, and tear-off all come from
that one proven path.

- Factored the drag-session tail of startDraggingCell: into
  -beginDragSessionForControl:... (shared by both starts).
- -startDraggingGroupWithChip:members:... seeds the sine
curve from the
  group's width and uses a run snapshot as the drag image;
  -distributePlaceholdersInTabBar:withDraggedGroupMembers:chip:
  replaces the whole contiguous run with ONE wide
  placeholder (the block analogue of the single-cell flank
  removal). mouseDragged: routes a chip drag here.
- At drop the assistant restores the source cells and
delegates the actual
  move:
  -tabView:dropGroupWithID:members:inTabBar:beforeTabViewItem:
  and -tabView:tearOffGroupWithID:members:atScreenPoint:,
  implemented in PseudoTerminal by the existing
  moveTabGroup:.../tearOffTabGroup:... (which already
  transfer the group definition and close an emptied source
  window). Same-window drops reorder in place.
  cancelDrag/finishDrag/dealloc clear the block state.
- Deleted the bespoke loop and gap preview
(trackGroupDragForChip:,
  moveTabGroupMembers:, groupDropBoundaryForPoint:, the
  whole updateGroupDropGapAtPoint:/step/apply/teardown/close
  gap machinery, the _groupDropGap* ivars, the
  availableCellWidthWithOverflow: reservation, and the
  tabBarControlForScreenPoint:/moveTabGroupWithIdentifier:/
  didReorderTabsInTabBar: delegate trio). Net -160 lines.

Kept the single-tab-into-a-group path
(groupContainingDropOfCell:, droppedInsideGroupID, the
front-of-group slot), which already rides the single-tab
machinery. Builds clean; PSMTabGroupCellTests and
iTermTabGroupTests green. Visual drag behavior still needs a
manual check.

2026-08-13: Factor shared drag-session start out of
startDraggingCell

Extract the drag-window creation, _dragTabOffset
computation, beginDraggingSessionWithItems, and
CVDisplayLink setup into
-beginDragSessionForControl:cell:image:mouseDownEvent:cellFrame:chipLeadingInset:.
Pure refactor (startDraggingCell: calls it with the same
values); prep for a group-drag start that reuses the same
tail.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: animated drop gap when dragging a
group into another window

While dragging a group's chip over another window's tab bar,
that bar now opens an animated gap where the group will
land, instead of appending silently: - The gap width is
reserved from -availableCellWidthWithOverflow: so the
  bar's real layout shrinks/redistributes the existing tabs
  (correct on stretched bars); the gap is a true space, not
  an overhang, sized to how wide the incoming tabs will be
  here (available*count/(existing+count) when stretching,
  else natural width * count).
- A timer animates it open/closed; it relayouts only when
the width or
  drop index actually changes.
- The chip-drag loop asks the delegate
(tabView:tabBarControlForScreenPoint:)
  for the bar under the cursor each frame and drives the gap
  there.
- On drop the group is inserted at the gap's index (not
appended); the gap
  is torn down without a restoring relayout so the insertion
  animates from the already-open state, and the destination
  snaps to its final layout to avoid intermediate
  per-tab-count animation glitches.
- The torn-off window is positioned from the drag snapshot's
top-left; the
  emptied source window is closed.

Also fix the drag snapshot being clipped 1pt short on the
bottom/right.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: drag a group out to a new or another
window

Dragging a group's chip out of the tab bar now moves the
whole group (chip + all member tabs, in order), following
the single-tab pattern via a new delegate hook the vendored
control calls with the group's tabs and the drop point: -
Dropped on another window's tab bar: the group moves there,
its
  definition (name/color) copied into that window's registry
  and each tab keeping its tabGroupID.
- Dropped outside any tab bar: the group tears off into a
new window,
  positioned from the drag snapshot's top-left like the
  single-tab tear-off.
- Moving the whole group out empties the source window,
which is closed.

Cross-window logic lives in PseudoTerminal
(terminalWithTabBarUnder..., moveTabGroup:tabs:toTerminal:,
tearOffTabGroup:tabs:atScreenPoint:) so the vendored control
stays window-agnostic.

2026-08-13: Tab groups: enforce contiguity, fix drag
decoration, drop-into-group

Contiguity invariant (all tabs of a group stay adjacent) is
now enforced regardless of how tabs reorder: -
-tabsDidReorder repairs any split via
-enforceTabGroupContiguityInvariant
  (stable per-group compaction; no-op when already
  contiguous), so drops, the Python API move, and keyboard
  moves are all safe.
- Keyboard cmd-shift-arrow reorders whole units (a group is
indivisible):
  moving toward a group jumps the whole group; a selected
  member moves the group. Ungrouped tabs behave exactly as
  before.
- The API tab-move routes through PseudoTerminal so it
triggers the repair.  - "Add Tab to Group" (existing group)
now repairs contiguity too.

Single-tab drag decoration: - The plain-tab drag image no
longer captures the run decoration (chip +
  outline): it is suppressed only during the offscreen image
  capture via a new suppressTabGroupRunDecoration flag.
- The run-decoration walk skips placeholder cells, so a
group's outline
  stays correct during a drag and expands to show the drop
  target when a tab is dragged into the group.

Drop a tab into a group (including a one-tab group): -
During a drag, a persistent drop slot is added between each
chip and its
  first member (the "join at front" target), alongside the
  existing before-group slot; a chip hover picks the side by
  the chip's midpoint so both are reachable from either
  direction.
- On drop, the assistant reports (chip-aware) whether the
tab landed inside
  a group's bracket; resolveDroppedTabGroupMembership joins
  it, else falls back to the order-only rule (leave /
  reorder / keep-lone).
- didDropTabViewItem now also fires on a membership change
even when the
  tab index is unchanged.

Diagnostic tabGroup: logging is retained for now.

2026-08-13: Add intra-window chip drag to move a whole tab
group

Dragging a group chip now moves the chip plus its contiguous
member tabs as a block to a new position within the same tab
bar.

- New -chipForPoint: hit-tests chips (which -cellForPoint:
skips).  - -mouseDragged: detects a drag starting on a chip
and runs a
  self-contained tracking loop
  (-trackGroupDragForChip:startPoint:), dispatched before
  the single-tab path and before window-drag conversion (a
  chip sits in the top edge strip). The loop shows a
  semi-transparent snapshot of the run following the cursor
  and a group-color insertion marker at the nearest valid
  drop boundary.
- Valid boundaries are between top-level units (an ungrouped
tab or a
  whole group run), so a group never lands inside another
  group; boundaries inside the dragged run are excluded.
  Releasing well outside the bar cancels.
- On drop the member tabs move as a block; _cells is
re-sorted to the
  new tab order and chips re-derived. Membership is
  unchanged, so this uses a new optional delegate hook
  tabView:didReorderTabsInTabBar: (PseudoTerminal ->
  tabsDidReorder) rather than the single-tab drop path,
  which would run per-tab membership resolution.

Intra-window, land-on-drop only; cross-window and live
per-frame reflow are deferred.

2026-08-13: Fix Compact/vertical tab-group run decoration

Share the squared tab-group decoration across Yosemite,
Dark, Compact, and Minimal (Minimal drops its no-op override
and inherits the base), and make it read correctly when tabs
fill the whole bar as they do in Compact and Yosemite:

- Tabs in a chip run now fill the full bar width. The chip
width path
  capped per-tab width at cellMaxWidth while the no-chips
  path has no such cap, so a grouped run shrank its tabs;
  drop the cap to match.
- The enclosing box is clamped to the bar bounds, its
trailing edge
  pulled in 1pt so the tab-bar/content divider a neighbor
  paints after us does not overpaint the stroke, and framed
  on whole pixels so the 1pt lines stay crisp and
  single-color.
- The trailing outset is gone so the box's far line sits
flush with the
  tabs' edge, and the chip's solid block bottom aligns with
  the box so the divider reads the same under the chip and
  under the tabs.
- On a vertical bar the chip is a one-row header, so it now
takes the
  normal tab-row height instead of the name-capsule height
  that made it dwarf the tabs. Drag/placeholder frames route
  through the same heightOfTabGroupChipCell: so the chip
  stays consistent while dragging.

2026-08-13: Draw the tab-group outline on vertical bars
(Tahoe)

Extend the Tahoe group-run decoration to vertical tab bars:
the same design rotated 90 degrees. The name capsule spans
the bar width at the top of the run (name left-aligned), the
group's tab pills stack below, a 1pt group-color outline
encloses the whole run with a 1pt gap around the tabs, and a
group-color connector below the name capsule hugs the first
(top) tab.

The vertical chip cell now reserves a style-aware height
(new -tabGroupChipCellHeightForName:, mirroring
-tabGroupChipCellWidthForName: for horizontal);
PSMTabBarControl uses -heightOfTabGroupChipCell: in the
vertical layout and cell setup so the reserved space matches
what the style draws.

2026-08-13: Draw a group outline around tab-group runs
(Regular/Tahoe, horizontal)

Replace the per-cell group chip with a run-level decoration
for the non-Minimal styles: a colored (group-color) pill
enclosing the whole run of a group's tabs, with the group
name in a capsule at the left. The enclosing pill spans
multiple cells, so styles draw it in -drawTabBar: via a new
hook; the chip cell itself then draws nothing.

New optional PSMTabStyle methods:
-usesExternalTabGroupDecoration (the chip cell defers to the
style when YES),
-drawTabGroupRunDecorationsForTabBar:clipRect:, and
-tabGroupChipCellWidthForName: (reserve extra width for the
name capsule plus the colored area between it and the first
tab).

Tahoe (the macOS 26 default): the 1pt group-color outline
clears the tab pills by 1pt; the name capsule is filled with
the tab-bar background with a 1pt group-color margin; the
colored area between the name capsule and the first tab has
a concave right edge that hugs the first tab's curve with a
1pt gap (4pt name-to-tab spacing); dividers touching a chip
or a grouped tab are suppressed so they don't cross the
outline. Yosemite gets a simpler reasonable version. Minimal
opts out and keeps its basic chip (its design is pending).
Horizontal only; vertical is still to come.

2026-08-13: Fix Minimal vertical tab background bleeding
into the overflow area

In a vertical Minimal tab bar with an overflow button, the
strip below the last visible tab was painted lighter than
the tabs, so the last tab appeared to bleed into the
overflow-button area. Two causes in the end inset (the area
after the last tab):

- Its frame, when the overflow button was visible, covered
only the
  button's slab, leaving the gap between the last tab and
  the button (from tabs not dividing the bar height evenly)
  unpainted, so it fell through to the lighter
  selected-background base. Extend it to cover everything
  below the last tab, to the bottom.

- Its color used the horizontal "selected tab scrolled into
overflow"
  branch, which is the lighter selected color. For a
  vertical bar the empty area should read as the sidebar's
  tab background, so use the non-selected tab color.

2026-08-13: Resolve tab-group membership on drop and keep
chips consecutive

A drag reorders tabs without changing membership, so a tab
dropped inside a group's run split it (a second chip
appeared) and a member dragged to the edge stayed grouped.
Resolve the dropped tab's membership from where it landed:
strictly inside a run joins that group, a member isolated
from a multi-tab group leaves it, and a lone one-tab group
is kept. Only the dropped tab changes, so a bystander is
never absorbed. The logic is a pure, unit-tested helper
(iTermTabGroupContiguity); PseudoTerminal calls it from the
drop path and pushes the result to the bar.

Fix the chip rendering the drop then exposed:

- On drop the chip was re-derived with a zero frame and
drawn collapsed
  (blink) or mid-animation (jump). Seed a freshly created
  chip with a valid frame in normalizeTabGroupChipCells, and
  lay the bar out synchronously on drop
  (updateWithoutAnimation cancels the in-flight animation so
  frames are final before the first draw).

- Dragging the sole member of a group left nothing to anchor
the chip, so
  it vanished for the drag. Carry the chip in the AppKit
  drag image, captured together with the tab from the bar so
  its alignment matches, and shift the drag window so the
  tab stays under the cursor.

2026-08-13: Keep tab-group chips visible during a drag

Chips are first-class cells but the drag machinery assumes
_cells is a pure alternating placeholder/tab list, so chips
were stripped for the whole drag and blinked out. Instead,
keep the index math in the pure tab-cell world (strip chips
before drop/cancel, re-derive in finishDrag) but re-insert
chips for display right after the placeholder math runs, so
they stay on screen while dragging.

Along the way, fix four issues this surfaced:

- Dead-zone jitter: cellForPoint skips chips and returns nil
over a chip
  or the intercell gap around one, which the target
  selection read as "past the end" and flung the drop target
  to the last placeholder, oscillating. Resolve an unhit
  point positionally instead.
- Group-creation overflow: the chip width-distribution
dropped the last
  tab (leaving a full-width gap) when floating-point
  rounding pushed the exact-fit sum a hair over available.
  Allow a sub-point tolerance.
- Drag-start blip: the chip was drawn at a stale origin
until the
  animation timer's first tick; lay the source bar out
  synchronously at drag start so the first frame is already
  correct.
- Drag-start shift: a placeholder standing in for the
dragged tab added
  no trailing spacing, shifting following tabs one
  intercell-spacing left. Scale a placeholder's trailing
  spacing by its expansion so a full-width slot matches the
  tab it replaces and a collapsed one adds none (no growth
  jump).

New pure helper cellsByInsertingDragChipsInto:controlView:
(placeholders transparent to run detection) with unit tests.

2026-08-13: Let tabs stretch to fill on a grouped horizontal
bar

P3 routed any horizontal bar with chip cells through the
natural-width scrollable layout to avoid the
fit-distribution math, which disabled stretch-to-fill
whenever a group was present. Instead, handle chip cells in
the fit path:
cellWidthsForHorizontalArrangementWithChipsWithOverflow:
keeps chip and pinned cells at their fixed width and shares
the remaining space among the tab cells, which stretch when
stretchCellsToFit is on (clamped to optimal/min/max
otherwise), returning a contiguous prefix so the overflow
menu still works. The no-chips path is unchanged.

2026-08-13: Center the chip label vertically using font
metrics

The label still rode high because line-box centering ignores
the font's leading/descent. Center on the cap height instead
(textTop = midY - ascender + capHeight/2, in the flipped
control). Also drop the bottom-gap-push (which shoved the
capsule to the top of the cell) in favor of a symmetric
vertical margin: the chip cell is 28px and the capsule is
inset 5px vertically, so it's centered in the row with a
clear gap above and below the neighboring tabs.

2026-08-13: Fix vertical chip: contain the label, left-align
it, add a bottom gap

Three issues on the vertical bar's chip cell: - The 18px
chip made the capsule interior (~12px) shorter than the
label's
  line height (~14px), so the text overflowed and rode the
  top ("not contained"). Raise the chip cell to 26px and
  inset the capsule less vertically so the label fits and
  centers.
- The label was centered while the tab labels below are
left-aligned;
  left-align it so they line up.
- The chip sat flush against the tab below it. Leave a
bottom gap: the
  cell draw shrinks the chip's draw frame by
  verticalChipBottomGap on a vertical bar (the control is
  flipped, so that empties the bottom).

2026-08-13: Tidy the basic chip draw: centered text, no
clipped capsule

The basic chip look carried over insets from the old overlay
(1px horizontal inset + a 4px trailing gap) that, on a
full-width vertical chip cell, pushed the rounded ends
against the edges and left the label left-aligned. Inset the
capsule 4px horizontally, drop the trailing gap, and center
the label (truncating tail if it doesn't fit).

2026-08-13: Make tab-group chips first-class cells; delete
the overlay/gap approach (P2+P3)

Chips are now real cells in _cells instead of overlay
PSMTabGroupChipView subviews positioned into reserved
phantom gaps. This removes the whole class of gap-vs-drag
bugs at the source.

P2 (tolerate + insert): - normalizeTabGroupChipCells
rebuilds _cells so one chip cell precedes
  each contiguous run of same-group tabs; called on
  membership change (setTabGroupIdentifier:) and after the
  tab<->cell reconciler, which now strips chips first and
  re-derives them so its tabView-index math stays correct.
  removeAllTabGroupChipCells is the strip helper.
- sanityCheck: and the update: count guard now compare only
tab cells;
  cellForPoint: skips chips (not
  clickable/draggable/droppable).

P3 (size + draw + delete old): - Chip cells are sized in
naturalHorizontalCellWidths and the vertical
  origins/_setupCells (short height); a horizontal bar with
  chips uses the natural-width (scrollable) layout so chips
  avoid the shrink-to-fit math.
- PSMTabBarCell draws chip cells via +[PSMTabGroupChipView
drawChipInFrame:
  name:color:] (reused basic look; per-style hook later).
- Deleted tabGroupLeadingGapForCellAtIndex:,
totalTabGroupGap, the
  availableCellWidth/scrollExtent/_setupCells/vertical gap
  insertions, the drag-time gap suppression, and the
  updateTabGroupChips/positionChipForGroup/
  removeTabGroupChipsNotInSet/_tabGroupChips overlay
  machinery.

Not yet done (P4): drag/drop and moveTabAtIndex: still use
_cells indices as tabView indices, and placeholder
distribution assumes no chip cells -- so DRAGGING grouped
tabs misbehaves until P4. Creating groups and chip rendering
(the layout) are what to test now.

2026-08-13: Tab-group chip cells: cell type + pure helpers +
tests (P1)

First step of making group chips first-class cells instead
of overlay views with reserved gaps. Adds
PSMTabBarCell.isTabGroupChip (inert like a placeholder -- no
representedObject -- but persistent) and three pure,
window-free helpers on PSMTabBarControl:

- +cellsByInsertingTabGroupChipsInto:controlView: inserts
one chip cell
  before each contiguous run of same-group tabs.
- +cellIndexForTabIndex:inCells: /
+tabIndexForCellIndex:inCells: convert
  between NSTabView index space and cell-list index space,
  skipping chips (the seam the drag/drop and move code will
  use).

9 XCTest cases cover normalization (runs, adjacency,
non-contiguous, stray-chip input) and the index conversions
incl. round-trip. Not wired into the live _cells yet, so
nothing changes at runtime; that's P2+.

2026-08-13: Suppress group gaps and chips during a tab drag

The reserved chip gap is invisible to the drag machinery
(placeholder distribution, drop hit-testing, the reorder
animation), so during a drag it threw the drop target off
and jerked the chip to wild spots (a log showed the chip at
y=-18, off-screen; drop slots opened far from the cursor,
and a tab slid under the chip).

While [PSMTabDragAssistant isDragging], make
tabGroupLeadingGapForCellAtIndex: return 0 (the whole layout
is gapless, so the drag behaves exactly as it does with no
groups) and hide the chips instead of repositioning them
onto shifting cells. The post-drop layout restores gaps and
re-shows the chips. The visible cost is a one-time reflow at
drag start/end (gaps collapse, chips hide; then snap back),
which is predictable rather than the previous jitter.

2026-08-13: Revert gap/chip changes to the drag reorder
animation

Making calculateDragAnimationForTabBar: reserve the group
gap and call -updateTabGroupChips created three drag
artifacts (seen in a debug log where the chip flip-flopped
between y=78 and y=122 every frame):

1. The chip jumped a row during a drag: the drag-assistant
chip re-place
   fought reallyUpdate's, positioning the chip from two
   different cell layouts 44pt apart.
2. A dark "hole" flickered in: the empty reserved gap
showing through as
   the chip oscillated.
3. The drop target was reluctant to open: the gap inserted
into the drag
   walk shifted the cell frames the drop hit-test relies on.

Revert both drag-walk changes. During a drag the chip now
stays at its steady position and the drop logic is
untouched; the group gap simply collapses for the duration
of the drag and is restored by reallyUpdate on drop. The
steady-state gap and the separate _animateCells fix are
kept.  Doing the during-drag gap "right" needs a single chip
positioner while dragging; deferred.

2026-08-13: Stop tab-group defs vanishing on drag; resync
membership on reorder

A debug log showed a window whose tabs referenced three
group ids but whose registry held only one: dragging a
grouped tab so it briefly left [self tabs] made its group
momentarily unreferenced, and updateTabGroups (which runs
constantly via updateTabColors) then pruned the definition.
When the tab returned it was orphaned -- id present,
name/color gone, no chip -- which read as "groups vanished /
weird gaps."

Remove that prune from updateTabGroups. Group defs are tiny;
keep them for the window's life (a def that outlives its
tabs is inert: no run, no chip). Also call updateTabGroups
from tabsDidReorder so the tab bar's per-cell group ids
track the new order instead of going stale after a drag.
Cross-window group transfer and a real cleanup/persistence
pass remain separate follow-ups.

2026-08-13: Keep group gaps and chips aligned during
animations

The two animated cell-position walks ignored the group-chip
gap and never moved the chips, so gaps/chips lagged during
add/remove and drag.

- _animateCells: (add/remove width animation) now re-places
the chips
  each frame and after its final layout; the gap is already
  preserved by the incremental walk, so the chips just need
  to track the cells.
- calculateDragAnimationForTabBar: (the drag reorder
animation) now
  reserves the same per-run gap the steady-state layout uses
  and calls -updateTabGroupChips after repositioning cells,
  so tabs keep their group spacing and the chips move with
  them while dragging.

Exposes -tabGroupLeadingGapForCellAtIndex: and
-updateTabGroupChips on PSMTabBarControl for the drag
assistant. Does not address the parked drop-into-a-group
contiguity bug.

2026-08-13: Instrument tab groups with RLog for debugging

Add RLog across the tab-group paths so the drag/contiguity
behavior is visible in a debug log without enabling debug
logging: membership changes pushed to a cell, the per-cell
group order + which runs got chips + total reserved gap
(gated on any grouped cell so the no-groups case stays
quiet), chip create/skip/frame and removal, the window-level
membership push + registry contents, the New Group /
add-to-existing / remove menu actions, the tab->group order
after a reorder, and the drop event (dragged/target group
ids + destination index). Per-cell gap math stays on
DLog/uninstrumented to avoid RLogging the layout hot path.

2026-08-13: Show tab-group chips on the vertical tab bar

The chip now renders on vertical bars too, above each run
instead of to its left. The gap logic is generalized to the
layout axis: the per-run gap is a chip width on a horizontal
bar and a fixed chip height (+[PSMTabGroupChipView
verticalChipHeight]) on a vertical one. On the vertical bar
the gap is baked into the cell y-origins (scroll and fit
branches) and added to the vertical scroll content extent;
the chip is positioned spanning the bar width just above the
run's first cell (the control is flipped, so "above" is a
smaller y). availableCellWidth only subtracts the gap for
horizontal bars, where it's a width.

2026-08-13: Reserve a leading gap for the tab-group chip

The chip now sits in reserved space instead of overlapping
the run's first tab. The gap width (per run, from the group
name via +[PSMTabGroupChipView preferredWidthForName:]) is
threaded through the horizontal layout: subtracted from
-availableCellWidthWithOverflow: so fit mode shrinks tabs to
make room and the scroll overflow test accounts for it,
added to _scrollContentExtent so a scrolled bar reaches the
end, and inserted into the -_setupCells: x-walk before each
run's first cell.  The chip is then positioned in that gap,
immediately left of the first cell. Horizontal only;
vertical still skips chips.

Known follow-ups: the width-animation path (_animateCells:)
doesn't add gaps yet (transient only), and chips aren't
culled outside the scroll viewport.

2026-08-13: Draw a basic tab-group chip, styled per theme

Add PSMTabGroupChipView: a name+color chip the control
positions at the leading edge of each contiguous run of tabs
sharing a group id (-updateTabGroupChips, run from
-reallyUpdate: so chips track scrolling).  Appearance is
delegated to the current tab style via a new optional
PSMTabStyle hook
(drawTabGroupChipWithName:color:selected:frame:inControl:),
with a basic rounded-pill default when a style doesn't
override, so Tahoe/Yosemite/Minimal can each render it
differently. The hook receives the control, whose
orientation lets a style diverge horizontal vs vertical.

v0 scope: horizontal bars only (the vertical bar wants its
own design, so chips are skipped there for now), and the
chip sits at the run's leading edge without reserving a gap
yet.

2026-08-13: Add Firefox-style "Add Tab to Group" tab context
menu

Right-clicking a tab now offers "Add Tab to Group" with "New
Group" (creates a group named from the tab's title, colored
from a cycling palette, and assigns the tab), the existing
groups (checkmarked when the tab is already a member), and
"Remove Tab from Group" when grouped.  Actions set
PTYTab.tabGroupID and refresh via updateTabColors, which
pushes membership to the tab bar and prunes emptied groups.
Rendering of the group chip comes next; until then this is
verifiable via the menu (a new group appears checkmarked and
the Remove item shows up).

2026-08-13: Own the tab-group registry on the window and
push membership

PseudoTerminal now lazily owns an iTermTabGroupRegistry and
sets it as the tab bar's tabGroupDataSource. A new
-updateTabGroups (piggybacked on -updateTabColors, which
already runs whenever tab membership or order can change)
pushes each tab's tabGroupID to the control and prunes group
definitions no tab references. No rendering or group
creation yet.

2026-08-13: Add per-tab tab-group membership
(PTYTab.tabGroupID)

A tab now records which group it belongs to, the source of
truth for membership. Persisted in the tab arrangement under
a new "Tab Group ID" key and round-tripped on restore. The
window controller will push this to the tab bar
(setTabGroupIdentifier:forTabViewItem:) and own the registry
that maps the id to a name/color.

2026-08-13: Define the tab-bar/tab-group ownership boundary
via protocols

The tab bar needs a group's name/color to draw its chip, but
must not own or even reference the concrete group model
(which lives on the window controller). Draw that line with
two protocols defined by the control:

- PSMTabGroup: a group's display attributes (name, color).
- PSMTabGroupDataSource: look up a group by identifier.

PSMTabBarControl gains a weak tabGroupDataSource and,
mirroring the tabColor precedent, per-tab membership
push/read (setTabGroupIdentifier:forTabViewItem: + getter)
backed by a new PSMTabBarCell.tabGroupIdentifier. The
control forms chip runs from contiguous cells sharing an
identifier and pulls attributes through the data source; it
never sees iTermTabGroup/iTermTabGroupRegistry.

iTermTabGroup conforms to PSMTabGroup and
iTermTabGroupRegistry to PSMTabGroupDataSource. No rendering
yet.

2026-08-13: Add tab group model and per-window registry

Groundwork for Chrome/Firefox-style tab groups.
iTermTabGroup holds a group's identity (UUID id, name,
NSColor) and round-trips through a window arrangement using
the same color encoding as KEY_TAB_COLOR.
iTermTabGroupRegistry is the per-window store of group
definitions keyed by id, with add/lookup/remove, orphan
pruning, and arrangement round-trip. Membership (which tabs
are in a group) is not stored here; it will live per-tab. No
UI yet.

2026-08-13: Cache announcement text measurements to fix a
tab-style beachball

Changing the tab style preference posts
kRefreshTerminalNotification, which synchronously refreshes
every window and re-fits every session's announcement view.
Each fit re-ran an O(font-range) size search plus a
truncation binary search, and every sizeThatFits allocated a
fresh NSLayoutManager/NSTextContainer/NSTextStorage. With
many sessions open this blocked the main thread for seconds.

AutoResizingTextView now reuses a single measuring layout
stack and memoizes (content, width) to size, and
setFrameSize skips the refit when the size is unchanged.
Adds unit tests for the cache behavior and the no-op-resize
guard.

2026-08-13: Fix top-right indicator smear when swiping
between tabs

The top-right session indicators were anchored to the text
view's visibleRect right edge. While swiping between tabs
the text view is horizontally clipped by the tab view, so
visibleRect shifts every frame. Because the live session
keeps redrawing during the swipe and the indicators are
composited translucently, anchoring to the shifting visible
edge painted them at a different content x each frame and
left a horizontal smear. Anchor them to the content (bounds)
right edge instead, which is identical in the unclipped case
but keeps them glued to their tab's content during a swipe.

2026-08-07: Extend scrollable tab bar to horizontal
orientations

The scrollable tab bar previously worked only for left/right
(vertical) tab bars. Generalize the scroll machinery to be
axis-neutral so top/bottom (horizontal) bars scroll too.

- Axis-neutral scroll offset, leading margin, viewport
length,
  content extent, and clamping; scrollWheel handles a
  trackpad horizontal swipe and shift+vertical wheel for
  horizontal bars.
- New horizontal natural-width layout branch that scrolls
instead
  of overflowing. Honors the "uneven tab widths allowed"
  setting: equal-width tabs use a uniform width, uneven tabs
  use natural widths with a floor. A new advanced setting,
  "Tab width when the tab bar is scrollable" (default 120),
  controls that width, distinct from the non-scrollable
  optimum width.
- drawRect clips cells to the scrollable region and repaints
the
  leading decoration band and trailing add-button gap so
  scrolled cells neither show under the window decorations
  nor draw over the button. Minimal keeps its selected-tab
  notch and bar-edge line correct at the button edge,
  including a cap border when the active tab straddles it.
- Fix a Yosemite margin-fill that painted nothing for an
empty
  clip rect, and extend Minimal's start/end insets across
  the scroll margins so the decoration color fills them (no
  bare or double-drawn strips on a transparent window).
- Drag assistant lays cells out at their scrolled positions.

2026-08-07: Fix fullscreen appearance-change refresh loop;
add loop instrumentation

safeSetAppearance: now skips assigning the window's
appearance when it already equals the requested value. A
redundant assignment still makes AppKit recompute effective
appearance and fire -viewDidChangeEffectiveAppearance, which
calls back into refreshTerminal: and could spin for many
seconds (worst in traditional fullscreen, where each pass
does a full-window setFrame:display:YES).

Also add RLog statements along the effective-appearance ->
refreshTerminal: path (refreshTerminal: entry,
viewDidChangeEffectiveAppearance, and
rootTerminalViewDidChangeEffectiveAppearance) so the loop
can be confirmed or ruled out from a retrospective debug
log.

2026-08-06: Improve the scrollable side tab bar and make it
a real setting

Follow-up to #721:

- Move the toggle out of Advanced Settings into a real
preference
  (Settings > Appearance,
  kPreferenceKeyScrollableSideTabBar) with a checkbox in
  PreferencePanel.xib.
- Drag-to-reorder honors the scroll offset (new
verticalScrollOffset
  accessor) so dragging while scrolled no longer snaps cells
  or drops on the wrong index.
- Newly created tabs, and any tab that becomes active,
scroll fully
  into view below the window decorations rather than under
  them.
- Keep tab content and accessory subviews (spinner, progress
bar) from
  drawing under the window decorations, using a drawing clip
  rather than clipsToBounds so window transparency is
  preserved.
- Re-sync hover/highlight after scrolling so cells don't
stay stuck
  highlighted when they move out from under the cursor.

2026-08-06: Add an option to make the side tab bar
scrollable (#721)

Adds an advanced setting that makes the left/right
(vertical) tab bar scrollable. When on, tabs that don't fit
stay in the bar and are reached by scrolling instead of the
"More tabs" overflow menu.  2026-08-06: Warn that closing a
workgroup session takes the workgroup with it

Closing any member of a workgroup drives the whole instance
through teardown (sessionWillTerminate -> exit -> teardown),
ending its peers and other tabs. The close confirmation only
described the one tab's own jobs, so the user got no warning
that the rest of the workgroup would close too.

Add
iTermWorkgroupInstance.closeCascadeWarning(forSessions:),
which names the workgroup and counts the live members that
aren't already in the closing set, and append it to the
confirmation via a new additionalMessage: parameter on
confirmCloseForSessions:. Wired into the tab-close and
pane-close paths; the whole-window close path is left alone
since it already closes everything.

Also fix makeWorkgroupPeer's comment, which claimed peers
should never prompt on close while the code (correctly)
forces PROMPT_ALWAYS; it now matches the
WorkgroupSessionSpawner rationale.

2026-08-06: Spawn nested workgroup peers from the entry
session

Peers of a tab/split-hosted peer group were spawned with the
host's immediate config-parent as their basis. That equals
the main session only when the peer host is a direct child
of root; for a peer group nested two or more levels deep it
was an intermediate spawned pane, so those peers inherited
the wrong profile, working directory, and size.

Spawn nested peers from mainSession (falling back to the
config-parent only if the main session went away mid-spawn),
so every peer inherits the session the workgroup was opened
on regardless of nesting depth -- e.g. a workgroup opened on
a remote host keeps its peers on that host.  At depth 1 the
two are the same session, so behavior there is unchanged.

Adds a depth-2 test asserting nested peers spawn from the
entry session.

2026-08-06: Refresh a workgroup peer-host's toolbar after
wiring its nested port

A workgroup .tab or .split child that hosts its own peer
group came up with no toolbar and no peer switcher. The
host's toolbar is applied when its tab/split is inserted
into the window, which happens inside the spawner call
before the nested peer port and the host's workgroupInstance
back-pointer exist, so desiredToolbarItems returns nil then.
Nothing re-applied it once the port was built, so the empty
toolbar stuck.

registerNonPeerOrPeerGroupHost now refreshes the host's
desired toolbar items after attachBackPointers (which sets
workgroupInstance synchronously for the pre-fulfilled host
promise), mirroring the refresh registerNonPeer already does
for the leaf non-peer case. The host then resolves through
its nested port to the real toolbar, and the peer switcher
appears.

Adds a tab-hosted nested-peer-group fixture and data-model
test covering the two-tabs-each-with-peers shape from the
report.

2026-08-06: Fix blank pane when a workgroup tears down while
a non-main peer is shown

A workgroup's main peer group shares a single tab pane; only
the active member's view is installed and the others are
buried. teardown() terminates every non-leader peer via
peerPort.invalidate(). If the pane was showing a non-leader
peer at that point, its now-dead view was left in the tab,
rendering blank (issue 12967). This was reachable by closing
a sibling tab (any member's termination drives the whole
workgroup through teardown) or by Exit Workgroup while a
peer was shown.

teardown now swaps the surviving leader back into the shared
pane via revealAsPeerWithoutActivatingWindow before
invalidating the peers, unless the visible member is itself
the terminating session (that pane's own tab is the one
closing, so there is no surviving tab to restore into). The
terminating session is threaded through exit(instance:) and
teardown().

2026-08-06: Grow left/right Minimal tabs to match top tab
height

Left and right tabs in the Minimal theme used
defaultTabBarHeight (24) while top Minimal tabs use
compactMinimalTabBarHeight (38), so each side tab was too
short to render a subtitle without looking cramped. Give
side Minimal tabs the same height as top Minimal tabs.

This is scoped to the Minimal theme and routes around
shouldHaveTallTabBar (which also drives stoplight
positioning that is specific to the top bar).

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: add DragAndDrop sources to the Xcode
project

Adds the sources/DragAndDrop/ KittyDnD files to the
iTerm2SharedARC target.  Consolidated here after rebasing
onto an advanced origin/master, whose project.pbxproj had
diverged; the file references were re-added in one step via
tools/add_file_to_xcodeproj.rb. Build and the full KittyDnD
test suites pass on the rebased base.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD drag-out: fix external/cross-session
drops, scope EPERM correctly

Three fixes found while testing real drag-out end to end:

- Finder (and other strict destinations) rejected the drop
because the drag
  mixed a file-URL item with a separate non-file text item;
  such destinations accept only all-file drags, while in-app
  destinations tolerated the mixed pasteboard (so a drop
  into another terminal worked but Finder did not).  When
  the offer includes files the drag is now file-only.

- The self-drag EPERM guard was scoped to the OS window,
refusing legitimate
  drops between two different programs in split panes of one
  window. The spec scopes it to the same kitty window, i.e.
  a program dropping its OWN drag onto its OWN session to
  read local files. That is exactly the per-session
  selfDragInProgress flag, so the OS-window-number check
  (originWindowNumber / sameWindowSelfDrag) is removed. A
  drop from any other session, split pane or separate
  window, is now served; a same-session self-drop is still
  EPERM.

- dragOperationForSender left numberOfValidItemsForDrop at
the caller's -1
  initializer in its "no valid drop" branch, so AppKit drew
  a bogus -1 count badge on the drag while it hovered over
  an area that could not accept it (e.g. a drag-out file
  passing back over our own window). It now reports 0.

Also adds DLog diagnostics along the drag-out path (the
gesture gate, the offer/start-drag handling, and the drag
host's beginDrag), which had no logging for field debugging.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: test the resource-guard and
stream-interruption paths

Add coverage for guards that existed but were unverified, so
a regression that dropped or mis-scoped one would fail CI:

- The 64-request EMFILE concurrent-entry cap and its "end
the drop" side
  effect.
- Interrupting an in-flight streamed file with a new drag:
the m=0
  terminator is emitted (exactly once) and no further chunks
  follow.
- The optimistic-operation security default: copy+move and
empty/unknown
  masks report copy, only move-only reports move.
- KittyDnDRemoteDragFetch in isolation with small injected
limits and a
  short timeout: the maxEntries breaches (start fan-out,
  per-push, and directory inflation), the maxBytes breach,
  the idle-timeout abort, and the drain-before-delete
  invariant (a finish while a write is in flight defers the
  outcome until the write completes).
- The reassembler over-cap abandon/drain/recover path, via a
new injectable
  cap on KittyDnDChunkReassembler (defaulting to the 512 MB
  static) so it can be driven without allocating half a
  gigabyte.
- The PNG decompression-bomb guard: a valid solid-color
image just over the
  decoded-pixel cap is rejected with EFBIG (the byte cap
  would otherwise be tripped first and never reach it).
- Tightened weak error-code assertions to check the specific
code (ENOENT /
  EINVAL) rather than just the presence of a t=R.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: clear stuck drop targets when Option
released mid-hover

Follow-up to the Option escape hatch: if the user held
Option during a kitty-routed hover (drawing the legacy
drop-target affordance) and then released it without
dropping, draggingUpdated: fell through to the program
branch without clearing showDropTargets, so the affordance
stayed on screen until drag-exit or drop. Clear it (with a
redraw) in that branch.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: token codeName + test-harness
conformance

- Add XTERMCC_KITTY_DND to VT100Token codeName so OSC 72
tokens print by
  name in debug logging instead of as a raw type number.
- drag_source.py now reports per-entry errors with the
spec's client form
  t=E (t=R is a terminal-to-client type); iTerm2 accepts
  both.
- drop_target.py treats a metadata-light continuation chunk
(only m, per the
  spec) as part of the open response sequence instead of
  dropping it.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: make Option escape hatch reachable,
fix image-drop UI

- During a kitty-routed hover, while Option is held
draggingUpdated: now
  advertises the legacy operation (the routing latch stays,
  so the program still gets hover updates). Without this, a
  program that rejected the drop made the drag spring back
  so performDragOperation: never fired, and the "hold Option
  while dropping" escape hatch could never engage.
- legacyDraggingOperationForSender: now shows drop targets
only when there is
  actually a valid drop. The view registers PNG/TIFF
  pasteboard types for kitty DnD, so an image-only drag with
  no accepting program would otherwise light up drop-target
  affordances for a drop that cannot land.
- Corrected the draggingEntered: comment that still
described the pre-
  optimistic "none until the program replies" behavior.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: harden accept/offer state machine
from review

Accept-drop correctness and safety:

- osDragOperation now reports 0 whenever the program is not
accepting drops.
  Otherwise, after the program sent t=A or t=a:x=2 (or reset
  ran), the optimistic branch still advertised copy/move
  while performDrop discarded the drop, so the OS and source
  saw a successful drop the program never received; a
  move-only source could delete its only copy.
- A drop-context change (new drag, drop, completion, or
reset) now closes an
  in-flight streamed file response with its m=0 terminator
  before it sends the interrupting escape, so a client is
  not left hanging on a half-open chunk sequence and the new
  escape cannot concatenate onto it.
- A present-but-unparseable directory handle (Y) or
sub-index (y) is now
  EINVAL echoing the raw key, instead of silently falling
  through to a plain MIME read the client cannot match.
- An out-of-bounds MIME index that also carries a sub-index
now echoes that
  sub-index in the ENOENT response so a pipelining client
  can match it.

DoS bounds:

- A t=a / t=o MIME-type list is now capped (64 KB, 4096
tokens) before it is
  split, and refused with EFBIG, so a hostile payload up to
  the reassembler cap cannot amplify into gigabytes of token
  Strings.
- A remote drag-out uri-list is parsed only up to the fetch
entry limit
  (line by line, not fully materialized first), and the
  fetch refuses more top-level entries than its limit before
  the fan-out rather than after it.
- An image/* MIME payload used only as a drag thumbnail now
passes the same
  decoded-pixel-count cap as pre-sent t=p thumbnails, so a
  small compressed bomb cannot force a multi-GB bitmap
  allocation in AppKit.

Protocol conformance:

- A bare t=E (no y) from the client before the drag starts
now replies
  EINVAL and aborts the offer, per the spec, instead of
  being ignored.
- A per-entry client error in the spec's t=E form (addressed
by x or Y:y)
  during a remote drag-out now skips just that entry,
  matching the existing tolerance of the t=R extension form.

Robustness:

- The chunker indexes from the payload's own startIndex, so
a future caller
  passing a non-zero-based Data slice cannot crash on the
  range.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: report move optimistically only for a
move-only offer

The optimistic pre-reply operation returned move whenever
the offered mask lacked the copy bit, which for an empty or
unexpected mask (op == 0 or anything without bit 1) meant a
destructive move. Invert the test so copy is the default:
report move only when the source offers move without copy,
and copy for every other mask. This keeps the reachable
copy/move/copy+move cases identical while guaranteeing an
unknown value can never optimistically authorize a move the
OS could act on before the program confirms.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: serialize entry responses; optimistic
op prefers copy

Follow-ups from the review of the async-streaming change:

- Making the file send async (yield periodically) let two
concurrent
  filesystem-entry responses interleave their t=r chunks on
  the pty, which the reassembler concatenates into one
  corrupt stream. Serialize entry responses through a single
  chain so each is emitted contiguously. (The small
  synchronous uri-list / plain-MIME sends stay synchronous;
  the narrow remaining pipelined-mixed-drop case is
  documented.)
- The optimistic pre-reply drop operation now prefers copy
(non-
  destructive), falling back to move only for a move-only
  source, so a move the OS acts on before the program
  confirms cannot delete the source's only copy.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: honor Option at drop time; clear
stale gesture on mouseDown

- performDragOperation re-checks Option at drop time and, if
held, sends
  the program a leave and diverts to the legacy paste/upload
  path, matching the release note's "hold Option while
  dropping" (macOS resolves drag modifiers continuously
  until release).
- performDragOperation reads the source's captured origin
window number for
  the self-drag check.
- mouseDownImpl notifies the bridge to forget a stale
pending gesture (if a
  prior gesture's mouseUp was swallowed by a modal panel or
  menu tracking), so a program's t=P cannot start a phantom
  drag from it.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: more DoS/correctness fixes to
controller and remote fetch

- t=p pre-send is gated on an active declared offer (both
the data and the
  image branches), and each image is size-capped at store
  time, so output the user merely cats cannot park ~256MB in
  offerImages.
- The same-window self-drag EPERM guard compares a window
number captured
  at drag start (survives the source view losing its
  window), not a weak live window reference that could nil
  out and fail open.
- The remote-fetch duplicate-push dedup key is built from
the parsed
  integers, so x=1 and x=01 map to one slot and cannot
  double-count and truncate the fetched tree.
- A t=r carrying y but no x/Y is a malformed request
(EINVAL), not the
  completion signal; a free-handle t=r requires x ABSENT (a
