  present but unparseable x is EINVAL, not a silent free).
- The remote fetch reports a failure reason so a
resource-limit breach is
  answered with EMFILE (spec), not EIO.
- Stationary hover updates are deduped so an idle drag does
not stream
  identical t=m over the pty.
- Serving a dropped file streams asynchronously with
periodic Task.yield
  (so the abort check fires and the UI stays live) and is
  size-capped (EFBIG) instead of a uninterruptible
  main-actor loop buffering the whole file.
- Before the program replies, the OS drop operation is
optimistically the
  offered one, so a fast drop released before the t=m:o
  round trip is accepted instead of springing back; only an
  explicit o=0 refuses.
- The stale-gesture guard checks the SAME button that
started the gesture
  is still down (not any button), so an unrelated later
  click cannot revive a dead gesture into a phantom drag.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: reset the bridge on terminal reset
and shell restart

The bridge was only reset on an OSC 133 prompt mark, so in a
session without shell integration a program that sent
t=a/t=o and then exited left the protocol enabled forever
(drops silently refused, drag-out dead). Also reset on a
terminal reset (RIS / Reset menu, via screenDidReset) and on
a shell restart (replaceTerminatedShellWithNewInstance).

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD test harness: conformance and teardown
fixes

- drop_target.py: register the MIME list and the machine id
as two separate
  t=a / t=a:x=1 messages (merged, the terminal discards the
  list); send the mandatory t=r:o drop-completion once all
  reads finish; use the FIRST chunk's metadata for the type
  flag; treat X=0 as a regular file; decode percent-encoded
  cross-machine names; send t=A on exit.
- drag_source.py: send the machine id by default (with
--no-machine-id) so a
  drag-out over plain ssh is detected as cross-machine,
  matching the README; send t=o:x=2 on exit.
- probe.py: send t=A on exit so it does not leave the
protocol registered.  - README: both directions are
implemented; drop the stale "once the AppKit
  adapter lands" language.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: refused-drop leave, mouseUp gesture
clear, lazy hover, log bad OSC

- performDragOperation's refusal branch now sends the
program a leave
  before returning NO, so a drag it never accepted does not
  leave it stuck in a hover state.
- On mouseUp without a native drag having started,
PTYMouseHandler tells
  the bridge to forget the pending gesture, so a later t=P
  cannot start a phantom drag from the released gesture.
- draggingEntered derives the hover MIME list from
pasteboard type presence
  only (no data reads), instead of eagerly reading all file
  URLs, the whole string, and a synchronous TIFF-to-PNG
  re-encode on the main thread; the full pasteboard is read
  only at drop time.
- An OSC 72 sequence whose bytes are undecodable in the
session encoding is
  now logged instead of silently dropped.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: wire/DoS/conformance fixes to the
controller

Review-round fixes (Swift):

- dataPayload re-pads unpadded base64 before decoding; the
spec permits
  omitting padding but Data(base64Encoded:) requires it, so
  conforming peers' pre-sent/lazy data was silently
  discarded.
- The 256MB offer budget now also covers the t=e:x=5
deferred-pull path
  (a program could bypass the t=p cap by deferring
  everything); and handlePreSend guard failures now reply
  t=E;EFBIG and cancel the drag instead of silently dropping
  data.
- Concurrent t=r entry reads are capped (EMFILE + end the
drop, per the
  spec) so a burst of requests for a large directory cannot
  spawn unbounded concurrent memory-mapped reads.
- A PNG drag image's decoded dimensions are validated from
its header
  (EFBIG) so a small compressed file declaring huge
  dimensions cannot force a multi-GB bitmap decode.
- t=A / t=a:x=2 clear acceptedDropOperation, so a drop after
the program
  stopped accepting is not reported to the OS as accepted
  then discarded.
- Filesystem read errors map to ENOENT / EPERM / EIO per the
spec instead
  of always EIO.
- Directory listings exclude special files (sockets, FIFOs),
which would
  otherwise make a conforming client abort the whole drop.
- A bare t=r (no x/Y) is the canceled drop-completion
signal, not a
  malformed request drawing EINVAL.
- A sub-index (y) read before the client requested the
uri-list is EINVAL.  - The in-flight dedup key is
generation-scoped so an old drop's completing
  read cannot remove a new drop's key.
- The remote fetch defers completion until in-flight writes
drain, so an
  aborted fetch's temp-dir removal cannot race a write that
  recreates it.
- The offer declaration and t=P are gated on
isOfferingDrags, an offer for
  an already-released gesture replies EPERM, and an
  unsolicited pre-drag t=e/t=E replies EINVAL and aborts; a
  generic t=E also aborts a local deferred-data pull
  (previously only a remote fetch).

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: restrict machine-id version to ASCII
digits

Character.isNumber also matches non-ASCII digit scalars; a
machine-id version is a plain integer, so require ASCII
decimal digits.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: clamp drop cell using exact grid
dimensions

Deriving the grid bounds from liveRect.size/_charWidth can
round a last-column/row drop one cell short for a
non-integer cell width. Use the authoritative integer
[_dataSource width]/height] instead.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: report pointer pixel position;
leave-and-unlatch on drag end

- t=m/t=M/t=o now carry the pointer's pixel position
relative to the grid
  origin (spec: "Pixel ... origin is 0, 0 at top left of
  screen", matching kitty), not a within-cell offset. The
  cell is also clamped to the visible grid so a drop in the
  right/bottom margin cannot report x==width.
- Implement draggingEnded: to send the program a leave (via
the latched
  bridge) and clear the routing latch when a drag routed to
  a Kitty DnD program ends without a delivered drop (the
  program never accepted, so
  performDragOperation:/draggingExited: did not fire),
  instead of leaving it stuck believing a drag still hovers.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: conformance and robustness fixes to
controller and drag host

Review-round fixes (Swift side):

- Remote drag-out now pulls a deferred uri-list (or any
other deferred
  MIME) via t=e:x=5 before the t=k fetch, instead of
  replying EINVAL:no files. Pre-sending is optional in the
  spec.
- A plain t=E (no y) from the program aborts an in-progress
remote fetch,
  so a client read error no longer leaves the user holding a
  dead drag until the idle timeout.
- Any numeric-version machine-id token ("2:...") is
recognized as a
  machine id: an unknown version differs from our "1:..." id
  and so is treated as a different machine (remote), and is
  kept out of the MIME list, per the spec's
  forward-compatibility rule.
- The accept-drop directory traversal caps live handles
(ENOMEM beyond
  512), so a program re-listing a directory in a loop cannot
  grow memory without bound.
- t=k directory handles may be any integer other than 0/1,
including
  negatives (case 0 file, 1 symlink, default directory), not
  just >= 2.
- Drag-out start failures now distinguish EPERM (the gesture
is gone /
  the user let go) from EIO (environmental failure) via a
  result enum.
- Drag-out puts every offered MIME type with data on the
pasteboard
  (typed by UTType, custom type for unknown MIMEs), so
  image/png and arbitrary flavors can be dragged out, not
  just uri-list and text.
- Serving a dropped file to a program streams the
memory-mapped file one
  chunk at a time (bounding base64 to a single window) and
  dedupes an identical in-flight t=r request, instead of
  buffering the whole file plus all its chunks.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: count chunk arrival as remote-fetch
idle-timeout activity

The idle timeout only rearmed in receive()/receiveError(),
which run for COMPLETE messages. A single large file is one
chunked t=k push whose chunks stream for the whole transfer
without ever completing a message, so the timer armed at
start() would fire mid-transfer and abort a healthy large
transfer over a slow link (the exact case the idle timeout
was for).

handleInboundSequence now calls
remoteDragFetch.noteActivity() for every inbound sequence
(each chunk included) while a fetch is in progress, which
rearms the idle timeout. The redundant rearms in
receive()/receiveError() are removed. Also drop a stale
comment claiming draggingUpdated: calls draggingEntered:
(the routing-latch fix made that deliberately false).

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: correct draggingExited leave comment

The leave is suppressed (the controller no-ops) if the
program sent t=A; the comment implied it always fires.
Clarify it holds for the Option case.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: synthesize the mouse-release report
when a drag-out starts

Drag-out is gated on mouse reporting, so a program has
already received SGR press and motion reports for the
gesture. When the program answers t=P and
beginDraggingSession takes over event tracking, the view's
mouseUp never runs: no button-release report is ever sent
and the mouse handler's per-gesture state stays stale, so a
TUI keeps believing the button is held.

When the native drag session begins (NSDraggingSource
willBeginAt), the drag host now notifies the session, which
tells the mouse handler to synthesize the SGR button-release
report at the gesture location and clear its per-gesture
state (_mouseDown, _committedToDrag, _mouseDownEvent,
dragOk_, _kittyDragOfferStarted).

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: latch drag routing so mid-drag
changes don't strand the program

kittyDnDBridgeForDrag re-read the Option modifier and the
program's accept state on every dragging callback, so
pressing Option (or a program toggling accept) mid-drag
silently flipped a drag that had been routed to the program
over to the legacy paste/upload path: the terminal pasted
the filenames and the program never got a leave or drop,
leaving its drag state dangling.

The routing decision is now latched at draggingEntered and
reused by draggingUpdated / prepareForDragOperation /
performDragOperation via a new latchedKittyDragBridge (the
legacy feedback body is factored out so an update no longer
re-enters and re-latches). A drag routed to the program
stays with it: on drop, if the program stopped accepting we
refuse rather than falling back to a legacy paste, and
draggingExited reports the leave using the latched decision.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: refuse same-window cross-pane
self-drag with EPERM

The self-drag EPERM guard was per-session state, so a drag
out of one split pane dropped onto a program in another pane
of the SAME window was handled by the second pane's
controller (whose flag is false) and its t=r reads were
served, defeating the spec's read-back protection.

The drag host now exposes its source window;
performDragOperation detects when the NSDraggingSource is a
KittyDnDViewDragHost in the same window and passes that into
the drop, which the controller ORs into the self-drag latch.
The existing per-session flag still covers same-session
drags.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: use a cancelable idle-timeout work
item in the remote fetch

The idle timeout rearmed by scheduling a fresh closure on
every received entry and relied on a token check to
neutralize stale ones, so a large transfer could leave
thousands of pending timer closures outstanding. Use a
single DispatchWorkItem that is canceled and replaced on
each rearm and canceled on finish.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: reject stale drag gestures; clear
pending gesture on reset

A drag-out begins from the mouse event stored when the
gesture was reported, and that event was only cleared when a
drag started or was canceled. So an unanswered gesture left
the event retained, and a program could later send t=o + t=p
+ t=P to start a phantom drag from the dead event with no
mouse button down.

beginDrag now refuses when no mouse button is pressed (a
real drag-out holds the button through the round trip), and
the controller forgets the pending gesture on reset() and on
offer-disable (t=o:x=2). It is deliberately NOT cleared in
resetOfferInProgress, which runs when the offer declaration
arrives after the gesture.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: robustness fixes for reset, memory,
timeout, and idempotency

Fixes from review of the drag-out paths:

- reset() no longer deletes the remote drag-out temp dir
while a native
  drag is still live (a prompt can appear mid-drag when the
  offering program exits). Those files back the live drag's
  uri-list; dragFinished's delayed cleanup disposes of them.
- The remote t=P branch now sets startingDrag synchronously,
so two t=P in
  one pty read batch cannot spawn two concurrent fetches
  (the second would otherwise orphan the first, whose
  timeout later drained offer state out from under the live
  drag).
- The remote-fetch timeout is now an idle timeout, rearmed
on each received
  entry, instead of an absolute 30s deadline that killed
  healthy large transfers.
- The chunk reassembler no longer misreads an interleaved
standalone
  message (a spec-legal mid-transfer query) as part of the
  pending sequence: it keys on the differing t. It also caps
  accumulation so a never-terminated m=1 stream cannot grow
  memory without bound.
- handlePreSend rejects indices outside the announced offer
and enforces a
  cumulative byte budget, so a program cannot store
  unbounded data.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: release _kittyDnDBridge in PTYSession
dealloc (MRC leak)

PTYSession.m is compiled MRC. _kittyDnDBridge is created
lazily on the first OSC 72 sequence but was never released,
so any session that ever received one (even a bare t=q)
leaked the whole bridge graph on close, including the
controller and any currentDrop pasteboard snapshot (which
can hold megabytes of dropped-file bytes). Release it in
dealloc.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: render RGBA drag thumbnails as
non-premultiplied alpha

Kitty's format-32 image data is non-premultiplied RGBA, but
the default NSBitmapImageRep assumes premultiplied, so
semi-transparent thumbnail pixels rendered with wrong
colors. Declare alphaNonpremultiplied.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: guard drag-image size math against
integer overflow

width*height*bytesPerPixel with attacker-controlled
dimensions could overflow Int and trap (a crash). The
validator now uses overflow-checked multiplication and
reports EFBIG for absurd dimensions; the raw-image renderer
bounds dimensions before its size math. Adds a regression
test with Int.max dimensions.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: validate and render drag images per
spec

Image conformance for the drag-out thumbnail:

- Raw RGB (format 24) and RGBA (format 32) images are now
rendered, not
  just PNG (100). Format 0 (text) and unknown formats fall
  back to no thumbnail.
- Raw image data whose length does not match
width*height*bytesPerPixel
  is rejected with t=E ; EINVAL, and an image over the size
  cap with t=E ; EFBIG, per the spec, before the drag
  starts.
- Multiple pre-sent images (indices -1, -2, ...) are kept;
the first (-1)
  is used as the drag thumbnail.

Also: directoryAwareURIString no longer appends a trailing
slash to a symlink that points at a directory, since a
symlink is served to the program as a symlink (X=1), not a
directory (from an earlier review).

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: harden remote drag-out fetch and
drag-start idempotency

Follow-ups from the Phase 6 and Phase 7 reviews:

- The resource limits now cover directory listings too:
every payload
  (not just regular files) is charged against the byte
  budget, and a directory's declared child count is bounded
  against maxEntries, so a program cannot inflate memory
  with a giant listing or a huge declared child set.
- Duplicate or contradictory pushes for the same entry (a
repeated data
  push, or a data push plus a t=R error) are ignored via a
  per-slot seen set, so they cannot over-decrement the
  outstanding count and finish the drag with a truncated
  tree.
- A single entry's I/O failure now skips just that entry
(dropping it from
  the drag) instead of aborting the whole transfer, matching
  how an entry-level t=R error is handled.
- t=P is now idempotent: a duplicate while a drag is live or
being
  assembled (local deferred-data pull or remote fetch) is
  ignored, so it cannot start a second native drag or drain
  an in-flight pull.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: pull deferred drag-out data via
t=e:x=5 before starting

The spec lets a program offer drag-out data lazily: pre-send
the well-known types and defer the rest, expecting the
terminal to request them. We only ever used pre-sent (t=p)
data, so a program that deferred a MIME type could not start
a drag.

At t=P the terminal now pulls any offered MIME the program
did not pre-send via the t=e:x=5 request path (the
requestDragData round trip already existed but was unwired),
then starts the drag once the data is in hand. The common
case (everything pre-sent) stays synchronous; only a
deferred type triggers the async pull.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: fetch remote drag-out directories via
the push model

The spec is explicit that in the drag-out (t=k) direction
"clients must send all children of directories, recursively,
terminals must not make requests for children of
directories, only for the entries from the text/uri-list."
Our old code violated this: it requested each directory
child with t=k:Y=handle:y=num. It only appeared to work
because the test client served files but never directories,
so the divergence never fired.

The terminal now requests only the top-level uri-list
entries; a new KittyDnDRemoteDragFetch collects the
unsolicited pushes (a directory declares X=handle with a
null-separated child list, then every descendant arrives via
Y=parent-handle:y=num) and assembles the tree, tracking the
outstanding-entry count to know when the transfer is
complete. It bounds a malicious/buggy program with
entry-count, byte, and time limits, skips symlinks,
sanitizes names, and gives directory URIs a trailing slash.

drag_source.py now offers a directory tree (with a nested
dir and a symlink) and pushes it per the protocol, so the
path is testable end to end.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: clear stale accept-operation; keep
mux id across live-drag reset

Review follow-ups:

- acceptedDropOperation was cleared only on drag
enter/leave/reset, so a
  value from a completed drag could leak into a later drag
  that entered while the program was momentarily not
  accepting and then re-registered, causing a drop to be
  delivered against a stale acceptance. It is now also
  cleared on drop completion and on t=a (re)registration.

- multiplexerID was dropped by reset() even while a native
drag was still
  live (reset can fire from a program-emitted OSC 133 prompt
  mid-drag), so the drag's remaining lifecycle events would
  lose their routing id.  It is now preserved across reset()
  while a drag is in flight, mirroring selfDragInProgress,
  and dropped at the next prompt once idle.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: propagate the multiplexer i key on
all sends

The spec: "When the terminal receives a t=a or t=o escape
code that has the i key set, all escape codes it sends to
the terminal program must include the i key with the same
value." We only echoed i on the query response, so under a
multiplexer (tmux) our t=m/t=M/t=r/t=e/t=o/t=k replies could
not be routed back to the right pane.

A t=a/t=o registration carrying i now sets a per-session
multiplexer id that is stamped on every outbound message at
the single send() chokepoint (so every chunk carries it
too). An explicit per-message i (a query echoing its own i)
still wins. Cleared on reset().

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: close self-drag EPERM bypass via
mid-drag prompt reset

The previous fix stopped offer/cancel messages from clearing
the self-drag guard, but reset() still cleared it, and
reset() runs from screenPromptDidStartAtLine: (an OSC 133
prompt-start the program can emit at will) without ending
the live OS drag. A program could therefore emit a prompt
mark mid-drag, re-send t=a, drop its own drag onto itself,
and read local files.

selfDragInProgress is now cleared only in dragFinished (the
authoritative end-of-drag callback, which always fires when
the NSDraggingSession ends). Adds a regression test for the
prompt-reset route.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: honor the program's drop-acceptance
reply (t=m:o)

The spec: after the terminal sends t=m (drag over the
window) the program replies t=m:o=O with the operation it
will perform (0 not accepted, 1 copy, 2 move), and "until
the client does so the terminal will indicate to the OS that
the drop is not accepted." We were ignoring the reply: the
drag cursor showed the source's operation and the drop was
delivered even when the program rejected it.

The controller now records acceptedDropOperation from the
reply (reset on drag enter/leave). The bridge maps it to an
NSDragOperation, which PTYTextView returns from
draggingEntered/draggingUpdated (none until the program
replies) and checks in performDragOperation to refuse a drop
the program has not accepted.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: close self-drag EPERM bypass via
mid-drag offer messages

The self-drag guard latched currentDropIsSelfDrag from
selfDragInProgress at drop time, but selfDragInProgress was
cleared in resetOfferInProgress, which runs from
program-controlled inbound messages (t=o:x=2 to disable
offers, a new t=o offer declaration, or t=E:y=-1 cancel).
None of those end the live OS drag (cancelDrag is
best-effort and cannot stop a running NSDraggingSession). A
malicious program could therefore start a drag, clear the
flag mid-drag, drop its own drag onto itself, and read local
files, defeating the EPERM protection.

The flag is now cleared only when the native drag truly ends
(dragFinished) or on reset(), never from inbound
offer/cancel messages.  Adds regression tests for all three
mid-drag clear attempts.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: make the accept-side "discard queued
requests" guarantee real

Follow-ups from the drop-completion review:

- lastCompletedDropOperation was only cleared on reset, so
it went stale
  across drops. Clear it at each new drag cycle and each new
  drop so a reader (e.g. move-vs-copy logic) cannot see a
  prior drop's value.

- finishDrop / reset / dragEntered / performDrop cleared
directoryHandles,
  but a filesystem read already dispatched to a background
  task could finish afterward and stream stale bytes or
  re-insert a freed handle (nextDirectoryHandle was reset to
  2). They now bump an acceptGeneration that each read
  captures before awaiting and rechecks after, discarding
  its result if the drop context changed. This gives the
  spec's "any queued data requests must be discarded" real
  teeth.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: refuse same-window self-drag data
reads with EPERM

The spec: "For security reasons, terminals must reply with
EPERM if the drag originated in the same window as the drop,
this prevents malicious programs from reading files on the
computer by starting their own drag."

A program that both offers a drag-out and accepts drops
could otherwise read arbitrary local files by dropping its
own drag back onto itself. We now track whether a native
drag we started is running, latch that onto the drop when it
lands here, and refuse every data read for that drop with
EPERM (latched so the deferred over-the-pty reads are still
refused after the native drag ends).

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: handle the drop-completion signal
(t=r:o=operation)

The spec: "Once the client program finishes reading all the
dropped data it needs, it must send t=r:o=operation. The
terminal emulator must then inform the OS that the drop is
completed. Any queued data requests must be discarded." We
were misreading this addressing-free t=r as a malformed data
request and replying EINVAL.

Now a t=r carrying o with no x/Y is recognized as
completion: it records the final operation (0 means
canceled), clears the current drop, and frees any open
directory handles so a later stray request cannot read stale
data.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: fix accept-drop error codes,
machine-id registration, and directory URIs

Spec-conformance fixes to the accept-drop path:

- Out-of-bounds MIME/sub/directory indices now return ENOENT
(was
  EINVAL). The spec reserves ENOENT for "index is out of
  bounds".
- A special file (socket, FIFO, device) now returns EINVAL
and is never
  opened for reading (opening a FIFO would block). The spec
  reserves EINVAL for "not a regular file or symlink or
  directory".
- "t=a:x=1 ; machine id" is the dedicated machine-id
registration and no
  longer clears the MIME types the program announced via
  "t=a ; mimes".
- file:// URIs pointing to directories now end with a slash,
as the spec
  requires, based on the on-disk type rather than the
  incoming URL shape.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: keep drag-out temp files alive for a
cross-session drop read

When a remote program's files are dragged out, the terminal
fetches them via t=k into a temp dir and drags those copies.
If the drop lands in ANOTHER terminal session (e.g. two ssh
windows), that session reads the copies lazily over the pty
AFTER the drag ends - but dragFinished was deleting the temp
dir immediately, so the read raced against the delete and
failed with EIO. Defer the temp-dir removal to a delayed
cleanup (still guaranteed) so the cross-session read can
complete.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: address remote drag-out (t=k) review

- X=0 is a regular file, not directory handle 0. Type is
now: absent/0 = file,
  1 = symlink, >=2 = directory handle (a spec-strict program
  sending X=0 for a file no longer gets a bogus directory +
  recursion).
- Same-basename entries no longer overwrite each other: each
offered entry is
  materialized in its own numbered subdirectory, so two
  files both named x.txt produce two distinct local copies.
- Symlinks are skipped rather than recreated. The target is
controlled by the
  untrusted remote and is meaningless locally, and a symlink
  into the dragged tree could exfiltrate a local file if the
  drop destination follows it.
- A superseded in-flight build can no longer stomp a newer
drag: an offer
  generation is captured at the start of the async build and
  rechecked after each await; on mismatch the build discards
  its own temp dir and bails. The no-files early return and
  every failure path now clean up the temp dir.

Tests: explicit X=0 regular file, duplicate basenames stay
distinct, and a symlink entry is dropped from the drag.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: implement remote drag-out (t=k)

Dragging a remote program's files OUT of the terminal now
works. When the offering program is on a different machine
(its t=o machine id differs, or the session has a
conductor), its file:// URIs are not readable here, so on
t=P the terminal fetches each offered entry from the program
via t=k -- regular file bytes, symlink target (X=1), or
directory (X=handle) traversed recursively with
Y=handle:y=num -- materializes copies in a temp directory on
this machine, and starts the native drag from those local
copies. The temp directory is removed when the drag finishes
(and on reset/prompt); untrusted entry names are sanitized
to a single path component; all file writes happen off the
main thread.

This fixes the EIO a user hit dragging between two plain-ssh
windows: the offered file lived on the remote host, and the
terminal was trying to read it locally.

Tests: remote single-file fetch and recursive-directory
fetch drive the controller with a fake remote program
answering t=k. drag_source.py now answers t=k (chunked via a
new kittydnd.chunked helper) so it is testable end to end.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD test harness: compute machine id on
Linux, send it by default

The machine id only worked on macOS (ioreg), so
drop_target.py running on a remote Linux host over plain ssh
could not produce one - it silently sent no id and the drop
was treated as local (a useless local Mac path). Read
/etc/machine-id (or /var/lib/dbus/machine-id) on Linux so
the client's id is derived from whatever host it runs on.

Also send the machine id by default (it matches on the same
host and differs across machines, so it is harmless locally
and enables the cross-machine path over plain ssh without a
flag). --machine-id is kept as a no-op; --no-machine-id
suppresses it.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: reset protocol state at a new shell
prompt

A program that enabled the drag-and-drop protocol (accept
and/or offer) should not leave it on for the shell after it
exits, the same way the terminal resets other modes at a
prompt. On screenPromptDidStartAtLine the session now clears
the DnD state via the bridge:

- KittyDnDController.reset() clears the accept/offer flags,
accepted/offered MIME
  types, peer machine id, the current drop, open directory
  handles, pending lazy-data completions, and any
  partially-reassembled message.
- KittyDnDBridge exposes reset(); PTYSession calls it from
  screenPromptDidStartAtLine:.

Tests: reset clears accept+offer state and errors a
subsequent request, invalidates an open directory handle,
and drains a pending offer data request with nil.

Also update the release note: drag-out requires an app with
mouse reporting (a full-screen TUI), and remote drops
transfer the file over the connection.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: drop conductor materialization;
always transfer remote drops in-band

Remove the non-standard "materialize the dropped file on the
remote host and hand back a path" optimization. It was not
part of the protocol, created unsolicited temp files under
~/.iterm2-dnd with no cleanup, took file placement out of
the program's hands, and only helped programs that
half-implemented the spec (a fully conformant program is
better served by the standard path, and an unaware program
never enters the protocol at all).

Now every remote drop uses the protocol-standard in-band
path: the uri-list is flagged X=1 and the program pulls each
file's bytes by sub-index, deciding where to write them.
Remoteness is still ground-truthed from the conductor (so an
it2ssh drop is in-band even with no machine id) OR the
machine-id comparison, so it2ssh and plain ssh now behave
identically.

KittyDnDEndpoint collapses to a single isRemoteHost signal;
materializeFile and the unused local-file reader are gone.
Copying a file to the remote host when dropping onto a plain
shell (no program opted in) remains a separate, non-protocol
concern.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: address cross-machine review

- Fix a stale-handle bug: open directory handles pointed
into the previous
  drop's files and were not cleared on a new drag cycle, so
  a late t=r:Y=<handle> could read a file the current cycle
  no longer represents. Clear directoryHandles (and reset
  the counter) in dragEntered alongside currentDrop,
  restoring the no-stale-reads guarantee and bounding handle
  growth per cycle.
- Tests: a directory handle is invalidated by a new drag;
nested-directory
  traversal (a directory child yielding a fresh handle)
  works server-side.
- drop_target.py: release a directory's parent handle even
when a child request
  errors (was leaking the handle), and refuse to write
  outside --outdir if the terminal ever returns an unsafe
  path component.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: implement in-band cross-machine file
transfer

Implement the deferred remote-drop mechanism so a program on
a different machine (e.g. over plain ssh, no it2ssh
conductor) can pull the actual bytes of dropped files, per
the protocol:

- When the uri-list is flagged X=1 (cross-machine), the
program requests each
  entry by sub-index (t=r:x=<mime>:y=<idx>). The terminal
  reads the local file and streams its bytes, reports a
  symlink target with X=1, or a directory as a new handle
  (X=handle) whose null-separated entry names it returns.
- Directories are traversed with t=r:Y=<handle>:x=<num>,
recursively; the program
  frees a handle with t=r:Y=<handle> (no index). Handles
  start at 2 (0/1 are the file/symlink type indicators).
- All filesystem access happens off the main thread; errors
are reported as t=R
  echoing the request's addressing keys.

drop_target.py now follows this: on an X=1 uri-list it pulls
each item in-band and recreates files, symlinks, and
directories (recursively) under --outdir.

Tests cover regular-file bytes, symlink target, recursive
directory traversal with handle freeing, and a bad sub-index
error. This complements the conductor (Tier 2)
materialization: cross-machine now works over any transport.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: materialize dropped files whenever a
conductor is present

Fix a bug where dropping a local file onto a remote program
over an it2ssh session pasted a useless local path instead
of copying the file to the remote host. Tier 2
materialization was gated on both the peer's machine id AND
the conductor, but a program need not send a machine id; a
conductor being present is itself the ground-truth signal
that the program is remote. Key Tier 2 off the conductor
(endpoint.canMaterializeFiles) alone.

The Tier 2 test now sends no machine id, reproducing the
reported scenario.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: drag_source.py enables mouse
reporting itself

The drag-out gesture only fires in mouse-reporting mode, so
the test client now turns on button-event mouse reporting
(1002/1006) on startup and off on exit, instead of requiring
the user to arrange it. The program ignores the mouse
reports themselves.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: address 4c review

- Fix a real drag-out bug: the uri-list parser split on the
characters \r or
  \n, but Swift treats "\r\n" as a single grapheme, so a
  standard CRLF-separated uri-list never split and produced
  one bogus URL. Split on $0.isNewline (matches CR, LF, and
  the CRLF grapheme) and add unit tests for CRLF, LF,
  comment/blank lines, and non-file URLs.
- Reset the in-progress offer when the drag host cannot
start, so stale offer
  data does not linger.
- Clamp the drop pixel offset to non-negative for drops in
the left/top margin.  - Keep the mouse drag hot path cheap:
gate the reportability test behind a new
  cheap mouseHandlerHasKittyDragOffer: check, so no extra
  work happens per drag event unless a program is actually
  offering drags.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD phase 4c: drag data out of the
terminal

Wire the offer direction so a program can start a native OS
drag of its own data.

- KittyDnDViewDragHost starts an NSDraggingSession on the
terminal view from the
  program's offer, mapping text/uri-list to file URLs (so a
  drop onto Finder works) and text/plain to a string item,
  using the offered image as the drag thumbnail. It relays
  the drag outcome back as t=e (dropped/finished).
- Because the offer needs a round trip (gesture -> t=o ->
the program's t=P)
  before the OS drag can begin, the drag starts from the
  stored gesture event, which AppKit permits.
- PTYMouseHandler hands a drag gesture to the program only
when the program has
  enabled offers, mouse reporting is on (otherwise the
  gesture is a text selection), Option is not held, it is
  not a three-finger drag, and only once per mouse-down.
  This runs just before the drag is reported to the app.
- The bridge exposes isOfferingDrags and noteDragGesture;
PTYSession vends the
  terminal view; the host is wired to the controller both
  ways.

Testable with tests/kitty-dnd/drag_source.py in a
full-screen (mouse-reporting) program: start a drag over the
window and drop onto Finder or TextEdit.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: address 4b review (drop coordinates
and image drags)

- Report screen-relative cell coordinates for drops,
matching iTerm2's mouse
  reporting (liveRect-relative), instead of coordForPoint:'s
  whole-buffer coordinate which was off by the scrollback
  line count once there was history.  This also drops the
  selection next-neighbor fudge, so the pixel offset stays
  within the cell and non-negative.
- Register the image pasteboard types (PNG, TIFF) so a
pure-image drag (no file
  URL or string) actually reaches a program that accepts
  image drops. Non-Kitty sessions still reject image-only

  behavior is unchanged.
- Report the drag leave only when we would have forwarded
the drag (same gate as
  enter/update), so an Option-held upload drag no longer
  sends a stray t=m leave.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD phase 4b: route drops onto the window
to the program

When a program has announced it accepts drops (OSC 72 t=a)
and Option is not held, PTYTextView now forwards drags to it
instead of pasting/uploading:

- draggingEntered/Updated report hover as t=m with the drop
cell, pixel offset
  within the cell, allowed operations, and (on entry) the
  available MIME types.
- draggingExited reports the leave; prepareForDragOperation
accepts so the drop
  is delivered; performDragOperation snapshots the
  pasteboard and sends t=M, then answers the program's t=r
  data requests.
- Holding Option forces today's paste/SCP-upload behavior
(the escape hatch).

KittyDnDPasteboardDropData snapshots the drag pasteboard
eagerly (it is only valid during the drop) into the MIME
list the controller vends: files -> text/uri-list, string ->
text/plain, image -> image/png. The bridge exposes the
accept-drop entry points to ObjC and resolves the live SSH
endpoint for Tier 2.

Now testable with tests/kitty-dnd/drop_target.py: drag
files/text/images onto a window running it. Drag-out (offer)
is the remaining piece.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD phase 4a: wire the controller into the
session

Connect the OSC 72 controller to PTYSession so the inbound
handshake actually works end to end (query, accept/offer
registration, and reports back to the program).

- KittyDnDBridge (@objc iTermKittyDnDBridge): the
ObjC-facing owner of the
  per-session KittyDnDController. Constructed with a report
  block that writes bytes back to the pty; forwards inbound
  OSC 72 content to the controller.
- KittyDnDSSHEndpointAdapter maps the session's SSHEndpoint
(conductor or
  localhost) to KittyDnDEndpoint, resolving the endpoint
  lazily and materializing dropped files under ~/.iterm2-dnd
  on the remote host for the Tier 2 path.
- KittyDnDMachineID.localHashed() derives our machine id
from the hardware
  platform UUID via IOKit.
- PTYSession creates the bridge lazily on the first OSC 72
sequence and routes
  screenDidReceiveKittyDragAndDrop into it; reports go out
  via screenSendReportData.

The AppKit drag adapter (drops onto the window and drags out
of it) is the next step. Tested: the bridge answers a query,
stays silent on announce, and never emits a newline in a
report.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: address phase 3 review

Completion-lifecycle and robustness fixes in the
offer/drag-out controller:

- requestDragData no longer leaks a completion: if a request
for the same MIME
  index is already outstanding, its completion is failed
  with nil before being replaced, so it is never dropped
  silently.
- A new offer declaration mid-drag now drains outstanding
data requests (it
  calls resetOfferInProgress before recording the new offer)
  instead of leaking their completions.
- A bare t=o (neither x nor o) is ignored instead of
destructively clearing an
  in-progress offer, matching the "unknown variants are
  no-ops" invariant.

Tests: double data request fails the first completion, drag
finish drains a pending request with nil, a bare t=o is
ignored, and a second full offer cycle reuses one
controller.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: address phase 2 review

Correctness fixes from the phase 2 accept-drop review:

- End-of-data framing (high): the protocol signals
end-of-data with a final
  empty payload and m=0, and every data-bearing chunk
  carries m=1. The chunker put the last data in the m=0
  message and emitted no terminator, so a compliant client
  waiting for the empty-payload sentinel would hang.
  KittyDnDChunker now emits m=1 data chunks followed by an
  empty m=0 terminator. The reassembler already completes on
  m=0, so it round-trips unchanged; tests updated.
- Tier 2 read no longer blocks the main thread: the dropped
file is read via a
  detached task before the (async) endpoint materialization.
- Peer machine id is normalized to lower case so a peer
echoing upper-case hex
  is not misjudged as remote against our lower-case id. Its
  intentional stickiness (a machine id cannot change
  mid-connection) is documented.
- currentDrop is cleared when a new drag enters so a stray
t=r cannot read the
  previous cycle's data; duplicate intValue("x") hoisted.

New tests: empty-payload transfer, x=2 unregister, t=r
before any drop and with a missing index (EINVAL), Tier 2
materialize failure (EIO), multi-file uri-list CRLF join,
and the cross-machine flag on every chunk.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD phase 3: offer / start-drag controller

Add the offer direction to KittyDnDController and the
KittyDnDDragHost abstraction, so a program can drag its own
data out of the terminal.

- t=o:x=1/2 enable/disable offering (with optional peer
machine id).  - On an AppKit drag gesture the controller
sends t=o with cell and pixel coords.  -
t=o:o=<flags>;<mimes> records the offer; t=p pre-sends
per-MIME data (chunked)
  and, at a negative index, the drag thumbnail image
  (format/size in y/X/Y).
- t=P:x=-1 assembles a KittyDnDDragOffer and asks the drag
host to start the
  native drag, acking with t=E;OK (or t=E;error if it cannot
  start).
- Drag lifecycle from the host is relayed as t=e events:
accepted (x=1),
  action changed (x=2), dropped (x=3), finished (x=4). The
  lazy data path is supported: requestDragData emits t=e:x=5
  and the program's t=e:y=idx;data (or t=E:y=idx error)
  reply is delivered to the pending completion.
- Inbound t=E:y=-1 cancels the native drag.

Tested with a fake drag host covering
enable/gesture/offer/pre-send/start, chunked pre-send, the
image thumbnail, all lifecycle events, the lazy
request/reply round trip, and cancel.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD phase 2: accept-drop controller

Add KittyDnDController, the @MainActor per-session state
machine, and the KittyDnDEndpoint abstraction. This phase
implements the accept-drop direction: a program running in
the terminal accepts a drop onto the window.

- Handles inbound t=q (query), t=a/t=A (register/unregister
with MIME list and
  optional peer machine id), and t=r (data request),
  reassembling chunks.
- On AppKit drag events emits t=m (move, with the MIME list
on entry only),
  t=m:x=-1:y=-1 on exit, and t=M on drop, all carrying cell
  and pixel coords.
- Answers t=r with base64 file/content bytes, chunked, and
t=R on error.  - Three-tier text/uri-list routing keyed off
the machine-id comparison and the
  endpoint's materialization capability: Tier 1 local
  returns local file URIs; Tier 2 (remote + conductor)
  materializes the files on the program's host and returns
  those paths with no cross-machine flag; Tier 3 (remote, no
  conductor) returns the local URIs flagged X=1.

The controller depends only on the report closure,
KittyDnDEndpoint, and KittyDnDDropData, so the whole accept
path is tested with fakes. AppKit and PTYSession wiring come
in a later phase.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD: distinguish plain-text and base64
payloads

The OSC 72 payload encoding depends on the message type:
MIME lists (t=a/m/M/o), machine ids, and error strings are
plain UTF-8 text, while file/image data (t=r/p/k) is base64.
Phase 0 wrongly assumed every payload was base64.

Rework the wire layer to store the payload undecoded
(rawPayload) and expose it as either textPayload or
dataPayload, so the caller decodes per message type:

- KittyDnDMessage gains textPayload/dataPayload accessors
and matching
  constructors; parsing keeps the payload raw and never
  fails (an invalid base64 data payload surfaces as a nil
  dataPayload at the point of use).
- KittyDnDChunker.messages takes binary data; the
reassembler concatenates raw
  payloads and defers decoding, so the earlier
  silent-empty-Data path is gone.
- Payload text is CR/LF-sanitized on serialize too, so the
no-newline invariant
  holds for plain-text payloads as well.

Also stop the phase-1 PTYSession stub from logging full
sequence content, since a data payload can carry
dropped-file bytes; log only the length.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD phase 1: parse OSC 72 and deliver to
the session

Wire the OSC 72 escape code through the parser and terminal
so each Kitty drag-and-drop sequence reaches the main-thread
session:

- New XTERMCC_KITTY_DND token; VT100XtermParser maps OSC 72
to it.  - VT100Terminal forwards the raw content after "72;"
(metadata plus optional
  base64 payload) via a new VT100TerminalDelegate method.
- VT100ScreenMutableState hops it out of the mutation thread
with a side effect
  to a new VT100ScreenDelegate method; PTYSession logs it
  for now (the per-session controller is wired in a later
  phase).

Stateful chunk reassembly and interpretation are
deliberately left to the controller, so this layer delivers
each sequence's raw content verbatim. Tested end to end
through the real parser via screen.inject, including a
payload with an internal semicolon, multiple sequences, a
full-size (4096 base64) payload, and the BEL terminator.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD phase 0: address review of the wire
layer

Fixes from the phase 0 correctness review:

- Reassembler no longer silently swallows an undecodable
payload. The final
  chunk is now rebuilt into complete OSC content and parsed
  through KittyDnDMessage(oscContent:), so base64 validation
  and the nil-vs-empty-payload distinction match the
  single-message path exactly; a corrupt payload yields nil
  (logged) instead of an empty Data.
- Correct the reassembler comment: concatenate-then-decode
is what supports a
  peer splitting the base64 stream at arbitrary positions
  (the protocol's post-encoding limit model), not per-chunk
  decode.
- Make the no-newline invariant hold for all inputs:
KittyDnDMetadata.serialize
  now strips CR/LF from metadata values, not just the base64
  payload.
- Close test gaps: chunk-count boundary, reassembler reuse
across messages,
  reassembler nil vs empty payload, and arbitrary
  base64-stream split.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Kitty DnD (OSC 72) phase 0: pure wire layer

Add the pure Swift wire layer for the Kitty drag-and-drop
protocol under sources/DragAndDrop, with no terminal,
AppKit, or I/O dependencies:

- KittyDnDMessage: parse/serialize one complete OSC 72
message
  (colon-separated k=v metadata plus optional base64
  payload), with a deterministic key order and typed
  accessors.
- KittyDnDChunker: split a payload into <=4096-byte base64
chunks tagged
  m=1/m=0, and KittyDnDChunkReassembler to put them back
  together.
- KittyDnDMachineID: HMAC-SHA256 machine-id hashing and the
cross-machine
  comparison used to decide the X=1 byte-transfer path.

Enforces the no-newline output invariant with a dedicated
test. See docs/kitty-dnd-design.md for the full design and
phase plan.

Issue 12961.

2026-08-06: Fix Advanced Paste hang and per-line behavior
(issue 5749)

Keystroke passthrough during a paused paste: when "Wait for
shell prompt before pasting each line" is on and the shell
sits at a prompt that emits no OSC 133 marker (a password
prompt, zsh spelling correction, etc.), the paste would wait
forever while queueing every keystroke, so the user could
not answer the prompt. The paste indicator now has a
keyboard toggle (in both the drop-down panel and the status
bar) that sends typing straight to the terminal instead of
queueing it; Esc and Ctrl-C then reach the shell too, and
Cancel still aborts. A lightweight callout points at the
toggle the first time a keystroke gets queued, and the
keyboard's tint reflects its state.

Single-envelope bracketed paste when not waiting for a
prompt: shouldPasteNewlinesOutsideBrackets existed only to
make wait-for-prompt work with bracketing on
newline-swallowing shells (zsh, bash, fish), so it is now
gated on wait-for-prompt being on. With it off, a multi-line
block is pasted inside one bracketed envelope and lands on
the command line as one editable buffer to run with a single
Return, matching a normal paste.

2026-08-06: Update CLAUDE.md

2026-08-06: Keep subparameters of a blank 16th SGR parameter

Follow-up to #722. When the 16th CSI parameter was an
implied blank created by a doubled semicolon,
parameterOverflowed was set the moment count reached
VT100CSIPARAM_MAX, so a subparameter attached to that
still-valid 16th parameter was discarded. Only treat the
list as overflowed when the semicolon did not open a
storable blank parameter.

Adds a VT100CSIParserTest case and a cat-able manual test in
tests/sgr-16th-param.txt.

2026-08-06: Add checklist for adding a new shell to shell
integration

2026-08-06: Fix off-by-one that dropped the 16th SGR trigger
parameter (#722)

* Fix off-by-one that dropped the 16th SGR trigger parameter

iTermParserAddCSIParameter refused to store a value once
count reached 15, so it accepted only 15 of the 16 slots in
CSIParam.p.  The terminal's own CSI parser accepts 16
(VT100CSIParser.m), so an SGR trigger and the terminal
disagreed about the same parameter string.

Besides losing the last parameter, SGRTrigger attaches
subparameters with iTermParserAddCSISubparameter(&csi,
csi.count - 1, sub), so when the 16th parameter was dropped
its subparameters landed on the 15th parameter instead  e.g.
the components of a 24-bit color were applied to the wrong
parameter.

* Fix iTermParserGetCSISubparameter ignoring its index
argument

The function always returned the first subparameter of the
requested parameter: i started at 0, the loop returned on
the first match because i == 0, and the i-- afterwards was
unreachable. It has no callers today, so nothing is broken
by it, but it silently returns the wrong value for any
subparameter_index other than 0.

* Discard subparameters of parameters that don't fit

When a CSI sequence has more parameters than CSIParam can
hold, the extra parameters are discarded, but their
subparameters were still attached to the last parameter that
did fit. That changes its meaning: in an SGR sequence with
17 parameters ending in 4;3:5, the :5 landed on the 4,
turning plain underline into dashed underline.

Both paths that build a CSIParam had this problem: the
terminal's own parser, where the subparameter branch used
count - 1 without checking whether the parameter had been
stored, and SGRTrigger, which calls
iTermParserAddCSIParameter and could not tell that the value
had been dropped. iTermParserAddCSIParameter now reports
whether it stored the value.

Adds regression tests for the parameter-list boundary.
2026-08-06: Fix typos for word punctuation (#720)
2026-08-06: Honor OSC 9;4;4 when the percentage is omitted
(#723)

The ConEmu progress protocol makes pr optional for st=4
(paused), and the comment on that branch said so, but the
code only acted when pr was in 0...100, so ESC ]9;4;4 ST did
nothing. st=2 (error) handles the same case by falling back
to a sentinel. The case 4 comment was a copy of case 2's and
wrongly said "set error state".

An absent pr now keeps the percentage already showing and
only switches it to the warning encoding, which is what
Ghostty does and what Windows Terminal's
Terminal::SetTaskbarProgress does. When there is nothing to
keep, it falls back to 10%: the bar's width comes from the
percentage, so 0 would draw an invisible indicator, and
Windows Terminal substitutes the same value under the name
TaskbarMinProgress.

A pr that is present but out of range is still ignored, as
in case 1, so ESC ]9;4;4;101 ST remains a no-op instead of
changing state.

VT100ScreenProgressPercentage() extracts the percentage from
whichever base is in use; VT100ScreenProgressIsVisible now
uses it instead of repeating the three range checks. No enum
values were added, so PSMProgress needs no corresponding
change.  2026-08-06: Fix Tahoe dark tab chrome ignoring
custom tab height (#715)

On macOS Tahoe dark themes, selected left/right tab chrome
was drawn at the cap images' intrinsic size instead of the
pill rect, so tabs with a custom Default tab bar height
showed a fixed-height foreground over a taller cell. Cap and
mid images now scale to the pill height (width clamped so
tall narrow tabs cannot overlap caps), and
fill/stroke/progress ring use a true stadium radius
(height/2). Also clarifies the Default tab bar height help
text.

Closes #715.  2026-08-06: Add working notes on bidi RTL
selection direction

Records the PR #709 discussion: the visual (TextEdit) vs
logical (browsers) mouse selection tradeoff, what we tested,
the open questions posted to the author, parked
implementation issues (notably the Metal selected fg/bg
coordinate mismatch), and next steps.

2026-08-05: Restore sessions whose working directory is gone
(issue 12955)

When a session's saved working directory no longer exists
(for example, it was on an external or network drive that is
not mounted at launch), restoring it could fail to relaunch
the shell and silently close the tab.

Two things went wrong. On the posix_spawn launch path (used
when "Disclaim ownership of children" is enabled), the
working directory was applied with
posix_spawn_file_actions_addchdir_np, whose failure aborts
the entire spawn. The shell never execs, the session gets an
immediate broken pipe, and because it ended right after
starting, the tab is closed. This differs from the fork+exec
path, whose chdir() failure is silently ignored so the shell
still launches in a fallback directory.

Fixes: - iTermSessionFactory now checks whether the resolved
working directory
  exists before launch and, if not, falls back to the home
  directory and records the original path. After the shell
  starts, the session shows a banner explaining that it
  started in the home directory instead. This is skipped for
  ssh (remote paths) and for attach (no shell launch).
- iTermSpawnInitializeActions only adds the chdir file
action when the
  directory exists, so a missing directory can no longer
  abort the spawn.  This makes the posix_spawn path
  best-effort like the fork path and is defense in depth
  against the same directory vanishing after the check.

2026-08-05: Fix two format string bugs in logging calls
(#712)

* Include parser error description in log message

I think it's been missing ever since this log call was
originally introduced in cee4e22.

Also adds NS_FORMAT_FUNCTION to the declaration so that it's
checked by the compiler.

* Use an explicit format string for calls to
SetPinnedDebugLogMessage

If the value string param happened to have had a percent
character in it, it would've led to undefined behavior.

Also adds NS_FORMAT_FUNCTION to protect against this issue
at other callsites and in the future.  2026-08-05: Compose
decomposed Hangul instead of splitting jamo. Issue 3063

Conjoining Hangul jamo (Hangul_Syllable_Type L, V, T) are
Grapheme_Base, so they were included in the
codePointsWithOwnCell exception set used by aggressive base
character detection. That set tells the composed-character
enumerator where it is safe to start a new cell, overriding
CoreFoundation's UAX #29 clustering. For jamo that override
is wrong: UAX #29 rules GB6-GB8 forbid breaking within an
L(+)V(+T) sequence, and
CFStringGetRangeOfComposedCharactersAtIndex already groups
such a sequence into one grapheme cluster. Re-splitting it
rendered decomposed Hangul (e.g. Korean filenames stored by
HFS+, or printf '') as separate jamo letters with the wrong
width, corrupting cursor movement.

Exclude conjoining jamo (L, V, T, including Jamo
Extended-A/B) from codePointsWithOwnCell by subtracting
Hangul_Syllable_Type L/V/T from own_cell_codes in
generate_nscharacterset.py and regenerating.  Precomposed
syllables (LV/LVT, U+AC00..U+D7A3) are single code points
and keep their own cell. The Tamil (issue 7788) and
halfwidth katakana (issue 6048) cases that motivated
aggressive detection are unaffected.

2026-08-05: Don't pin toolbelt list columns to a zero width
(issue 12964)

When a new window is created with the toolbelt showing, the
toolbelt is briefly laid out at zero width. The list tools
(Command History, Recent Directories, Captured Output)
pinned their table column's min/maxWidth to
contentSize.width minus a fudge factor, which went negative;
maxWidth clamped to 0 and later relayouts could not recover
because minWidth got clamped back down to the poisoned
maxWidth. Rows were present but drew at zero width until a
window resize forced a clean relayout.

Only pin the column when the target width is positive, and
set maxWidth before minWidth so a previously poisoned
ceiling cannot clamp the floor.

2026-08-05: Autorelease the frame canonicalizer to fix a
window leak

The iTermFrameCanonicalizer was alloc/init'd in MRC code
without a balancing release. Its strong window reference
kept the NSWindow and its entire view tree alive after the
window was closed, leaking a full terminal window per
open/close cycle.

Fixes gitlab issue 12966.

2026-08-04: Offer physical-key bindings when a shortcut
fails after a layout change

When a keyDown carries Cmd/Ctrl/Opt, matches no key binding
by character, but a binding exists for the same physical key
and modifiers (e.g. after switching keyboard layout, or a
Chinese/Pinyin input method changes the character a key
reports), iTerm2 now offers to turn on "Interpret key
bindings based on physical key".

Detection happens once per keyDown at the single point in
iTermApplication where no binding claimed the event. When a
terminal responder is focused the offer is an in-session
announcement; otherwise it is a modal. The offer is made at
most once per launch (auto-repeat is ignored), so it neither
nags nor storms. "Don't Ask Again" suppresses it permanently
(NoSync); enabling the preference also stops it. Learn More
opens a modal with the full explanation. The Keys
preferences checkbox now stays in sync when the preference
is toggled from the offer.

This supersedes the unconditional keycode-only fallback
approach, which would have silently changed matching for
users who deliberately want character-based bindings across
layouts.

2026-08-04: Fix typo in session.py (#714)

Fix typo in session.py: `uknown` to `unknown` 2026-08-04:
Add option to draw mark indicators using the theme's ANSI
colors

New per-profile setting "Use ANSI colors for marks"
(Profiles > Colors, off by default). When enabled,
command-prompt mark indicators are drawn using the color
preset's ANSI blue (success), red (error), and yellow
(other) instead of the built-in constants, so they harmonize
with the current theme. Success stays blue to match the
previous built-in color.

The setting is mode-independent (excluded from the
light/dark color amendment and from the per-mode bulk-copy
machinery) since the resolved ANSI colors already adapt to
appearance on their own. Applies to both the CoreGraphics
and Metal render paths; the non-Metal image cache is keyed
by (type, useThemeColors) and evicted on colormap generation
change, and the Metal atlas is rebuilt in the driver's
per-frame update phase when the resolved colors change.
Repaints on profile reload so the toggle and ANSI edits take
effect immediately, and keeps the auto-composer separator
color in sync.

Implements issue 9983.

2026-08-03: Default the uv Python runtime to on for macOS
13+

The pythonRuntimeUsesUV advanced setting now defaults to on
for macOS 13 and later and off for macOS 12 (the last
version this app supports, whose bundled runtime stays in
use; the uv interpreters require macOS 13). The default is
computed at settings-load time from the running OS via a
static helper, so users can still override it in Advanced
Settings. Already-provisioned scripts are unaffected; the
gate only governs creating, importing, and migrating
scripts.
