2026-08-03: Add release notes for 3.7.0beta9

2026-08-03: Tweak wording

2026-08-03: Remove duplicate IT2_SUITE block after rebase

The rebase landed both my menu-launched-script IT2_SUITE fix
and an equivalent fix that had gone into upstream
independently, leaving two copies of the suiteName block in
environmentFromEnvironment: (a redefinition error). Keep the
more complete version, which also clears a stale inherited
IT2_SUITE via the else branch.

2026-08-03: Don't call a missing-runtime AutoLaunch script
"malformed"

The re-provision offer was gated on explicitUserAction, so
an AutoLaunch (non- explicit) launch of a uv script whose
interpreter went missing skipped the offer, failed the
.py/.scpt checks (it is a container dir), and fell through
to the generic "The script X is malformed / Cannot Run
Script" modal, a wrong diagnosis with no route to the fix,
for exactly the scripts (long-running AutoLaunch daemons)
most likely to hit it. Handle the reprovisionable state for
both cases: an explicit launch still offers to rebuild; a
non-explicit one logs the real cause and the recovery
gesture ("Launch it from the Scripts menu to rebuild it") to
the Script Console rather than popping a consent dialog and
progress window at startup.

2026-08-03: Keep couldLaunchScriptWithAbsolutePath in sync
with the re-provision offer

cfaaedce3 taught launchScriptWithAbsolutePath to offer
rebuilding a uv script whose interpreter went missing, but
its "// keep in sync" companion
couldLaunchScriptWithAbsolutePath was not updated: for that
state it returns NO (nil venv, not .py/.scpt), so the status
bar RPC component's onClick dialog omits its "Launch Script"
recovery button. Long-running AutoLaunch status-bar daemons
are the likeliest victims of a deleted uv dir, and clicking
the broken component is the natural recovery gesture, which
dead-ended at "OK". Return YES when
uvScriptContainerNeedsReprovision is true so the button
appears and routes to the rebuild-and-run offer.

2026-08-03: Release note: no more Intel-only-binary warning
during script migration

2026-08-03: Stop uv python list from probing (and running)
search-path interpreters

`uv python list --all-versions` still discovers and EXECUTES
every interpreter on the search path to read its metadata,
even under UV_PYTHON_PREFERENCE=only-managed (that only
filters selection, not discovery). availableMinors runs it
during every provision/migration resolve, so on a machine
with an Intel Homebrew python on PATH it execs an Intel
binary under Rosetta each time: macOS 26 raises the
intel-only-binary warning (observed in the field), it is
wasted work on a cold cache, and it fails outright on a
Rosetta-less macOS.

Add --only-downloads. Verified against uv 0.12.0: the old
form probes 16 search-path pythons (incl. Intel
/usr/local/bin/python3.8) while --only-downloads probes zero
and returns the identical 143-entry list with the same
minors in the same JSON schema, so
availableMinors(fromListJSON:) is unchanged. The download
metadata is embedded in the uv binary, so this stays
offline-safe. Also verified `uv venv --python X` under
only-managed searches only managed installations (no
search-path probing), so venv creation is unaffected.

2026-08-03: Offer to rebuild a uv script whose interpreter
went missing

A uv full-environment script's .venv/bin/python is a symlink
into the shared uv python dir. If the user deletes that dir
to reclaim disk, renames their home, or restores the Scripts
folder onto another machine, the interpreter dangles,
environmentForScript returns nil, and the script was
declared "malformed" with no recovery, a regression from the
legacy self-contained iterm2env clone. Detect the
marker+setup.cfg-present/interpreter-dangling state on an
explicit launch and offer to re-provision the venv from
setup.cfg (createSetupCfg:NO preserves it), then relaunch.

2026-08-03: Add regression tests for the twice-regressed
data-loss paths

Add hermetic coverage for the exact code paths that already
regressed, with small injectable-root refactors to make them
testable:

- recoverStaleReplaceBackups: injectable directory +
ScriptImporterRecoveryTests
  covering symlink-target => restore (the data-loss case),
  real-target => delete backup, coincidental .replacing-*
  name => untouched, orphaned .installing-* => removed.
  Writing the test exposed a real bug: the restore used the
  live scripts path rather than the target path, which is
  now passed explicitly.

- invalidateSharedVenvRemaps: venvsRoot-parameterized
overload + tests that the
  per-file merge preserves records left by a prior upgrade
  and that a newer resolution wins for a same-named minor
  (the two bugs it regressed on before).

2026-08-03: REPL falls back to any provisioned shared venv
when offline

openREPL always requested the default Python (3.12); if only
another minor's shared venv exists (e.g. provisioned via a
remapped 3.7-shebang script) and the machine is offline, the
REPL failed with "Python Environment Unavailable" despite a
working iterm2-equipped venv on disk. Add
newestProvisionedSharedVenvInterpreter and fall back to it
before failing, matching how legacy apython used whatever
was installed.

2026-08-03: Reclaim orphaned uv artifacts (install temps,
stray iterm2env, verify temp)

Several crash windows leaked large artifacts no sweep ever
reclaimed:

- verifyDownloadedTarball registered its cleanup defer after
the write, so a
  failure mid-write leaked a partial ~37 MB temp. Register
  it before the write.

- Each uv-binary install copies to a fresh
.uv-install-<uuid> temp (~90 MB) in
  uv/bin/ and never cleaned old ones on crash. Add
  sweepOrphanedInstallTemps, run at launch.

- A uv-backed container (.venv + marker authoritative) could
carry a stray iterm2env
  (multi-GB) restored by an old-build downgrade round-trip,
  or a .venv.building temp from a crash mid-swap, that only
  saved-iterm2env reclamation removed. Reclaim all three via
  reclaimOrphanedArtifacts at launch for confirmed-uv
  containers.

2026-08-03: Dependency Editor: lock edits during rebuild;
preserve setup.cfg metadata

- A uv Python-version change rebuilds the .venv from a
click-time dependency
  snapshot and swaps it in atomically, but the editor stayed
  interactive, so a package added during the (minutes-long)
  rebuild was installed into the discarded venv and then
  erased when setup.cfg was rewritten from the snapshot. Set
  a rebuild-in-progress flag that early-returns the
  add/remove/upgrade/version actions and disable the editing
  controls until the rebuild finishes.

- The uv setup.cfg rewrite hardcoded name = folder name and
environmentVersion =
  the minimum, silently renaming a script whose [metadata]
  name differed and dropping its pinned environment version.
  Preserve the existing setup.cfg's name and environment
  version when one is present (the create path still
  defaults).

2026-08-03: Harden the predictive scan; make the signature
test non-vacuous

- collectLegacyFullEnvironmentScriptsUnder treated any
directory with a setup.cfg
  as a container and stopped recursing, so a stray setup.cfg
  in the Scripts root aborted the whole version-bump scan
  and silently suppressed every warning. Require an
  environment (iterm2env / .venv / saved-iterm2env)
  alongside setup.cfg to count as a container, and include
  saved-only containers (an interrupted migration that the
  next launch restores and migrates) in the warning, which
  were omitted.

- testRejectsInvalidSignature passed a non-tar payload that
fails at tar regardless
  of signature, so deleting signature verification would not
  fail it. Use a real, extractable tarball with a bogus
  signature so bypassing verification would install it and
  break the test, actually pinning the trust invariant.

- Document the cross-build limitation of exporting a
full-environment script from a
  uv-only machine (python_requires hint newer than the
  legacy manifest offers).

2026-08-03: Improve uv error reporting and cut remap-warning
fatigue

- The offline stale-remap fallback engaged for ANY build
failure (disk full, a
  yanked wheel, a broken uv) and returned success with no
  trace, so a broken environment presented as a script
  silently on an older Python. Log it and write a Script
  Console line naming the underlying error.

- selectedEntry reported an empty/all-filtered manifest as
"uv is not available for
  this version of macOS" (a wrong diagnosis that invites
  on-OS-version bug reports for a hosting problem), and
  upgradeUvBinaryIfNewerAvailable reported every
  fetch/parse/floor/size failure as "offline". Distinguish
  not-valid-JSON, no-usable-entries,
  none-compatible-with-this-macOS, and propagate the
  specific reason into the update-check message.

- reportForcedRemap showed a per-script modal at each
migration in addition to the
  consolidated startup modal, so N affected scripts meant
  N+1 modals, and a uv upgrade (which invalidates remap
  records) made the modal recur every cycle. Make the
  per-script report Console-only per the design; the one
  modal stays the startup predictive warning. This also
  removes the duplicated suppression-identifier literal,
  since only the menu now shows that modal.

- Log inherited UV_* environment variables (UV_OFFLINE,
UV_INDEX_URL, ...) that
  silently alter provisioning, so field debugging can see
  them.

2026-08-03: Bound uv subprocesses with a timeout so a hang
can't wedge the queue

uv invocations ran via blockingRun, which waits on the
process forever with no kill path. A stalled uv (e.g. a
dropped connection with no TCP reset) would float the
provisioning panel indefinitely and queue every later
provision/launch/Dependency Editor pip call behind the stuck
item on the serial provisionQueue until app restart. Add
blockingRunWithTimeout: (SIGKILLs the task past the deadline
and returns its nonzero exit) and apply a generous 1-hour
safety net to build operations (venv/pip) and a 60s timeout
to quick metadata queries (uv --version, uv python list).
This is a hang backstop, not a slow-link cap; legitimate
slow provisioning finishes well under it.

2026-08-03: Fall back to a non-atomic venv swap on non-APFS
volumes

provisionFullEnvironment swapped the rebuilt .venv into
place with renamex_np(RENAME_SWAP), which is APFS/HFS+ only.
A custom scripts folder on SMB/NFS/exFAT returned
ENOTSUP/EINVAL, failing every rebuild forever with an
unactionable errno-only message. On those errnos, fall back
to move-old-aside, rename-new-into-place, delete-old
(restoring the old venv if the new one does not land), and
include the path in the remaining error message.

2026-08-03: Enforce the minimum-environment-version gate
after a failed migration

The gate-on branch launched the restored legacy environment
directly when a uv migration failed or was declined,
skipping the environmentVersionAt /
iTermMinimumPythonEnvironmentVersion check the gate-off path
enforces. A user who declined the download could thus run an
environment the legacy path refuses to launch for security
reasons. Apply the same minimum-version check to the
restored env and route to the runtime upgrade prompt when it
is below the floor.

2026-08-03: Make script install atomic at the destination
volume

Two related replace-import data-loss fixes:

- The final install moved the provisioned container from the
temp dir into the
  scripts folder with moveItemAtPath. A custom scripts
  folder (customScriptsFolder) can be on a different volume,
  where that is a non-atomic copy+delete; a crash mid-copy
  left a partial REAL directory at the target, which the
  crash-recovery sweep mistakes for a completed install and
  then deletes the user's only backup.  Stage on the
  destination volume under a dotted name and rename into
  place (same-volume, atomic), so the target exists only
  once fully populated regardless of volume topology. The
  recovery sweep now also cleans orphaned .installing-*
  staging dirs.

- When moving the existing script aside fails during a
replace, the code fell back
  to deleting it and proceeding; a later canceled download
  then reported a quiet success with the original gone (the
  bug the move-aside flow fixed). Abort the replace with an
  error instead and leave the script untouched.

2026-08-03: Don't delete a remap record that failed to move
during merge

invalidateSharedVenvRemaps merged each record from .remaps
into .remaps-stale, then unconditionally removed .remaps. A
record whose moveItem failed was still inside
.remaps at that point, so removing the directory deleted it from both the live and
the stale cache, losing the script's offline fallback.
Remove .remaps only when it is empty after the loop; a
failed-to-move record stays live (harmless) and the next
upgrade retries the merge.

2026-08-03: Add release notes for the Rosetta prompt, crash
recovery, and dotfile hiding

2026-08-03: Don't stack two modals on a failed Apple Silicon
runtime refetch

On a genuine download failure the runtime downloader shows
its own "Python Runtime Unavailable" alert; the launcher
then showed a second, describing the same failure.  Alert
from the caller only when the download reported success yet
the binary is still not arm64 (a case the downloader does
not itself report); on a download failure or user cancel the
downloader already presented a modal, so stay silent.

2026-08-03: uv provisioner: pip-fail fallback, merge stale
remaps, one provisioned predicate

Three follow-ups to the round-2 provisioner fixes:

- The offline stale-remap fallback fired only when `uv venv`
failed. When the
  resolved minor's CPython is already on disk, `uv venv`
  succeeds offline and it is the subsequent `uv pip install`
  that needs the network; that branch returned an error with
  no fallback. Apply the same fallback to both build
  failures.

- invalidateSharedVenvRemaps replaced .remaps-stale
wholesale, so a second uv
  upgrade dropped records left by the first for scripts
  never relaunched between upgrades, losing their offline
  fallback. Merge per file instead.

- The "venv is provisioned" predicate (executable
interpreter + .provisioned
  marker) existed in three copies that could silently
  diverge. Hoist one venvsRoot-parameterized
  sharedVenvIsProvisioned/sharedVenvInterpreterPath used by
  the fast path, the stale-remap fallback, and the
  module-refresh scan.

2026-08-03: Fix crash-recovery sweep deleting the user's
only replace backup

recoverStaleReplaceBackups decided the replacement had
completed with fileExistsAtPath:, which follows symlinks.
During a full-environment replace the target Scripts/<name>
is a symlink into a temp extraction dir that survives an app
crash and relaunch, so in the most likely crash window
(mid-provision) the sweep resolved the symlink to the
surviving temp dir, concluded success, and took the delete
branch, permanently destroying the user's original script.

Use attributesOfItemAtPath: (lstat, does not follow
symlinks) and treat a symlink (or a missing target) as "did
not complete", taking the restore branch. Also validate the
fixed-length tail really is "-<UUID>" via NSUUID before
acting, so a user file coincidentally named ".replacing-" is
never renamed to a truncated name.

2026-08-03: Derive legacy Python version by numeric max and
from saved env

legacyEnvironmentPythonVersion, used to warn about forced
version bumps for pin-less scripts, picked the
lexicographically-first version directory (ordering "3.10.4"
before "3.8.6") and read only iterm2env/versions. A
container killed mid-migration has only saved-iterm2env at
derivation time, yielding nil and a silent migration to the
default. Pick the numeric maximum (matching interpreter
selection) and fall back to saved-iterm2env/versions.

2026-08-03: Restore the Building Script window for gate-off
full-env imports

Deferring the please-wait window to provisioningDidBegin
dropped it entirely for the legacy (gate-off)
full-environment install path, which never invokes that
callback, so a multi-minute env copy plus pip install ran
with no progress UI.  Invoke provisioningDidBegin before
installPythonEnvironmentTo on that path, after the
download-consent prompt.

2026-08-03: Hide and crash-recover replace-import backups

The move-aside backup created while replacing a script had
two gaps: the Scripts menu walk skipped only .DS_Store, so
during a long provision the transient
".replacing-<name>-<UUID>" backup showed as a broken menu
item; and nothing recovered it if the app died between
move-aside and restore/cleanup, leaking the user's script as
a hidden orphan.

Skip all hidden entries in the menu enumeration (and don't
descend into hidden directories), and add a launch-time
sweep (recoverStaleReplaceBackups) that restores the
original when the target is missing or deletes a leaked
backup when the replacement completed, mirroring the
saved-iterm2env reclamation.

2026-08-03: Fix Apple Silicon runtime refetch: consent
first, silent on cancel, deduped

The new full-environment rebuild path
(ensureArm64StandardRuntimeForPythonVersion) collapsed a
user cancel into a plain failure, so
reallyUpgradeFullEnvironmentScriptAt showed "could not be
downloaded, check your network" after a deliberate cancel,
the same bogus-blame bug just fixed in the basic path, and
it duplicated ~10 lines of the refetch logic that then
diverged. It also deleted the Intel-only runtime before
asking consent.

Hoist one shared
refetchArm64StandardRuntimeForPythonVersion:completion: that
confirms BEFORE the destructive delete and reports the
downloader status. Both the rebuild and basic-launch paths
now stay silent on user cancel and only show the network
error on a genuine download failure.

2026-08-03: uv provisioner: actually interleave refresh,
defer remap report, offline fallback

Three follow-ups to the previous provisioner review fixes:

- enqueuePeriodicSharedVenvModuleUpgrades bulk-enqueued one
provisionQueue item
  per venv, but provisionQueue is serial FIFO, so a user
  request arriving during the refresh still queued behind
  all N network-bound pip upgrades, exactly the pre-fix
  behavior. Self-schedule instead: each venv enqueues its
  successor only after it finishes, so user work submitted
  in between lands between venvs.

- The forced-remap record+report was hoisted before the venv
build, so a failed
  build (transient PyPI outage) told the user the version
  changed and left a record pointing at a nonexistent venv,
  repeating the modal every launch. Report before the early
  return only in the already-provisioned case; on the build
  path defer until the build succeeds.

- invalidateSharedVenvRemaps deleted records outright, so an
offline launch right
  after a uv upgrade could fail: re-resolution picks the
  now-available minor whose CPython download needs the
  network, even though a previously-resolved venv exists.
  Move records to .remaps-stale instead and, on a build
  failure, fall back to a still-provisioned stale-resolved
  venv.

2026-08-03: Fix legacy launch edge cases: nil pin, macOS 27
arch, dangling restore

Five launcher fixes from the review, all on the legacy
full-environment path:

- A script whose setup.cfg has no exact python pin (absent,
or a range like
  ">=3.7") migrated to the current default with every
  version-bump warning bypassed. Derive the real prior
  version from the on-disk iterm2env/versions tree (new
  iTermScriptRuntime.legacyEnvironmentPythonVersion) so the
  forced remap is detected and warned, both at migration and
  in the startup scan.

- A rebuild hard-links the shared standard runtime, which on
a Rosetta-less
  macOS (27+) may be Intel-only yet version-current. Ensure
  that runtime is arm64 (refetching it if needed) before
  linking, so the rebuilt env can run.

- After a failed uv migration the interpreter was relaunched
from the
  pre-rollback path, which dangles when the container
  started saved-iterm2env only (killed mid-migration).
  Re-resolve the interpreter after the restore.

- With the gate on and an unparseable setup.cfg, the early
exit skipped the
  macOS 27 Intel-only check, so an unrunnable env
  exec-failed cryptically instead of showing the clear
  error. Route it through the same arch check.

- Canceling the Apple Silicon runtime download reported a
bogus network error;
  it now stays silent on user cancellation.

2026-08-03: Don't destroy a replaced script when its import
is canceled

Two import-flow fixes:

- The Replace Script flow ran removeScriptNamed (which kills
and deletes the
  user's installed script) before
  installFullEnvironmentTrusted showed the uv
  download-consent prompt. Declining translated to a quiet
  success, so the user's working script was gone with
  nothing installed and no dialog. Move the existing script
  aside instead of deleting it, restore it on failure or
  cancellation, and surface the outcome; delete the backup
  only on success.

- The "Building Script" please-wait window was ordered front
the moment it was
  created, floating over the intervening replace and
  download-consent prompts despite the provisioningDidBegin
  deferral. Add a factory variant that creates the window
  without ordering it front, and show it for the first time
  in provisioningDidBegin.

2026-08-03: Stop re-walking the Scripts tree on every menu
rebuild

maybeWarnAboutUvVersionBumps latched its "shown" flag only
after producing a non-nil warning, so in the common case
(gate on, no pending bumps) it re-ran
collectLegacyFullEnvironmentScriptsUnder on every -build: a
synchronous main-thread recursive walk with a per-entry stat
and setup.cfg parse, fired by every launch, every SCEvents
file event under the Scripts tree, every install, and
scriptsFolderDidChange. Latch after the (single) scan
regardless of outcome, while leaving the gate-off case
unlatched so enabling uv later still scans once.

2026-08-03: Harden uv provisioner: slow-link upgrades, remap
cache, interleaved refresh

Four review fixes in the uv provisioner:

- boundedDownload capped the ENTIRE transfer at 120s. That
is right for the
  tiny manifest on provisionQueue but broke the ~37 MB
  tarball upgrade on any link below ~2.5 Mbps, so a broken
  installed uv could never self-heal. The tarball now uses a
  generous total cap plus a 60s idle timeout.

- A forced Python version remap (e.g. 3.7->3.9) was recorded
and reported only
  on the venv build path, so when the resolved venv already
  existed the offline fast path never matched for that
  script (every launch spawned uv) and the user was never
  told. Record/report before the already-provisioned early
  return.

- .remaps records were never invalidated, pinning a remapped
script to the
  bumped minor forever even after a newer uv could provide
  the requested one.  Clear them after a uv binary upgrade
  so the next launch re-resolves.

- The daily shared-venv module refresh ran as one block on
provisionQueue,
  making a launch or Dependency Editor action that arrived
  mid-refresh wait for N sequential PyPI round trips.
  Enqueue each venv as its own queue item so user work is
  serviced between them.

2026-08-03: Version-qualify the hosted uv tarball filename

The build named the archive uv-<triple>.tar.gz with no
version, sign_and_copy derived the hosted filename from that
basename, and mv -f clobbered the previously published file.
Because the manifest deliberately keeps older entries (the
macOS-bracket design), the second annual publish left the
older entry's RSA signature no longer matching the bytes now
served at its shared URL, so every user whose macOS selected
the older bracket could never install or upgrade uv, exactly
the population the bracket protects.

Embed the uv version in the hosted filename
(uv-<version>-<triple>.tar.gz) so each manifest entry
references immutable bytes, and keep old files hosted.

2026-08-03: Tolerate unknown uv manifest entries; treat max
macOS as a family cap

Two forward-compatibility fixes to the annually-updated uv
manifest:

- iTermUvManifest.parse decoded the array all-or-nothing
with every field
  required, so a single future entry with an unfamiliar
  shape (missing or renamed field, retyped value) made the
  whole decode throw and stranded every shipped build with
  "the uv manifest could not be parsed", even when a
  compatible entry was still listed. Decode per entry
  tolerantly and skip only the entries this build cannot
  understand.

- maximum_macos_version was compared inclusively against the
always-three-part
  running version, so a cap written as "13.4" excluded every
  later point release of the family it meant to cap (13.4.1
  > 13.4.0). Treat the maximum as a family cap at the
  precision given: "13.4" bounds all of 13.4.x, "13" bounds
  all of 13.x.

2026-08-03: Swap the rebuilt uv venv into place atomically

The transactional re-provision built the new .venv aside and
swapped it in with FileManager.replaceItemAt, which is a
multi-step move/backup/move dance with brief windows where
.venv is partial or absent. A concurrent reader (the
Dependency Editor runs `uv pip show` against .venv to
display installed versions) could catch one of those windows
and fail to open a dist-info file (observed as "Invalid
argument (os error 22)" on direct_url.json), surfacing a
spurious [Error] even though the rebuild succeeded.

Swap the freshly built venv with the old one using
renamex_np(RENAME_SWAP), a single atomic syscall that
exchanges the two directory inodes with no intermediate
state, then discard the old venv. A fresh container with no
existing .venv still uses a plain rename into place.

2026-08-03: Make uv full-environment provisioning
transactional

Changing a uv script's Python version re-provisions its
.venv. The previous code removed the existing .venv before
rebuilding, so if the rebuild's pip install failed (e.g.
downgrading to a Python minor where a pinned dependency such
as aiohttp>=3.14.3 has no wheel), the script was left with
an empty, broken venv and its working environment was gone.
The Dependency Editor then showed [Error] for every
dependency.

Build the new venv aside (.venv.building) and swap it into
place only after uv venv and the package install both
succeed. On any failure the temp build is discarded and the
existing .venv is untouched. The venv is created
--relocatable, so the rename keeps its shebangs valid. Fresh
containers (create/import/migrate) have no existing .venv,
so the swap is just a rename.

2026-08-03: Populate uv version pickers from the installed
uv, not a hardcoded list

The new-script and Dependency Editor Python-version pickers
offered a hardcoded knownAvailableMinors (3.8-3.14), which
is wrong in both directions: an older installed uv might not
support the newest entries, and a newer uv would not surface
versions past the list. uv's installable set is embedded per
uv release, so the picker must reflect the installed binary.

Add iTermUvProvisioner.availableMinorsWithCompletion: (runs
`uv python list` off the main thread, delivers on main,
empty if uv is absent) and use it to fill both pickers
asynchronously: - Dependency Editor: shows the current
version immediately, then fills the full
  list the installed uv supports (current always included),
  guarding against the user switching scripts mid-query.
- New script: shows the default as a placeholder, then
replaces it with the
  installed uv's list (default selected if provided, else
  the newest); if uv is not installed yet, keeps the default
  and lets the first provision resolve.

knownAvailableMinors now backs only the one-time startup
version-bump prediction (which runs before uv may be
installed and re-resolves against live uv at migration
time).

2026-08-03: Offer and apply uv Python-version changes in the
Dependency Editor

The Dependency Editor's Python version popup only listed the
currently installed version for a uv script, and selecting a
version only ran `uv pip install` into the existing
(old-version) .venv, so the interpreter never changed.

- Populate the popup with the minors uv can provide
(iTermUvMigration
  knownAvailableMinors) plus the current version, sorted
  numerically.
- On change, re-provision the full environment at the new
version via
  downloadAndProvisionFullEnvironment (with a progress
  window), which rebuilds the .venv, reinstalls
  dependencies, and rewrites setup.cfg.
- provisionFullEnvironment removes any existing .venv before
`uv venv` so a
  version change rebuilds cleanly instead of relying on uv's
  replace behavior (a no-op for fresh
  create/import/migrate).

2026-08-03: Use the bare package name for pip show in the
Dependency Editor

A dependency may be a full requirement (e.g.
"aiohttp>=3.14.3", as written back when a package is pinned
to its installed version). Passing that to `pip show` fails,
and uv's pip is strict ("Not a valid package or extra
name"), so the Dependency Editor showed [Error] for the
installed version.

fetchVersionOfPackage now passes the bare name (via
-[NSString pythonPackage], now exposed in the header) to
`pip show` on both the uv and legacy paths. The re-pin after
an upgrade also uses the bare name so an already-versioned
dependency does not become "aiohttp>=3.14.3>=3.14.3". Adds
tests for the name extraction.

2026-08-03: Launch full-env scripts by their main.py path as
full environments

A full-environment script launched by its inner main.py path
(Foo/Foo/Foo.py) rather than its container (Foo) was treated
as a basic script and run on the shared standard runtime, so
it could not import its own dependencies
(ModuleNotFoundError). This happens when launching from a
stale index, e.g. Open Quickly built before the script's
environment finished installing, or by the .py path
directly.

launchScriptWithAbsolutePath now redirects such a path to
its container via the new
iTermAPIScriptLauncher.fullEnvironmentContainerForMainPyPath:
(which only redirects when the container actually has a
runnable environment), so it launches with its own
environment. This covers Open Quickly, the menu,
AppleScript, and the status bar since they all funnel
through here. Hermetic tests cover the redirect and the
negative cases (plain .py, non-main .py, main-shaped path
with no environment).

2026-08-03: Document macOS 27 Rosetta handling (manual plan
+ release notes)

Add a macOS 27 section to the uv manual test plan (no
Rosetta prompt; gate-off fresh install fetches arm64; x86
fixture full-env rebuilds; x86 shared runtime is replaced;
gate-on migrates; forced migration failure yields the
explanatory error, not an exec failure) and a release-notes
entry.

2026-08-03: Handle x86-only legacy environments on
Rosetta-less macOS

On macOS 27 an Intel-only iterm2env cannot run and Rosetta
is unavailable, so exec would fail cryptically. At the three
legacy launch sites, when Rosetta cannot be installed and
the interpreter has no arm64 slice
(iTermRosettaSupport.legacyLaunchDisposition):

- Gate off, full-environment: rebuild the env from setup.cfg
against the current
  arm64 runtime via reallyUpgradeFullEnvironmentScriptAt
  (the same path version-based upgrades use), instead of
  exec-failing. A failed rebuild shows its existing error.
- Gate on, migration-failure fallback: the rollback restored
the (Intel-only)
  legacy env; show a clear alert + Script Console error and
  do not exec it. The env stays intact for turning the gate
  off (which then rebuilds).
- Basic scripts, shared runtime: the version-based
downloader will not refetch a
  current-but-Intel-only runtime, so remove it and download
  the arm64 build the manifest now serves, then launch
  (clear error if it still is not native).

The arch check only runs when Rosetta is actually
unavailable, so macOS <= 26 launches (and the pre-migration
Rosetta ordering there) are unchanged.

2026-08-03: Add macOS 27 Rosetta decision layer and skip the
un-installable prompt

macOS 27 removes Rosetta 2. Add iTermRosettaSupport with
pure, injectable decisions
(canInstallRosetta(osMajorVersion:), shouldPromptForRosetta,
legacyLaunchDisposition) and a Mach-O arm64-slice reader
(thin + fat headers).  installRosettaIfNeededThen: no longer
shows the "Install Rosetta?" prompt or runs `softwareupdate
--install-rosetta` when Rosetta cannot be installed (macOS
27+); behavior on macOS <= 26 is unchanged. Unit-tests cover
the OS/gate/arch matrix and the Mach-O reader against
crafted header fixtures.

2026-08-03: Add user-invokable uv install / check-for-update
under the gate

Scripts > Manage now offers the uv equivalent of the legacy
runtime flow:

downloads uv (existing
  consent + progress UI) and eagerly provisions the

  offline.
- Gate on, uv installed: "Check for Updated Runtime" calls a
new
  userRequestedUpgradeCheck that bypasses the daily

2026-08-03: Launch remapped-shebang basic scripts offline

A python3.7 shebang provisions the shared 3.9 venv (3.7 is
unavailable). On a later offline launch the fast path missed
(venvs/3.7 does not exist), uv python list failed, resolve()
kept "3.7", and the launch failed even though the
provisioned 3.9 venv would work.

Persist the requested->resolved mapping under
venvs/.remaps/<requested> when a shared venv is provisioned
for a remapped version, and consult it in the fast path
(provisionedSharedVenvInterpreter) so a repeat launch finds
the working venv with no uv and no network. The resolver
takes an injected venvs root and is hermetically tested
(direct match, remap follow, unprovisioned-remap and
marker-required cases).

2026-08-03: Fix async-delete test race, download timeout,
and download-window close

Third-pass review follow-ups:

- discardLegacyBackup gains an optional completion (run on
the utility queue
  after the removal); testDiscardBackupRemovesSaved waits on
  an expectation instead of asserting synchronously against
  the fire-and-forget delete, which raced and failed.
- boundedDownload sets timeoutIntervalForResource (120s).
The byte cap does not
  bound time, so a host trickling bytes slower than the idle
  timeout could wedge provisionQueue (fetchSelectedEntry
  runs there for user-visible launches).
- The optional-component download window is now
non-closable. Its title-bar
  close box bypassed the Cancel button, leaving the transfer
  running with no completion delivered and wedging the
  caller (e.g. a stuck .its import); the always-present
  Cancel/OK button remains the dismiss path.

2026-08-03: Harden the version-bump scan and the background

  attributesOfItemAtPath, so a symlink cycle can't recurse
  forever) and bounds the depth. advancedSettingsDidChange

  provisionQueue, so it never blocks a user-visible

2026-08-03: Fix uv install/download regressions from the
review hardening

Second-pass review follow-ups:

- update_uv_manifest.sh chmods the temp file 0644 before
os.replace. mkstemp
  creates it 0600 and replace preserves that, so the first
  regeneration after the atomic-write change would have
  served manifest.json as 403, killing first installs and
  background upgrades in the field.
- Force 0755 on the uv binary after the atomic replace.
replaceItemAt preserves
  the ORIGINAL file's permissions, so a non-executable
  partial left by an interrupted attempt would stay
  non-executable and isInstalled would never turn true
  (endless re-download). Test now covers the 0644->0755
  self-heal.
- Validate the manifest-declared size (0 < size <= 200 MB)
at selection, since
  the manifest is unsigned. This removes an Int-overflow
  crash on a huge size and the size<=0 that disabled the
  download cap. The interactive fetcher clamps defensively
  too, and the silent background download is now bounded via
  a streaming URLSession that cancels past the cap (was an
  unbounded Data(contentsOf:)). The manifest fetch itself is
  bounded at 1 MB.

Adds hermetic tests: zero/huge size rejection, inclusive
floor, and shouldUpgradeUv("unknown") self-heal.

2026-08-03: Make the uv upgrade decision and manifest
selection unit-testable

Split the pure parse -> select -> floor step out of
fetchSelectedEntry as
selectedEntry(fromManifestData:runningMacOSVersion:), and
extract the upgrade decision as
shouldUpgradeUv(installedVersion:manifestVersion:). Both are
now hermetically tested with crafted manifest bytes (no
network, no real uv): newest-compatible selection, rejection
below the minimum-version floor, macOS-bracket rejection,
garbage manifest, and upgrade-only-when-strictly-newer.

This covers the bug-prone half of the background upgrade
path ([7]); the actual download + RSA-verify + atomic swap
is the same installDownloadedTarball path already covered by
the RSA-reject unit test and the live install test.

2026-08-03: Add periodic background upgrades for uv and the
iterm2 module

Once a day at most (persistent rate limit, checked at
launch), if the manifest offers a newer uv than installed,
silently download and atomically replace it (RSA-verified;
the minimum-version floor still blocks downgrades), then
upgrade iterm2/certifi/pyobjc to the latest in every
provisioned shared basic-script venv. Full-environment
scripts manage their own dependencies and are left alone.
Without this a first-installed uv was never re-checked, so a
broken uv could never be replaced and basic scripts' iterm2
module never updated.

Also note in the download consent prompt that additional
Python versions are fetched automatically later, and record
the deferred uv disk GC in Phase 5.

and bumps it, report
  the old to new change: a Script Console line and a
  suppressible modal. Every provisioning path (create,
  import, migrate) funnels through
  downloadAndProvisionFullEnvironment, so reporting it there
  covers all of them.  Previously marker.remappedFrom was
  computed and discarded everywhere.
- Dependency Editor failure alerts no longer say "pip3"
specifically (uv runs
  `uv pip`); reworded to "the pip output".
- Do not migrate a legacy script to uv when its setup.cfg is
missing or
  unparseable: migrating with an empty dependency list would
  silently produce a broken environment. Log loudly and keep
  the script on its working legacy env.
- Use the shared default-version constant instead of another
hardcoded "3.12".

Menu-launched scripts received only ITERM2_COOKIE and

instead of the socket of the build launched with -suite.

2026-08-03: Support OpenAI Zero Data Retention orgs (issue
12521)

ZDR orgs cannot retain responses server-side, so the
Responses API rejects previous_response_id with a 400 on the
second message. Add a global "zero data retention" setting
under Settings > General > AI.  When enabled,
supportsPreviousResponseID returns false so every turn is a

2026-08-02: Fully linkify long URLs: fix hard newlines, IDN,
and images

Follow-up refinements to the long-URL forward-walk:

- Build the walk on the shared enumerateCharsInRange:
machinery
  instead of a hand-rolled successorOfCoord loop, so it
  respects hard line breaks and no longer joins a URL to
  unrelated text across a hard newline in the default
  configuration.
- Decode each cell to its actual character(s) and judge
membership
  with the passed-in character set, so IDN hosts that use
  composed graphemes are no longer truncated.
- Exclude image cells before interpreting theChar.code,
whose value
  for an image is an image number that could collide with a
  DWC sentinel.
- Add URLForwardWalkTests coverage: soft-wrap crossing,
coordinate
  mapping, maxChars cap, hard-newline stop, image stop,
  composed grapheme inclusion, and non-member complex-char
  stop, plus a manual test fixture in tests/.

2026-08-02: Linkify long URLs past the -click capture window
(#706)

When -clicking a plain URL, iTermURLActionFactory only sees
a bounded window of text around the click
(maxSemanticHistoryPrefixOrSuffix, 2000 chars each side,
further clamped to 10 lines). A very long URL (e.g.  7-8k
chars) therefore linkified only its first ~1-2k characters:
the URL-legal run reached the edge of the captured suffix
and stopped.

Rather than re-run the matcher on ever-larger windows,
detect when the run reaches the end of the captured suffix
and walk the grid forward one cell at a time, consuming
URL-legal characters until a separator (or null, complex
char, or buffer end), capped at 100 KB. The walked
characters and their grid coords are appended to the located
suffix, so the existing punctuation trimming, validation,
and coord mapping all operate on the widened window
unchanged, including the underline drawn on hover.

Add -[iTermTextExtractor
locatedStringByWalkingForwardFrom:characterSet: maxChars:]
and unit tests in ExtendURLSearchResultsTests.

Test: tools/run_tests.expect ModernTests/URLForwardWalkTests
2026-08-02: Add provider presets and a Test Connection
button to the AI model editor

The manual AI model editor now offers one-click presets for

using the stored key for the resolved vendor (prompting for

a spinner while it runs.

Issue 12904

2026-08-02: Keep edge-aligned window width when adding a tab
(issue 12917)

PseudoTerminal.setup(session:) unconditionally called
updateDesiredSize, so adding a tab to a left/right-of-screen
window re-seeded the window size helper's
desiredColumns/desiredRows from the new tab's profile. A
later canonicalizeWindowFrame (e.g. from a
display-configuration change via windowDidChangeScreen:)
reads those values on the cell-based width path and resized
the window to the tab's profile width: a 50%-width second
profile grew the window to half the screen, and a narrower
profile shrank it. Nothing cleared the leaked value, so the
window never returned to its own width.

Only seed the desired size when this is the first session of
a brand-new window (existingViewSize == nil). Adding a tab
no longer disturbs the window's width, so canonicalize keeps
it anchored to the window's own profile/live frame.

2026-08-02: Probe local network on every launch to keep TCC
permission (issue 12956)

The local network permission prompter was gated on the app
version, so it only ran once per version. After a tccutil
reset or an OS upgrade that clears the TCC record, macOS
drops iTerm2 from the Local Network privacy list and ping
fails with "no route to host", but the version gate
prevented re-prompting until the app version changed.

The NWBrowser probe is idempotent: macOS only shows its
prompt when there is no recorded decision, so re-probing an
already-allowed or already-denied app is silent. Drop the
version gate and probe on every launch so the permission
recovers on the next relaunch.

2026-08-02: Make enclosure-less profile controls searchable

profileTypesForView: returned nil.visibleForProfileTypes (0,
ProfileTypeNone) for any control not wrapped in a modal
enclosure. Such controls are never conditionally hidden, so
they apply to every profile type, but the query-time filter
(doc.profileTypes & allowedProfileTypes) excluded them
whenever a concrete profile was being edited. That hid
several Colors-tab settings from preferences search,
including Border around active pane, Use custom tab color,
Use separate colors for light and dark mode, the Light/Dark
mode popup, and Color Presets. Treat a missing enclosure as
ProfileTypeAll.

2026-08-02: Fix typo in daemons.rst (#707) 2026-08-02:
Render sixels with a doubled-up ST terminator (issue 12259)

Some tools emit a sixel whose String Terminator is ESC ESC \
instead of the well-formed ESC \. The sixel hook treated the
second ESC as a broken sequence, discarded the entire
accumulated image (VT100_NOTSUPPORT), and left the trailing
backslash to print as a stray glyph.

The hook now renders the pixels accumulated so far and lets
the trailing bytes reparse as a fresh sequence, matching how
real hardware and xterm behave (sixels draw incrementally,
so an unexpected ESC just ends the string). Specifically:

- ESC ESC \ (doubled terminator): put back only the second
ESC so the
  remaining ESC \ reparses as a harmless ST.
- ESC <cseq> (an aborting escape such as ESC [ 2 J): put
back the whole
  sequence so it reparses and executes, instead of losing
  its ESC.
- An empty-body introducer aborted by an escape emits
nothing rather than
  stamping a broken-image glyph and paying a synchronous
  decode per fragment (avoids spew when binary output merely
  contains ESC P ... q).

Also stop VT100Parser's CopyToClipboard capture from
clobbering savedData that a DCS hook already assembled,
which otherwise dropped the image (the split
doubled-terminator case emits a zero-consumed DCS_SIXEL
token).

Follow-up TODOs left for the same pathology in the C1 ST
scan and in OSC string parsing.

2026-08-02: Keep OSC 9;4 progress bar above the active-pane
border

The active-pane border's top stroke drew at the same top
edge as the progress bar with the same 2pt thickness and was
added topmost, so it painted over the bar and hid it
whenever the border was enabled on the active pane. Keep the
border directly behind the progress bar via a single helper
called from both creation sites; the other three border
edges remain visible since the bar spans only the top.

Issue 12960

2026-08-02: Fix Cmd-Click failing to open files in JetBrains
IDEs (issue 12958)

Semantic History dispatched JetBrains IDEs (IntelliJ IDEA,
WebStorm, Rider) through `open -nb <bundleid> --args ...`.
That has two problems:

- `open -n` forces a brand new IDE instance instead of
forwarding the
  open-file request to the instance that is already running,
  so the file silently never appears in the user's project
  window (open returns 0 the moment it hands off to
  LaunchServices, hiding the failure).
- The current JetBrains native launcher rejects option-first
argument
  order, reporting "unrecognized option: --line" when --line
  precedes the path.

Invoke the IDE's launcher binary directly
(Contents/MacOS/idea, webstorm, rider via
CFBundleExecutable), which detects the running instance and
forwards to it, and pass the path before --line, which both
the old and new launchers accept. This restores the pre-2022
direct-launch behavior that was replaced when WebStorm and
Rider were added, generalized across all three IDEs.

Remove the now-dead openAppWithBundleIdentifier: and the
long-unused intelliJIDEALauncherInApplicationBundle:
helpers.

2026-08-02: Tmux: correct pane geometry for
pane-border-status (issue 12925)

In tmux control mode the layout string tmux sends the client
reports the full window height for every pane, ignoring the
row tmux reserves for pane-border-status. iTerm2 sizes its
sessions from that layout, so with pane-border-status
top/bottom the session ended up one row taller than the real
pane grid the shell was given: the bottom line went unused
and the cursor landed a row high after reattaching.

Correct the geometry, verified against tmux 3.7b for single,
side-by-side, stacked, three-stacked, L-shaped, and 2x2
layouts:
  top    - a leaf at yoff 0 gives up its first row (yoff +=
  1, height -= 1) bottom - a leaf whose bottom edge is the
  window bottom gives up its last row

Inbound: - TmuxController caches each window's
pane-border-status, learned from a
  new #{pane-border-status} field in the detailed and
  runtime list-windows formats (tmux >= 2.3).
- TmuxWindowOpener parses every layout through
parsedAdjustedLayoutFromString:,
  which applies TmuxLayoutParser
  -parseTree:adjustedForPaneBorderStatus:.
- The zoom/maximize path builds a single-leaf tree from the
uncorrected
  root height, so it applies the same correction there too.

Outbound (keeps the size we report to tmux one row taller so
tmux has room for the border row it carves out, instead of
us shrinking to match the reduced pane and spiraling the
window down a row at a time): - iTermTmuxLayoutBuilder grows
the leaves on the affected window edge,
  which nets exactly one extra row for the window
  (side-by-side panes combine with MAX, stacked with SUM),
  matching tmux's own accounting.
- PTYTab -tmuxSize adds the row back for the refresh-client
-C path.

tmux sends no notification when pane-border-status is
toggled mid-session, so a runtime change is not reflected
until the next relist; the common case of setting it in
.tmux.conf before attaching is handled. The
non-variable-window MIN client size across windows with a
per-window option remains approximate; both limitations are
noted in code.

Adds parser and builder unit tests, including
outbound/inbound round-trip symmetry.

2026-08-02: Fix Kitty keyboard protocol SET behavior (#711)

The SET form should modify the flags on the current stack
frame, not destroy the entire stack.  2026-08-02: Make
Centered windows behave like normal windows (issue 12918)

The Centered window style was modeled on the edge-attached
window types, so its windows had no title bar or
traffic-light controls, snapped back to the profile grid
size on minimize/restore, and did not adopt the merged
chrome of the Minimal and Compact themes.

A Centered window is now a normal window in every respect
except that it stays centered:

- It has a title bar and close/minimize/zoom buttons,
reclassified
  to match WINDOW_TYPE_MAXIMIZED across the appearance
  switches.
- Routine canonicalization preserves the user's size and
only
  re-centers, so minimize/restore no longer resizes it.
- A new WINDOW_TYPE_COMPACT_CENTERED theme variant,
mirroring
  WINDOW_TYPE_COMPACT_MAXIMIZED, gives it the compact merged
  chrome in the Minimal and Compact tab styles, and
  updateWindowType now accepts the centered pair so
  switching themes rebuilds the chrome in place instead of
  tripping an assert.

(issue 4361)

Bidi reordering and CoreText-based Arabic/Persian cursive

in Settings > General > Experimental.

2026-08-01: Report Caps Lock with its Kitty keycode instead
of "A". Issue 12922

Toggling Caps Lock under the Kitty keyboard protocol with
"report all keys as escape codes" enabled sent \e[65u
(codepoint 65 = "A") instead of the Caps Lock functional key
code 57358.

The flagsChanged branch of KeyEventInfo computed
unicodeKeyCode from a switch that listed the
shift/control/option/command keys but omitted kVK_CapsLock,
so csiUNumber was 0. With no key-number, the CSI u encoder
dropped the number parameter entirely and the caps-lock
modifier value (65) slid into the first parameter slot,
which any application reads as the codepoint for "A".

Add kVK_CapsLock -> 57358 to the switch. A toggle now
reports \e[57358;65u. Adds a mode-8 Caps Lock step to the
record-replay harness.

2026-08-01: AI chat: let session-bound chats register
watchers on their linked session

Watchers (register_watch / unregister_watch / list_watches)
were only available in orchestration chats. A chat linked to
a terminal session can now watch that session for a
target_state or a screen-judged condition, reusing the
orchestration dispatcher machinery via the broker rather
than duplicating it. The target is implicitly the linked
session; a foreign session_guid is rejected.

Session-bound watches stay governed by the per-category
permission model: a condition (screen) watch needs View
Contents; a target_state watch needs Check Terminal State
(plus View Contents on a statusless session). These are read
from the raw permission setting (not the tool-exposure set,
which drops Check Terminal State at Always), enforced at
registration and at every point of use (fire gates,
poller/escalation mayRead, reconcile, regate) so a live or
global-default revoke can never leak a read. When View
Contents is Ask, a one-time consent prompt is shown;
watchers record that consent so a later Always->Ask
downgrade stops unconsented reads.  Tab-status watches
without a screen backstop are flagged to the model.

The read-category policy lives in one place
(WorkgroupWatcher), the watch target/permission resolution
is unit-tested, and the register_watch prose is built from a
shared spine so the orchestration and session-bound
descriptions can't drift.

2026-08-01: tmux: stop injecting focus reports into panes
that never enabled mode 1004

iTerm2 queried the server-global focus-events option once at
attach and, if it was on, force-enabled focus reporting on
every tmux pane by seeding terminal.reportFocus = YES. That
injected stray focus-report escape sequences (\e[O / \e[I)
into every pane on each focus change, even panes whose
foreground app never sent \e[?1004h. With cat -v the
sequences appear as visible input; with a shell they pollute
the prompt (and flip bash's vi mode, the symptom in issue
12352).

The seed was both unnecessary and wrong: tmux forwards the
app's DECSET 1004 in %output, which iTerm2 un-escapes and
feeds to the pane's VT100Terminal, so the per-pane emulator
already tracks focus reporting correctly on its own. Remove
the whole seed chain (the focus-events query and handler,
the _focusEvents ivar, the windowOpener.focusReporting
property, the kLayoutDictFocusReportingKey layout stamping,
and the SESSION_ARRANGEMENT_TMUX_FOCUS_REPORTING arrangement
seed) so tmux panes behave like non-tmux sessions: focus
reporting turns on only when the pane's app requests it.

The one case this cannot reconstruct is an app that enabled
1004h before iTerm2 attached, because tmux exposes no way to
query per-pane focus state; in practice the attach-time
resize causes full-screen apps to re-assert their modes.

Fixes issue 12933.

2026-07-31: Fix Make Screenshot dropping ASCII ligatures;
dedupe offscreen render

The screenshot/streamed-frame offscreen render path
hardcoded _asciiLigaturesAvailable = NO, so ASCII
programming ligatures (-> => != etc.) never appeared in
screenshots even when the profile enabled them and the font
supported them. Compute the value the same way the onscreen
path does, and defer the offscreen helper's didFinishSetup
until after it is computed so the corrected value (and zippy
state) propagates to the attributed string builder.

Also pin isRetina/antiAliasedShift to the render scale in
renderLinesToImage: (it was only done in
renderImageWithLines:), so stroke weight does not depend on
window state.

Cleanup: - Fold renderLinesToImage: and the
renderImageWithLines: family
  into one shared renderOffscreenImageForLines:... core so
  the bitmap/context/transform/draw scaffolding has a single
  copy.  The duplication is what let the isRetina fix land
  in only one method.
- Extract computeAsciiLigaturesAvailable so the onscreen and
  offscreen paths share one definition of ligature
  availability.

2026-07-31: Export IT2_SUITE to menu-launched API scripts

A Python script launched from the Scripts menu got
ITERM2_COOKIE but not IT2_SUITE in its environment. The
iterm2 library derives the API socket path from IT2_SUITE
(defaulting to the "iTerm2" suite), so a script run by a
secondary instance started with -suite would connect to the
main instance's socket and operate on the wrong app.
Terminal sessions already export IT2_SUITE via PTYSession's
environment (which also covers the Python REPL); this does
the same for the NSTask-based API script launcher.

2026-07-31: Close the new window when move_session fails to
insert its tab

move_session to a new window creates and registers the
destination window via terminalDraggedFromAnotherWindow
before moving the pane in. If the insert fails, close that
freshly created window instead of leaving it
registered-but-empty in the controller's terminal list,
where it would confuse window enumeration (including the
Python API's focus reporting).  The shared interactive drag
path is intentionally left alone since tab tear-off relies
on the window being briefly empty.

2026-07-31: Bump iterm2 module version number

2026-07-31: Fix empty windows from Undo Close crashing
Python API scripts

Pressing Undo Close (Cmd-Z) when the last closed session
could not be revived left an empty PseudoTerminal registered
in the controller's terminal list. Because a window is only
deregistered when it is closed, that phantom lingered
invisibly. It also broke the Python API: the focus info
reported a selected_tab of "0" (from messaging a nil
currentTab) for a tab that exists in no window, sending the
iterm2 library into an infinite async_refresh loop and
crashing scripts with a RecursionError.

Three fixes: - undoCloseSession: close the window if the
restore added no tab, so it
  never lingers empty.
- apiServerFocus: skip windows with no current tab instead
of emitting a
  dangling selected_tab, mirroring how list_sessions handles
  them.
- iterm2 Python library: guard async_refresh against
re-entrancy so an
  unresolvable selected_tab or session can no longer recurse
  without bound.

2026-07-31: Add advanced setting to control compact tab bar
window-button reserved width

The reserved width before the inline tab bar in the Compact
and Minimal themes was hardcoded to 75 points.
ExtraSpaceBeforeCompactTopTabBar can only add to that (it is
clamped to a nonnegative value), so there was no way to
reduce the empty gap between the stoplight buttons and the
tabs.

Add CompactTabBarStoplightButtonsWidth (default 75,
preserving current behavior) and read it in
tabBarInsetsForCompactWindow, clamped to be nonnegative. The
macOS 26 +3 adjustment is preserved.

2026-07-31: Let split-pane dividers snap into alignment
(issue 12932)

Dividers in independent split views (e.g. the top and bottom
rows of a 2x2 grid) are laid out separately and each snaps
to whole character-cell steps relative to its own current
position. When two such dividers ended up misaligned by less
than one cell, that sub-cell offset was unreachable by
dragging, so the rows could not be lined up and
double-clicking did not help either.

constrainSplitPosition now also considers every parallel
divider elsewhere in the tab and, when a drag comes within
half a cell of one, snaps to it (compared in window-base
coordinates so it is correct across flipped and nested split
views). The snap respects the existing min/max constraints
and falls back to the old quantized behavior when there is
nothing nearby to align to.

Also fix a typo in equalizeViewsAdjacentToSplitter where the
horizontal case summed the second view's height twice
instead of first + second, so double-clicking a horizontal
divider equalized to the wrong size.

2026-07-31: Authenticate the peer before adopting a
mono-server socket

The orphan-server adopter scans P_tmpdir (/var/tmp), a
world-writable sticky directory shared across users, for
iTerm2.socket.<pid> and connects with no check on who is
listening. A local attacker of any uid could pre-create such
a socket before iTerm2 launches, hand us an attacker-owned
pty master via SCM_RIGHTS, and man-in-the-middle the
"restored" session, capturing keystrokes and output
(including credentials typed into sudo).

Authenticate the peer in iTermFileDescriptorClientConnect
before trusting anything it sends. The authoritative check
is getpeereid() on the connected socket: the kernel reports
the peer's captured credentials, so it is race-free and
rejects a foreign-uid impostor before we recvmsg its
SCM_RIGHTS fd. A cheap lstat() ownership/type early-reject
avoids a needless connect() to the obvious planted-socket
case (explicitly not a security boundary, since it is TOCTOU
with the following connect). This does not defend against a
same-uid process, which is not a security boundary on macOS.
The legitimate spawn path binds and listens as the same
user, so it is unaffected.

Reported by NSEcho (Erhad Husovic).

2026-07-31: Command info popover: honor system 24-Hour Time
setting

The Started at time used a hardcoded 12-hour format
(h:mm:ss), ignoring the macOS 24-Hour Time setting. Derive
the time format from the j skeleton template so it respects
the user locale and hour-cycle preference. Issue 12929.

2026-07-31: Keep modifier on function keys in tmux -CC
sessions (issue 10717)

A tmux -CC client session sets its term type to tmux's
default-terminal (tmux-256color), not xterm-256color.
VT100Output.keyFunction:modifiers: only applied the xterm
CSI modifier encoding (e.g. Shift+F5 -> ^[[15;2~) when
isLikeXterm was true, which tested only for an xterm prefix.
So modified function keys in a tmux integration session fell
through to the bare terminfo lookup, which has no
modified-key entries, silently dropping the modifier and
sending the unmodified sequence.

Treat tmux and tmux-256color as xterm-like as well. They
parse the xterm modified sequences on input exactly as a
plain xterm-256color client would; TERM=screen stays on the
terminfo path.

Adds unit tests for the modified-key encoding across term
types.

2026-07-31: Use filename-safe separators in Save contents
suggested name

The localized time format uses colons regardless of the
dot-separated template, and macOS renders colons (and
slashes) as / in the save panel and Finder. Replace : with .
and / with - in the suggested filename. Issue 12931.

2026-07-30: InlineImages: add RLog tracing for missing-image
(brown) diagnosis

Add always-on RLog at the four ImageRegistry lifecycle
points (restore, remove/ReleaseImage, collectGarbage,
clearProvisionalFlag) so a brown placeholder (registry
lookup returns nil) can be diagnosed from a debug log
captured at normal speed. DLog perturbs the async restore/GC
timing enough to hide the race, so RLog is required here.

Each line is tagged "ImageRegistry:" and carries the image
code. The two smoking guns: "GC reap still-provisional code
N" (mark never cleared the provisional flag during
interval-tree fixup) and "clear provisional for code N
(MISSING: no registry entry)" (a mark tried to un-provision
a code already removed by dealloc).

2026-07-30: AI chat: send Return for newlines in
insert_text_at_cursor

The insert_text_at_cursor tool wrote the AI-supplied text
raw to the pty, so a newline went out as LF (0x0A). Raw-mode
programs (shell line editors, REPLs, TUIs like Claude Code)
treat a bare LF as "insert a literal newline" rather than
"submit the line", so the intended Return did nothing.
Translate LF to CR (0x0D), the byte a real Return key sends,
and collapse CRLF to a single CR so we never emit a double
Return.

Also replace the bare "Inserted" tool result with a
character count.  The terse response read as ambiguous to
the model (did anything get typed?) and invited a duplicate
insertion to be sure.

2026-07-31: docs(PushRelay): require a Sandbox & Production
APNs key + troubleshooting

A Sandbox-only APNs auth key delivers to development builds
but 403s `BadEnvironmentKeyInToken` on every push to a
release/App Store device (the relay routes those to
api.push.apple.com). A .p8 doesn't encode its environment,
so neither the env file nor deploy.sh can catch the mismatch
 it only surfaces in the service log at push time. Document
 the
requirement where the key is configured, add a
Troubleshooting section with the key-swap procedure, and
note in the metrics section that APNs failures are now
logged with Apple's status + reason.

Docs only; no behavior change.

<noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-07-30: PushRelay: log APNs delivery failures on the
self-hosted host

Node was silent on every APNs failure: the reason (Apple's
status + reason string) lived only in the 502 response body,
and Cloudflare replaces an origin 502 with its own "Bad
gateway" page before the client ever sees it  so a
push-failure incident was undiagnosable after the fact (we
only had the aggregate pushrelay_push_apns_error_total
counter).

host/server.js now console.errors two cases:
  - a push that APNs rejects (non-2xx -> 502), logging
  Apple's status and
    reason verbatim, e.g. `APNs 410:
    {"reason":"Unregistered"}`  covers 400/403/410/429/5xx
    alike. The body carries only Apple's error, never a
    device token or secret, so it is safe to log.
  - the pre-response catch (APNs transport timeout / dead h2
  session ->
    500), which was previously silent.

Kept in the host rather than worker.js so the worker stays
platform- agnostic for Cloudflare. Adds a host test covering
the 502 log line.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context)
<noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-07-29: AI chat: live-cover Responses delta mode
surviving pre-reduce (Phase 7)

The blob-replay live matrix had no multi-turn Responses
DELTA test: the existing Responses test forces
previous_response_id omission (full stateless replay), so
the delta path a pre-reduced chat depends on was unvalidated
against a real vendor. That path is where the send-side
pre-reduce is most fragile -- pre-reducing drops the prior
assistant turns, so the delta anchor must be carried forward
(carriedPreviousResponseID) or the turn silently falls back
to a full replay.

Adds test_chat_responses_deltaModeSurvivesPreReduce: a
three-turn Responses chat whose turns 2+ are pre-reduced.
Asserts the first request omits previous_response_id, a
LATER request carries it (delta mode engaged from the
carry-forward, not a full replay), and the server-held
context is intact (the model recalls a secret from turn 1).
Passes live against OpenAI.

Also records the reachability audit outcome for the pipeline
retirement: translate()/repair/stabilize are now reached
ONLY as the round encoder at capture, the reconstruction
fallback, and the legacy migration codec -- never on the
per-request steady-state send path. Nothing is dead
(migration is not universal), so the pipeline is retired
from the hot path but retained as the codec, per plan.

2026-07-29: AI chat: fix blob pre-reduce edge cases from
review

Four fixes to the send/capture pre-reduce fast paths:

1. Bail dropped the current round (serious). On a
pre-reduced turn, when
   blob replay declines (cross-protocol switch, oversized
   round, corrupt blob), outgoingRequest rebuilt the history
   from fullHistoryProvider, which is translate(history) =
   the PRIOR rounds only (history excludes the current
   turn). It returned just that, discarding
   conversation.messages (the current round), so the request
   resent the prior conversation with no new question. Now
   it sends full + messages (prior + current round). Pinned
   by a new outgoingRequest test via a test seam.

2. Explain chats diverged from the full path.
MessageToPromptStateMachine.mode
   is the one translate() state that crosses a round
   boundary (an .explanationRequest sets a mode consumed by
   the NEXT round's first user turn). The reduced send path
   skips translate and the tail-only capture path uses a
   fresh state machine, so a leaked mode would re-frame the
   boundary turn's bytes (and perturb Anthropic's
   byte-prefix cache). Both fast paths now fall back to the
   full translate when the history uses Explain
   (historyUsesExplainFeature).

3. carriedPreviousResponseID could send a stale
previous_response_id. Its
   parallel role(from:) scan diverged from translate for a
   squelched function_call==nil request (which replay skips)
   and a repair-synthesized terminal tool_use. Rewritten as
   a CONSERVATIVE walk that applies the same content filter
   and returns nil unless the last included message is an
   assistant turn, so it never returns a non-nil id
   translate would not (the only unsafe outcome); a nil just
   forces a safe full resend. Covered by the reviewer's
   skipped-request and orphan-tool_result cases.

4. deleteMessages(after:) latent no-op on the pre-reduced
path. A non-tail
   inResponseTo anchor can't truncate the dropped prior
   rounds. Unreachable today (an edit clears blobs ->
   non-reduced turn), but added an RLog tripwire so a future
   violation surfaces rather than replaying stale history.

Validated live: 13/14 chat_ AILiveHarness tests pass (1
transient Gemini 429 skip), including Responses delta and
the multi-turn pre-reduce/capture.

2026-07-29: AI chat: translate only the new rounds when
capturing blobs

captureBlobsForCompletedTurn re-translated the WHOLE display
history each turn only to have captureNewRounds slice off
and freeze the tail (the rounds beyond the stored prefix)
and discard the rest. That is the capture-side twin of the
send-side waste just removed: O(history) per turn for a
result that is thrown away.

For an established same-protocol chat, capture now
translates only the new rounds' display messages
(displayTailForNewRounds) and appends them via
ChatBlobCapture.appendNewRounds. It re-derives from the full
history for migration (no blobs yet), a protocol switch
(re-frozen wholesale), or if the translated tail does not
begin at a clean user turn. The display->round boundary
filter now lives in one place (roundStartIndices), shared by
reconstructedRoundCount and displayTailForNewRounds so the
two can't drift.

appendNewRounds and captureNewRounds share the encode+append
loop (appendRoundBlobs) and the same protocol guard and
single-round token crediting, so both persist identical
blobs; only the amount of history translated differs. Pinned
by test: the translated tail equals the tail of the full
translate for every prefix length, appendNewRounds is
semantically equal to captureNewRounds (chatCompletions) and
byte-identical on Anthropic (sorted keys, where the cache is
a byte-prefix match), and the protocol guard is honored.

Validated live: all 14 chat_ AILiveHarness tests pass,
including the multi-turn Anthropic/DeepSeek/Gemini chats
whose turns 2+ now freeze via the fast path, plus migration
(full then fast) and the tool round.

2026-07-29: AI chat: skip re-translating frozen history on
the send path

Blob-native chats stored their whole history as verbatim
wire-fragment blobs, but load() still ran the full
translate() pipeline over every prior round each turn only
to have the send path (messagesPastFrozenRounds) throw the
frozen prefix away and splice it back from blobs. That is
O(history) CPU per turn (per tool-loop sub-request) for a
result that is discarded.

load() now computes a cheap exact round count
(reconstructedRoundCount, which builds no message body) and,
when a chat's blobs already cover its whole history, sets
conversation.messages to just the current round and lets the
frozen prefix come from the blob bytes. The frozen-prefix
translate is skipped entirely in the steady state. A partial
capture or a brand-new chat falls to the full translate as
before; a live bail (protocol switch, oversized round,
undecodable blob) rebuilds the full history via
fullHistoryProvider so the codec fallback never sends a
history-less request.

Pre-reducing drops the prior assistant turns from
conversation.messages, so two selections that read them are
carried forward explicitly: -
reducedHistoryPreviousResponseID keeps Responses delta mode
alive on a
  turn's first request. It mirrors AIConversation's
  lastAssistantMessage selection nil-inclusively
  (carriedPreviousResponseID), so a terminal assistant turn
  with no id falls back to a full resend rather than
  reviving an earlier turn's stale id.
- needsRenaming is guarded (a blob-native chat has prior
history).

reconstructedRoundCount and carriedPreviousResponseID are
pinned by test against translate()+rounds so a drift that
would drop un-frozen history or send a stale delta fails
loudly. blobReplayPlan gains a messagesAreReduced flag whose
wire is proven byte-identical to the full path.

Validated live across every protocol (all 14 chat_
AILiveHarness tests): Anthropic cache stays warm
turn-over-turn, Responses/DeepSeek/Gemini and migration
accepted.

2026-07-29: AI chat: recover the just-appended blobID via a
1-row query, not a full decode

captureBlobsForCompletedTurn linked firstBlobRef using
blobs(inChat:).last?.blobID, which SELECTs and fully decodes
every ChatBlob payload (whole-round wire fragments - images,
large contexts) just to read one UUID, an O(rounds) cost on
every completed turn. Add ChatDatabase.lastBlobID (select
blobID order by seq desc limit 1; decodes only the id
column, mirroring storedBlobProtocol) and use it. No
behavior change (.last by seq asc == newest by seq desc).

2026-07-29: AI chat: inherit blobs on fork so the fork keeps
the prompt cache (Phase 6)

The fork copies the source chat's blobs for the retained
message prefix (fresh blobIDs), copies Chat.blobProtocol,
and remaps the cloned messages' firstBlobRef to the new
blobIDs, so a forked chat is blob-native immediately instead
of re-migrating on its first turn. Message.clone now clears
firstBlobRef (a source-chat reference) so a fork that copies
nothing leaves no dangling ref.

Safety: ChatBlobAssembler.forkBlobPrefix copies ONLY a
fully-linked clean prefix (the retained messages'
firstBlobRefs must equal the source blob prefix exactly, in
order); any gap or migration-era unlinked round copies
nothing and falls back to re-migration, so the fork never
ships blobs that don't match its display history.  Pure and
offline-tested (clean / no-refs / mismatch / over-count).

Link each round's first user message to its blob so fork can

2026-07-29: AI chat: capture real per-round token weight by
turn-over-turn subtraction (#9)

Thread the vendor's reported input tokens from the parse
site to blob capture and store each round's real weight:

- AITermController.lastPromptTokens holds the turn's
reported total input (set at
  both parse sites, last non-nil across a stream so
  Anthropic's message_start usage is kept);
  AIConversation.complete resets it per turn (no stale
  carryover) and exposes it via a passthrough.
- ChatAgent reads it before swapping in the amended copy
(fresh controller), and
  keeps previousTurnPromptTokens in memory to derive the new
  round's weight = max(0, thisTurn - lastTurn)
  (roundTokenWeight): the envelope cancels between adjacent
  turns, leaving the round's own contribution; nil -> byte
  estimate when either turn had no usage.
- ChatBlobCapture credits that count to the blob ONLY when
exactly one new round is
  frozen (incremental); a multi-round migration capture
  can't attribute one turn's usage across rounds, so those
  byte-estimate.

Offline tests for roundTokenWeight (delta / first-turn /
no-usage / shrunk-clamp) and the single-round-only
crediting. planTruncation already prefers a stored
tokenCount over the byte estimate, so this transparently
sharpens truncation.

2026-07-29: AI chat: populate promptTokens from vendor usage
in each parser (#9)

Surface the vendor's reported total input tokens on the
response types: - chatCompletions (ModernResponse, also
covers DeepSeek non-streaming which reuses
  it) and legacy: usage.prompt_tokens.
- Responses: body.usage.input_tokens (already the total;
cached is a subdetail).  - Anthropic (streaming + non):
input_tokens + cache_read + cache_creation, since
  Anthropic reports input_tokens as the NON-cached portion
  only - summing avoids a massive undercount on a cached
  request (the common blob-native case). Added the cache
  fields to both AnthropicUsage structs.
- Gemini: added usageMetadata.promptTokenCount (streaming +
non).

Streaming paths without a usage field
(chatCompletions/DeepSeek/legacy streaming) stay nil and
fall back to the byte estimate. Additive; still nothing
reads it.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add LLM.AnyResponse.promptTokens
(input-token usage plumbing, #9)

First increment of real per-round token capture: a
promptTokens: Int? on the response protocols carrying the
vendor's reported total input (prompt) tokens, which blob
capture will use to derive each round's real token weight by
subtraction (falling back to the byte estimate when nil).
Defaults to nil on both AnyResponse and
AnyStreamingResponse; LlamaResponse (which conforms to both,
and carries no usage) declares it explicitly to resolve the
two defaults. Additive and inert - no parser populates it
and nothing reads it yet.

2026-07-29: AI chat: live-validate Responses full stateless
replay (Phase 4 recovery target)

Proves OpenAI accepts a full Responses replay with
previous_response_id omitted and uses the replayed history
(recalls a seeded name) - exactly the request the Phase-4
retry re-issues on an unusable id, and the last unproven
live splice path (Responses input array). A fresh agent's
first turn nulls previous_response_id (systemMessageDirty),
so the turn IS that full replay.

The auto-retry TRIGGER stays covered by
ChatAgentExpiryTests: the stack never puts a BAD id on the
wire without a real server-side expiry (turns 2+ use the
valid in-memory id, store is always on), so forcing one live
would need a production seam not worth adding.

2026-07-29: AI chat: retry a turn as full stateless replay
on unusable previous_response_id

Extract the turn-completion handler into handleTurnResult
and add a one-shot Phase-4 retry: when a Responses turn
fails with an unusable previous_response_id (classified
narrowly), re-issue the SAME turn as a full replay from
blobs with the id omitted. systemMessageDirty forces
complete() to null previousResponseID (otherwise it
re-derives the stale id from the last assistant message).
Only retried when an id was actually sent (delta mode); a
full replay that fails is a real failure, and didRetryExpiry
bounds it to one retry.

streamID is passed as a live () -> UUID? accessor (not a
snapshot) so the retry's re-streamed message commits under
the post-retry id. Extraction is behavior- preserving
otherwise; builds clean.

2026-07-29: AI chat: classify unusable previous_response_id
errors (Phase 4)

isUnusablePreviousResponseIDError detects a Responses-API
previous_response_id that expired / was not stored / was
deleted, the trigger for retrying a turn as a full stateless
replay from blobs. Deliberately narrow (must mention the
previous response AND a not-usable reason) so a generic 4xx
is never misread as an expiry and silently retried. Pure +
offline-tested; not yet wired.

2026-07-29: AI chat: don't let the truncation-budget peek
consume the auto-provided screen

The envelopeTokens thunk sized the volatile context by
calling the volatile provider, which runs
autoProvidedContext() and (for View Contents = Always)
advances the lastAutoProvidedScreen dedup watermark as a
side effect. Since the budget runs in outgoingRequest BEFORE
the per-vendor builder appends the real volatile text, the
budget grab consumed the screen: the actual send then saw it
as unchanged and emitted <visible-screen unchanged="true"/>,
so the model never received the screen on session-bound
blob-native chats with auto-provide on. It fired on the
first turn and every tool-loop continuation.

Fix: thread a  flag through
currentVolatileText/autoProvidedContext. The budget path
peeks with commit:false - it computes the full block for
sizing but does NOT advance the watermark or show the
consent prompt; the real send (default commit:true) is
unchanged and still emits the screen. commit defaults to
true, so every existing caller is unaffected.

Not offline-testable: the path needs a live PTYSession +
View Contents = Always + granted consent, which the unit
suite and the fabricated-guid live tests can't reach.
Correct by construction (default-true preserves all current
callers).

2026-07-29: AI chat: live-validate blob replay on Gemini;
add transient-error skip guard

Gemini test covers the third splice path (contents array,
system separate, afterCount 0). These ChatAgent-turn tests
have no 503 retry (unlike AILiveDriver), so a vendor
capacity blip would flaky-fail; add
skipIfTransientVendorError that skips (not fails) only on
the transient set (503/502/500/429/overloaded/
RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED/UNAVAILABLE), run before the hard
assertions. A real splice rejection (HTTP 400, e.g. broken
tool adjacency) still fails rather than being masked.
Applied to all four blob-replay live tests.

2026-07-29: AI chat: live-validate tool-round blob replay

Adds a ChatAgent-turn live test where turn 1 runs a tool
