2026-07-29: AI chat: budget blob-replay against
tools+volatile and the AiMaxTokens pref

Two review fixes to the truncation budget in blobReplayPlan
/ ChatAgent:

1. fixedCost omitted the tool schemas and the volatile
per-turn context (terminal
   screen / workgroups snapshot), which the per-vendor
   builder adds at send time and are not in the reduced
   message list. Because the frozen prefix is bounded ONLY
   here (the legacy truncate never touches the spliced
   frozen bytes), the undercount could let an over-window
   request through with no fallback. Now folds an
   envelopeTokens thunk (tool-definition tokens + volatile
   text) into fixedCost; a thunk so the
   potentially-expensive volatile estimate (a screen grab)
   runs only on the blob path, after the safety gate and
   reduction.

2. The blob path budgeted against the model's full context
window, silently
   bypassing the user's AiMaxTokens preference that the
   legacy truncate() honors, so a large-window model could
   retain far more (costlier) history than configured.  Now
   clamps to min(contextWindowTokens, maxTotalTokens) and
   reserves output the same way the legacy path does.

Offline: new envelopeTokens-counted-in-budget test + all
blobReplayPlan calls updated. Live: re-ran the Anthropic
harness; cache still warm (creation 8164 once, reads
8164-8188 thereafter).

history replay

2026-07-29: AI chat: bind the blob-replay live test chat to
Anthropic; guard empty blobs

The provider binding locks a chat to the first turn's model
(else the global default, whatever vendor the machine is
configured for), so a per-turn configuration.model naming
Anthropic on a machine defaulting to another vendor was
rejected as a cross-provider switch and no Anthropic call
happened. Bind the chat to the Anthropic model at creation
via setModel so every turn runs on it.

Also skip (not index-crash the host, since asserts are
enabled) if no blobs were captured.

Live run confirms the flip: turn 1 writes an ~8164-token
cache; turns 2-3 read ~8164-8188 back with ~0-27 created,
i.e. blob replay keeps the prefix byte-stable and the prompt
cache warm (the original bug was cache_read stuck at 0).

2026-07-29: AI chat: add ChatAgent-turn live harness for
blob-native replay

The existing AILiveDriver drives a bare AITermController and
never sets blobReplayProvider, so it cannot exercise the
flip. This test drives the real ChatBroker -> ChatService ->
ChatAgent -> AIConversation -> LLM stack (modeled on
AILiveChatQueueTests), so blob-native replay actually
engages.

Three Anthropic turns: turn 1 seeds a secret in a large
(reliably cacheable) message; turns 2-3 replay that history
purely from stored blobs. Asserts every turn was accepted
(one blob per round, since a rejected turn writes none), the
model recalls the secret (frozen history reached it), the
stored blob bytes are spliced verbatim into later requests
(byte-stable prefix), and the Anthropic prompt cache is READ
on a later turn (replay did not churn the prefix).

Captures wire traffic via iTermAIClient.liveObserver. ~3
Anthropic round-trips, cents per run. Added to the
ModernTests target (where the AILiveHarness class lives).

2026-07-29: AI chat: flip on blob-native replay in ChatAgent

Set conversation.blobReplayProvider each turn (like
trailingVolatileTextProvider) so a chat with wire-fragment
blobs frozen under the turn's protocol sends [system] + the
current round with the frozen history spliced verbatim,
instead of reconstructing the whole conversation every turn
(which churned Anthropic's cached prefix). Anthropic
truncates deep (50% of context) for a long caching runway;
other vendors drop just enough to fit. Falls back to full
reconstruction for legacy/blobless chats, protocol
mismatches, misaligned round counts, or a turn too large to
fit after dropping every blob.

Byte-faithfulness is proven offline across all 7 protocols
(ChatBlobAssembler tests); this is the read-side switch
those proofs were building toward.

reduction, whole-round truncation (byte-estimate weights via
an injected closure, or stored tokenCount when present), and
the verbatim byte splice. Returns the reduced outgoing
messages + frozen bytes, or nil to fall back (not
blob-native, misaligned, or needsElision).

Tests compose all pieces and byte-match the live builder fed
the truncated history, plus under-budget, tail-over-budget,

2026-07-29: AI chat: cover truncation/reduction/migration

- planTruncation: add realistic large-output regime (200k
context, 128k
  reserve, so halve target 100k > fit budget 72k) proving
  the clamp both fits and elides correctly at real
  magnitudes.

messages (all tests
  used exactly one), and an all-system list with a positive
  frozen count must refuse (nil).
- ChatBlob.tokenCount: end-to-end ADD COLUMN migration on a

  table (drop+recreate without the column, run the

2026-07-29: AI chat: clamp anthropicHalve truncation target
to the fit budget

The deep-cut target (contextWindow/2) could sit ABOVE the
fit budget on a large-max-output model, where outputReserve
> contextWindow/2 so fitBudget = contextWindow -
outputReserve < contextWindow/2. In that regime a request
with running in (fitBudget, contextWindow/2] passed the
trigger guard (running > fitBudget) but skipped the drop
loop (running > target already false), returning dropCount 0
and needsElision false for a request that does not fit, so
an over-budget request would be sent with no elision
fallback.

Clamp target to min(contextWindow/2, fitBudget) so
anthropicHalve can never truncate less than fitOnly; the
pathological regime degrades to fitOnly and needsElision is
computed correctly. Normal Anthropic models (200k context,
small reserve) are unaffected. Adds two regression tests in
the outputReserve > contextWindow/2 regime.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add blob-native whole-round truncation
planner

planTruncation decides how many whole head blobs (oldest
rounds) to drop so a blob-native request fits. Dropping
whole rounds can never split a tool_use/tool_result pair,
since a pair always lives inside one blob.

Two policies: anthropicHalve cuts deep (to 50% of the
context window) when we near the limit, so the shorter
prefix stays byte-stable for many subsequent turns and the
prompt cache keeps hitting (one big cut, long runway,
instead of shaving every turn); fitOnly drops just enough to
fit and keeps as much history as possible for vendors
without cache-based pricing. Truncation triggers only when
the request would exceed the fit budget (context - output
reserve), so a conversation under the limit is never cut. If
dropping every blob still leaves the envelope + current
round over the fit budget, needsElision signals the caller
to fall back to in-message text elision for that one
request.

Pure and offline-tested; not yet wired to a live send.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add ChatBlob.tokenCount column for
round-granular truncation

Each blob will store its round's real token weight (derived
by subtraction from the vendor's reported usage), which
assembly-time truncation sums to decide how many whole head
blobs to drop to fit the budget. Stored as TEXT so nil (no
usage reported / legacy blob -> fall back to the byte
estimate) stays distinct from a real 0, which the integer
result-set accessor cannot express.

Adds the ADD COLUMN migration path ChatBlob lacked (it is
created with create-table-if-not-exists, so existing beta
databases need the alter), wired into createTables the same
way Chat/Message migrations run.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add send-time reduction helper for
blob-native replay

messagesPastFrozenRounds peels the leading system message(s)
and drops the first N rounds (the frozen prefix) from a full
outgoing message list, keeping system + the current round
(including in-progress tool-loop items). Spliced back with
the frozen-history bytes, its output reproduces the whole
conversation byte-for-byte (proven by the existing send-path
parity tests).

Returns nil on misalignment (fewer rounds present than
frozen, or exactly as many) so the caller falls back to the
full un-spliced request rather than send a history-less one.
Pure and inert: nothing calls it on the live path yet.

Blob capture assumes append-only history, but edit/retry
truncates the display log via ChatListModel.delete. Clear
the chat's blobs there so the next completed turn re-freezes
the edited history from scratch instead of recording a
stale, misaligned sequence.

Also reorder the ChatBlobCapture doc comments so the
captureNewRounds append-only note sits above
captureNewRounds (the refactor had stranded it above
captureTurn, where it contradicted the protocol-switch
behavior).

2026-07-29: AI chat: extract captureTurn for protocol-switch
handling + test it

Factors the capture hook's protocol-switch orchestration out
of ChatAgent into ChatBlobCapture.captureTurn: when the chat
already has blobs under a different protocol than this
turn's (the user switched provider/model mid-conversation),
clear them and re-freeze the whole history under the new
protocol; otherwise a normal incremental capture. This makes
the logic unit-testable offline -- same protocol stays
incremental; a switch clears the old blobs and re-freezes
every round under the new protocol -- rather than living
inline in the untestable live-turn hook. The ChatAgent hook
is now a thin caller (translate + captureTurn + stamp
Chat.blobProtocol).

2026-07-29: AI chat: capture wire-fragment blobs at the end
of each completed turn

Wires blob capture into the live turn path (Phase 2's
remaining piece). At true turn end -- the .success branch of
AIConversation.complete, after the reply has committed
synchronously via ChatService.finishTurn, and only when no
tool call is still parked -- ChatAgent reconstructs the
whole chat as [LLM.Message] via the same translate() the
reload path uses and lets ChatBlobCapture freeze any round
not yet stored (incremental per turn; the whole legacy
history on first capture, through one path). On a protocol
switch it clears and re-freezes under the new protocol.
Stamps Chat.blobProtocol on first capture.

Routes through broker.listModel (== ChatListModel.instance
in production, a temp DB under test) instead of the
ChatDatabase.instance singleton, so capture writes to the
same store the turn used and can be exercised by a temp-DB

Nothing READS blobs yet (the reconstruction switch is next),

Build passes; turn-lifecycle tests still pass with capture

2026-07-29: AI chat: pin the surprising iTermAIAPI NS_ENUM
decode behavior

A reviewer flagged the ChatBlob.init? comment as backwards,
arguing standard Swift enum semantics make init?(rawValue:)
validate and return nil for unknown values. An empirical
test settles it the other way: iTermAIAPI is an imported
ObjC NS_ENUM, and iTermAIAPI(rawValue: 99) is Optional(99),
NOT nil -- so an unknown-protocol row DECODES and is not
dropped, exactly as the comment says.  Imported NS_ENUMs do
not validate init?(rawValue:) against declared cases.

Adds two tests pinning this (iTermAIAPI(rawValue:
99)?.rawValue == 99; an unknown-protocol row keeps
blobs(inChat:).count == blobCount). Sharpens the
stitchedHistoryIfSafe doc: the count-mismatch guard catches
STRUCTURAL decode failures (corrupt blobID/role/payload),
while the protocol check catches unknown/mismatched
protocols -- both guards are load-bearing for distinct
cases, neither is redundant.

2026-07-29: AI chat: wire frozen-history splice into
anthropic, gemini, responses

Completes the blob send path for all 7 protocols (Option A:
reuse the builders).  Each builder splices the chat's
verbatim frozen history into its message array: anthropic
and gemini at the start (system is a separate top-level
field), responses after the system item(s) and only in
full-replay mode (previousResponseID == nil; in delta mode
the server already holds the history).  Gemini excludes the
new field from its Codable body via CodingKeys.

Also fixes a cache-correctness bug: the anthropic and
responses builders serialize with .sortedKeys (their prompt
caches are byte-prefix matches and tool/schema dict order is
otherwise random), so their blobs must be encoded with
.sortedKeys too, or a turn's frozen bytes would differ from
the sorted bytes it was first sent with and miss the cache.
Verified byte-identical: the blob-native anthropic messages
array equals the live full-history one byte-for-byte
(max_tokens, a top-level sibling outside the cached prefix,
may differ). Semantic send-path parity tested for all 7
protocols.

Extends the blob send path (Option A) to the remaining

the chat-completions builder onto it). llama and deepSeek
splice after the leading system message(s); earlyO1 uses the

Byte-faithful: the blob-native spliced messages array equals
the live full-history messages array for all four
messages-key protocols.

2026-07-29: AI chat: wire frozen-history splice into the
chat-completions builder

First protocol of the blob send path (Option A: reuse the

LLMRequestBuilder and ModernBodyRequestBuilder: when set,
the builder serializes the envelope + this turn's new
messages as usual, then splices the chat's verbatim
frozen-history wire bytes into the messages array after the
system message(s) via JSONArraySplice. Adds
ChatBlobAssembler.stitchInner (the verbatim inner element
bytes, no brackets) that feeds it.

Proven byte-faithful: a blob-native request (envelope + new
turn + spliced history) produces the same messages array as

with and without a system message. Existing builder callers

2026-07-29: AI chat: add verbatim JSON array byte-splice
utility

JSONArraySplice.insert drops pre-serialized elements into a
JSON array inside an already-serialized JSON object at the
byte level, preserving everything else verbatim. The blob
send path uses this so each per-vendor request builder keeps
owning its body shape (reuse, no duplication) while the
chat's frozen history bytes are spliced into its message
array without a JSONSerialization round-trip, which could
reorder keys and break Anthropic's byte-prefix cache.
String- and depth-aware so commas and brackets inside string
values or nested structures are never miscounted as element
boundaries.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add verbatim byte-level blob stitch for
cache stability

ChatBlobAssembler.stitchBytes concatenates the verbatim
inner bytes of each blob's payload array into one JSON
array, without parsing and re-serializing the message
objects. This is what the send path needs: Anthropic's
prompt cache is a byte-prefix match, so the frozen history
bytes must be identical turn over turn, which a
JSONSerialization round-trip (which can reorder keys) would
not guarantee. Validates each payload is a JSON array first
(corrupt -> throw -> caller falls back to the codec).

Tests: single blob reproduced verbatim, each blob's inner
bytes appear unchanged in the concatenation,
parse-equivalence with the object-level stitch, empty ->
"[]", corrupt -> throws.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add blob-replay safety gate
(stitchedHistoryIfSafe)

ChatBlobAssembler.stitchedHistoryIfSafe returns a chat's
stitched wire history only when blob-native replay is
provably safe, else nil so the caller falls back to codec
reconstruction. Not-safe cases: no blobs (legacy chat); a
decoded-blob-count vs stored-row-count mismatch (a
structurally corrupt row would hole the spliced history);
any blob not under the turn's protocol (an un-refrozen
switch, or a future-iTerm2 unknown protocol); a corrupt
payload.

Also corrects a ChatBlob.init? comment: iTermAIAPI is an
ObjC NS_ENUM, so iTermAIAPI(rawValue:) accepts any
nonnegative raw (an unknown protocol is NOT dropped on
decode). The unknown-protocol defense is the assembler's
expectedProtocol check, not init?. Tests cover
count-mismatch (corrupt blobID), unknown protocol, protocol
mismatch, corrupt payload, and no-blobs.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add blob assembler with end-to-end
round-trip proof

ChatBlobAssembler.stitch merges a chat's stored per-round
blob payloads into one wire message array at the JSON level
(never decoding into the encode-only wire structs), the read
side of the redesign. A corrupt or non-array payload maps to
corruptPayload so the caller falls back to codec
reconstruction rather than splice a holed history.

Adds the crux proof: capture a multi-round conversation into
stored blobs and assert stitch(blobs) equals the live
per-vendor builder's whole-conversation message array,
across all 7 protocols (Anthropic compared against
convertMessages, the unmarked array it freezes; the live
cache marker is assembly-time envelope). This ties capture +
storage + the JSON merge together and confirms replay is
byte-faithful across the DB, not just at the encoder level.
The envelope (system/tools/volatile) and Anthropic cache
markers are still applied by the per-vendor builder at send
time.

2026-07-29: AI chat: guard blob capture against protocol
mismatch; count rows cheaply

Two fixes from review:

- captureNewRounds refuses to append when the chat already
has blobs under a
  DIFFERENT protocol than the current api. A blob is only
  replayable under the protocol it was frozen for, so
  appending a new-protocol round onto old-protocol blobs
  would produce a mixed, unreplayable sequence; a protocol
  switch must re-freeze the whole history via replaceBlobs
  first. Guarded by a new O(1) storedBlobProtocol peek
  (reads one integer column, no payload decode).

- The already-captured-round count now uses
ChatDatabase.blobCount (select
  count(*)) instead of blobs(inChat:).count, which ran
  select * and fully decoded every payload just to take
  .count (O(history) per turn, unbounded).  count(*) counts
  rows without decoding, so it is O(1)-ish and, unlike a
  decoded-blob count, is not undercounted by a row whose
  protocol this build cannot read (which would otherwise
  re-freeze and duplicate stored rounds).

2026-07-29: AI chat: add per-round blob capture
orchestration

ChatBlobCapture.rounds splits a reconstructed [LLM.Message]
history into rounds (one per user message, so truncation by
whole rounds can never split a tool_use/tool_result pair).
captureNewRounds freezes any round not yet stored, appending
one blob per round via ChatDatabase. Incremental and
idempotent: a blob-native chat freezes only the new round; a
legacy chat with no blobs freezes its whole history through
the SAME path (capture and migration unified). Stops on
encode/write failure rather than leaving a gap in the stored
sequence, and records the round's tail responseID for the
Responses fast path. Not yet wired into the turn lifecycle.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add attachment byte-faithfulness matrix

Cross-checks the round encoder against the live builder for
10 MIME classes (raster images, svg-as-text, pdf,
plain/markdown/json/xml text, audio, octet-stream) across
the 6 vendors whose message array carries no assembly-time
envelope: chatCompletions, llama, earlyO1, deepSeek, gemini,
responses. Each cell records independently so one run
surfaces every divergence. Anthropic is excluded on purpose:
its encoder freezes convertMessages verbatim, so a live-body
comparison would differ only by the rolling cache marker
(envelope) while a convertMessages comparison is
tautological. This matrix would catch the llama joinText
class of bug on the textual-attachment cells.

2026-07-29: AI chat: thread hostedTools into the responses
round encoder

The responses encoder passed a fresh HostedTools()
(codeInterpreter=false) to transform, but a .fileID user
attachment is encoded differently by code interpreter state:
dropped from content when on (the file rides the tools
envelope), emitted as an input_file when off. Freezing with
an empty set would resurrect an input_file that a
code-interpreter chat's live request never sent as content,
which can 400 on replay (input_file with a code-interpreter
file id is only valid for PDFs).

Add a hostedTools parameter to encodeRound (defaulting
empty; the capture site passes the live set) and use it in
the responses case so the frozen content matches the live
request. Add byte-faithful fileID tests for both
code-interpreter states; verified the codeInterpreter=on
case fails without the fix.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add responses round encoder

Reuses ResponsesBodyRequestBuilder's exact per-message
transform: one message maps to multiple input items
(reasoning items precede the function call they produced;
keepItemIDs is gated on reasoning presence; a user turn
becomes a message item). Makes transform and its helpers
(reasoningEntries, assistantEntries, hasReasoningItems,
mimeTypeIsTextual) static since they depend only on their
arguments, not builder state, so the encoder shares them;
the encoder emits the full-replay input independent of
previous_response_id (which body() strips later on the whole
list).

Byte-faithful tests against the live builder for multi-part
and reasoning-bearing tool rounds, plus an assertion that a
reasoning item is frozen ahead of its function_call and the
call keeps its item id and call_id.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add gemini round encoder

Reuses GeminiRequestBuilder's exact conversion (role
mapping, coalescingConsecutiveRoles, and the model-dependent
thoughtSignature fallback) by constructing the builder for
the round and freezing its contents. Adds an optional
modelName to encodeRound because Gemini's wire form depends
on whether the model requires thought signatures.
Byte-faithful tests against the live builder for multi-part
and signed tool rounds, plus gen-3-injects / gen-2-omits
fallback tests. Updates the unsupported-protocol test to
.completions since gemini is now supported.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add deepSeek round encoder

Pure per-message map to DeepSeekRequestBuilder.Message (its
own wire type: string content, tool_calls, and
reasoning_content echoed on assistant turns). Adds
byte-faithfulness tests including a
reasoning_content-bearing round, plus wire-level
compositionality.

2026-07-29: AI chat: fix llama round encoder to apply
joinText (byte-faithfulness)

The .llama encoder case was folded in with .chatCompletions,
dropping the joinText pass the live llama builder applies
(it collapses adjacent text parts within an array-content
message because llama rejects multiple text parts). A round
with multi-text-part content (e.g. a text preamble plus a
.code attachment, which map to two adjacent text parts) would freeze bytes
that diverge from the live request and replay as multiple
text parts llama rejects, breaking the encoder's core
"freeze the bytes the vendor accepted" invariant.

Factor joinText out of LlamaBodyRequestBuilder as a static
method so the encoder reuses the EXACT same conversion, and
give .llama its own case that applies it. Add
byte-faithfulness tests (frozen bytes == the live builder's
message array) with a multi-text-part fixture. The prior
compositionality tests could not catch this: joinText is
per-message, so compositionality holds regardless, and the
fixtures only produced .string content.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add llama + earlyO1 round encoders

Both reuse the chat-completions per-message map: llama is
identical, and earlyO1 adds the system->user swap (a no-op
for rounds, which carry no system message, but applied for
faithfulness to the live request). Generic wire-level
compositionality tests prove the stitch invariant for each.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add chat-completions round encoder +
JSON-level compositionality test

Adds the chatCompletions case to ChatBlobWireEncoder (a pure
per-message map via CompletionsMessage) and a
protocol-agnostic wire-level compositionality test that
compares the stored JSON payloads directly. Documents that
the assembler must stitch payloads by merging JSON arrays,
not by decoding into the typed wire structs:
CompletionsMessage is encode-only (it emits role "tool" but
its Role cannot decode "tool"), so a typed round-trip
throws.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add per-round wire encoder (Anthropic)
for blob capture

Phase 2 of the blob redesign.
ChatBlobWireEncoder.encodeRound freezes one completed
conversational round into its protocol's request-shape wire
form (the bytes stored in a ChatBlob), reusing the vendor
builder's exact conversion so the cross-message passes run
once at capture and never again at reconstruction. Anthropic
implemented; other protocols throw until added.

Makes AnthropicRequestBuilder.convertMessages static (it
never used builder state) so the encoder can share it.
Proves the load-bearing invariant in a test: convert(r1+r2)
== convert(r1)+convert(r2), which is what makes stitching
stored rounds byte-equivalent to serializing the whole
conversation at once. Also pins the mutator-B fix (a text
preamble plus a tool call coalesce into one wire message)
and the JSON round-trip.

2026-07-29: AI model catalog: drop retired DeepSeek and
OpenAI models

Remove deepseek-chat, deepseek-coder, and deepseek-reasoner
(DeepSeek now serves only deepseek-v4-flash and
deepseek-v4-pro, both already present) and gpt-5.1-codex
(deprecated by OpenAI, returns HTTP 404). These dead entries
made every DeepSeek live-harness method and several OpenAI
ones fail with 400/404 in the baseline sweep. None was
flagged default or recommended, and none is referenced in
code. Bump the catalog version to 2 so a shipped snapshot
supersedes a cached one.

2026-07-29: AI chat: add ChatBlob storage for wire-fragment
reconstruction

Phase 1 of the blob-based chat reconstruction. Adds a
ChatBlob table holding each chat's verbatim wire-message
fragments (one per role turn), keyed on the protocol they
were captured for and ordered by a delete-immune
autoincrement seq, with a stable blobID that survives a
fork-copy. Adds a nullable Chat.blobProtocol column that
stamps the frozen protocol (TEXT, so a NULL "not yet
migrated" is distinct from protocol 0, completions) and a
Message.firstBlobRef column linking a display message to its
first blob for forking.

Storage and columns only. Capture and reconstruction come in
later phases; nothing writes or reads blobs yet.

2026-07-29: AI chat: keep volatile per-turn context out of
the prompt-cache prefix

The orchestration <workgroups> snapshot and the
session-bound auto-provided terminal/screen block were baked
into the outgoing user body. They change every turn and are
not persisted, so each turn's rebuilt history diverged from
the previous turn's cached prefix, forcing Anthropic to
re-create the whole conversation cache instead of reading it
(cache_read repeatedly collapsed to the system+tools floor).

Route both through a trailingVolatileText channel. For
Anthropic the builder appends it as an unmarked user message
after the rolling cache breakpoint, so the frozen history
stays byte-stable and only the snapshot is uncached each
turn. Every other vendor has no cache-prefix placement
concern, so LLMRequestBuilder folds it in as a trailing user
message for them; consuming it only in the Anthropic path
would silently drop it for
OpenAI/Gemini/DeepSeek/Llama/legacy completions, a
functional regression since the terminal/screen block is
permission-gated.

The snapshot is regenerated fresh for every request
(evaluated via a provider closure at request-build time),
not frozen once per turn, so mid-turn session changes reach
the model. A stale turn-start snapshot otherwise kept
pointing the agent at a reloaded session's dead guid and it
retried the same failing tool call forever.

Tests: AIChatTrailingVolatileTests (builder contract +
byte-stability), AIChatHistoryCacheStabilityTests (fixed
behavior + latent guid mutator),
LLMRequestBuilderVolatileContextTests (one assertion per
vendor), and live AILiveHarness cases for wire acceptance,
per-request regeneration, and non-Anthropic acceptance.

2026-07-29: Bound the relay keepalive ping so a half-open
socket cannot wedge it

A black-holed half-open relay connection (an idle socket
reaped by the edge or a middlebox without a close frame)
could leave sendPing() awaiting a pong that never arrives:
URLSession never fires its ping completion and the plugin's
JS ping promise never settles, so the await blocked with no
error and no cancellation signal. That wedged the keepalive
loop on a single ping forever, so it never reported the
socket dead, never cancelled it, and never reconnected. The
Mac kept believing it was connected (observed: "connected"
for 45h over a socket the relay had already dropped, its
ping loop silent the whole time).

Run each ping through withPingTimeout, an unstructured race
that returns nil if the ping does not come back within
relayKeepalivePingTimeoutNanos (10s, above a healthy RTT and
below the 15s ping interval). A timed-out ping is treated
like a failed one: cancel the socket so the parked
receive()/accept() throws and the existing reconnect path
engages. The race is intentionally not a task group, which
would join the hung child and reintroduce the hang; the
abandoned ping task unblocks on its own when cancel() fires
URLSession's pending completion.

2026-07-29: Bump shell integration: shrink it2check drain
timeout to 10ms

Reduces the it2check pending-input drain timeout from 0.1s
to 0.01s, cutting the per-run latency added on bash 4+ (the
common no-input case) by 10x while keeping the hang fix.

2026-07-29: Bump shell integration: it2check bash 4+ read

The reload button and its neighbors had no instrumentation,

2026-07-29: Intern attribute dictionaries in the text
builder; clarify dirty-rects setting

The attributed-string builder rebuilt a 15-key NSDictionary
for every color span, which dominates the per-frame CPU cost
on color-heavy content (issue 12763). Cache and reuse one
dictionary per distinct attribute combination, keyed by the
fields that determine it: foreground color by value, font by
identity, everything else packed into one word. A reused
lookup key makes a cache hit allocation-free; the cache is
bounded and disables itself on pathological all-unique-color
content so that case never regresses.

Measured about 1.5x faster on the attributed-string build
for realistic low-cardinality (palette) content; flat on
random truecolor and monochrome.

Also adds an app-free benchmark for this hot path
(iTermAttributedStringBuilderBenchmark plus a ModernTests
test, scoped to the Benchmark test plan and skipped by the
default plan), and clarifies the
showDirtyRectsInLegacyRenderer advanced-setting text to say
it has no effect in the GPU renderer.

2026-07-28: Open web URLs handed to iTerm2 in the built-in
browser. Issue 12431

When the OS (as the default browser), "open -a iTerm2
<url>", Velja, or vim's gx hands iTerm2 an http/https/file
URL via the GetURL Apple event, open it in the built-in web
browser instead of launching a terminal, unless the user has
bound the scheme to a specific profile. If the browser
plugin isn't installed, offer it once (silenceable) and fall
back to the legacy behavior.

2026-07-27: Apply profile custom tab title to new tmux
windows

When a tmux window is created manually with Command-T and
its profile has "Use custom title for new tabs using this
profile" enabled, apply that title. Previously the custom
title was applied only to non-tmux tabs, so new tmux tabs
fell back to the tmux window/session name.

The title is an interpolated-string template, so it is
evaluated against the tab's scope up front and the concrete
result is used. For a tmux tab, setting a title override
renames the tmux window (shared with every attached client),
so this ensures the server-side name matches what iTerm
displays rather than the raw template text. Only manually
opened windows are affected, so attaching to an existing
session no longer clobbers window names, and a blank result
is ignored so the tmux window is never renamed to an empty
string.

Fixes iTerm2 GitLab issue 12945.

Fixes three VoiceOver problems when interacting with
terminal content, none of which occur in Terminal.app
(GitLab issue 12920):

- QuickNav navigation did not engage after interacting with
the terminal
  until QuickNav was toggled off and on. The terminal
  advertised an AXDocument attribute and a settable AXValue
  (settable only because of the no-op setter kept for Full
  Keyboard Access, Issue 10023), which made VoiceOver treat
  it as more than a plain text area. Both are now hidden

  via isAccessibilitySelectorAllowed:.

when output
  arrived during an interaction. The settle path posted a
  layout-changed notification with
  NSAccessibilityUIElementsKey, which relocated the cursor
  and left VoiceOver's cached text stale. Replaced it with a

Stopped script/connection entries accumulated in the Script

iTermScriptHistory gains removeTerminatedEntries and
removeHistoryEntry, both posting the count-changed

pseudo-entries (the app, APS, dynamic profiles, browser
loggers) are always running and are left untouched.

Restart) become SF Symbol icons to make room, and a trash
icon runs Clear Terminated. It enables only when a stopped

2026-07-27: Fade the compact proxy icon in step with the
stoplights

The compact-window proxy icon is laid out next to the
stoplight buttons but its alpha was never tied to them. In a
compact window with bottom tabs (where enableStoplightHotbox
is YES), the stoplights hide to alpha 0 until the hotbox is
hovered, yet the proxy icon stayed at alpha 1, so it was
always visible even with the buttons hidden.

Mirror _standardWindowButtonsView.alphaValue onto
_compactProxyIconView at the three sites that drive
stoplight visibility: the steady-state layout in
layoutWindowPaneDecorations (which also covers the moment
the icon is first created) and the hotbox mouse enter/exit
animations.  Messaging a nil proxy icon is a no-op, so this
is safe when the icon does not exist, and it composes with
the view's own isHidden-when-no-URL behavior.

2026-07-27: Forward three-finger drags to mouse-reporting
apps

A three-finger drag over an app that has mouse reporting
enabled (tmux with mouse on, vim with mouse=a, etc.) could
produce both the app's own selection and a native iTerm2
selection at once.

Once macOS's Accessibility "three-finger drag" feature
engages it lifts the physical touches and synthesizes a
pointer drag, so the touch count iTerm2 sees during the
gesture is unreliable (0, 1, 2, or 3 for the same physical
drag). The old code decided per drag event whether to report
or select locally, keyed partly on that count and partly on
whether the pointer had crossed into a new cell, so a single
drag ended up split between the app's selection and a local
one, and identical gestures could land on different owners.

Now, when mouse reporting is active, every drag is forwarded
to the app and no native selection is made, exactly as a
one-finger drag already did. A reportable event that emits
nothing only because the pointer stayed in the same cell no
longer falls through to a local selection.  Hold Option
during the drag to force a local selection. When mouse
reporting is off, a three-finger drag still makes a local
selection.

The threeFingerDragSendsMouseReports advanced setting is now
obsolete and has been removed.

Fixes issues 12953 and 12950.

2026-07-27: Trim high-volume retrospective (RLog) sites

On busy multi-session hosts the 10 MB retrospective ring was
filling in minutes because several recently promoted RLog
sites logged large payloads at layout/appearance frequency.
Trim the offenders while keeping the useful breadcrumbs:

- Drop the full window-controller description from
  -shouldShowPermanentFullScreenTabBar and
  -safeSetAppearance:.
- Demote the recursive view-hierarchy dump in
  -updateTabBarControlIsTitlebarAccessory and the fullscreen
  path of -tabBarProvidesProgressVisibility to DLog.
- Demote the PTYFontInfo bold/italic computation logs to
DLog
  (cached font lookups, recomputable offline).
- Remove NSThread callStackSymbols from the app-activation
and
  miniaturize/deminiaturize breadcrumbs.

The key/event breadcrumbs (-handleKeyDownEvent:,
-sendEvent:) are left as RLog since unreproducible keystroke
reports are the intended use case. Addresses issue 12949.

2026-07-27: Bound WebSocket resource use to stop API
ping-flood memory exhaustion

An authenticated Python API client could flood the server
with WebSocket ping frames without reading the pongs. The
server buffered pongs without limit and its memory grew
quickly. Bound both directions:

- Server (iTermWebSocketConnection): cap the number of
in-flight inbound
  read chunks, and disconnect a peer that floods pings but
  stops draining pongs once outstanding pongs exceed a cap,
  rather than buffering pongs without limit or blocking the
  work queue (which teardown also runs on, so blocking it
  would make the connection un-abortable).
- iTermHTTPConnection.writeAsynchronously now always
delivers its
  completion, reporting an error when the stream is gone, so
  backpressure accounting cannot leak and wedge the
  connection.
- it2 client (WebSocketClient): handle a flood of control
frames
  iteratively instead of recursively so it cannot overflow
  the stack.

Adds unit regression tests for the completion contract and
the pong-flood teardown, plus a live repro harness under
tests/.

2026-07-27: Document required -suite argument when running
the app

2026-07-27: Restore keyboard focus after apply_layout
reshapes a tab

App.async_apply_layout rebuilds a tab's view hierarchy via
iTermSplitTreeRebuilder.replaceViewHierarchy, which calls
PTYTab.setRoot:.  setRoot: reparents every SessionView
(empties flexibleView_ and re-adds the new root), detaching
whatever view held first-responder status and leaving the
window with a nil/stale responder.

The active-session promotion block treated "active session
survived" as "nothing to do", and setActiveSession:'s
makeFirstResponder: is gated on the active pointer changing,
so when the active pane survived the reshape (the common
case: a fresh one-session tab gaining new_session panes)
nothing ever restored focus. Result: no pane had keyboard
focus and typing went nowhere until the user clicked a pane.

Fix: after the rebuild, unconditionally restore the window's
first responder to the active session, matching what the two
tmux rebuild paths already do.  Guarded to the current tab
to avoid making a non-visible textview first responder (same
NSTextInput guard setActiveSession: uses).

Adds tests/apply_layout_focus_repro.py and a venv runner
that exercises the in-tree iterm2 Python module.

Reported on iterm2-discuss (3.7.0beta5).

2026-07-27: Avoid redundant key reporting refreshes on
screen switches (#705)

* Test unchanged key reporting across screen switches

Exercise real injected keyboard-protocol escape sequences
through VT100Screen, record the flags visible inside the
delegate callback, and assert that buffer switches only
refresh when the effective flags change.

Cover both zero-to-zero switches and screens with the same
nonzero reporting flags.

* Avoid redundant key reporting refreshes on screen switches

Switching between screen buffers can select a different
key-reporting mode stack without mutating either stack.
Compare the effective flags before and after the switch, and
refresh the session only when that value changes.

This preserves manual key-reporting choices when both
screens report the same flags while retaining the existing
refresh path for real main-to-alternate transitions.

2026-07-26: Pin workgroup member sessions to the default end
action

A Claude Code workgroup peer (Diff, Code Review) or
split/tab could end up with "Close Sessions On End" as its
end action even though spawn code overrides it to the
default: the override only lived in the in-memory profile,
and a later profile sync reconciles non-overridden keys back
to the shared profile's value. When that happened, the
member auto-closed the moment its command exited (e.g. a
Diff pane whose difftool finished), and closing a member
session tears the whole workgroup down.

Add PTYSession.forceDefaultEndAction, honored by
-[PTYSession endAction] so it always reports the default
regardless of the profile or a later setEndAction. Set it on
every spawned or adopted workgroup member (main and nested
peers via makeWorkgroupPeer, splits/tabs via the spawner,
restored members via registerNonPeer and adopt). The leader
is left alone so it still follows its profile and its
termination still exits the workgroup.

2026-07-25: Generalize the orphaned-modal-sheet guard and
harden it for nesting

Factor the parent-window-close guard from -[NSAlert(iTerm)
runSheetModalForWindow:] into a shared
iTermRunModalForWindowAbortingIfParentCloses() and route the
five hand-rolled beginSheet: + runModalForWindow: sheets
through it (paste-special, coprocess, session parameter,
split, and add-account panels), so a parent window destroyed
while a sheet is up aborts the modal instead of deadlocking
the app in runModalForWindow: forever.

Hardening beyond the alert version: - Nesting: -abortModal
ends only the topmost modal session, so a nested
  modal (alert, open/save panel, another sheet) over the
  sheet would be torn down instead of ours, leaving us
  blocked. Re-abort on each run-loop pass until our own
  session ends.
- nil parent: skip the observer when the parent is nil,
since object:nil
  would abort on any window closing.
- Deferred completion: beginSheet:'s completion runs a
run-loop turn
  later, after we may have aborted, so gate each stopModal
  on NSApp.modalWindow == the sheet.
- Parameter panel: its empty string default is a valid
value, so treat
  an abort as a cancel (return nil) instead of launching
  with an empty substitution.

2026-07-25: Show system focus ring in settings search on
macOS 26.5.1+

On macOS 26.2 the system focus ring for the settings search
field was broken (it hugged the internal editor instead of
the capsule; FB21919006), so 1292e52 disabled it and drew a
custom ring. As of macOS 26.5.1 the system ring works again,
and the custom ring now leaves thin angled fragments below
the capsule corners.

Gate the workaround on the OS version: below 26.5.1 keep
drawing the custom ring and suppressing the system one; on
26.5.1 and later skip the custom ring and let the system
draw it. The vertical-centering cell swap is unchanged and
still applies to all of macOS 26. Also drop two
NSSearchFieldCell rect overrides that only returned super.

Issue 12708.

iTerm2 previously updated the active grid without notifying

2026-07-25: Revert "Generalize the orphaned-modal-sheet
guard to non-alert sheets"

This reverts commit
56bd564a8c041fbd462f21191a6fb7d4d85c218a.

2026-07-25: Generalize the orphaned-modal-sheet guard to
non-alert sheets

The fix in -[NSAlert(iTerm) runSheetModalForWindow:] guards
alert sheets against the parent window being destroyed
mid-modal, which orphans the sheet so its completion handler
never fires, nothing calls stopModal, and the main thread
stays blocked in runModalForWindow: forever (an
unrecoverable, Force-Quit-only hang). The same hazard exists
in the hand-rolled beginSheet: + runModalForWindow: call
sites, and those are worse because a custom panel has no
always-clickable default button to recover with.

Add iTermRunModalForWindowAbortingIfParentCloses(), which
wraps runModalForWindow: with an
NSWindowWillCloseNotification observer on the parent that
aborts the modal, and route the paste-special, session
parameter, split, coprocess, and add-account panels through
it.

2026-07-25: Fix orphaned modal sheet deadlock when parent
window is destroyed

-[NSAlert(iTerm) runSheetModalForWindow:] paired
beginSheetModalForWindow: with a synchronous
runModalForWindow:. If the parent window was destroyed
before the sheet was dismissed, the completion handler never
fired, so stopModalWithCode: was never called and the main
thread stayed blocked in the modal run loop forever. Observe
NSWindowWillCloseNotification on the parent and abort the
modal when it closes.

Adjustments while reviewing: remove the observer on every
exit path (so a modal unwound by a third party, e.g.
abortModal during termination, can't leave a stale observer
that later aborts an unrelated modal), and fix the
regression test to close the window on the main queue with
no sleep-based timing.

2026-07-25: Honor OSC 22 pointer shapes while mouse
reporting is enabled (#700)

updateCursor: previously forced the IBeamWithCircle cursor
whenever the app had mouse reporting on, so OSC 22 shapes
never displayed exactly when mouse-aware apps need them.
Prefer the app-requested shape and keep IBeamWithCircle as
the fallback. Also add missing shape names (fleur, watch,
plus, minus) and map X_cursor to operationNotAllowedCursor.
2026-07-24: Screenshot window: confirm Copy to Clipboard
visually

The Copy to Clipboard button was silent (unlike Save, which
closes the panel), so it read as dead even though the image
reached the clipboard. Briefly show a green checkmark and
"Copied!" for about 1.2 seconds, wired into both copy paths,
with a generation counter so a rapid second copy cannot
leave the label stuck or revert early.

2026-07-24: Fix synthetic-session layout when the live
session has extra chrome

A synthetic session (filter, instant replay, screenshot,
zoomed lines) is swapped into the live session's SessionView
with newView.frame set to oldView.frame. When the frames
match, resizeSubviewsWithOldSize: does not fire, so the
synthetic session's grid, copied from the live session, is
never re-fit to its own view. If the synthetic view lacks
chrome the live view has (notably the workgroup peer-mode
toolbar), the scrollview is taller than the grid and the
uncovered strip renders as a black bar, and click
hit-testing is off by the row difference.

Fix the black bar centrally in
replaceActiveSessionWithSyntheticSession: by fitting the
session to its view after the swap (session conforms to
view, window untouched). This covers filter, screenshot, and
zoomed lines; the redundant hand-rolled fit in
setDvrInSession: is removed.

Filtering wants the window to stay put and the session to
conform to it, which the above already handles on enter and
which PTYTab's showLiveSession does on exit. Instant replay
wants the opposite: the window conforms to the recorded
session. Its exit previously fit the window while the
chrome-less synthetic was still active, shrinking the window
by the chrome height and never restoring it. Snapshot the
pre-replay window frame in replaySession: and restore it in
showLiveSession: after the chrome is back, so the window
returns exactly to its original size and the session re-fits
to it.

2026-07-24: Fix cmd-click crash when command-mark data is
unavailable

Guard -[VT100ScreenState
commandMarkAt:mustHaveCommand:range:] against out-of-bounds
coordinates, a missing mark, and a missing interval-tree
entry so command-click URL/prompt resolution falls through
cleanly instead of crashing when command-mark data is
unavailable. Adds regression tests for the out-of-bounds,
no-mark, and mark-without-command cases.

2026-07-23: Improve tab dragging performance and drop-target
stability

Keep Metal enabled during normal tab drags; only tmux
split-pane drags fall back to legacy drawing. This avoids
forcing every session onto Core Text for the whole drag,
which starved the main thread and made the floating tab
preview lag behind the cursor.

Use the live pointer for split-pane drop highlights during
tab drags, since AppKit's draggingLocation can be stale
while PSMTabDragAssistant is updating the floating drag
window.

Add hysteresis around tab midpoints for reorder slots and
around split-half boundaries, reducing flicker.

2026-07-23: Working directory lookup: demote expected
root-login miss from RLog to DLog

When the session task pid is /usr/bin/login (running euid
0), the async working directory lookup can't read its vnode
info and recovers via the eldest-child fallback. That
first-attempt miss is expected, so log it at DLog. Reserve
RLog for a genuine give-up: no child to fall back to, or a
non-fallback read that fails.

2026-07-23: add_file_to_xcodeproj: write a minimal diff
instead of churning the project

The xcodeproj gem re-serializes the whole project on save,
normalizing formatting in ways Xcode does not (dropping
redundant name= keys, re-expanding
PBXFileSystemSynchronizedRootGroups, rewriting comments), so
every file add produced a large unreviewable pbxproj diff.

Let the gem compute the new objects, then splice only the
newly-created lines (definitions plus child/build-phase
references, including multi-line blocks) into the original
file text and discard the gem's cosmetic churn. Each
inserted run is anchored to the stable line that precedes it
in the gem output, and that anchor must be unique in the
original or we bail. If a minimal splice is not possible (a
new group had to be created, or an ambiguous anchor), fall
back to the gem's full output with a warning so the file is
still added.

2026-07-23: tmux -CC: deliver modified keys by name so tmux
re-encodes them

In a tmux control-mode (-CC) pane, iTerm2 encoded
modifyOtherKeys "other keys" itself as the CSI-27 form
(ESC[27;m;k~) and injected the bytes with send-keys,
bypassing tmux's own per-pane key encoding. When the pane
app negotiated a different extended-keys-format (csi-u, as
crossterm/Codex use), the keys arrived in a form the app
could not decode, so Ctrl-J, Shift-Enter and similar chords
produced no event. It worked outside tmux, and in normal
tmux tmux itself did the encoding, so only -CC was affected.

Hand these keystrokes to tmux by name (send -t %pane 'C-j',
'S-Enter', ...) so tmux's input_key re-encodes them per the
pane's own mode and extended-keys-format, byte-identical to
a native (non -CC) tmux client.

- iTermTmuxControlModeOtherKeyName() is the single source of
truth for
  whether a keystroke is delegated and as what tmux key
  name. It mirrors the modifyOtherKeys byte path:
  Control+anything, option acting as meta, the command keys
  Return/Tab/Escape/Backspace, and Shift+Space when Shift is
  the sole modifier. Plain shifted printables,
  application-keypad keys, function/navigation keys, and
  unmodified keys stay on the byte path.
- Delegation runs at the pre-Cocoa hook (Ctrl/Shift keys)
and the post-Cocoa
  site (option-as-meta, marked-text and Bypass-Terminal
  cases).
- Gated on tmux 3.2+ (extended-keys); older servers keep the
byte path, as do
  broadcasting and companion-injected keystrokes and dead
  panes.
- The gateway single-quotes the name for tmux's command
parser so ';' and
  '$'/'#' are inert, double-quoting only the apostrophe key.
- Only the modifyOtherKeys mappers delegate; the kitty and
CSI-u mappers
  already emit the format the pane negotiated, so they
  inject verbatim.

Fixes iTerm2 GitLab issue 12928.

2026-07-23: Cache hot preference reads and refresh fast
caches synchronously

Idle terminal windows read a handful of preference keys
(TerminalMargin, TerminalVMargin, PerPaneBackgroundImage,
SplitPaneDimmingAmount) thousands of times per second
through uncached accessors in drawing, layout, and
mouse-tracking paths. Each read hits CFPreferences and, with
debug logging, emits a log line, which is both noisy and a
little wasteful of CPU.

Route those hot paths through the dispatch_once-cached fast
accessors. The margin caches already existed but many
callers bypassed them; add new fast accessors for
PerPaneBackgroundImage, SplitPaneDimmingAmount, and
DimBackgroundWindows and convert the
int/bool/float/double/Swift spellings of these keys
uniformly.

The fast-accessor caches previously updated only via the
async KVO observer, so an in-process write (for example
dragging the split-pane dimming slider) lagged one runloop
turn behind a synchronous redraw. Refresh the cache
synchronously from setWithoutSideEffectsObject:forKey: so
every write path keeps it coherent. The refresh re-reads the
current resolved value (never the value we wrote) so it
stays a read-through mirror of CFPreferences' cross-process
resolution, and read plus assign happen under a lock so
concurrent refreshers converge to the current value.
Installation registers the sync and KVO refreshers before
the initial read to avoid missing a write during
construction, and asserts that fast accessors are never
attached to computed keys, which could deadlock the refresh
lock against dispatch_once.

Adds unit tests covering the synchronous-refresh path for
the bool, int, and double caches.

2026-07-23: Fix cross-thread scrollback corruption via stale
metadata provider pointers

iTermLineBlockMetadataProvider captured a raw pointer into
LineBlockMetadataArray's guts at creation time. GetMutable
then invoked willMutate, which can replace the guts with a
private copy-on-write split, but returned the pre-split
pointer. Every write through it (the DWC cache populate and
the wrapped-line-count cache, both reached from read paths
like drawing and search) landed in the guts owned
exclusively by the other sharer, typically the mutation
thread's live LineBlock.  Racing objc_storeStrong calls on
the same slot could then double-release a cache object, and
a displaced release onto a reused chunk silently under-owns
whatever object was allocated there next. This is the
leading explanation for issue 12941, an intermittent
use-after-free crash in -[VT100ScreenState mergeFrom:] when
closing a tmux window resizes surviving sessions
(invalidating every width-keyed cache) while output is still
streaming.

The provider now stores (array, index) and both accessors
resolve the entry through the array's current guts at call
time, after willMutate in the mutable case, so writes land
in the caller's own post-split copy.  The one caller that
cached the immutable pointer across a GetMutable re-resolves
it.

Providers also capture a structural generation, bumped by
every operation that creates, destroys, or replaces entries
and preserved by the CoW split. The accessors assert it is
unchanged, so an index cannot silently alias a different
logical entry if a future caller holds a provider across
removeLast/append churn. The assert is live in release
builds to convert any such misuse into a labeled crash
instead of heap corruption.

Issue 12941.

2026-07-22: Keep private data out of the always-on
retrospective log ring

The retrospective log ring (RLog) is always on, has no user
opt-in, and can be exported by anyone at the machine via
Save Retrospective Debug Logs. Recent promotions of
per-event handlers from DLog to RLog caused it to accumulate
typed characters (passwords included), command lines,
credentials, URLs with embedded secrets, keychain
identifiers, and AI chat content with debug logging off.

Redact these on the ring path only. RLog feeds the live
debug log when debug logging is on (an explicit opt-in whose
log should stay complete) and the ring otherwise, so
redaction is conditional on gDebugLogging:

- Swift: RLog now takes an RLogMessage with a custom string
interpolation,
  \(redacted: full, or: placeholder), that emits the full
  value when debug logging is on and the placeholder when
  off (os_log's \(x, privacy:) pattern).  This removes the
  need to pair an RLog breadcrumb with a separate DLog
  carrying the full value. RLog no longer accepts a bare
  String; a few string-concatenated log calls became single
  interpolations to match.
- ObjC: RLogRedact(full, redacted) does the same for the
macro-based RLog.  - Add -[NSEvent it_redactedDescription]
(keycode and modifier flags, no typed
  characters) and it_redactedDescription on NSURL and URL
  (scheme, host, and path only, dropping userinfo, query,
  and fragment where secrets hide).

Command lines are the highest-volume category, so they are
redacted wherever they reach the ring. An object's
-description can leak too: -[VT100ScreenMark description]
and -[iTermBackgroundCommandRunner description] embed the
command line, so logging one with %@ leaks it. Give those a
-redactedDescription (command omitted) and wrap every site
that logs them with RLogRedact(obj,
obj.redactedDescription). Covered: shell-integration command
marks and command start/end, auto command history, session
start (command, env, substitutions),
coprocess/launch/single-use/AppleScript/API-script commands
and arguments, the context-menu mark command, the git
status-bar component, the background command runner, the AI
command-safety checker, command-started/ finished/running
and script triggers, login-shell wrapped commands, the
constructed ssh command line and SSH conductor hook params,
iTermCommandRunner arguments, drag-drop/upload file paths,
the SSH framer VT100 token, the AI prompt template and an
unsupported-subpart AI body, and the JSFetch error response
body and headers.

Three sites that log an actual plaintext user secret
(snippet body, echoed password during autofill,
password-adapter login token) stay redacted on both paths,
since even opt-in debug logs travel with bug reports.

As defense in depth, scrub chars=/unmodchars= on the
ring-write path so a future RLog of a raw NSEvent cannot
silently reintroduce the keystroke leak.

2026-07-22: Status bar: reject RPC components with no
registration request

A saved layout whose RPC-provided text component was missing
both registration-request keys produced a nameless blank
ghost cell: -[GPBMessage initWithData:nil error:] returns a
non-nil empty message, so the existing nil-check never
fired. Reject nil data after the V2 base64 fallback so the
corrupt component is dropped by the layout deserializer
instead of being rendered.

Semantic follow-up to #12861, which fixed only the crash
half of this input path.

2026-07-22: Companion: suite-qualify the push-nonce keychain
account

KeychainNonceStore used a bare account name, so two
instances launched with different -suite values (each a
distinct pairing) shared the single push-nonce-registry
keychain item. save() blindly overwrites the whole list, so
concurrent instances clobbered each other's outstanding
nonces; after the displaced instance reloads, its own
solicited NSE fetch is misclassified as an unsolicited
connection and warns the user, with no keychain error to
hint at why.

Namespace the account by the -suite name the same way
CompanionMacIdentity.suitedAccount does the identity items.
Shipping builds have no -suite, so the account stays the
bare name and existing items are found unchanged; only
side-by-side dev builds get their own isolated list. Nonces
are ephemeral, so no migration is needed.

2026-07-22: Companion: skip the launch-time keychain prime
when never paired

resumePairedListeningIfNeeded primed the companion keychain
on every launch before its own gate check, including a
push-nonce-registry WRITE that planted a keychain item even
for users who never paired. For anyone alternating
differently-signed builds (a notarized nightly and an ad-hoc
self-build) that planted item pops a code-signature
confirmation prompt that Always Allow can't fix.

Gate the whole prime block on evidence companion is or was
paired: a live pairing (pairedPID) or a new
NoSyncCompanionKeychainHasMaterial hint that
CompanionMacIdentity reconciles from ground truth (set true
on any write and on a found read, false only when every
account reads back a genuine absent; a transient unreadable
never clears it). The hint only gates the launch
optimization, never an on-demand connect/push read, so a
stale value can't prevent a genuinely-needed read, and
deleteKeyPair clears it at unpair. everPaired keeps its
sticky onboarding semantics untouched.

2026-07-22: Debug logging: cap memory at 200MB and stop
freezing on save

DebugLogImpl accumulated every entry into one
NSMutableString bounded at 10^9 code units (~1-2GB) under a
single lock, and FlushDebugLog assembled header+body+footer
into a second full-size string plus an NSData before one
synchronous write. On a busy instance that meant
gigabyte-scale memory (paging, near-zero-CPU freezes during
capture) and a multi-second UI freeze on toggle-off.

- Lower the in-memory cap to 200MB (matching the menu tip,
which was
  wrongly claiming a 200MB circular buffer). Overflow still
  discards the oldest half, which is what you want: a debug
  log is captured right after the bug reproduces.
- FlushDebugLog encodes header, body, and footer as three
separate
  chunks and streams them through one file handle instead of
  concatenating, removing the peak-memory spike at stop. The
  overwrite and append paths are unified, and the append
  path now reports write errors it previously swallowed. A
  nil NSError (failed create/open) falls back to a concrete
  message instead of "(null)".
- DebugLogImpl formats the whole entry before taking the
lock, so the
  only work in the critical section is the append plus the
  overflow check.
- Correct the Toggle Debug Logging menu tip to describe the
real
  behavior.

2026-07-22: Metal renderer: shape flag emoji by pairing
regional indicators

A flag is stored as two regional-indicator cells (one
codepoint per cell for wcwidth compatibility). The Metal
glyph-key builder rasterized each cell independently, so
each indicator shaped alone into AppleColorEmoji's invisible
lone-indicator fallback and the flag drew as blank cells
(and flickered on frequently-redrawn lines). The legacy
renderer is unaffected because it shapes the row as one
CoreText run, which pairs the indicators.

Pair adjacent regional indicators greedily left to right in
the glyph-key builder, matching CoreText: the opening cell
absorbs the closing indicator via combiningSuccessor and
shapes the flag, and the closing cell is suppressed. Under
the default fullWidthFlags=YES each indicator is
double-width, so the two indicators are separated by a
DWC_RIGHT spacer; the pairing treats spacers as transparent
and points the successor at the real partner, exactly as the
legacy renderer does when it skips spacers building its run.
A lone or trailing odd indicator stays drawable and renders
its fallback. Pairing state is per-row, so a flag split
across a soft wrap yields a lone indicator on each row,
identical to legacy.

The new pairing lives in a pure
iTermRegionalIndicatorPairingForCell helper in ScreenChar so
it is unit-testable. Both cache layers key into the fix
correctly: combiningSuccessor is part of the glyph-key hash
and equality, and the per-row output cache keys on content
identity, which fully determines the (pure) pairing.

Fixes iTerm2 issue 12927.

2026-07-22: Companion: renumber the relay migration to

Rebasing onto upstream landed revision 10 for phone-driven

CompanionProtocolVersion already carries current = 11 /
minimumPeer = 11 (a revision-10 peer has no resolver
support, so it must still be refused); this updates the
remaining doc comments in the migration code to match.

No behavior change: the migration flags are keyed by name,
not by the revision number.

2026-07-22: Companion: refine the rev-10 migration update
notice

Make the "update your other device" notice show only when it
is actually needed, and never on an unpaired device.

- Deferred: the migration arms the notice instead of showing
it. A
  reconnect that lands within a grace window (phone 20s, mac
  30s) cancels it, so a peer that already updated triggers
  no notice.
- Decoupled from the endpoint rewrite: armed for ANY paired
device on its
  first paired rev-10 launch, not only ones that moved off
  the legacy direct relay. Whichever side is upgraded first,
  the un-upgraded peer cannot connect (it stays on the old
  relay and the raised minimumPeer refuses it) and so never
  reaches the version handshake's upgrade wall; without this
  a device already on the resolver would sit silently
  broken.
- Armed on the first PAIRED launch, not the first launch: a
device that
  is unpaired when it first runs rev-10 re-checks once it is
  paired, instead of marking itself done while unpaired.
- Unpair-safe: every unpair path (mac unpair/peerDidUnpair,
phone
  forgetStoredPairing) clears the pending flag and cancels
  the mac timer, and the arm/timeout paths guard on the
  device still being paired, so a leaked flag can never nag
  an unpaired device.

Adds RLog/companionLog instrumentation across the mac
decision path (quiet in steady state) to make this flow
diagnosable from a debug log.

2026-07-22: Companion: migrate off the direct relay onto the
resolver (revision 11)

Bumps the companion handshake to revision 11 and raises
minimumPeer to 11: a peer that has not upgraded stays on the
direct main relay (https://relay.iterm2.com) and cannot
rendezvous with an upgraded peer that has moved to the
sharded resolver, so it is refused with the existing upgrade
wall rather than allowed to pair into a broken state.
(Revision 10 is phone-driven keyboard input; a revision-10
peer has no resolver support, so it must still be refused.)

On upgrade, a device still pointing at the direct legacy
relay converts itself to the default resolver, once, and
shows a one-time modal telling the user the OTHER device
must also update to keep connecting:

- Phone: at launch, before the reconnect, a stored
direct-relay pairing
  is rewritten to the default resolver
  (CompanionRelayMigration). The rewrite preserves the
  pairing identity (room name and bucket derive from rs+pid,
  unchanged), so only the transport endpoint moves.
- Mac: at launch, a mac parking on the direct legacy relay
(resolver
  setting empty, relay origin the legacy host) has its
  CompanionResolverURL advanced setting set to the default
  and reloaded, so the park that follows resolves through
  the shard map.

Only an actual conversion shows the notice; a device already
on a resolver (or a custom relay) is left untouched, since
the handshake wall already covers a too-old peer at connect
time. Both sides guard the migration with a NoSync done flag
so it runs once. Shared constants live in
CompanionRelayMigration; the resolver URL is kept in
lockstep with the mac's CompanionResolverURL default.

2026-07-22: Companion: fetch the shard map from the

The resolver= value now points directly at the shard-map
JSON and is GET'd exactly as configured, with nothing
appended. Removes the directory-base + shardmap.json append
logic (and the shardMapFilename constant) from
ShardMapLoader, so what a caller configures is what is
fetched. The CompanionResolverURL advanced setting default
becomes https://resolver.iterm2.com/shardmap.json and is
relabeled accordingly.  canonicalResolverURL validation is
unchanged (https, path allowed, no query/fragment/userinfo).
Makes the resolver trivially pointable at any test fixture
or self-hosted map.

2026-07-22: Companion: show the paired relay room name in
hex

Both the phone's Settings screen and the mac's Companion
Device Settings window now display the pairing's relay room
name (the 64-char lowercase hex pseudonym both devices and
the relay log a room under) when paired, so a user can match
this device's room against the other device and the relay
logs when reporting a problem.

The value is derived, not stored: the mac from its static
key + paired pid, the phone from its stored pairing code,

2026-07-22: Companion: address sharding client-side review
findings

Persist the shard-map version floor across launches (6.4).
Adds ShardMapVersionFloorStore (protocol + a
UserDefaults-backed impl); ShardMapLoader seeds
highestVersion from it at init and writes every adopted
version back, threaded through ShardHostResolver and
ShardResolverCache. The phone stores under .standard, the
mac under its iTermUserDefaults suite with a NoSync prefix.
A freshly launched client now rejects a below-floor map from
a lagging CDN edge instead of adopting it, closing the
relaunch regression the floor exists to prevent.

Apply the displaced 60s long-backoff on the phone (6.9).
Adds TransportError.displaced; RelaySignal.transportError()
maps longBackoff(.displaced) to it so the phone's URLSession
transport backs off long instead of the short jittered
cadence, matching the mac park loop. The mac short-circuits
to RelayDisplacedError before this bridge, so its 60s
backoff is unchanged.

Force a fresh map re-fetch on a bare connect failure for a
resolved pairing (6.4 connect-failure class). Both phone
loops now re-resolve (force-fresh, no-op for v1, swallows a
fetch blip) before the next attempt, so a fully-down
decommissioned host is dropped onto its replacement
deterministically rather than leaning on the resolver's
opportunistic background refresh.

Validate that a shard-map host is a bare authority
(6.3/6.10): reject a scheme, path, userinfo, query,
fragment, whitespace, uppercase, or a trailing dot, since
that same string is the connect target, the cert SAN, and
the proof origin. A malformed entry would otherwise silently
break every proof for that host's buckets with no failed
connect.

Document the parked-mac-no-phone reshard latency (7.4): a
lone park is not evicted until the drain defer elapses, so a
phone connecting in that window gets a retry-here "mac
offline" and the reconnect gap is about the drain defer, not
a few seconds. Left as-is deliberately.

371 package tests pass; both apps build.

2026-07-22: Companion phone: re-resolve mid-pairing on a
stale-map reject

pairingTicketIfNeeded now loops on TransportError.reResolve
(HTTP 421, 6.9) instead of falling through to the reconnect
path. The ticket's App Attest clientData binds the origin,
so it is only valid at the host it was attested for; on a
reshard we re-resolve the new owner force-fresh (which also
refreshes the cache the following connect reads) and
re-attest there, so the ticket matches the host the connect
reaches.  Bounded by reResolveMaxAttempts so a lagging CDN
edge cannot spin; exhausting the bound joins with an empty
proof and lets the reconnect re-attest. The
verifierRegistered check moves first so an established room
skips the ticket dance entirely.

registerRelayVerifier now classifies a 421 explicitly: the
registration token is minted at admission on the old host
and cannot be replayed at the new owner, so retrying is
futile. It logs the reason and returns false; the eviction
tears the connection and the reconnect re-registers with a
fresh token on the new owner.

2026-07-22: Companion: mac park loop re-resolves on a
reshard evict (421/4421)

The symmetric half of the phone reconnect change. When
acceptLoop's park accept() throws TransportError.reResolve -
the transport mapping of a WS 4421 evict or HTTP 421 reject,
meaning a reshard moved this pairing's bucket - the mac now
forces the shared shard resolver to fetch a fresh map (via
the same plugin-keyed cached resolver parkAndAccept reads)
so the re-park resolves the new owner instead of the cached,
rejecting host, then re-parks on a full-jitter delay
(RelayReconnectBackoff) that flattens a whole-fleet reshard
storm. Slotted in beside the existing displaced (60s) and
quota (30m) cases; not a user-facing fault, so no onFailed.

This also covers the live-bridge eviction case: a reshard
tears the splice, the bridge closes, the mac re-parks on its
cached (old) host, that host reject-on-doubts the park, and
this accept()-path reResolve then force- resolves onto the
new owner - converging without a special bridge path.

2026-07-22: Companion: phone reconnect loop re-resolves on
421/4421 and jitters backoff

Wire the 6.9 re-resolve signal and the 7.3/Appendix C
backoff into the phone's two reconnect loops (the
connectionLost reconnect and the pair/isReconnect retry).

On a TransportError.reResolve (the transport layer's mapping
of an HTTP 421 / WS 4421 stale-map reject or evict), the
loop now calls forceReResolve - forcing the shared shard
resolver to fetch a fresh map - so the next attempt targets
the new owner instead of the cached, rejecting host, rather
than bouncing off it until a background refresh happens to
land.  Because it uses the same cached resolver the
connector reads, the refresh it performs is exactly what the
next connect picks up.

Both loops also replace the fixed 2s retry with
RelayReconnectBackoff (full jitter: [0,3s] then exponential
to 30s), keyed on the per-episode consecutive-failure count,
so a reshard drain or host-death reconnect storm flattens
instead of arriving in lockstep. The old
reconnectRetryDelayNanos constant is removed.

The data-plane HTTP endpoints reject-on-doubt with 421 when

surface that as TransportError.reResolve rather than a

treating it as an ordinary failure. A shared
throwIfStaleMapRejection helper gates it (owner hint
omitted: RelayHTTPClient does not surface response headers,
and the hint is diagnostic-only). Wired into every
room-scoped call: /attest/challenge and /attest
(RelayAttestationClient) and /attest/challenge and /delete
(RelayRoomDeleter). Tests cover both.

Completes the package-level transport surfacing. The
/register POST lives in AppModel and is handled with the
app-layer re-resolve+retry dispatch, which consumes
.reResolve on all these paths, next.

2026-07-22: Companion: surface re-resolve (421/4421) as a

Wire the 6.9 re-resolution signals into the transport layer

  mapCloseSignal, which classifies the close code + reason
