2026-07-22: Companion: align reshard owner-hint extraction
with the classifier guard

ownerAfterReshardSentinel matched a looser
hasPrefix("reshard") than the classifier's exact `reshard` /
`reshard ` guard, so a 4421 close whose reason merely begins
with those letters (e.g. "resharding now") sliced mid-word
into a garbage owner hint. Harmless today (the hint is
diagnostic-only, never dialed, and a correct server only
sends the sentinel forms), but it was two rules for one
sentinel. Use the same guard in both, so a non-sentinel
reason yields no hint. Test added.

2026-07-22: Companion: tested units for the sharding client
work (signals, backoff, force-fresh)

Test-first foundation for the resolved-mode client work, the
pure pieces that both the phone reconnect loop and the mac
park loop will dispatch on, so the taxonomy and schedule are
pinned by tests rather than re-derived in each loop.

- RelaySignal: classifies a WS close (code + reason) or an
HTTP status
  into re-resolve / retry-here / long-backoff / fatal per
  the 6.9 wire codes, reason-disambiguating the overloaded
  1008/1000 and parsing the diagnostic reshard owner hint.
  Tests cover 4421/reshard, 421, the quota/displaced
  long-backoff, the transient 1008 reasons, and the "only a
  re-resolve code leaves the host" invariant.
- RelayReconnectBackoff: the full-jitter schedule of 7.3 /
Appendix C
  (initial 3s, exponential base 1s to cap 30s). Tests cover
  the window values, monotonicity, the cap, and that every
  draw is within its window.
- ShardHostResolver gains a forceFresh resolve (skip the
cached host,
  block on a fresh fetch) for the 6.9 re-resolve path; the
  protocol keeps the no-arg form via a default. Tests cover
  force-fresh picking up a new owner vs the cached path
  returning the stale host.

The transport-layer wiring (surfacing 421/4421 as typed
errors) and the app-layer dispatch build on these next.

2026-07-22: Companion relay: fix 6.9 close-code taxonomy
against the code

Review fixes: - Wrong citation: the client-side
match-on-code+reason precedent is
  RelayWebSocket.swift, not host/server.js (that path is the
  push relay and has no quota close). Also the matched
  substring is "daily quota", not "quota".
- Missing WS 1000 + "displaced": the relay hands the room's
single
  mac/phone slot to a newer connection (room.js) and the
  client (RelayDisplacedError) backs off 60 s so duplicate
  instances do not ping-pong. Add it to the long-backoff
  table; it is same-host, not a re-resolve. Implementing
  "every non-421/4421 -> short retry" literally would
  reintroduce the eviction storm.
- 1008 is overloaded (frame rate, bad hello, too many
pending,
  admission timeout, generic), and 1000 is normally a plain
  close; note both are reason-disambiguated so only the
  named reasons take the long backoff.
- 1001 decommission story split: up-but-drained earns a 421;
  a fully-down box is a connect failure handled by 6.4.
- Also fix a stale "uses the owner hint" line left from the
  diagnostic-only change.

2026-07-22: Companion relay: map is the sole authority for
redirect targets

A relay's re-resolve signal (421/4421) now means only "not
me"; the client always derives the new target from the shard
map, never from the relay's x-relay-owner hint. Demote the
hint to diagnostic-only (logging/debugging), force a map
refresh on a re-resolve, and record the property in 9 (a
relay can reject but never redirect).

This bounds a bad or malicious config: clients only ever
reach map-sanctioned hosts and cannot be bounced in an
endless X->Y->X loop; any residual retry is spaced by the
shared backoff (6.4). The cost is the lost round-trip the
hint once saved, which is cheap (a sub-KB, usually-cached
file).

2026-07-22: Companion relay: define connect-failure vs
re-resolve retry policy

6.4 step 5 lumped "host rejects" and "connect fails" into
one "re-fetch and retry". Split the connect outcome into
three classes and give each a where-to-go rule, with a
shared backoff for how-long-to-wait:

- Re-resolve signal (421 / 4421): host disowned the bucket;
go to
  the new owner.
- Retry-here code (429/503/500, 1011/1006/1001/1008): same
host,
  do not re-resolve.
- Connect failure (DNS/TLS/refused/timeout/unreachable):
ambiguous;
  re-fetch the map, switch if it names a different host,
  else presume transient and retry the same host (cached
  host if the map can't be fetched, per 8).

Add the loop bound: one shared consecutive-failure counter
drives the Appendix C backoff (reset on success), which
spaces out a skew-window 421-bounce; a must-stay-available
role retries indefinitely and surfaces status, while a fresh
foreground connect may give up with an actionable error.

2026-07-22: Companion relay: state relay-switch =
re-establish as a principle

6.7 described the re-attest/re-register flow as a reaction
to a verifier-absent state; frame it up front as the event
it actually is: switching relays (reshard, restart, death)
means the room is re-established on the new box. Make
explicit that it is silent, that the phone re-runs
attest/register while the Mac only re-parks, and that the
phone tries a signed join first and re-attests only when
told "unestablished" (no speculative re-attestation).

2026-07-22: Companion relay: specify reject-on-doubt for the
HTTP endpoints

6.5 described reject-on-doubt only for the WS join. Make it
explicit that /attest/challenge, /attest, /register, and
/delete share the same ownership gate (HTTP 421), and pin
the second-order behavior:

- Check ownership early, before body read / attest verify /
the
  /attest rate-limit charge, so a stale-map bounce is not an
  attest failure and does not burn the limiter.
- No drain defer for these one-shot calls: a host that has
lost a
  bucket rejects its HTTP calls immediately (the 7.4 defer
  keeps only the live splice alive).
- On a 421 the client restarts the sub-flow against the new
owner
  rather than resending: challenges/tickets/tokens are
  host-local and origin-bound (6.7, 6.10), so old-host state
  fails at the new host. The origin binding is the fail-safe
  backstop.
- One resolved host per attempt (WS join + all HTTP calls),
so a
  client never registers on one host while joining another.
- /delete stays best-effort; drain + socket close reclaim
the room.

2026-07-22: Companion relay: set concrete parameter defaults
(Appendix C)

Fill in the starting values the doc left as placeholders: -
SHARDMAP_POLL_INTERVAL = 10 s - SHARDMAP_TTL (CDN max-age) =
5 s - RESHARD_DRAIN_DELAY = 20 s (2 x poll) -
RESHARD_EVICTION_RATE = 10 rooms/s - Reconnect jitter (7.3):
full jitter, uniform [0, 3 s] initial,
  full-jitter exponential backoff base 1 s / cap 30 s.

Add Appendix C as the single source of truth (table + the
ordering relationships the 6-7 arguments depend on), and
wire the concrete numbers into 6.3, 6.8, 7.3, and 7.4
(worked example retimed to the 10 s poll).

2026-07-22: Companion relay: pin version-counter semantics
and the publish pipeline

7.1 now specifies that `version` is a monotonically
increasing integer counter (never a timestamp): a total
order that cannot regress on clock skew or collide on
near-simultaneous edits, and the unambiguous "next" that
roll-forward recovery (6.4) needs. Records that a
to-be-built web admin tool is the sole writer, holding the
authoritative counter and replacing shardmap.json
atomically, and that hand-editing out of band is unsafe. 6.3
cross-references it.

2026-07-22: Companion relay: mark shard-map signing as not
required

Reframe 6.8 and 9 so map signing is explicitly optional
rather than a recommended belt-and-suspenders step: HTTPS
from our domain plus the QR-anchored resolver already
authenticate the map, and E2EE/Noise bounds a tampered map
to DoS/metadata, never content.  Record it as a 11 future
item, worth revisiting only if the map is ever fronted by a
third-party edge that terminates our TLS.

2026-07-22: Companion relay: specify the shard-map host
authority grammar

State the format of the map's host field in 6.3 and 6.10: -
It is an authority, not a URL (no
scheme/path/userinfo/query/
  fragment); the client prepends https:// so transport is
  always HTTPS/WSS and the field never carries a scheme.
- May be a DNS name or a bare IP literal (IPv6 bracketed).
- May carry an explicit :port; omit for the default 443.  -
Whatever form is used, that byte string is the box's
provisioned
  identity, its TLS cert name, and its origin base.

2026-07-22: Companion relay: specify the proof origin and
host identity (6.10)

The design never mentioned that the admission/delete
transcripts and the App Attest clientData bind an origin,
nor that client and relay must derive byte-identical
strings. Add 6.10:

- The join, delete, and App Attest proofs bind origin =
"https://"
  + host; a one-byte mismatch silently fails every signed
  join.
- A managed box's one provisioned hostname is the source of
truth
  for three things that must agree: its TLS cert SAN, its
  shard-map host entries (ownership), and its proof origin.
- The origin is server-side, anchored to the TLS cert, never
taken
  from the Host header.
- The client builds "https://" + host from the map verbatim
  (resolved) or the QR relay= value (direct), so the host
  stays in one canonical form.

Wires 6.7, 6.8, 9, and Appendix A invariant 7 to it.

2026-07-22: Companion relay: pin the re-resolution wire
codes (6.9)

The doc named the new re-resolve outputs but left them
unspecified, blocking client and relay work. Fix concrete
codes:

- HTTP 421 for reject-to-re-resolve (wrong host / no owner),
on the
  WS upgrade and the HTTP data-plane paths.
- WS close 4421 for evict-to-re-resolve on a live spliced
socket,
  with a "reshard" reason sentinel so stacks that cannot
  read a 4xxx code (URLSessionWebSocketTask) still detect
  it.
- Optional MOVED-style owner hint: x-relay-owner header
(HTTP) or
  "reshard <host>" reason (WS).
- Documents the retry-here set (429/503/500, 1011/1006/1001,
1008,
  403/413) as client convention, with the invariant that
  only 421/4421 move a client to a different host.

Wires 6.4, 6.5, 7.2, 7.4, and the 8 failure table to 6.9.

2026-07-22: Companion relay: add relay sharding design doc

Draft of the horizontal-sharding design for the iPhone
companion relay: roomName to bucket to host mapping, static
CDN shard map, reject-on-doubt admission, gradual drain, and
the two rendezvous modes (direct v1, resolved v2).

2026-07-22: Companion: phone self-heals a signed join a
fresh host can't verify (6.7)

The phone only self-healed one direction: an attested join
the relay wanted signed. It missed the reverse - the core
resharding case in 6.7.  When a bucket moves to a fresh box
(reshard, restart, death) the soft verifier state is gone,
so the box is back in pairing mode; a phone that believes
the room established SIGNS its join, and under the
attestation posture the relay rejects it "ticket required"
(room.js: no verifier -> phone must present a ticket). With
no handler, establish threw and the reconnect loop re-signed
forever, so a reshard broke reconnect.

Add admissionNeedsTicket and a catch for an established join
rejected "ticket required": forget the verifier-registered
marker, re-attest to earn a ticket, and reconnect fresh. The
post-connect lockRelayRoom then re-registers the identical
HKDF(roomSecret) verifier, restoring the room on the new
host with no QR/SAS re-pair. The mac needs no change (it is
admitted pre-auth on a verifier-less host, room.js line
400).

2026-07-22: Companion: resolve the attestation/registration
origin in v2

The phone's App Attest ticket, verifier registration, and
/register POST all keyed on code.relayOrigin, which is nil
in resolved (v2) mode. So a v2 fresh pairing against an
attestation-required relay earned no ticket and, had it
proceeded, would have attested/registered over the wrong
origin: the relay verifies the clientDataHash and join
signature against its configured RELAY_ORIGIN, and the phone
signs its join over the resolved shard host, so the two must
agree.

Add resolvedOrigin(for:) - the resolved shard host in v2,
the relay origin in v1 - and use it for
pairingTicketIfNeeded and lockRelayRoom (which feeds
registerRelayVerifier). It goes through the same session
resolver as the connect, so the attest origin and the join
origin resolve to the same host from the same cached map. A
v2 resolve failure yields nil (a consistent failed/open-mode
attempt), never a mismatched origin.

2026-07-22: Companion: surface fresh-pairing resolve
failures; bound the resolve timeout

Three review fixes.

1. Fresh-pairing park-setup failures are no longer
swallowed. In resolved
   mode startPairing() returns after showing the QR and the
   resolve happens later on the accept task; if the resolver
   is down or names no host, the resolve/listener-build
   catches previously did a silent retry, so the QR sat dead
   with no error and the pid re-minted forever with no
   feedback.  parkSetupDidFail now routes fresh-pairing
   failures through onFailed +
   scheduleFreshPairingRegeneration, the same path a
   direct-mode park death reaches via acceptLoop, so the
   window shows "could not reach the relay".

2. The shard-map resolve is now bounded by the caller's
connect timeout.
   ResolvingTransportConnector.connect wraps the resolve in
   the caller's budget and passes only the remaining time to
   the inner connect, so a cold fetch in a fresh process
   (the NSE, 10s budget) can no longer blow the timeout the
   caller set. PluginShardMapFetcher's GET is bounded too:
   plugin HTTP now sets a 30s per-request timeout (it cannot
   set one through the signed JS bridge, so it is applied
   natively in performHTTP), down from the ~60s session
   default; the parked WebSocket is unaffected.

3. Stale doc comments that described a version-pointer +
shardmap-vN.json
   scheme the loader never implemented now say
   shardmap.json, and ParseError.unsupportedVersion no
   longer says "v != 1" (v2 parses).

2026-07-22: Companion: unify the shard-resolver cache and
unpair delete across mac/phone

The per-session resolver cache and the unpair delete-origin
logic were implemented twice (mac park path and phone
connect path), so the load-bearing invariants
(floor-survives-reconnect keying, v1/v2 delete fallback)
were encoded in two places that could drift, exactly the
split the design's "both endpoints run the same resolution"
(6.4) warns against.

Add two shared pieces in CompanionTransport: -
ShardResolverCache: one ShardHostResolver keyed by (resolver
URL, egress
  token). The mac passes the plugin client identity so a
  reload rebuilds against the new egress; the phone (no
  plugin) passes nil.
- ShardHostResolver.resolveDeleteOrigin: resolve the owning
shard host in
  v2, else the direct origin, else nil (best effort; TTL
  reclaims).

Both mac and phone now use these, so the invariants live
once. Tests cover the cache keying (reuse on same key,
rebuild on different token).

2026-07-22: Companion: don't block/fail connect on the shard
map; no-redirect fetch

Resolution correctness and the mandated egress posture.

ShardHostResolver.relayOrigin no longer blocks every connect
on a full shardmap.json GET and no longer fails a connect on
a transient CDN blip.  When a map is already adopted (the
resolver is reused across a session's reconnects) it returns
the cached host immediately and refreshes in the background
(ShardMapLoader.refreshInBackground, deduped); only the
first resolve of a session, with no map yet, blocks on a
fetch. This restores the design's "reconnects still work off
the cached host" (8) and "no steady-state dependence on the
control plane" (6.6). A reshard still converges: the relay
evicts the room, and the next reconnect uses the
just-refreshed map.

The phone shard-map GET now uses the no-redirect
CompanionURLSession (as every other companion egress does),
injected at the three phone construction sites, so a
compromised CDN edge cannot 3xx-redirect it to a rogue host
(9). URLSessionShardMapFetcher also bounds the GET with a
timeout so a stalled fetch cannot blow the NSE's connect
budget.

Also: RelayRoom.name uses the module's
Data.hexEncodedString() (the roomName is a cross-language
wire value, so one canonical encoder), and the makeCode
comment is corrected to say both endpoints resolve the shard
host in resolved mode (not "the mac parks at its own relay
origin").

2026-07-22: Companion: v2 polish (move-prompt, host
disclosure, unpair delete, phone resolver)

Four resolved-mode polish items, none blocking v2 function.

1. Mac relay-move prompt: skip the main-relay-origin
comparison for a
   resolved (v2) pairing. That check is a v1 concept (the QR
   bakes in a relay host); in v2 the pairing is anchored to
   the resolver URL and the host moves freely via the shard
   map, so comparing CompanionRelayOrigin would raise a
   spurious "server moved, re-pair" prompt. A resolver-URL
   move is a DNS concern, so there is nothing to detect.

2. Host disclosure: the phone's non-official-server warning
now discloses
   the resolver host (v2) or relay host (v1), compared
   against the right default (resolver.iterm2.com vs
   relay.iterm2.com). Generalized RelayHost.hostToDisclose's
   parameter to originURL.

3. Unpair delete-room: resolve the owning shard host in v2
(through the
   plugin egress on the mac, URLSession on the phone) so the
   delete hits the room's actual host; best effort, idle TTL
   reclaims on any miss.

4. Phone shard resolver: hoist one session-lived resolver
into AppModel
   (via productionConnector) so a v2 pairing's map cache and
   monotonic floor survive across reconnect attempts,
   reducing host flapping while a reshard propagates. Not
   durable; a relaunch starts fresh.

2026-07-22: Companion: honor cancellation in parkAndAccept
errors; key resolver cache to plugin

Two fixes in the mac resolved-park path.

parkAndAccept's resolve-failure and listener-build catch
blocks were asymmetric with the success path: they
unconditionally nil'd acceptTask and scheduled a retry even
when stopAdvertising had cancelled the task mid-flight. That
could clobber a replacement accept task's handle (leading to
two listeners parking in the same room and displacing each
other) and re-arm a retry stopAdvertising had just
cancelled, re-parking after an intended teardown. Both
catches now mirror the success path: if Task.isCancelled,
return without touching acceptTask or rescheduling
(stopAdvertising owns that teardown).

The shard-resolver cache was keyed on resolverURL alone, so
on a cache hit the passed-in fetcher was dropped. The
controller is a long-lived singleton, so after
CompanionPlugin.reload() the relay socket used the new
plugin client while the shard fetch stayed pinned to the
resolver built with the old PluginShardMapFetcher. Key the
cache on the plugin client identity too, so a reload
rebuilds the resolver against the current egress.

2026-07-22: Companion: assert the phone-only URLSession
shard fetch isn't used off-iOS

The resolved connector's URLSession shard-map fetch is the
phone path (the phone has no consent plugin). The Mac never
reaches this factory, so guard that claim: assertionFailure
under #if !os(iOS) when the URLSession fallback is taken, so
a Mac call site that would fetch off the plugin's sanctioned
egress trips in debug/tests instead of silently bypassing
it.

Scoped to the fallback branch (not the whole factory) so
passing a resolver skips it, and expanded the ?? so it only
fires when the fallback is actually used. Updated the macOS
selection test to pass a stub resolver (as both apps do) so
it does not exercise, and trip on, that assertion.

2026-07-22: Companion: log the shard resolution path and
remove a URLSession footgun

The resolve/park path is where rendezvous bugs (wrong
bucket, stale map, mac/phone landing on different hosts)
will hide, and it was near-silent.  Add CompanionLog lines
(which surface in both apps' field logs) at each decision
point: the shard-map GET URL and its fetch/decode failures;
map adopt vs ignore-not-newer vs validation-failure; the pid
-> bucket -> host resolution and no-host case; the resolved
origin joined; and, on the mac, whether a park used resolved
or direct mode.

Also remove the URLSessionShardMapFetcher() default from
ShardHostResolver.init. The mac must fetch only through the
consent plugin (its one sanctioned egress), and a URLSession
default would let a future mac call site fetch a URL
directly without noticing. Egress is now an explicit choice
everywhere: the mac passes the plugin fetcher, the phone (no
plugin) passes URLSession.

2026-07-22: Companion: fail closed when the consent plugin
is unavailable at park

startListening fell back to a raw URLSession for both the
relay socket and the shard-map fetch when
CompanionPlugin.instance() was not success. That undermines
the plugin's whole purpose: it is the only sanctioned
outbound path for the feature, so a raw-URLSession fallback
carries relay traffic (and the CDN shard-map GET) around the
consent gate. Since startListening is only reached when the
gate is allowed, which already requires the plugin, the
branch is defensive anyway. Make it fail closed: if the
plugin is unavailable, do not park (throw, so the caller
schedules a retry) rather than egress off the sanctioned
path.

2026-07-22: Companion: resolve v2 codes to a host on the mac
park path

Wire resolved (v2) mode into the mac's park. startListening
splits into a sync shell and an async parkAndAccept on the
accept task: when a resolver URL is configured it looks the
owning host up in the shard map (via a plugin-routed
ShardHostResolver, so the CDN GET takes the same consent
egress as relay traffic) exactly as the phone does, so both
land on the same box; otherwise it uses the statically
configured relay origin (direct mode). The resolved origin
is passed to the unchanged origin-based listener, so
admission/splice are shared.

Re-resolution on reshard needs nothing extra: within one
park the origin is stable across phone connect/disconnect,
and a reshard drops the park with an error that exits
acceptLoop and retries, re-entering parkAndAccept to
re-resolve onto the new owner. A resolve failure is a
transient park failure (retry), not a hard error. One
resolver is cached per resolver URL so the map's monotonic
floor and cache survive reconnects in a session.

Add CompanionPlugin.shardMapFetcher() /
PluginShardMapFetcher to route the shard-map fetch through
the plugin.

2026-07-22: Companion: resolve v2 codes to a host on the
phone connect path

Wire resolved (v2) mode into the phone connector.
ShardHostResolver maps a pairing's resolver URL + room
bucket (RelayRoom.bucket looked up in the shard map from
ShardMapLoader) to an owning relay origin; the
ResolvingTransportConnector resolves that origin at connect
time and then joins it with the exact same admission proof
and splice as direct mode, so a reconnect after a reshard
lands on the new owner.

CompanionTransports.connector now returns the resolving
connector for a v2 code (creating a transient resolver from
the code's resolver URL when no shared one is passed) and
keeps direct (v1) mode unchanged. The mac park side still
resolves nothing yet, so end-to-end v2 needs the mac wiring
that follows.

2026-07-22: Companion: fix shard-map restart bootstrap and
room-name hex check

ShardMapLoader.refresh() persisted only the highest-seen
version, not the map, so after a relaunch current is nil
while highestVersion is the seeded floor. The old guard
ignored any version <= floor, so a restart against an
unchanged publisher (version == floor) never adopted the map
and resolved mode could not pick a host until the next
version bump. Make the guard bootstrap-aware: with no map
loaded yet, adopt a version equal to the floor, but still
reject one strictly below it (the stale-edge regression the
persisted floor exists to prevent); with a map loaded, keep
requiring a strictly newer version.

RelayRoom.bucket(forRoomName:) used Character.isHexDigit,
which follows Unicode Hex_Digit and admits uppercase and
fullwidth forms (U+FF10...), so a non-canonical 64-char
string could pass the guard and return a bucket. Restrict to
literal lowercase ASCII [0-9a-f], matching the form name()
emits.

2026-07-22: Companion: derive the shard bucket from the room
name (+ vector)

Add RelayRoom.bucket: the shard bucket is the last two bytes
of the room-name SHA256 digest taken big-endian (digest[30]
<< 8 | digest[31]), i.e. the numeric value of the room
name's last four hex characters, in [0, 65535]. Two entry
points: from rs/pid (shares the one digest with name(), so
they can never disagree on the preimage) and from an
already-computed room-name header.

Pin it with RoomBucketVectors, a canonical JSON vector the
Node relay must reproduce byte-for-byte (Appendix A
invariant 1): the values are fixed forever since N_BUCKETS
is immutable. RoomBucketTests checks both entry points
against it, plus endianness, sign, bounds, and reject cases.

2026-07-22: Companion: add the shard-map loader (phone, not
yet wired)

Standalone loader for resolved mode: ShardMap is the parsed,
validated map (version + [{low, high, host}] ranges) with a
bucket -> host lookup; ShardMapLoader fetches it and applies
monotonic versioning.

The map is a single shardmap.json under the resolver base
URL, served short-TTL and replaced atomically, so one fetch
always yields the latest (no version pointer, no second
round-trip): the map carries its own version, so a stale
read from a lagging CDN edge is just an older version and is
ignored. It does not carry n_buckets; the client owns the
fixed constant and the ranges are validated to tile [0,
65535].

Networking is behind an injectable ShardMapFetching so the
loader is tested entirely offline; the URL is built with
URLComponents; version persistence is the caller's concern.
Not called from the app yet.

2026-07-22: Companion: carry a resolver URL in the pairing
QR (v2 resolved mode)

Add resolved mode to the pairing code. The QR now carries
either a relay= origin (direct, v1) or a resolver= URL
(resolved, v2), selected by which is present and tagged by
the version; the two are mutually exclusive. The resolver is
the base URL of the static, versioned shard map on a CDN, so
a path is allowed and preserved verbatim while
userinfo/query/fragment and non-https are refused.

The mac emits a v2 resolver QR when the new
CompanionResolverURL advanced setting is non-empty (default
https://resolver.iterm2.com/), and falls back to a v1 relay
QR when it is emptied. The mac still parks at its own relay
origin either way; the resolver only tells the phone how to
find the owning shard (resolution itself is not wired up
yet).

The handshake prologue version is decoupled from the QR
version: the two number independent concerns (handshake
binding vs rendezvous format), so a v2 pairing still
handshakes identically to a v1 one and does not break
already-paired devices.

2026-07-21: Keychain: pin the login-keychain queries to the
file keychain (fixes data loss)

The migration lost items on relaunch: a paired companion
device asked to re-pair, and the identity key / push nonce
read as absent from both stores.

Root cause: for an app carrying the keychain-access-groups
entitlement, a SecItem query that OMITS
kSecUseDataProtectionKeychain does not reliably target the
file keychain. legacyQuery omitted it, so operations
split-brained: SecItemCopyMatching read the file item, but
the reap's SecItemDelete resolved to the DATA-PROTECTION
keychain and deleted the copy we had just written and
readback-confirmed. Every write thus reaped its own
data-protection copy; items with a lingering login copy
re-migrated forever, and items with none (the companion
identity key and the push nonce) vanished on the next
launch.

Fix: legacyQuery now pins kSecUseDataProtectionKeychain:
false, forcing every legacy op (read, delete, upsert) to the
file keychain so it can never touch the data-protection
copy. Validated end to end on a signed build: pair,
relaunch, device reconnects with no re-pair prompt.

Also: report the actual reap OSStatus instead of an
unconditional "reaped" (reapLegacyReportingStatus), and
settle the write/miss/absent logs at DLog with RLog reserved
for migrations and genuine errors.

2026-07-21: AI keys: suite-namespace the keychain account
(Phase 2)

Under a custom -suite (side-by-side dev builds, e.g. make
run / run-keychain), the AI keychain account is now
namespaced with the suite name, mirroring
CompanionMacIdentity.suitedAccount, so a dev build gets
isolated API keys and never reads, migrates, reaps, or
clobbers the user's real (bare-account) keys.  Applied at
the keychain I/O boundary (readKeychainPassword /
persistAPIKey) so per-vendor and the legacy shared account
are namespaced uniformly. No -suite (every shipping build)
keeps the bare account name, so production is unchanged.
This is what would have prevented the real-key loss during
earlier testing.

2026-07-21: Keychain: point shipping configs at the iTerm2
Dev ID Keychain profile

The keychain-access-groups entitlement requires a
provisioning profile under Manual signing. Set
PROVISIONING_PROFILE_SPECIFIER on Beta/Nightly/Deployment to
the iTerm2 Dev ID Keychain profile (authorizes
keychain-access-groups: H7V7XYVQ7D.* and holds the current
Developer ID cert). Identity stays name-based now that the
redundant Developer ID certs were removed, so no cert SHA is
pinned.

2026-07-21: Keychain: migrate AI and companion secrets to
the data-protection keychain

Approach A of two being compared. Stops the "iTerm2 wants to
use your confidential information" prompt that recurs after
every upgrade by moving app-owned secrets out of the login
keychain (ACL-gated by the exact writer code signature) into
the data-protection keychain (authorized by the
keychain-access-groups entitlement, stable across any build
the team signs).

sources/Keychain/iTermUpgradeSafeKeychain.swift is the core:
a drop-in for the generic-password SecItem primitives that
reads the data-protection keychain first and, on a miss,
transparently migrates a login-keychain value forward
(write, verify readback, then reap the login copy). It pins
kSecAttrAccessGroup explicitly (read from our own
entitlement, matched by suffix) so reads and writes target
the same group across launches, falls back to the login
keychain when the data-protection keychain is unavailable or
a hard write error occurs with no stale item present, and
deletes the login copy before the data-protection copy so a
cleared secret can never resurrect via migration.

Wired through it: AITermControllerObjC (AI keys,
WhenUnlocked), CompanionMacIdentity and
CompanionPushNonceRegistry (this-device-only). Password
Manager and adapter credentials deliberately stay in the
login keychain.

Entitlements are split so local dev stays friction-free:
Development uses iTerm2-Development.entitlements (no
keychain group), Beta/Nightly/Deployment use
iTerm2.entitlements (with the group). make run-keychain /
make purge-keychain-test plus
tools/codesign_keychain_test.sh exercise the signed path
locally by re-signing a Development build with an installed
Dev ID profile; the purge support is compiled out of release
via ITERM_DEBUG.

Known open items on this approach: signed release builds
need a provisioning profile carrying the entitlement and the
current cert (empty specifier currently fails to build
signed); AI accounts are not suite-namespaced so dev builds
touch real keys; the migration surfaced several cross-store
consistency hazards. These motivated evaluating the
partition-list alternative.

2026-07-20: Makefile: allow overriding the defaults suite
via SUITE

The run, runbg, and open targets defaulted the -suite
argument to the directory name. Add a SUITE variable
defaulting to that so it can be overridden, e.g. SUITE=xxx
make run.

2026-07-18: Companion: on-screen keyboard for live sessions

Add phone-driven keyboard input to the companion app's live
session view.  Tapping the terminal raises the software
keyboard; an accessory bar adds Esc, Control-C, Tab, and
arrows, and expands to sticky Control and left/right Option
dead-keys, function keys, and Home/End/PgUp/PgDn/Del.

Protocol: new sendKey message (revision 10,
keyInputRevision). The phone shows the keyboard only when
the mac advertises support, so an older mac never leaves
dead keys.

Mac: injectSynthesizedKeyEvent runs the synthesized event
through the session's own key path (accept-gate, profile key
bindings, key mapper), so control/option encodings honor the
profile (per-side Option behavior), special keys honor
cursor/keypad/CSI-u key-reporting modes, and a tap delivers
a matching key-up under Kitty report-all-event-types. Key
codes come from the active keyboard layout so input
round-trips on non-US layouts; characters with no
single-keystroke mapping are written literally. Broadcast to
split panes is suppressed while injecting. The streamed
frame now draws a focused (filled) cursor.

Sends are serialized on the phone so bursts keep order.

Release notes in Companion/docs/notes.txt.

2026-07-18: Hotkey window: wait for resign-active before
ordering out on macOS 26

On macOS 26 (Tahoe) the previously active app, activated via
activateWithOptions:, becomes frontmost a few runloop turns
later than it did on earlier systems. Ordering out the
hotkey window while iTerm2 is still active let AppKit
briefly promote a background iTerm2 window to the front,
which the user saw as a flash (issue 5313).

Defer the order-out until iTerm2 actually resigns active,
with a 0.3s fallback for the case where we never yield
(keeping iTerm2 frontmost is correct then). This preserves
the issue 11372 fix (no fixed delay; we order out exactly
when the app switch lands) while avoiding the flash.

Cherry-picked from confirmed adhoc build
0.20260629_111322-adhoc.

2026-07-18: Workgroup auto-send: delay the submit Return
after pasting review

When a code-review peer went idle, the auto-send pasted the
results into the main session and immediately sent a Return
to submit them.  The Return was correctly ordered after the
content (it serialized through the paste helper's queue),
but it was written the instant the content drained, so it
rode in the same stdin read as the bracketed- paste-end
marker. Claude Code applies every key parsed from one read
in a single batched render, so the paste and the Return ran
together and the Return's submit saw the pre-paste (empty)
input and no-oped, leaving the review unsent. This showed up
as "sometimes the Return does not take effect."

Add an initialDelay to PasteEvent, honored in the paste
helper by scheduling the first write via a timer (keeping
isPasting true during the wait so nothing jumps the queue).
The delay is measured from when the prior paste drains, so
it can't reintroduce the old fixed-timer-
races-the-chunked-paste bug. Expose it as
pasteLiteralString:afterDelay: on iTermPasteHelper and
PTYSession, and use it for the auto-send Return. The delay
lands the Return in a later read, after the paste has
committed, so it submits.

The delay is tunable via the new
workgroupAutoSendSubmitDelay advanced setting, defaulting to
0.5s to match Claude Code's 500 ms paste- settling window
(PASTE_TIMEOUT). Set to 0 to send immediately.

2026-07-18: AI chat: add restart_session; rename Type for
You permission to Control Terminal

Adds a restart_session remote command that relaunches the
linked terminal session (equivalent to Session > Restart
Session), gated on isRestartable(). Renames the "Type for
You" AI permission to "Control Terminal", which now also
gates restart_session. The persisted UserDefaults key and
the PermissionCategory rawValue accept the legacy "Type for
You" string so existing grants survive the rename.

2026-07-18: Companion: add "go to session" button and fix
defunct-session view

Add a toolbar button in a session-bound chat that jumps to
its session view. It appears only while the bound session is
still open; unbound and orchestrator chats (whose
terminalSessionGuid is nil) never show it.

The flat sessions list cannot answer "does this session
still exist": it is built from allSessions() and omits
buried and peer/workgroup-member sessions, which are valid
session-bound targets. So liveness is probed via the
screen-info request, which resolves the guid through the
Mac's full session lookup and reports a typed
.unknownSession error when the session is truly gone.
probeBoundSessionLiveness runs on conversation appear and
records the verdict per chat; a transient failure leaves the
prior verdict untouched so the button does not flicker on a
blip.

Also fix the live session view showing a perpetual black
screen when you open a session that has already closed:
startLiveStreamIfPossible swallowed every start error as
transient, so a gone session never surfaced. Catch the typed
.unknownSession there and end the view with
.sessionClosed so it shows "Stream Ended" instead. Force that overlay to
the dark color scheme so its text and icon stay legible on
the canvas's always-black background in light mode
(mirroring the nav bar).

2026-07-18: Companion: live session view fills the screen
edge to edge

Host the live canvas full-bleed (a background stack that
ignores the safe area, rather than .ignoresSafeArea on the
representable, which does not reliably expand a
UIViewRepresentable), so the video reaches the real screen
edges behind the translucent nav bar, tab bar, and the
dynamic island in landscape.

The scroll view keeps .never inset adjustment and derives
its content insets from its own safeAreaInsets on all four
sides, so adjustedContentInset equals them exactly. At rest
the terminal sits within the safe area; the initial layout
pins the bottom and places the left edge just clear of the
left inset. Because the scroll limits, the initial pin, and
the alt-screen wheel-mode edge detection all read
adjustedContentInset, the content only reaches behind the
bars/island (and only then wheels) once scrolled past the
inset. The horizontal insets are what let you scroll edge
columns out from under the dynamic island; live-output
re-pins keep the user's horizontal scroll.

2026-07-18: Global search: extract snippets from the
searched snapshot

Blank global-search snippets appeared for main-screen
matches while an alternate-screen app was running. The
cursor found results against the search engine's immutable
snapshot but then extracted snippets from a freshly taken
snapshotWithPrimaryGrid, which could drift out from under
the result coordinates (e.g. an emptied primary grid),
yielding out-of-bounds coordinates and empty snippets.

Expose the engine's searchedSnapshot and extract from it
instead of re-snapshotting, so the result coordinates and
the extractor's data source are consistent by construction.

2026-07-18: Companion: zoom to 60/120 columns instead of
40/80

2026-07-18: Browser: prompt for credentials on HTTP auth
challenges

The WKNavigationDelegate auth challenge handler was a stub
that always performed default handling with no credential,
so HTTP Basic/Digest/NTLM challenges failed to load instead
of asking the user. Now prompt for a user name and password
and respond with a session-scoped URLCredential, while
leaving server trust and other methods on default handling.

2026-07-18: Workgroups: reload a diff session with the
picker's current selection

Reloading a .diff workgroup session replayed the session's
last-launched _program verbatim. That program can silently
diverge from what the file picker shows: when the file the
popup was on stops being a diffable change, a git poll
resets the popup back to "All Files" in
CCDiffSelectorItem.set(fileStatuses:) without restarting the
session, leaving _program pointed at a now-clean per-file
diff.  Reloading then ran `git difftool` on a file with no
changes, which prints nothing and exits 0, ending the
session instead of re-diffing.

Reload now runs the command matching the picker's current
selection (per-file when a file is picked, All Files
otherwise) instead of _program.
reloadDiffWithDeferralIfNeeded takes a resolveCommand
closure that is evaluated as late as possible: immediately
on the ready path, or inside the deferred pendingDiffLaunch
closure at fire time, so a pick or gitBase change between
the reload click and the relaunch is reflected. A nil
resolver falls back to a plain restart() for callers
(arrangement restoration) with no selection to resolve.

2026-07-18: Companion: skip launch-time keychain priming
under unit tests

The Companion launch hooks in applicationDidFinishLaunching
(resumePairedListeningIfNeeded and the promptToRepair
pairing completeness checks) read keychain-backed pairing
material at launch. On a locked login keychain that read
becomes a modal unlock prompt, which blocks the app before
the test runner connects and fails an unattended run with
"test runner hung before establishing connection." A test
host has no paired device, so none of this work is needed
there. Gate the whole block on [NSApp isRunningUnitTests].

It is skipped in the orchestration session_* mirror because

Also fix session-bound system prompt selection. A chat can
be linked to a terminal, a browser, both, or neither, but a

to mention get_screen_contents.

2026-07-17: Companion: hide scrollback and translate scroll

2026-07-17: Companion: session chat button returns to the
session, not home

The "Chat about this session" button opened the conversation
via openConversation(replacingPath: true), which forced the

the Chats home screen no matter how the session was reached,
and flipped tabs if you'd started on the Sessions tab.

Reuse the reply-notification path's behavior instead:

the conversation above the existing stack (de-duping via

and create-new branches through it, so Back returns to the

2026-07-17: Global search: instrument empty-snippet
extraction

Add diagnostic logging to track down blank snippets in
global search results. attributedStringForSnippetForRange:
now logs when the match text (or the whole
prefix+match+suffix result) comes back empty for a range
that should contain a cell, dumping the abs range, the
converted relative range, scrollback overflow, and the
actual buffer line at the match coordinate so a scrolled-off
line can be told apart from a repainted-blank one.

The cursor logs a per-result heartbeat with the pass,
alternate-screen state, raw match coordinates, resulting
snippet length, and how far the buffer scrolled since the
search began, to measure the suspected drift between find
time and snippet-extraction time.

Logging only; no behavior change.

2026-07-16: Companion: a content-bearing wakeup no longer
shows the generic placeholder

A contentless wakeup push always satisfied its own iOS
notification with the generic "Your agent has an update."
fallback, even when the NSE had just fetched real messages
and delivered them as their own notifications.  So an active
session (which fires a wakeup per burst of new messages)
left a redundant standing placeholder beside the real
content.

Route the newest real message through the wakeup push's own
notification instead; the generic stands only when there is
genuinely nothing to show.  Older messages keep their own
unique-id notifications. The push's own notification carries
the fixed wakeup collapse-id, so that newest-message slot
collapses across wakeups (always the latest) while older
ones persist with their own banners.

Also add an NSE build stamp (logged on every push) so a
device log confirms which extension build is installed (the
app and the NSE install as separate binaries), and log
whether each wakeup was satisfied with a real message or the
placeholder.

2026-07-16: Orchestration: announce a milestone only once

Strengthen the display policy so the agent does not
re-announce a milestone (a fix applied, a review kicked off)
when a later tool result or status_update merely re-confirms
it. It had been posting several near-duplicate messages per
outcome, which on the phone also produced several wakeup
pushes.

2026-07-16: Release notes: orchestration watches survive a
peer reload

2026-07-16: Orchestration: keep session/workgroup internals
out of user-facing prose

Item 3: the always-applied display policy told the agent to
"use a short human-readable name" for a session, while the
prompt template requires the FULL @-prefixed id (which
iTerm2 renders as the session's clickable name). The agent
split the difference and emitted bare, abbreviated ids,
which render as dead text. Replace that line so both agree:
point the user at a session with its full @-id or its role
name, never an abbreviated one, and never surface internal
fields (session ids, workgroup ids, status_source, role_id)
in prose.

Item 5: "workgroup" is an internal grouping the user did not
name, so the agent should not say "workgroup" or show a
wg-/workgroup_id to the user.  Refer to a session by its
role, using the workgroup's human name only to disambiguate
when truly necessary.

2026-07-16: Orchestration: key watches on the stableID so
they survive a peer reload

A watcher stored session.guid as its key, but
replaceTerminatedShellWithNewInstance rotates the guid on an
in-place shell reload (e.g. a Code Review peer restarting).
The stable-ID work upgraded the resolver (sessionByGUID now
calls anySession(forReference:)) but left the store side
unchanged, so a reloaded session's stored key went stale,
the session no longer resolved, and the watch neither fired
nor was dropped: it died silently for hours. The
sessionByGUID comment even claimed watchers persisted the
stableID, which they did not; the reload-durable keying was
plumbed but inert.

Key watchers on the reload-durable stableID via a single
watcherKey(for:) at both registration sites. Route every
watcher/session match through one chokepoint,
WorkgroupWatcher.targets(stableID:guid:), which compares
against both ids so a stableID-keyed watcher follows a
reload while a legacy guid-keyed one still matches its
session. The tab-status handler now resolves the
notification's guid to its session (safe because
anySession(forReference:) covers peer-port sessions that
allSessions missed) and keys sessionStateHistory by
stableID, so transition detection survives a reload too. A
reload posts PTYSessionTerminated and keeps the stableID,
while a true teardown posts iTermSessionWillTerminate, so
the watch follows the former and is dropped on the latter.

Tests drive a real PTYSession and rotate its guid the way a
reload does, pinning both the store-side key and the match
logic.

2026-07-16: AI chat: document and pin the known streaming
tail-splice bug

The finalization tail-recovery in parseStreamingResponse
assumes the live-streamed text is a character-count prefix
of the reparsed authoritative body. When the two diverge
(ordering, whitespace, or across multiple text
content-blocks) it appends an overlapping fragment instead
of the true missing suffix, garbling the finalized message.

Add a KNOWN BUG comment at the site and a characterization
test that pins both the correct aligned-prefix behavior and
the current garbled divergent behavior, so the real
reconcile fix (handled separately) has a regression target
to flip.

2026-07-16: Release notes: quieter AI chat orchestration

2026-07-16: Companion: harden log-zip staging against the
delete race

Follow-up to the email-logs zip feature:

- Split CompanionLogArchive.write into stage + zip. stage
hard-links the
  log files (O(1), no byte copy; falls back to copy across
  volumes) rather than copying, so a large history is not
  duplicated on disk.
- makeLogArchive now flushes and stages inside one
queue.sync on the log
  queue, serialized against the delete-all that toggling
  logging off runs on that same queue. A concurrent delete
  either happens fully before the snapshot (nothing to
  archive) or fully after (the hard links keep the data
  alive), never interleaved with a half-staged set.
- Verify the built zip is readable and non-empty before
returning it, so
  the mail composer is never presented with nothing
  attached.
- Add a test that the archive survives deletion of the
sources after
  staging.

2026-07-16: Orchestration: stop the agent narrating every
tool-call step

The orchestrator treated each step of an autonomous loop as
a chat message, so a single event (a review finishing clean,
a watch firing) produced several near-duplicate bubbles
restating the same thing. The prompt encouraged it: it told
the agent to post a summary and ask what to do next on every
status_update.

Add a "Talking to the user" section to the always-applied
orchestration display policy: be concise, do not narrate
routine mechanics (watch registration, screen checks, the
steps of a multi-tool action), one message per meaningful
outcome rather than per tool call, treat a status_update as
a signal to act on rather than acknowledge, and run a
user-set-up standing loop silently between milestones.
Revise the watch-fired guidance in the prompt template and
the register_watch tool description to match: act on the
event and message the user only when a step advances, a task
finishes, or a decision is needed.

2026-07-16: Orchestration: render defunct @-mentions as
grayed, inert links

A mention whose session or workgroup no longer resolves used
to render as the plain literal "[defunct session]". Render
it like a live mention instead (leading terminal glyph, the
same bracketed wording the phone shows) but tinted a
de-emphasized gray and carrying no click action, cursor,
underline, or inherited .link, so it reads as a session
reference that is gone and clearly cannot be clicked.
Clearing .link matters: a defunct run inside a markdown link
would otherwise fall through to ClickableTextView's link
branch and still open a URL.

2026-07-16: Screen watches: widen poll cadence and wake the
orchestrator less

A screen-judged watch used to hard-stop at 5 minutes and
fire a watchTimedOut status_update, waking the orchestrator
(a full, user-visible model turn) for every watch that
outran the cap. A code review routinely runs longer, so this
produced a stream of "watch timed out, re-registering"
chatter for no user benefit.

The poller now keeps watching on its own with a tiered
cadence that widens over time to bound token cost: the
existing quadratic backoff for the first 5 minutes, then no
tighter than every 2 minutes, then no tighter than every 10
minutes past an hour. The give-up deadline rises to 6 hours,
so a routine long watch never wakes the orchestrator and the
timeout survives only as a rare backstop for a condition
that may never come true. The dispatcher timeout message and
the register_watch tool/prompt text are updated to match,
with deadlineDescription naming the wait so it cannot drift
from the deadline constant.

2026-07-16: Companion: email diagnostic logs as one zip, not
N attachments

Emailing several multi-megabyte log files as separate
attachments exceeded the mail composer's limits, so the logs
never sent. Bundle them into a single .zip and attach that
one file.

The zip is built by the system, not by hand:
NSFileCoordinator's
.forUploading read intent returns a .zip of a directory (the same code
path the Files app "Compress" and the share sheet use), so
there is no third-party dependency and the archive opens
everywhere. The app and NSE log files are staged into one
folder first, which also makes the archive extract to a tidy
top-level folder.

- CompanionLogArchive.write clears any stale archive up
front,
  disambiguates basename collisions so no readable file is
  silently dropped, and throws nothingToArchive when there
  is nothing to zip.
- makeLogArchive distinguishes "no logs" from a real failure
(which it
  logs) so the caller can report each correctly, and runs
  the copy/zip off the main thread with a "Preparing Logs"
  progress state.
- MailComposeView sends application/zip and logs any
attachment it
  cannot read.

2026-07-16: it2 over ssh: regenerate embedded
shell-integration with the updated it2.py

Re-embed the current it2.py into the assembled
bash/zsh/fish/tcsh/xonsh scripts (new content-hash filename)
and bump each shell's integration version so remotes
re-fetch. Generated by tools/copy_shell_integration.sh; the
shell-integration submodule is updated separately.

2026-07-16: it2 over ssh: add Shell > SSH > "Remote host can
control iTerm2" menu item

A checkable menu item that toggles whether the current
session's ssh connection may use it2, mirroring the
in-session authorization announcement.  It is enabled only
for an it2-capable ssh session (framer framing and the proxy
activated), and shows a checkmark whenever the connection
has an explicit grant, whether from the announcement or a
previous toggle. Selecting it grants or revokes for the
connection and persists the decision; if an announcement is
pending it resolves that too so a blocked command unblocks
immediately.

2026-07-16: it2 over ssh: per-connection API authorization,
restorable proxy state, recovery

Gate it2-over-ssh behind an explicit per-connection grant,
presented as an in-session announcement (never a silent
cookie; the local "allow all apps" setting deliberately does
not apply to a remote reaching back over the conductor). One
prompt per connection, fanned out to concurrent commands via
an iTermPromise whose seal the Conductor owns so it resolves
exactly once (on answer, or fail-closed on teardown).

Consolidate the proxy's nonce, socket path, and
authorization decision into a single IT2ProxyState value so
restorable-state coding, the accessors, and the SSH recovery
hand-off each move it in one step. Restorable decode
migrates the older two-scalar-key blobs
(it2Nonce/it2SocketPath) into it2Proxy so a restored session
keeps working; authorization is carried in process across
recovery and must never be re-derivable from
remote-controlled state.

2026-07-16: it2core: route embedded command I/O to the
remote and harden edge cases

When it2 runs embedded inside iTerm2 for an SSH client, its
output and confirmation prompts must reach the remote user
over the ssh channel, not the GUI app's invisible
stderr/stdin. Thread an injected err sink (and a confirm
provider that fails with a clear "re-run with --force"
message, since there is no remote tty to prompt) through
IT2Context, so config parse warnings and prompts surface
remotely. Also map the in-process host channel's cancel
(remote Ctrl+C -> clean exit 0) vs abort (real failure)
NSError codes to the right exit codes, and reject a negative
--lines up front instead of trapping in Array.suffix (which
crashed the whole app in embedded mode).

2026-07-16: it2 over ssh: fix stdout/stderr selector macro
collision

The @objc(runArguments:stdout:stderr:channel:) selector on
IT2Runner registered literally, but the Objective-C call
site in iTermInProcessIt2.m had <stdio.h>'s stdout/stderr
macros expand stdout:/stderr: into __stdoutp:/__stderrp: in
both the generated header and the call, so the two matched
at compile time and only diverged at runtime (Swift's @objc
string bypasses cpp). The embedded runner then crashed with
an unrecognized selector on the live SSH path, which the
Swift-only unit test could not reach.

Rename the selector pieces to stdoutHandler:/stderrHandler:
on both sides so nothing collides with the stdio macros.

2026-07-16: it2 over ssh: expose it2 on the remote via shell
integration

Make it2 usable on hosts reached through SSH integration.
The shell-integration scripts now self-materialize the it2
client and define it2 when it is not already available, so
activation no longer ships it2.py from the conductor.

- Each shell integration script (bash/zsh/fish/tcsh/xonsh)
carries it2.py as an
  embedded gzip+base64 blob, substituted at build time with
  envsubst. Unless it2 already exists, the script decodes
  the blob to ~/.iterm2/it2.<hash>.py on first use (via
  python3's stdlib, so no gzip/base64 command is needed on
  the remote) and defines an it2 function or alias. The
  content hash in the filename gives an upgrade path; a
  python3-less remote gets a clear "it2: python3 is
  required" message.
- it2.py's source of truth moved to the
iTerm2-shell-integration submodule;
  copy_shell_integration.sh copies it back to OtherResources
  and puts gettext's envsubst on PATH. gettext added to the
  Makefile setup target.
- Conductor no longer ships it2.py (removed the payload +
app resource); it still
  injects IT2_SOCK/IT2_NONCE and sends it2Listen once
  framing starts.

Bumps the iTerm2-shell-integration submodule to the commit
that embeds it2.py.

2026-07-16: it2 over ssh: activate the proxy at conductor
startup

Wire the previously-inert it2 path so the machinery goes
live. During the shell-integration setup (which implies
framing), mint a per-session auth nonce and a remote socket
path, inject IT2_SOCK/IT2_NONCE into the login shell's
environment so the remote it2.py can find and authenticate
to the socket, set it2Nonce so the demux authorizes HELLOs,
and ship it2.py alongside the shell-integration files.  Once
framer is running (doFraming), send it2Listen so framer
binds the socket.  it2.py is now bundled as an app resource
(mirroring framer.py).

Security: the socket lives in a dedicated owner-only
directory under the remote $HOME (framer makes it 0700
before binding), not a shared world-writable place. The
socket file is chmod 0600, but an owner-only parent
directory is the portable guarantee that no other local user
can reach it (bind-time socket perms are not portable).
framer unlinks the socket on shutdown so it does not
accumulate.

Recovery: it2Nonce/it2SocketPath are persisted in
RestorableState so it2 survives state restoration, and
re-read from the surviving framer environment on SSH
resynchronize (init(recovery:) starts fresh), so it2 does
not silently break after a reconnect.

The ergonomic `it2` command exposure on the remote is a
separate follow-up; for now the shipped it2.py at
~/.iterm2/shell-integration/it2.py is directly runnable and
reads IT2_SOCK/IT2_NONCE from the environment.

demux: two connections run independently (output routing +
per-connid cancel),
  CANCEL before HELLO tolerated, malformed HELLO -> exit 2,

  the childInfo path that makes nested-ssh routing work.

2026-07-16: it2 over ssh: SSH_IT2 conductor token and
in-process demux

Route the remote it2 CLI's requests back to the local iTerm2
over the conductor channel and dispatch them in-process
through the API server.

- SSH_IT2 token: framer emits whole "%it2 <connid>
open|data|close" OSC 134
  frames; a dedicated token (modeled on SSH_TERMINATE)
  carries each line intact to Conductor.handleIT2 without
  the SSH_OUTPUT child re-parse that would fragment a long
  payload. Plumbed through VT100Parser/Terminal, the screen
  delegates, and PTYSession, with depth routing for nested
  ssh, and exempted from the download and copy-mode receipt
  guards since it interleaves on an async stream.
- Down-path framer commands framerIT2Listen/Send/Close.  -
ConductorIT2Demux (Conductor+IT2.swift) reconstructs
it2.py's length-prefixed
  wire protocol per connection, validates the session nonce
  (read lazily), runs the embedded it2 command tree via
  iTermInProcessIt2, and streams stdout/stderr/exit back
  down. iTermInProcessIt2 gains a cancellation hook so a
  remote Ctrl-C or a conductor unhook/deinit can stop a
  streaming command (monitor --follow).
- framer bounds each %it2 data frame so a hostile client
cannot feed one giant OSC
  frame to the conductor parser; the demux caps declared
  frame length and cancels all connections on unhook/deinit.

The startup wiring that mints the nonce and sends it2listen
is not included yet, so this path stays inert (every HELLO
is rejected) until that lands.

Tests: SSH_IT2 parser (whole-line delivery, split
reassembly), framer command wire format, demux (streaming,
nonce-after-creation, cancel, teardown, oversize), and
framer chunking.

2026-07-16: it2 over ssh: framer.py unix-socket proxy for
it2

Add three dormant framer handlers so the conductor can proxy
a remote unix socket to iTerm2's embedded it2: - it2listen
<path>: asyncio.start_unix_server at <path>, chmod
owner-only.  - Each accepted connection is a byte pipe
surfaced to iTerm2 as
  "%it2 <connid> open" / "%it2 <connid> data <base64>" /
  "%it2 <connid> close" frames. framer stays a dumb pipe;
  the embedded it2 in iTerm2 reconstructs the it2.py frame
  stream, runs the command, and streams responses back.
- it2send <connid> <base64> / it2close <connid>: iTerm2 -> a
connection's socket.

Additive and inert until iTerm2 sends "it2listen", so
existing SSH integration is unaffected. Adds
tests/framer_it2_test.py driving the handlers in isolation
(listen -> client connects -> data up -> it2send down ->
close).

2026-07-16: it2 over ssh: remote it2.py client + wire
protocol

Add OtherResources/it2.py, the stdlib-only (Python 3.7+)
remote client for it2 over SSH integration. It connects to
the framer-provided unix socket ($IT2_SOCK), forwards argv +
terminal context in a HELLO frame, and streams
STDOUT/STDERR/EXIT back. Length-prefixed frames: [1B
type][u32 length][payload]. The client builds no API
request; the embedded it2 in iTerm2 runs the command, so it
stays trivially in sync with the Swift it2.

Robustness: - Missing/unreachable socket exits 2.  - EXIT is
contractually the last frame; a disconnect without it
(server crash,
  killed job, reset) exits 2 with a note rather than falsely
  reporting success.
- Two-stage SIGINT: first Ctrl-C sends CANCEL, then restores
the default handler
  so a second Ctrl-C always kills the client even if the
  server never answers.  Ctrl-C during connect/HELLO exits
  cleanly (130) instead of a traceback.

Adds tests/it2py_test.py: round-trips it2.py against a fake
framer socket (argv/ nonce up; stdout/stderr/exit down) and
a premature-close case (nonzero exit). The server thread is
a daemon with an accept() timeout so the test can never
hang.

2026-07-16: API: ObjC IT2Channel bridge to run embedded it2

2026-07-16: Link it2core into the app via an @objc facade

Consume it2core from Objective-C instead of `import it2core`
(Swift). A Swift import forces the importing target to
resolve it2core's whole transitive module graph
(ProtobufRuntime + Yams/CYaml + ArgumentParser) under
Xcode's explicit modules, which iTerm2SharedARC can't do.

- it2core gains an @objc facade:
IT2Runner.run(arguments:stdout:stderr:channel:)
  and an @objc IT2Channel protocol the app implements.
  it2core now emits its generated ObjC header
  (SWIFT_INSTALL_OBJC_HEADER=YES) as it2core-Swift.h.
- The app depends on it2core and force-loads libit2core.a
(which Xcode builds
  self-contained, embedding ArgumentParser/Yams/CYaml).
  Protobuf resolves from libiTerm2Shared.a. No Swift import
  of it2core anywhere; iTerm2SharedARC and the bridging
  header are untouched.
- Dropped the app's separate ArgumentParser/Yams product
deps: they are already
  archived into libit2core.a, so linking them again
  duplicated symbols.

Verified: full app build (tools/build.sh Development)
succeeds and links; it2core target builds for x86_64+arm64
and emits the facade header; 29 standalone tests pass (adds
IT2Runner facade tests).

2026-07-16: it2cli: use let for shortcut delegation commands
(fix warnings)

The shortcut conversions changed `let cmd` to `var cmd`
before calling `try cmd.run(ctx)`, but the concrete run(_:)
implementations are non-mutating, so `var` is never needed
and swift emits 9 "variable was never mutated; consider
changing to 'let'" warnings on a clean build. Flip all 9
back to `let`. (var is only required for the IT2Runnable
existential in runParsedCommand, not for direct concrete
calls.)

2026-07-16: it2cli: add IT2Context.exit(code) and the
IT2Exit signal

Add a proper analog of exit(2): ctx.exit(_ code: Int32)
throws an IT2Exit signal that the boundary realizes
(standalone: Foundation.exit(code); embedded: the host will
send the code back to the remote it2 over the ssh channel).
This lets a command terminate with an arbitrary code, exit 0
early, or exit without a message, and keeps commands from
calling Foundation.exit directly, which would take down
iTerm2 when embedded.  defer blocks still run on the way
out.

Use it for the alias-not-found path, restoring the original
message-less exit(3) (the config commit had temporarily made
it a targetNotFound error).

Adds a test asserting the missing-alias path exits with code
3.

2026-07-16: Release notes: AI chat auto-provide consent
prompt

2026-07-16: AI chat: prompt once for auto-provide consent
when a legacy Always is present

When a turn wants to auto-send (a category is Always) but
the user has never decided (consent Unknown), show a
one-time, non-blocking prompt - 'Share Terminal Contents
Automatically?' with Turn On / Not Now. Turn On records
Granted (auto-send begins next turn); Not Now records Denied
(never nag again; the user can still opt in per chat, which
also grants). Shown at most once per launch and skipped once
Granted/Denied persists, so it never stacks even though
autoProvidedContext re-evaluates every turn. The suppressed
turn still sends, just without the screen.

2026-07-16: AI chat: gate auto-provide on explicit consent,
not just Always

Auto-sending a session's terminal state + visible screen
with every message was driven purely by the View Contents /
Check Terminal State permission being Always. A legacy 'View
History = Always' grant is carried across the rename to
'View Contents = Always', so it reaches the Always state
without ever passing the per-chat 'Send Automatically'
confirmation - silently escalating to auto-sending the
screen on upgrade.

Add a NoSync tri-state consent (iTermAutoProvideConsent) and
gate auto-send on it: a turn auto-sends only when a category
is Always AND consent is granted.  The existing per-chat
'Send Automatically' confirmation and the phone's 'Share
This Session?' grant both record the consent, so an explicit
opt-in is never asked twice. Until consent is granted
auto-send is simply suppressed (the turn still sends,
without the screen). The one-time prompt that asks for
consent when a legacy Always grant is present follows in a
later commit.

2026-07-16: Companion: don't watch a session view that
departed before its send claimed

A send from the live session view runs an async consent
check before it claims the reply-watch slot, so the claim
can execute AFTER the view genuinely popped (the send Task
outlives the pop). claim() then set activeToken to the gone
view's token and cleared the departed set, so
beginWatchingSessionChat's isActiveOwner guard passed and
installed a watch + Mac subscription (and a reply
notification) for a view that no longer exists.

Have claim() honor an already-recorded departure of its own
token: leave activeToken unset so recordIntent bails and no
watch installs. The message still publishes; only the
reply-watch is skipped. A view that departed and then
reappeared (a tab switch un-marks it) still claims normally.

2026-07-16: Companion: distinguish an absent keychain item
from an unreadable one

pairingCompleteness() reported the pairing incomplete
whenever a required keychain item read back nil, but
keychainLoad() returned nil for ANY non-success OSStatus and
load() cached that nil for the whole process. So a single
transient read failure (a denied keychain confirmation
prompt at launch, a locked keychain, a code-signature
mismatch after a rebuild) poisoned the cache and made the
pairing look permanently incomplete: resume skipped parking
forever and the re-pair modal spammed every launch.

Make the read tri-state (found / absent / unreadable), cache
only the two stable states so a transient failure is re-read
rather than remembered, and count only a genuine
errSecItemNotFound as missing. Also drop the relay room
secret from the completeness decision: the phone re-couriers
it on every connect, so a missing one self-heals and must
not block resume.

2026-07-16: AI chat: escape control-tag delimiters in
auto-provided terminal context

autoProvidedContext() interpolated the raw visible screen
and terminal state into <visible-screen> / <terminal-state>
wrappers with no escaping.  Terminal output containing a
literal closing tag (a file the user cats, a hostile log
line) closed the block early, so everything after it read as
trusted top-level model context: prompt injection.

Neutralize our control-tag delimiters in the untrusted
content with the guillemet lookalikes AutoModeClassifier
already uses, while preserving newlines so the visible
screen's row layout survives for the model.

2026-07-16: Resolve session bindings by reference in
ChatAgent and resize

Post-rebase reconciliation: three session-reference
resolutions still used anySession(withGUID:), which cannot
match a stableID:

- CompanionHostBridge.handleResizeSession (upstream-added):
the phone now
  sends a stableID reference, so a resize from the phone
  could not find the session.
- ChatAgent.autoProvidedContext and the session-claim
precheck: both read
  the chat's terminalSessionGuid/browserSessionGuid binding,
  which is now a stableID for new links, so auto-provided
  context and the claim check silently stopped working.

Route all three through anySession(forReference:), the safe
superset.

2026-07-16: Translate old session guids to stableIDs for the
AI

Instead of migrating existing chat bindings in the database,
translate session guids to their stableIDs when replaying a
chat's history to the provider. When a chat that predates
stableIDs is continued, the model's past references to a
session (a guid) are rewritten to that session's current
stableID, so they stay consistent with the stableIDs the
<workgroups> snapshot now emits and the model copies
stableIDs forward.  No stored data changes, so nothing has
to re-key the permission grants a DB migration would have
touched.

The rewrite also turns a bare session id (one the model
wrote without the @ sigil) into a proper @-mention by
prepending @, so those render as clickable links in a
pre-stableID chat; an id already written as a mention (@,
@session:, @wg-) keeps its form.

Only free text (assistant/user prose) is rewritten, and only
for a guid that still resolves to a live session; a
workgroup id, a dead session, or a bare uuid in output is
left verbatim. Structured tool-call arguments are left
untouched because they carry vendor signatures / encrypted
reasoning-item ids that must replay verbatim, and tool
output stays a faithful copy of the terminal.

Session-bound (non-orchestration) chats intentionally do not
survive a shell reload: their binding is not sent to the AI,
and re-linking after a restart is acceptable.

2026-07-16: Release notes: reload-durable AI chat session
links

Note the stable-session-identity feature: an AI chat linked
to a terminal session (and its command permissions and
orchestrator @-mentions) now survives the shell restarts the
Claude Code integration triggers. Added to the 3.7 AI
Improvements section and the Companion bug-fix notes.

2026-07-16: Companion: group terminal alerts by stableID

The two terminal-alert posters (mark-set and trigger alerts)
passed self.guid as the threadKey, which the phone hashes
into a notification thread id. Since the guid rotates on a
shell reload, a session's alerts split into a new thread on
every reload. Pass self.stableID instead so a session's
alerts stay grouped across reloads. The phone treats
threadKey as opaque HMAC input, so this is a one-time
grouping boundary for already-delivered alerts, not a break.

2026-07-16: Companion: project sessions to the phone by
stableID

CompanionSessionLister.summary(of:) and the workgroup-member
listing emitted session.guid into the wire's
session-reference fields, which the phone sends back for
link/content/stream/copy/paste. With the bridge now
resolving those via anySession(forReference:), emit the
reload-durable stableID instead, so a session picked or
linked on the phone survives a shell reload that rotates the
guid. Screen-info/content responses already echo the
phone-supplied reference, and mention resolution already
carries the stableID revealGuid.

The wire field is still named `guid` and the phone treats it
opaquely (no shape check), so this is additive: no
protocol-version bump. Bonus: a session no longer appears to
change identity in the phone's picker when its shell
reloads.

2026-07-16: Orchestration prompt/schema: describe session
ids, not UUIDs

The <workgroups> snapshot now emits ptys_ stableIDs in the
session_guid field, so the system prompt's UUID example and
"never write just the first block" guidance were stale.
Update the example to @ptys_9QK3ZM7WX4VBT, reword the
copy-verbatim guidance to talk about an opaque id rather
than a UUID, and adjust the do-not-abbreviate examples to
the ptys_ form (copying character-for-character matters even
more now: a truncated or reformatted stableID also fails its
checksum). The tool- schema comment and the renderer header
comment drop their GUID/UUID wording too. The orchestration
prompt is not mirrored in preferences.py, so nothing to
update there.

2026-07-16: Orchestration: key session claims on the
canonical stableID

The session_* write-gating path resolved the session
tolerantly via anySession(forReference:) but then called
ensureSessionClaim with the raw model-supplied id, which
builds the scope by verbatim concatenation
("session:<raw>"). Grants are recorded canonically
("session:<stableID>" uppercase), so a
resolvable-but-non-canonical reference (a lowercased/
confusable stableID or a legacy guid from an older
transcript) would key the claim under a scope that never
matches the granted one, missing a standing grant and
re-prompting. Pass the resolved session's stableID so the
claim key is always canonical.

2026-07-16: Companion: link chats by stableID like the Mac
path

performLinkSession stored the raw phone-provided guid as the
chat binding, while ChatViewController.link stores the
resolved session's stableID. So a chat linked from the phone
was not reload-durable: after the shell reload rotated the
guid, the stored guid no longer resolved and the link went
dead. Resolve the session once and store its stableID
(falling back to the guid when unresolvable), mirroring the
Mac path.

2026-07-16: Complete chat binding migration: command-exit
observer + firstChatID scope

Two stragglers from the reference migration:

- The PTYCommandDidExit observer that drives auto-streaming
of command
  output compared the notification's live guid against the
  stored binding with raw ==. Since a new binding is now a
  stableID, that was always false and auto-streaming
  silently stopped for any chat linked after the change.
  Match the binding against the live guid's reference-key
  set, like sessionWillTerminate.

- firstChatID(forSessionGuid:) was migrated via
isLinked(toReferenceIn:),
  which also matches browser bindings; the sole caller (the
  reveal-AI-chat deep link) passes a terminal guid, so this
  silently broadened terminal-only matching to
  terminal-or-browser. Restrict it back to the terminal
  binding.

2026-07-16: Keep AI-chat draw predicates off the
session-tree walk

textViewSessionIsLinkedToAIChat and
textViewSessionIsStreamingToAIChat run on the legacy draw
path (configureIndicatorsHelperWithRightMargin, reached from
drawRect:). After the reference migration they routed
through iTermSessionReferenceKeys(forGuid:), which resolves
guid -> session -> stableID via anySession(withGUID:), a
full session- tree walk (all windows, buried sessions, peer
ports). With many panes drawing, that turned per-frame O(1)
work into O(N) per pane.

Both callers already hold the PTYSession and thus its {guid,
stableID} pair, so pass both in and match directly: -
chatIDsForSessionGuid:stableID: unions the reverse index
over the two
  keys without re-resolving.
- isStreamingToGuid:stableID: matches the binding against
the pair via a
  new direct terminalBinding(_:matchesReferenceKeys:) core
  (the resolving convenience stays for the non-hot
  stopStreaming / setSelectedText URL handlers).

2026-07-16: Companion bridge: resolve session references,
not just guids

Recent commits changed the session references the phone
receives and sends back to be stableIDs:
OrchestrationMentionRenderer now returns session.stableID as
a mention's revealGuid, and a Mac-linked chat stores its
binding as a stableID. But every CompanionHostBridge handler
still resolved with anySession(withGUID:), which cannot
match a stableID, so:

- Tapping a @session mention on the phone failed with "That
session no
  longer exists" and never started the live stream
  (contentSession, driveStream, fetchHistoryTile, selection,
  copy, paste).
- Approving a remote-command request from the phone for a
Mac-linked
  chat was refused ("The user did not link a terminal
  session") because handleRemoteCommandDecision read the
  stableID binding.

Switch all ten resolvers to anySession(forReference:), a
safe superset that resolves a guid identically and a
stableID too.

2026-07-16: Fix three missed guid==binding comparisons in
ChatWindowController

"Resolve chat session bindings by reference" migrated every
