a stableID or a legacy guid, but missed three
direct-comparison sites in ChatWindowController that
compared the stored binding to a live session guid with raw
==.  A new binding is now a stableID (ptys_...) and stableID
!= guid, so they were always false:

- isStreamingToGuid: (PTYSession
textViewSessionIsStreamingToAIChat)
  never reported streaming, so the streaming indicator never
  showed.
- setSelectionText:forSession: never offered selected text
to the chat.  - stopStreamingSession: (the URL handler)
never stopped streaming.

Route all three through a
terminalBinding(_:matchesSessionGuid:) helper that matches
via iTermSessionReferenceKeys(forGuid:), consistent with
isLinked(toSessionGuid:).

2026-07-16: MentionParser: recognize and resolve ptys_
stableID mentions

Commit 5 made the orchestrator emit stableIDs in the
<workgroups> snapshot, but the mention parser/renderer still
only understood UUIDs, so a model @mention of a standalone
session (@ptys_... or @session:ptys_...) rendered as raw
text or "[defunct session]" and clicks did nothing. This
closes that gap so the two are safe to ship together.

- MentionParser widens its pattern to also match
iTermStableSessionID's
  token, matched case-insensitively and folded to canonical
  form so a lowercased/confusable stableID still resolves
  and its identifier stays stable for claim-scope
  comparison. Mention.uuid becomes Mention.token; UUIDs
  still match verbatim.
- OrchestrationMentionRenderer resolves and reveals via
  anySession(forReference:), and reveals by the session's
  stableID, so a link keeps working after a reload.
  resolve(identifier:), used by the Companion bridge,
  inherits this.
- The compose-field mention picker inserts the session's
stableID, so a
  user-typed @mention is reload-durable too.

The Companion bridge's own session-open path (which consumes
revealGuid) is made reference-aware in the Companion commit;
land these together.

2026-07-16: Orchestration: emit and resolve the stableID,
not the guid

The <workgroups> snapshot's session_guid field and the
synthetic "session:<...>" claim scope now carry the
reload-durable stableID instead of the rotating guid. The
model copies session_guid verbatim into its tool calls, so a
copied reference (and a claim granted against a
"session:<stableID>" scope) now survives a shell reload that
would previously have invalidated the copied guid.

- WorkgroupIntrospection.sessionSummary emits
session.stableID;
  syntheticWorkgroupID builds "session:<stableID>".
- The shared resolvers
(WorkgroupIntrospection.session(forReference:),
  OrchestratorDispatcher.sessionByGUID, and the session_*
  tool dispatch) go through anySessionForReference:, so a
  model-provided reference resolves whether it is a stableID
  (new) or a legacy guid the model echoed from an older
  transcript. This also lets a watcher, which persists the
  model-provided reference, survive a reload.
- Claim consistency is preserved: applyGating derives the
required scope
  via context (now "session:<stableID>"), and
  ensureSessionClaim and session: mentions build the same
  scope from the stableID the model copied, so grant and
  requirement still match.

Live-session liveness checks (by a session's own guid) and
the @mention parser/renderer are intentionally untouched;
the latter is the next commit.

2026-07-16: Resolve chat session bindings by reference,
store stableID on link

A chat's terminal/browser binding was the session guid,
which rotates on every shell reload
(replaceTerminatedShellWithNewInstance), so a linked Claude
Code chat lost its session on every reload. Now the binding
is a "session reference" that is either the reload-durable
stableID or a legacy guid, and the whole resolve/match path
handles both.

- Linking stores the session's stableID (falling back to the
guid if
  unresolvable). Because RemoteCommandExecutor keys
  permission grants by the same reference, the grants now
  survive a reload too; previously they were keyed by the
  rotating guid and lost on every reload.
- Session resolution goes through anySessionForReference: (a
safe
  superset of anySessionWithGUID:: a guid resolves
  identically, a ptys_ stableID resolves across reloads).
  Mechanical at the chat resolve sites.
- Session-to-chat matching (isLinked, the reverse index, and
the
  firstIndex/lastChat/mostRecentChat/firstChatID lookups) is
  now reference-aware: iTermSessionReferenceKeys(forGuid:)
  resolves a live session's guid to {guid, stableID} and
  matching checks both, so a chat stored under either form
  is found.
- sessionWillTerminate matches on the terminating session's
stableID or
  guid, so a real terminate still unlinks.

Scope: new links get full reload durability. Backfilling an
existing guid binding to its stableID is deferred; it would
re-key the chat's existing permission grant, which deserves
its own change. Old bindings resolve as before (fine until a
reload, no regression).

2026-07-16: Serialize PTYSession.stableID into the
arrangement

Without this, an arrangement restore (app relaunch, system
window restoration) built a fresh PTYSession that minted a
brand-new stableID while restoring the old guid, so a
chat/companion binding stored against the stableID would
silently fail to resolve after a restart - strictly worse
than the guid it is meant to supersede.

Encode _stableID under SESSION_ARRANGEMENT_STABLE_ID next to
the guid, and in sessionFromArrangement: adopt the saved
value only where the saved guid is adopted (registered
contents, startup, or system-restoration contents present).
Mirroring the guid guards is what keeps a duplicate unique:
Duplicate Tab/Session restores a still-live session's
arrangement, none of these guards hold, and the new session
keeps its freshly-minted, unique stableID instead of
colliding with the source.  Older arrangements without the
key, or a corrupted value, fall back to the freshly-minted
id. Extraction goes through a new +stableIDInArrangement:
(mirrors +guidInArrangement:) that type-checks with
castFrom: before the ObjC->Swift bridge and canonicalizes.

stableID is now durable across both shell reload and app
restart, so the "survives state restoration" contract in the
header comments now holds.

2026-07-16: Mint PTYSession.stableID and add reference-based
lookup

Every PTYSession now mints a StableSessionID at init,
alongside its guid.  Unlike guid,
replaceTerminatedShellWithNewInstance (the reload path)
leaves it untouched, so it is a durable handle on a session
across shell restarts. It is released in dealloc and, being
immutable, needs none of setGuid:'s re-keying.

Adds two controller lookups: anySessionWithStableID:
(mirrors anySessionWithGUID: but matches the canonicalized
stableID across the same tab/buried/peer-port locations) and
anySessionForReference:, which dispatches on the form so a
stored binding that is either a "ptys_" stableID or a legacy
guid resolves through one call. Both guard nil (canonical:
bridges to a nonnull NSString and would trap otherwise), so
they stay as nil-tolerant as anySessionWithGUID:.

Scope note: this delivers reload durability only.
Serializing stableID into the arrangement (state-restoration
durability) is the next commit; comments here are limited to
what this commit actually provides.

2026-07-16: Add StableSessionID: a reload-stable session
identifier

PTYSession.guid rotates on every in-place shell restart
(replaceTerminatedShellWithNewInstance), which the Claude
Code integration triggers constantly, breaking anything that
binds to a session by guid. This adds the identifier that
will replace guid as the binding key: "ptys_" + 12 Crockford
base32 chars + 1 checksum char, e.g.  ptys_9QK3ZM7WX4VBT.

The prefix makes it greppable and unmistakable for a UUID,
hex hash, or base64 blob. The checksum rejects a truncated
or mangled id (which an AI agent is prone to produce when
echoing one) instead of resolving it to the wrong session.
canonical() folds case (prefix and body) and Crockford
confusables so a lightly mangled id still resolves;
detection (tokenPattern) stays strict canonical to keep
free-text false positives low. charClass/tokenPattern derive
from the prefix/alphabet constants so they cannot drift.

Shared into the Companion iOS app so MentionParser can
reference it there: added to SHARED_MAC_SOURCES and to the
committed companion project. Inert for now: nothing mints or
consumes it yet.

2026-07-16: AI chat: decouple turn lifecycle from typing
status

Introduce an explicit turnLifecycle wire event (turnStarted
/ turnEnded) so the phone drives its reply notification off
real turn boundaries instead of inferring them from
typing-status edges. Typing becomes a pure spinner hint, so
a mid-turn park (which toggles typing) can no longer be
misread as a turn boundary and corrupt or drop the reply
notification. On subscribe, a phone that joins a turn
mid-flight is seeded turnStarted from TurnStatusModel (which
stays true across a park) so it still notifies.
CompanionProtocolVersion is at rev 8
(turnLifecycleRevision): a rev-8 peer consumes
turnLifecycle, while a pre-8 peer keeps inferring boundaries
from the typing edges the mac still emits. Both send gates
route through one peerConsumesTurnLifecycle predicate (both
ends at rev 8) and the phone's source gate is its exact
complement, so the trigger is fed by exactly one source in
every version pairing.

Centralize the park/resume typing bracket. Every park site
cleared the spinner and republished it on resume with its
own bookkeeping; extract one emitter pair, typingParkOnUser
/ typingResumeFromPark, backed by a ParkedTypingCounter, so
a new park site gets correct behavior for free.  The
ref-count also fixes concurrent approval parks (spinner off
once on the first park, back on once when the last resolves)
and is reset in cancelPendingCommands, the one choke point
that abnormally resolves every parked completion, so it
can't leak at stop(), transitionToOrchestration(), or
fetchCompletionForRegularMessage().

Adapt the live harness: the queue-discipline tests inferred
a turn's end from a typing=false edge, which now fires
mid-turn on a park. Key the BrokerEventRecorder and all four
test_chat_* queue tests off the authoritative
turnLifecycle(.started/.ended) boundary instead.

Also fixes issues reachable in the shipping build, found
along the way: - share the approval-park predicate
(Permission.parksOnApproval) between
  the real gate (ChatClient.processRemoteCommandRequest) and
  the prediction (ChatAgent) so an always+unsafe or
  browser-category command can't mispredict and strand the
  phone spinner
- widen the phone's approval mapping so any parked classic
command (not
  just a content-unsafe one), and the orchestration-enable
  request, surface as an action request rather than a
  premature or missing reply
- suppress the Mac chat-view scroll-to-bottom on a
turn-lifecycle boundary - close the turn via completion(nil)
for a user-authored renameChat/append
  so it cannot wedge the queue or strand the turn status

Release notes: the "View History" -> "View Contents"
permission rename and its automatic screen-contents
provision (docs/notes-3.7.txt), and the "Share This Session
with the AI?" consent prompt plus the more reliable
reply/approval notifications (Companion/docs/notes.txt).

2026-07-16: Companion: prompt to include terminal contents
before sending from a session

Step 3b. When sending from a session view, if the mac
supports it and consent isn't already in effect for the
target chat, block the send on a modal asking to include
this session's terminal state and visible screen with AI
messages. On Allow, the phone grants "provided
automatically" for the resolved chat before publishing, so
that first message already carries the state + screen (via
the server-side injection); on Not Now it sends plain and
remembers the decline for this session this run. Gated on
the mac advertising autoProvideConsentRevision.

2026-07-16: Companion: add the auto-provide consent protocol
and mac handlers

Step 3a of the phone consent flow. Add companion messages so
the phone can ask, before sending, to include a session's
terminal state and visible screen with AI messages:
fetchAutoProvideConsent(sessionGuid) ->
autoProvideConsent(satisfied), and
grantAutoProvideConsent(chatID). The mac reports whether the
chat it would send to (the session's most recent chat, else
the global default) already has both Check Terminal State
and View Contents at "provided automatically", and the grant
sets both to .always on the resolved session-bound chat.

Behind protocol revision 7 (additive; an older mac ignores
the unknown messages, so the phone offers the prompt only
when the mac advertises autoProvideConsentRevision).  The
phone modal is step 3b.

2026-07-16: AI chat: steer execute_command away from TUIs
toward insert_text_at_cursor

execute_command blocks until the command exits, so running
an interactive or full-screen program (vim, less, top,
claude, an ssh session, a REPL) with it never returns. The
description didn't say so, and the agent used it to try to
drive a running TUI. Spell out that execute_command is for
commands that finish, and that insert_text_at_cursor is the
way to launch or drive a TUI (type the command plus a
newline, choose a menu option, send keystrokes) since it
does not wait for the program to finish.

2026-07-16: AI chat: clear typing status when a turn parks
on user approval

A turn that parks waiting for the user never emitted
typing(false) while blocked, which left two bugs: the
phone's typing indicator stuck on at the prompt, and,
because SessionReplyTrigger fires the accumulated reply text
on typing(false), the reply notification suppressed entirely
so the agent looked unresponsive. This happened on both
kinds of mid-turn park:

- request_orchestration_enable: the agent replies then asks
the user to
  Enable/Not Now, and the turn parks until they answer.
- a session-bound remote-command request that parks in
pendingRemoteCommands
  waiting for Allow/Deny.

Publish typing(false) when the turn parks on user input and
typing(true) when the response resumes it (which also lets
the post-response continuation notify as its own turn). For
remote commands, do this only when the command actually
blocks on approval (its category permission is "ask"),
recorded on the parked command as clearedTyping;
auto-approved (.always) and auto-denied (.never) commands
resolve without the user mid-turn, so they leave typing
alone.  Re-emitting typing(true) for an auto-executed tool
call would make the phone read the resume as a new-turn
boundary (it resets on every typing(true)) and drop the
turn's accumulated reply text before it notifies.

2026-07-16: Companion: don't stack two re-pair modals at
launch

promptToRepairAfterRelayMoveIfNeeded and
promptToRepairIfPairingIncompleteIfNeeded have independent,
non-exclusive triggers (a recorded relay-origin change vs a
missing/denied keychain credential), so a machine that hit
both a relay move and a keychain denial (a reinstall/rebuild
around a relay migration) showed two modal alerts in a row,
both asking to re-pair. Have the relay-move prompt yield
when the pairing is already incomplete, since that prompt
already asks to re-pair and re-pairing subsumes the
relay-move fix.

2026-07-16: AI chat: log the auto-provide gate decision

The auto-provide only fires when the chat's Check Terminal
State / View Contents permission is at "provided
automatically" (.always). Log the resolved permission values
(and a missing linked session) so it is clear why a
session-bound turn did or did not carry terminal state / the
visible screen.

2026-07-16: AI chat: auto-provide terminal state and visible
screen server-side

Step 2. Move the "provided automatically" injection into
ChatAgent, so a session-bound chat's turn carries the linked
session's terminal state (when Check Terminal State is set
to always) and its current visible screen (when View
Contents is set to always) regardless of whether the message
came from the desktop or the phone. Read at request time so
the state/screen are fresh; an unchanged visible screen
collapses to a <visible-screen unchanged="true"/> marker
instead of resending the grid. Only session-bound chats are
affected; orchestrator chats keep their own workgroups
snapshot path.

The desktop compose-time terminal-state injection is removed
so it is not doubled. Nothing fires until a chat actually
has the matching permission at "always" (the per-chat
consent flow is step 3).

2026-07-16: AI chat: keep View Contents on-request tools
when provided automatically

Step 1b. "Provided automatically" was hiding the on-request
tools for every autopopulated category, but that only makes
sense for Check Terminal State, whose autopopulated state
fully replaces its state queries. For View Contents the
autopopulated visible screen covers only the current grid,
while the history tools (command history, an earlier
command's full output, the partially-typed command) reach
off-screen content, so hiding them would lose access to
scrollback and history on a primary-screen shell.

Split the concept: autopopulatedWhenAlways still governs
whether content is sent unbidden, and a new
suppressesOnRequestToolsWhenAlways (true only for Check
Terminal State) governs whether the tools are hidden. So
View Contents at "always" now sends the screen automatically
AND keeps its tools available.

2026-07-16: AI chat: rename the View History permission to
View Contents

Step 1 of giving session-bound chats terminal state + screen
contents. Rename the "View History" AI permission category
to "View Contents" and give it a "provide automatically"
mode (autopopulatedWhenAlways), so its top level means the
visible screen is sent unbidden on every message (like Check
Terminal State does for terminal state) rather than exposing
the history tools on request. Add its autopopulation title
and consent warning.

The rawValue doubles as the persisted per-chat permission
key and the whole [Key: Permission] blob fails to decode if
any category is unknown, so a custom decoder maps the legacy
"View History" string to .viewContents to preserve existing
grants. The underlying userDefaults key is left unchanged so
global settings are untouched.

No injection yet (that is step 2), and no per-chat
re-consent yet (step 3).

2026-07-16: Companion: detect a half-paired mac and prompt
to re-pair

A mac can be marked paired (pairedPID in UserDefaults) while
the relay room secret (login keychain, gated by the binary's
code signature) is unreadable after a rebuild/reinstall or a
-suite mismatch. The mac then parks unsigned and the relay
refuses every attempt with "signature required", looping
forever.

Add pairingCompleteness(): with a pairedPID set, verify the
identity key, the pinned phone key, and (on the relay) the
room secret are all present. A launch prompt tells the user
to re-pair and opens settings when the set is incomplete,
and the park loop no longer spins on a pairing that can
never authenticate.  Log the keychain read status (absent vs
access-denied) and whether the park is signed or open-mode,
so this is diagnosable rather than an opaque "signature
required".

2026-07-16: Companion: fix live session view nav bar title

On the live path the view fills behind the translucent nav
bar with black, so the default label-colored title rendered
dark-on-dark and was invisible in light mode (only a colored
emoji in the title showed through, reading as "truncated to
the emoji"). Force the bar content light there. Render the
title as a principal item with maxWidth:.infinity so it uses
the width between the bar-button groups instead of the
system title's symmetric reservation, and re-key it on the
view width so it re-measures on rotation instead of keeping
the prior orientation's width.

2026-07-16: Companion: instrument relay sockets and stream

Chasing reliable ~30s relay-park drops and a live view that

RelaySocketLifecycle, so a close reports how long it was

2026-07-16: Companion: add phone-driven session resize for
legibility

Add a resize button to the Companion app's live session view
that fits the Mac terminal grid to the phone: 40 columns in
portrait, 80 in landscape, and however many rows fill the
viewport at that font. The phone computes the grid from the
stream's cell geometry and its viewport and sends a new
resizeSession message; the Mac drives -[PTYSession
reallySetCellSize:], re-validating server-side that the
window is actually resizable (not full screen, maximized,
edge-attached, or width-locked) via
-companionSessionCanResizeWindow.

Whether the window can be resized rides the stream config
(canResize), detected in the streamer's pre-dedup path so an
idle-screen change still propagates, and disables the
button. The control is gated on a new protocol revision
(sessionResizeRevision) so an older Mac, which would ignore
the message, never shows it.

2026-07-16: Release notes: coalesced companion push
notifications

2026-07-16: Route companion wakeup pushes through the
coordinator

Wire the coordinator into the agent-activity notifier, the
alert bridge, and the host bridge's syncSince responder
(revision >= 2 phones; legacy per-chat pushes are
unchanged).

The notifier classifies a delivery as content (a completed
turn, or a
.classic permission / session request - all renderable) or nothing.
.external orchestration tool calls (scroll_wheel, get_screen_contents;
auto-executed, never blocking) are ignored: firing for them
flooded pushes and, since they do not render, drained to
empty placeholders. The per-chat debounce is gone -
coalescing is the coordinator's job, and the debounce was
stamped by that .external stream, starving real replies.
Deliveries log their content kind so "why did (or didn't)
this notify" is a one-liner. Alerts (which render) go
through the content path; handleSyncSince reports the
fetched floors and the rewind reset so the render check
reasons about the phone's real floor.

2026-07-16: Add CompanionWakeupCoordinator: the global
wakeup-push gate

One place decides WHEN to send a contentless-wakeup push. A
wakeup tells the phone's NSE to fetch everything new in one
syncSince round trip, so more than one in flight is waste
and a wakeup for non-renderable content produces the empty
"Your agent has an update." placeholder.

Stateless about DB content: "is there anything to show" is
answered on demand by hasRenderableContentSince (the
responder's own predicate over the current store), so a
deleted / rewound / cleared store needs no reconciliation -
the next check reads the truth. The only long-lived state is
the phone's acked FLOOR (message + alert), which the phone
reports via syncSince (advanced on a fetch; assigned down on
the responder's rewind signal), plus rate-limit state.
Everything worth notifying - replies, alerts, pending
permission prompts - is renderable content, so there is ONE
outstanding condition and no special-casing.

A wakeup fires when there is renderable content above the
floor AND the coalesce interval allows - a hard invariant:
the first fires immediately, later ones collapse into one
trailing wakeup. No "fetched since last push" override (it
defeated the interval; the NSE fetches within ~1s of every
push). Every decision RLogs renderable/floors/action/reason
so a field log explains each push without a DB query. Pure
and unit-tested; legacy revision-1 per-chat pushes do not
route through here.

2026-07-16: Companion: one render predicate (incl. pending
permission prompts) + a probe

Extract Message.isCompanionRenderable - the single
definition of "the NSE would show this as a notification
item" - and route MessagesSinceResponder.summarize through
it. Renderable = agent-authored, not hidden, with
displayable substance; that now includes a .classic remote
command and a session pick (both BLOCK the agent on the
user, so they are critical to show), while a .external
orchestration tool call stays non-renderable (auto-executed,
informational only). A .classic request renders from its
permissionDescription ("The AI Agent would like to execute
`git push`") so the phone says what decision is blocked.

But only while it is a LIVE prompt. ChatClient's broker
processor squelches auto-run (.always) and auto-denied
(.never) requests from live delivery, yet still persists
them for history; the DB-reading companion paths bypass that
processor, so a request whose uniqueID already appears as
the answeredID of a remoteCommandResponse in the window is
resolved bookkeeping and is dropped. Only unanswered .ask
prompts surface; once you Approve/Deny, the response
resolves it.

Add ChatDatabase.hasRenderableContentSince, which answers
"would the next syncSince return anything renderable above
these floors" by reusing that same predicate and
answered-request gate over the same oldest-first window the
responder drains, plus the muted-chat set - the one source
of truth the coordinator uses, so it can never push for
content that drains to an empty placeholder.

2026-07-16: Add the companion wakeup coalesce-interval
advanced setting

The contentless-wakeup push coordinator rate-limits how
often the Mac nudges a paired phone. Expose the minimum
interval between wakeups as an Experimental advanced setting
(companionWakeupCoalesceInterval, default 5s) so it can be
tuned in the field.

2026-07-15: Companion: repair the project generator to emit
all three targets

generate_companion_project.rb had drifted far behind the
hand-maintained iTerm2Companion.xcodeproj. Re-running it
(the documented "re-run after adding source files" workflow)
produced a project that failed to build: it referenced a
nonexistent single iTerm2Companion.entitlements, and it
recreated only the app target, silently dropping the
PushService notification-service extension, the
iTerm2CompanionTests bundle, the WhisperKit package
dependency, and 9 of the 24 shared Mac sources.

Rewrite the generator to reproduce the current project
faithfully: the app plus the embedded PushService
app-extension and the hosted test bundle, the WhisperKit
remote package, per-config Debug/Release entitlements
(development vs production APNs), the full shared-source
list, and a scheme wired for build/run/test. Verified by
regenerating from scratch: clean device build embeds and
validates PushService, and all 28 unit tests pass on an iOS
26 simulator.

Also document in CLAUDE.md that this project is generated
and that structural changes made in Xcode must be mirrored
into the generator, so it cannot drift like this again.

2026-07-15: Write release notes for 3.7.0beta7

2026-07-15: Tab titles: ignore leading spinner glyph in
smart truncation

Smart tab-title truncation decides head-vs-tail for the
whole tab bar by comparing how many tabs share a common
5-character prefix vs suffix.  Claude Code session titles
begin with an animated spinner glyph (a braille or dingbat
symbol such as the star that ticks about once a second)
whose animation phase differs per tab. That glyph was the
first character of the prefix, so when two otherwise-similar
tabs' spinners fell out of phase the unique-prefix count
crossed the decision boundary and flipped the entire bar
between head and tail truncation, roughly once a second.

Skip a leading run of decoration (whitespace, symbols, and
punctuation, which covers the braille and dingbat spinner
glyphs) before forming the prefix so the heuristic keys off
the stable text. Enumerate by composed character sequence so
a multi-scalar glyph is handled as a unit, and fall back to
the raw prefix when a title is entirely decoration.

2026-07-15: Diff peer: restore terminal focus after deferred
launch

On the first visit to a .diff peer, the peer swap parks the
window's first responder on the diff-waiting overlay's Run
Anyway button, because -mainResponder routes there while the
overlay is up. firePendingDiffLaunch then dismisses the
overlay with removeFromSuperview() and starts the command,
but nothing reassigned first responder, so the window was
left with a dead first responder and typing beeped.

Capture whether the overlay owns first responder before
dismissing it, and if so hand focus back to mainResponder
(now the freshly-running text view) once the overlay is gone
and the command has launched. Guarding on ownership leaves
first responder alone when a poller-driven launch fires on a
peer the user is not looking at, and also fixes the same
beep when Run Anyway is clicked manually.

2026-07-14: Tweak some logging levels

2026-07-14: Load shell integration for a remote zsh login
shell over SSH

For a plain interactive SSH-integration session we named the
remote shell as modifiedCommandArgs[0], so framerLogin sent
args=["/usr/bin/zsh"]. The framer wraps non-empty login args
in `guess_login_shell -c "<shell>"`; when the login shell is
itself zsh that runs `zsh -c "/usr/bin/zsh"`. The outer
non-interactive zsh reads the injected .zshenv, which unsets
ZDOTDIR and skips its interactive-only shell-integration
load, so the inner interactive zsh starts with ZDOTDIR gone
and never loads integration. (bash survives the same double
shell because its $ENV loader is read by any interactive
POSIX shell and the outer zsh does not touch ENV.)

Only name the shell when the injection actually modified
argv (bash's --posix). For env-only injection
(zsh/fish/xonsh) on a plain interactive session, leave
modifiedCommandArgs empty so the framer execs the login
shell interactively with the injected environment (e.g.
ZDOTDIR) in place.

2026-07-14: Inject shell integration into a remote /bin/bash
over SSH

The Shell.init?(path:) guard refused any /bin/bash so we
would not try to inject into macOS's un-injectable 3.2.57.
That path-based check is only correct for the local Darwin
host: on a remote host /bin/bash is normally a modern
injectable bash. Conductor.shellSupportsInjection already
vets the remote bash by version string, but createInjector
then returned nil for /bin/bash, so
modifyRemoteShellEnvironment left env/argv unmodified and
the staged shell-integration files were never sourced.

Thread an allowSystemBash flag through
createInjector/Shell.init and set it from
modifyRemoteShellEnvironment (the remote path) so a remote
/bin/bash gets injected. The local modifyShellEnvironment
path keeps the default false, so macOS's /bin/bash stays
excluded.

2026-07-13: Tab subtitle: let the focused pane's status win

When a tab has multiple split panes,
updateAggregatedTabStatus picked the highest-priority status
across all panes, with the focused pane only breaking ties.
A background pane with a higher-priority status (for example
an idle pane when idle outranks working in the user's
priority list) would override the working pane the user was
looking at.

Now the focused pane (self.activeSession) drives the
subtitle whenever it has a status, falling back to the
highest-priority sibling pane only when the focused pane has
none.

2026-07-13: Companion: reuse the session tree in the
new-chat picker

The picker for a new session-bound chat rendered a flat
ForEach(model.sessions) with its own row, so the list looked
disorganized next to the Sessions tab and the @-mention
picker, which both render the window > tab > pane > peer
hierarchy via SessionTreeBrowser. Reuse that shared
component here so the list reads identically everywhere, and
add a per-row eye button that previews the session read-only
before committing, like the mention picker.

Present the preview in its own NavigationStack inside a
sheet rather than pushing onto the app's shared, path-bound
NavigationStack. CreateView is itself a pushed entry on that
stack, so a second navigationDestination(item:) push would
compete with async navigationPath mutations that can land
while the preview is up (a reply-notification tap appending
.conversation, or openConversation(replacingPath:)), risking a
botched transition or a resurrected preview. The sheet
sidesteps the shared stack entirely, matching how the
mention picker isolates its own preview.

2026-07-13: Log the multiserver daemon's exit status when it
dies

iTerm2 is the server's parent (single fork, deliberately not
daemonized), so it reaps the zombie in the
DISPATCH_PROC_EXIT handler. That handler discarded the wait
status, so when a shared daemon died and took every session
on its connection down with it, the retrospective log showed
only the downstream socket EOF with no indication of why the
server went away.

Decode and RLog the wait status (terminating signal, exit
code, or a failed reap) so a recurrence is diagnosable from
the log alone. Capture serverPID and a socketPath copy into
locals so the block doesn't retain self.

2026-07-13: Fix ModernTests build under AddressSanitizer and
-Werror

Two pre-existing breakages kept the ModernTests target from
building:

- iTermImage.m: under AddressSanitizer,
DECODE_IMAGES_IN_PROCESS is 1 and a
  #warning fired. Turning on treat-warnings-as-errors for
  iTerm2SharedARC promoted it to a hard error, but that
  change was only validated for the non-ASan
  Development/Deployment builds, so the ASan test build
  broke.  Replace the #warning with a #pragma message: the
  note still surfaces in the build log (as an informational
  -W#pragma-messages diagnostic) but is not promoted by
  -Werror.

- WorkgroupAutoSendClippingsTests: one clippingsToAutoSend
call omitted the
  required mainSessionState: argument that the function
  gained, so the whole test target failed to compile. Pass
  .idle, which isolates the working -> unknown edge as the
  reason the call returns nil (the edge guard short-circuits
  before the main-session-state gate), matching the test's
  intent.

2026-07-13: Derive ancestry names and pids from one walk so
they stay aligned

foregroundJobAncestorChainPids (used only by the ancestry
diagnostics) mirrored an older version of the
foregroundJobAncestorNames walk: it was missing the
last-known-title cache branch and the cached-title
login/server boundary break. So when an intermediate
ancestor's fresh name read empty but survived via the cache,
it appeared in the names array but not in the pids array,
shifting every entry above it. The diagnostic then
misattributed the vanished ancestor to the wrong pid (or
lost it) - precisely the transient name-failure case the
diagnostics exist to investigate.

Extract the single canonical traversal into
foregroundJobAncestorChain(), which emits (pid, lowercased
title) pairs, and derive both foregroundJobAncestorNames and
foregroundJobAncestorChainPids from it.  Alignment is now
guaranteed by construction rather than by two loops kept in
sync by hand.

Adds a regression test that warms the name cache, rebuilds
with an intermediate name reading empty, and asserts the
names and chain pids stay index-aligned (the pre-fix code
dropped the cache-recovered pid).

2026-07-13: Add gated foreground-job ancestry diagnostics

Behind the new logForegroundJobAncestryDiagnostics advanced
setting (off by default, SECTION_DEBUGGING), the process
cache logs a detailed trace whenever a tracked session's
foreground-job ancestor list shrinks: which pid held each
vanished ancestor, whether it is still in the process table
and alive (kill(pid,0)), and the full upward walk from the
deepest foreground job (pid/ppid, parent pointer vs the
collection's own ppid lookup, names, and the reason the walk
stopped).

This makes a spurious job-ended event self-explanatory in a
retrospective log (an intermediate ancestor like the claude
CLI dropping out for a single process-cache update, which
can tear down a Claude Code workgroup). The chain pids that
back it are only captured while the setting is on, so there
is no per-cycle cost in normal use.

2026-07-13: Retain foreground-job ancestors via strong
parent links

iTermProcessInfo.parent was weak, but callers hold a
deepest-job iTermProcessInfo past the lifetime of the
ProcessCollection that built it: PTYSession keeps one in
_lastProcessInfo, and the process cache swaps in a fresh
collection on its work queue. The ancestor infos are owned
only by that collection, so once it was freed the weak
parent chain dangled to nil and foregroundJobAncestorNames
collapsed to just the deepest job. To GlobalJobMonitor a
missing ancestor is indistinguishable from an exited one, so
it fired a spurious job-ended and tore down the claudeCode
workgroup even though the intermediate process (e.g. claude)
never exited.

Make parent strong so a retained leaf keeps its ancestor
spine alive. There is no retain cycle: a parent references
its children only as pids (childProcessIDs), resolved
through the weak collection, so there is no parent -> child
strong edge.

Adds ForegroundJobAncestryTests reproducing the collapse
(deepest job read after its collection is freed) and a
contrast test showing a dropped node in a live collection
over-collapses instead, ruling out the naive theory.

2026-07-13: Treat warnings as errors for iTerm2SharedARC and
iTerm2Shared

Turn on GCC_TREAT_WARNINGS_AS_ERRORS and
SWIFT_TREAT_WARNINGS_AS_ERRORS for all four configurations
of both shared targets, then fix the pre-existing warnings
this surfaced. The fixes were validated with clean -Werror
builds of Development and Deployment plus a warnings
inventory of Beta (Nightly matches Beta):

- ResponsesAPIRequest: var -> let for a never-mutated local
- iTermWorkgroupInstance: discard the unused
activate(identifier:) result - iTermProcessInfo: explicit
fallback for optional-string interpolation -
WorkgroupIntrospection: nonisolated shellJobNames so the
nonisolated
  accessor can read it
- iTermApplicationDelegate/iTermOnboardingWindowController:
declare
  makeScreenshot: so the selector is no longer undeclared
- LineBuffer: mark the genuinely optional out-params
_Nullable - ProfilesTextPreferencesViewController: adopt
NSMenuItemValidation to
  match the non-deprecated protocol method
- iTermScreenshotOnDemandPreview: drop an unused guard
binding - iTermScreenshotPanel: remove the deprecated no-op
copiesOnScroll - iTermMetalView: remove the now-redundant
CAMetalLayer Sendable
  conformance (the SDK inherits it from the superclass)
- iTermApplication / iTermTexturePageCollection: contain
debug-only
  locals so they are not set-but-unused in release builds

2026-07-12: Throttle Companion history-tile requests to
avoid flooding the relay

A fast fling through scrollback fired hundreds of tile
requests at once, tripping the relay's per-connection
frame-rate limit and dropping the bridge to the Mac while
the phone still looked connected.

Cap in-flight tile fetches (6) with a bounded pending queue
and drive re-requests from a slot-available signal paced by
replies rather than a self-scheduled refresh, so a wide
viewport re-requests without busy looping. Scope the
throttle to a per-stream epoch so a session switch,
reconnect, resume, host stream-end, or reflow neutralizes
stale in-flight/queued requests (cancelling their fetches)
instead of leaking slots, wedging the pipeline, or caching
one session's tiles under another. Give each fetch a bounded
deadline so a lost reply cannot pin a slot forever, and
recover flushed tiles via a 4 Hz growthTick pass that runs
regardless of zoom. Distinguish throttle drops from real
failures so a transient drop keeps its image instead of
flashing a failure X, and skip host-reported failures in the
idle re-drive to avoid strobing.

2026-07-12: Companion: stop nagging a working pairing to
re-pair

The mac records which relay hosts a pairing was established
against so a later host move can prompt a re-pair.
recordCurrentRelays ran only on a fresh pairing, so a device
paired before this tracking (or before the relays moved) had
nil recorded origins, and relayConfigurationChanged read nil
!= current as "moved" and nagged to re-pair on every launch
even though the pairing worked fine.

Treat a nil (never-recorded) origin as "unknown, not moved",
and on each successful reconnect refresh the recorded MAIN
relay, which the reconnect genuinely proves (the connection
is carried over it) and which backfills an older pairing so
a real main-relay move is still caught.

Record the push relay only at fresh pairing, never on
reconnect: a reconnect proves nothing about which push relay
the phone's APNs token is registered against. The phone
registers against its own build's push-relay URL, which
after a mac-only upgrade differs from ours until the phone
app is itself updated. Stamping our push URL on reconnect
would falsely satisfy relayConfigurationChanged and suppress
a legitimate re-pair prompt while the phone still cannot
receive pushes.

2026-07-12: Companion: back off + show UI when the relay
hits its daily quota

The relay closes a room with WebSocket 1008 "daily quota
exceeded" once it hits its per-room daily byte quota. The
mac and phone treated that like ordinary churn and
reconnected on their routine fast timers (mac ~5s, phone
~2s), which just re-tripped the same limit all day and left
the mac silently unreachable until relaunched.

Surface the close distinctly as TransportError.quotaExceeded
(matched on the reason text, since 1008 is also used for the
transient frame-rate limiter) and preserve it through
RelayTransport and the session/bridge close handlers. Both
sides now back off 30 minutes instead of fast retrying, and
show why: the mac's Companion settings shows "Daily relay
data limit reached", and the phone shows a banner with a
Reconnect now button that overrides the backoff.

No relay change: it already sends the 1008 close.

2026-07-11: Auto-request/auto-send: switch peer switcher
without stealing focus

The workgroup auto-request-review and auto-send-clippings
paths called PTYSession.reveal(), which orders the window
front, activates the app, and selects the containing tab in
addition to swapping the peer view in.  Only the
peer-switcher swap was wanted: focus should follow the
active side within the shared pane without yanking the
user's window, tab, or app focus around.

Add -revealAsPeerWithoutActivatingWindow, the peer-port swap
portion of -reveal and nothing else (the same
activate(identifier:) that keyboard peer-switch shortcuts
use), guarded on activeSession != self so it is a no-op when
already active. Call it from both auto paths in place of
reveal().

2026-07-11: Add Auto-Request Review When Idle toggle for the
main session

Companion to the code-review peer's Auto-Send Clippings
toggle, in the other direction: a toolbar toggle on the
workgroup's main session that requests a code review from
the review session each time the main session transitions
working -> idle.

- New .autoRequestReviewWhenIdle toolbar item on the main
session
  (checkmark.seal when off, checkmark.seal.fill when on),
  mirroring the auto-send toggle's borderless outline/filled
  styling so state reads the same across window-active
  changes. Runtime per-session flag, defaults off, saved and
  restored with the session.
- The peer port detects the main session's working -> idle
edge and, if
  the workgroup has exactly one code-review session, asks
  the workgroup instance to run a review on it. The
  exactly-one guard keeps the target unambiguous; the
  decision is factored into the pure static
  shouldAutoRequestReview for testing.
- Included by default on the Claude Code workgroup's main
session via
  the shared preset builder.
- Reveal behavior: when review results are auto-sent to the
main
  session it is brought forward, and when a review is
  auto-requested the review session is revealed, so focus
  follows the active side.
- Loop break: auto-sent clippings are archived (live list
cleared) on
  the leader, so a subsequent review-idle finds nothing to
  resend and the auto-request/auto-send pair cannot
  ping-pong.

Rebased onto master: the auto-send path already on master
(the main session working-guard and the serialized paste +
bracketing-disabled Return submit that fixed the
large-review race) is kept; this change layers the
request-review direction, leader-side clipping reads, and
the reveal/loop-break behavior on top.

2026-07-11: Downgrade several noisy/expensive RLog calls to
DLog

These RLog sites run on hot or high-frequency paths, or
capture a stack trace, which RLog is not meant to do (it
runs even with debug logging off). Move them to DLog so they
cost nothing in the field:

- iTermApplication: per-event mouse and modifier-remap
logging.  - PseudoTerminal: window-create, tmux resize, and
set-title logging,
  including callStackSymbols captures.
- iTermProcessCache: per-update controlling-tty derivation.
- iTermNSKeyBindingEmulator: full key-binding dictionary
dump on load.

Also echo RLog entries to stderr in ITERM_DEBUG builds so
they are visible while developing without generating a debug
log.

2026-07-11: Companion: harden session-view watch, dictation,
and reply-notification paths

Structural and correctness fixes from several rounds of code
review of the iTerm2 Buddy companion app, plus supporting
tests.

Session-view reply watch: - Extract SessionWatchState as the
single owner of the watch token/tab/
  chatID/claim-sequence/departed-token invariant (previously
  scattered inline across AppModel).
- Make the pop-vs-tab-switch teardown decision token-aware
(count mounted
  destinations per guid) so a watch no longer leaks when the
  same session is mounted twice.
- removeWatch clears the in-flight intent for its own token
so a deleted
  watched chat's Mac subscription is released instead of
  leaked.

Reply trigger / accumulator: - Merge
StreamingReplyAccumulator into SessionReplyTrigger as one
id->text
  surface (no second map kept keyed by convention).
- Strip only the separator (not content punctuation) from a
growing-id suffix,
  fixing a sign flip ("-5" -> "5").
- Gate the accumulator prepend branch on non-label so two
attachment labels
  no longer concatenate reversed.
- Feed notification previews untruncated so @-mentions
render before the single
  final truncation.

Dictation: - Re-check state after start()'s awaits and add a
recording epoch so a stale
  finalize/pass task can't tear down a freshly-started

- Latch re-entrant Send so a double-tap while dictating no
longer shows a false
  "interrupted" alert.

AppModel: - Scope the failed-send echo rollback by target
chat (also purge transcriptCache).  - Exclude both synthetic

  live transcript before the reconnect re-serve so it can't

2026-07-11: Fix security-review findings in the orchestrator
safety gate and auto-send

Safety gate (OrchestratorSafetyGate /
OrchestratorDispatcher): - Treat a CRLF (\r\n) as a real
submit boundary. It is a single Swift
  grapheme equal to neither "\r" nor "\n", so the
  Character-level .contains checks missed it and
  send_text("rm -rf /\r\n") routed to accumulate-only,
  unclassified. Route submit detection, residual, and
  trimming through scalar-aware helpers.
- Re-check the post-kill text for disturbing bytes in the
Ctrl-C/Ctrl-U
  branch. A kill followed by DELs was stored verbatim with
  contamination cleared, letting the next submit classify a
  mistokenized subpath that bypassed the rm -rf / tripwire.
- Factor the post-await concurrency reconciliation into a
pure
  resolveConcurrentSend so a raced submitting send fails
  closed and the decision is unit-testable.

Terminal hard rules (TerminalHardRules): - Make the
block-device redirect check quote-aware by scanning the raw
  segment text, so a > inside a quoted string is not
  mistaken for a real redirect operator (echo "run cat foo
  >/dev/sda" no longer over-prompts).
- Exempt $! and ${!ref} parameter expansions from the
history-expansion
  scan; a bare {! still flags.

Orchestrator client (OrchestratorClient): - Retain and
remove the chatWasDeleted block observer in deinit;
  removeObserver(self) cannot remove block observers, so
  deinit-able clients leaked one each.

Workgroup auto-send clippings (iTermWorkgroupPeerPort): -
Submit the Return as an ordered bracketing-disabled paste
instead of a
  fixed 0.2s timer, so it lands after the full content
  drains regardless of size and outside the bracketed
  region.
- Defer the auto-send when the main session is working so an
autonomous
  paste/submit never clobbers an in-progress turn.
- Archive the sent clippings so each run delivers only new
findings.  - Remove the dead setOn(_:) toggle accessor.

Adds regression tests for each fix; full ModernTests suite
passes.

2026-07-11: Reuse last-known process names so a transient
read failure doesn't drop a live ancestor

A single failed read of a live process's name (sysctl
KERN_PROC_PID momentarily returning an empty p_comm while
the process table churns) made foregroundJobAncestorNames
skip that ancestor. To GlobalJobMonitor a missing ancestor
is indistinguishable from an exited one, so it fired a
spurious "Job Ended" event, which for the claude-code
workgroup tore down the whole peer group even though the
leader's claude never exited.

Add ProcessNameCache: record each ancestor's resolved title
(keyed by pid, validated by ppid to guard against pid reuse)
and reuse the last-known title when a name reads back empty,
rather than dropping the process. Pruned to live pids each
process-cache update so it stays bounded.

When a name reads empty on the ancestry path, log it once
per episode (per-pid, reset on a normal read) with a precise
cause from iTermLSOF.nameFailureDiagnosisForPid: (sysctl
error, pid not matched, matched-but-empty comm, or
readable-again) so a recurrence is diagnosable without
spamming the log.

2026-07-11: Defer a workgroup Diff session's launch until it
is first shown

A .diff workgroup session's git diff previously ran on the
first git poller tick that reported a change, regardless of
whether the session was visible. For a buried Diff peer (the
Claude Code flow) that meant the diff reflected the tree at
whatever instant the leader first touched a file, so
switching to the peer later showed a stale diff.

Gate the deferred launch on the session actually being
visible:

- fireDeferredDiffLaunches (the poller path) now only fires
sessions
  that pass isVisibleForDeferredDiff (a live delegate whose
  tab is the foreground one), so a buried peer's diff does
  not start while hidden.
- fireDeferredDiffLaunchIfVisibleNow fires the deferred
launch the
  moment a session becomes visible, driven by a new
  didSwapInPeer hook on the peer swap path and the
  tab-selection path in PseudoTerminal.  It self-checks
  visibility so a swap that lands on a background tab
  (restoration across tabs, a programmatic reveal) does not
  launch early; the tab-select hook or the next poll tick
  picks it up once it reaches the foreground.

The launch still fires exactly once, so switching between
peers never re-runs the diff. Use the Reload button to
refresh on demand.

2026-07-11: Add release notes for workgroup restore and
server signal fixes

2026-07-11: Restore code-review/diff workgroup sessions
without relaunching

When a code-review or diff workgroup session is restored and
there is no running program to attach to (its server died),
don't launch a replacement program: that would spawn a stray
shell over the restored contents. Instead leave the last
output on screen and, for the code-review peer, only show
the prompt panel if it was visible at save time.

- Add PTYSessionArrangementOptionsInhibitRelaunch. When set,
or when the
  anchor's workgroup config says code-review/diff and it
  wasn't showing its pre-launch overlay at save time,
  sessionFromArrangement skips the launch and marks the
  session exited so its program stays restartable.
- WorkgroupRestorationCoordinator passes the option for
non-pending
  code-review/diff peers, and now always restores the
  anchor's mode tag and code-review raw command (adopt
  already does this for peer members but not the leader, and
  the anchor may be the leader) so the reload/restart and
  toolbar affordances work.
- Persist codeReviewLastUsedPrompt in the session
arrangement so a reload
  after restore defaults to the prompt the user last
  submitted rather than the store default.

2026-07-11: setsid the launched iTermServer so a signal to
iTerm2 can't kill it

iTermServer is not daemonized (see "Don't daemonize
iTermServer", which keeps it a child of iTerm2 for issue
9276 so tools can walk the shell's parent chain to the app).
That commit removed the double-fork's setsid without a
replacement, and the server-launch path (fork + iTermExec)
never had its own setsid, so the server inherited iTerm2's
session and process group.

When iTerm2 is launched from a terminal, it sits in that
terminal's foreground process group, so every iTermServer it
forks does too. A signal to iTerm2 (e.g. ^C sending SIGINT)
then also kills the servers, and on the next launch their
sockets are stale, so restored sessions can't reattach and
fork fresh shells instead.

Call setsid in the forked child before exec so the server
gets its own session and process group. This keeps the
parent pointer at iTerm2 (preserving the 9276 ancestry
behavior) while making the server immune to terminal
job-control signals.

2026-07-11: Add script to tail multiserver logs

2026-07-11: Harden the orchestrator typed-input safety gate
against split-payload laundering

Follow-up hardening on the autonomous command safety gate.
The core threat is a payload split across back-to-back sends
within one LLM turn (or across two chats on one PTY) so that
no single classification ever sees the whole command line.

Accumulator and contamination: - Share the per-session
typed-input accumulator across all of a client's
  per-chat dispatchers (OrchestratorTypedInputStore owned by
  OrchestratorClient, keyed by session GUID), so a fragment
  typed by one chat is visible to another chat's gate on the
  same PTY.
- Track a per-session contamination flag: a disturbing
keystroke
  (arrows, Ctrl-E, Ctrl-L, Tab, ESC, NUL) that is allowed on
  a primary-screen shell wipes the linear accumulator but
  leaves the typed prefix on the prompt, so force every
  subsequent submit to screen-aware classification until a
  clean residual-free submit resets the prompt. A full-line
  kill (Ctrl-C / Ctrl-U) clears it instead.
- Guard the classify-then-write-back window: if a concurrent
send mutated
  the accumulator or contamination flag across the classify
  await, fail closed by contaminating rather than
  clobbering.
- Classify the submitted portion at leading/interior submit
newlines so a
  laundered `rm -rf /\rhome/safe` is judged as the real
  submitted line.

Screen-aware routing: - When shell integration is present
and the live line EXTENDS our
  accumulator with un-accumulated content, route to
  screen-aware so the visible about-to-run line is judged,
  not the stale accumulator.

TerminalHardRules deterministic blocklist: -
redirectsToBlockDevice scans the whole word for redirections
so
  `cat x>/dev/sda`, `echo x&>/dev/sda`, `echo x2>/dev/sda`
  are caught.
- effectiveCommand skips a command modifier's boolean option
flags so
  `sudo -i rm -rf /`, `env -i rm -rf /`, `sudo -- rm -rf /`
  surface rm.
- findIsDestructive treats a shell-interpreter exec target
as destructive
  so `find / -exec sh -c ...` is caught.

Also seed the safety classifier with the driving chat's
recent history so it can tell a risky command the user asked
for from one it did not, and correct several stale doc
comments.

2026-07-11: Add Auto-Send Clippings When Idle toggle for
code review sessions

New code-review toolbar toggle. When on, the review
session's clippings are pasted into the workgroup's main
session and submitted with a Return each time the review
session transitions from working to idle.

- New .autoSendClippingsWhenIdle toolbar item (toggle
button, borderless,
  outline/dim when off and filled/accent when on so state is
  stable across window-active changes). Runtime per-session
  flag, defaults off.
- Peer port detects the working to idle edge and delivers
the shared
  clippings list to the main session; decision logic
  factored into a pure static for testing.
- Shared clipping formatting hoisted into
PTYSessionClipping+Formatting
  and reused by the Clippings panel's Send to terminal
  action.
- Included by default on the Claude Code review peer via the
shared
  preset builder, plus a one-time backfill (NoSync latch)
  that adds it to existing installs' review peer, covering
  the in-place upgrade and reinstall-over-existing cases.
- Settings add-menu offers the item only for
code-review-mode sessions.  - Tests: formatting, Codable
round-trip, registry, preset default,
  backfill scoping/idempotency, and the send decision.

2026-07-11: Session Status merge: represent a workgroup by
its visible peer

With "Merge workgroup statuses" on, the merged row picked
its representative by recency (the most recently changed
peer wins), falling back to priority only on a timestamp
tie. In a chat plus code-review workgroup that surfaces the
wrong peer: a busy code-review peer holding a steady
"working" status stops bumping its lastChanged, so the
moment the sibling chat peer flips to idle it becomes the
freshest change and takes the row, showing "idle" while
review is still working. Which peer won was effectively a
race, so sibling tabs in the same state disagreed.

Represent a workgroup by the currently visible peer of its
switcher (the pane the user is looking at) instead.
mergeRepresentativePrefers now ranks non-snoozed, then
visible switcher peer, then recency, then priority, then
sessionID. The visible tier sits below snooze so a snoozed
visible peer still yields to a non-snoozed buried sibling,
and above recency so a stable-status busy peer keeps the
row. When there is no visible switcher peer (every member
buried, or a non-switcher group) the prior recency/priority
rules still apply.

For the record: the original merge commit (3c31c6f82)
message claimed the representative was the highest-priority
status with ties broken by the most recent update, but the
shipped code was recency-first from the start. The two never
matched; this makes the intended behavior explicit.

2026-07-11: Prewarm AI API keys when a companion device is
paired

When a companion device is paired, load and cache the AI API
keys at launch and on a fresh pairing so an AI query driven
from the phone while the user is away serves its key from
memory instead of blocking on, or prompting for, keychain
access with nobody present to approve it.

- AITermControllerObjC.prewarmAPIKeyCache() reads the
selectable
  providers' keys into the existing cache off the main
  thread. It reuses apiKeyOnQueue so caching and
  legacy-migration semantics are unchanged, and is
  idempotent.
- CompanionPairingController warms the cache from
  resumePairedListeningIfNeeded (launch and post-disconnect,
  gated on hasPairedDevice and the AI gate) and on a fresh
  pairing in acceptLoop while the user is present.

Also stop reading the keychain when the Settings panel
opens: remove the N of 4 configured status label along with
the outlet, the XIB text field, and the read paths that
populated it from awakeFromNib and viewDidAppear. Opening
Settings no longer touches the keychain.

2026-07-09: Orchestrator AI safety gate for autonomous
command execution

Route every orchestrator code-execution path through the AI
safety classifier, closing the gap where
session_execute_command, submitted send_text, create_file,
and TUI keystrokes dispatched to the PTY on a one-time
workgroup claim with no per-action classification.

Classifier and prompt hardening (AutoModeClassifier /
TUISafetyPrompt): - Untrusted-content trust rules: only a
direct user turn authorizes a
  risky action; agent prose is never projected into the
  transcript.
- Prompt-injection defenses: fence the untrusted screen with
a random
  sentinel, neutralize block/thinking/reason and transcript
  delimiters, collapse every line-break scalar so a payload
  cannot forge a row or a role turn, and a fail-closed
  any-yes parse against injected verdicts.
- Transcript count/size caps that keep the newest turn and
the most
  recent user turn, truncating middles rather than dropping
  intent.
- Recent chat history seeded into both the orchestrator and
the
  session-bound safety checks so intent is visible.

Screen-aware TUI keystroke classification with an offline
eval harness, scorer, and fixtures captured from real
terminal programs.

Whole-line reconstruction plus a lag-free per-session
accumulator so a command split across back-to-back sends,
spliced mid-line, or staged across calls is classified as
one line rather than dodging the gate.

TerminalHardRules is now a tokenized blocklist, not an
allowlist: it never permits and never hard-denies. It
tokenizes each command (argv, flags, target) and surfaces
only a narrow set for one-tap approval (catastrophic
rm/chmod/chown/dd/mkfs/find at root or a device, carriage
returns, unbalanced quotes, pipe-to-shell, obscure zsh
builtins, terminal escapes); everything else defers to the
LLM, which reads it in full and judges it against what the
user asked for. This mirrors Claude Code's auto mode and
removes the read-only allowlist that let an autonomous agent
cat secrets or trigger a poisoned pager unchecked.

Wire needsManualApproval and deny through the existing
awaitPermission bubble ("Run this command?") so a flagged
command is offered for one-tap approval instead of
dead-ending as a text message to the model.

2026-07-09: Fix nil-font crash when a session note updates
its font

sessionNoteFont() returns nil when the session's textview or
font table is torn down. The ObjC direct caller guarded
this, but the Swift protocol declared a non-optional NSFont
return, so the nil slipped through
fontNotificationReceived's guard and reached -[NSTextView
setFont:], which raised NSInvalidArgumentException and
aborted. Declare the return optional so the existing guard
bails.

2026-07-09: Sync snippets, notes, and icon customizations to
the custom folder

Extend "load settings from a custom folder" to also carry a
curated allowlist of data files (snippets.plist, notes.rtfd,
graphic_colors.json, graphic_icons.json) to and from a Data/
subfolder of the custom folder, riding on the existing prefs
sync. GitLab issue 12890.

Design: data files are strictly safer than the prefs plist.
Consent is asked once per folder (notes and snippets can
hold secrets). Only one side moved reconciles silently
(union-push local, mirror-pull remote, propagating
deletions); a genuine both-sides change prompts "keep this
Mac's or the folder's" rather than overwriting, and backs up
the losing side first. Anything unreadable (dataless
placeholder, unlistable package, symlink) defers the pass
instead of acting on a phantom.

Engine: iTermRemoteDataFileSync (pure, path-in/string-out,
unit-tested) does the atomic mirror, content/stat
signatures, namespaced staging-temp sweep, and deferral.
Orchestrator: iTermRemotePreferences drives the per-folder
baseline / lastPushedLocal three-way reconcile, debounced
and deferred pushes that poll rather than busy-spin behind a
modal, and the import-suppression depth that keeps an import
from re-arming a push.

Owners keep memory, disk, and folder consistent without
clobbering unsaved edits: snippets guard against publishing
an empty set from a corrupt file; ToolNotes distinguishes a
corrupt note from an undownloaded placeholder, gates
re-serialization on a dirty flag, and backs up before
overwriting using a dev/size/ctime/inode token;
iTermGraphicSource reloads its maps under a lock and posts
synchronously inside the suppression window.

Save Now runs the full reconcile so an interactive save
surfaces a conflict prompt (and doubles as a sync) instead
of silently deferring.

URL-hosted custom locations skip file sync entirely (a URL
can serve one plist but not a file tree). Dynamic Profiles,
scripts, secure settings, and history/DB/cache remain out of
scope.

2026-07-09: Mark the FPS meter row uncacheable

setDebugString: writes the FPS meter text into row 0's
screen char line every frame but did not change the row's
content identity, so with the per-row output cache enabled
the lookup could serve the stale underlying row and hide the
meter -- reliably so when row 0 was stable enough to already
be cached (e.g. a settled screen with scrollback history).

Mark the row uncacheable after writing the meter text,
matching the offscreen-command-line and IME-marked-text
paths, which modify a row's content in place the same way.
These three are the only sites that mutate a row's build
inputs after its content identity is computed.

2026-07-09: Keep source-columns key absent when end column
is unset

Follow-up to building the run's attributes in one pass: only
insert iTermSourceColumnsAttribute when _endColumn has been
assigned, matching the previous behavior where the key was
absent until setEndColumn: added it. The two callers always
assign the end column first, so this is purely defensive
against a future caller that does not.

2026-07-09: Build a run's final attributes dictionary in one
pass

Per run the attributed-string builder rebuilt its attributes
dictionary twice: setEndColumn: did a full
dictionaryBySettingObject to add the source-columns range,
and then -attributedString mutableCopy'd the whole thing
again to add the two cell-index blobs. Since
-attributedString is always called right after
-setEndColumn:, and the source-columns range is derivable
from the already-stored _startColumn/_endColumn, add all
three keys in the single -attributedString construction and
drop the setEndColumn: rebuild. That removes one full
per-run copy of the base attributes. The final dictionary
and the built string are unchanged, so the source-columns
range read back from the string is identical.

2026-07-09: Intern the per-run attribute arrays in the
string builder

dictionaryForCharacterAttributes builds a fresh attributes
dictionary per run (per cell in a per-color animation, issue
12763). Two of its values were freshly-allocated arrays
every call: the writing-direction array (only two possible
values) and the underline-color components array (only read
when hasUnderlineColor, which is rare). Intern the two
writing-direction arrays and use a shared placeholder for
the underline-color array when there is no underline color,
so the common case allocates neither.

This does not touch the dominant cost (the 15-key dictionary
build itself), which is inherent: the run's attributes are
unique per cell in the animation so the dictionary cannot be
cached, and its keys are read downstream by the glyph
emitters so they cannot be dropped.

2026-07-09: Skip the per-cell ColorSync conversion for
native-space 24-bit colors

fastColorForKey:colorSpace: converted every 24-bit truecolor
key through colorForKey: -> NSColor -> colorUsingColorSpace:
(a ColorSync round trip) on every cell -- ~9% of the render
thread in issue 12763's sample, plus the NSColor allocation.
The existing vector cache is keyed on the exact color value,
so an animation with per-cell-varying colors misses on
nearly every cell and converts anyway.

When the target color space is the app's native 8-bit space
(Display P3 or sRGB per the p3 setting), a 24-bit key's raw
r/255,g/255,b/255 components are already correct, so return
them directly with no conversion and no NSColor. This also
eliminates the colorWith8BitRed: allocation for those
colors. The native-space check is memoized on color-space
object identity since it is constant across a frame's cells.

Bit-identical to the conversion path in this case; any other
key or a non-matching display profile still converts. A test
asserts the fast path matches the full colorWith8BitRed: ->
usingColorSpace: conversion.

2026-07-09: Make precise timers lock-free with atomics

The render sample in issue 12763 showed the precise-timer
instrumentation taking a recursive @synchronized on every
call (~12 per rendered cell), whose per-call id2data
class-hash was a meaningful chunk of the row build. The
enable/disable flag was also dead: nothing ever turned
timers off.

Remove the SetEnabled on/off API (and its four callers) and
drop the per-call locking. The stats/timer struct fields are
now _Atomic and the accumulation functions use relaxed
atomics: integer counters via atomic add (correct under any
number of writers), and the double fields via relaxed
load/store (exact given the single-writer-per-struct
invariant: one render pass writes a struct, the display/log
thread only reads).  Every access is atomic, so cross-thread
reads are well-defined with no tearing and no lock on the
hot path.

Only sLogs (an NSMutableDictionary) still needs mutual
exclusion; it uses a cheap os_unfair_lock, off the render
path. The iTermPreciseTimersLock class is kept because other
subsystems use it as their own lock token.

scripts for 12763

Two manual test animations under tests/: -
fullscreen_ascii_animation.py: every cell changes every
frame (the
  Bellavene worst case; 0% row-cache hits, exercises the
  full per-row build and color path).
- single_char_animation.py: a spinner on an otherwise static
screen (the
  case the cache wins: hit rate ~(rows-1)/rows).

2026-07-09: Fetch a row and its content identity in one
block walk

When the per-row cache is enabled the row build called both
screenCharArrayForLine: and contentIdentityForLine: for the
same line, each of which resolved the containing scrollback
block independently (and recomputed numLinesWithWidth: and
gridTopIsDWCEligible). Add a combined
screenCharArrayForLine:contentIdentity: that returns the row
and its identity from a single block walk, and use it from
the row build.  The line buffer gains a
screenCharArrayForLine: variant that reports the block's
generation/mutationCount/remainder from the same lookup that
builds the array.

Test: the combined call returns exactly what the two
separate calls return, for every displayed line across the
history/grid boundary.

2026-07-09: Gate the grid generation bump on the row cache
being enabled

The per-line grid generation is consumed only by the per-row
draw cache (via contentIdentityForLine:), which is off by
default, yet setDirty: bumped a shared process-wide atomic
on every dirtied line for every session. Gate the bump on a
flag the metal glue turns on the first time it renders with
the cache enabled. Default sessions now pay only a relaxed
atomic load, not a contended read-modify-write.

The flag is sticky (never turned off): once the cache has
been active, generations keep advancing for the rest of the
process, so the cache can never be re-enabled into a state
where a stale generation aliases changed content. Only the
grid counter is gated; the LineBlock counter must keep
advancing because the reloadBidiInfo watermark depends on
it. Tests turn tracking on in setUp since they exercise the
generation directly.

Also document that the two generation counters (grid vs
LineBlock) are intentionally separate, not a duplicate to
consolidate: they have different gating policies, and the
cache key's source field discriminates them so they need not
share a sequence.

2026-07-09: Skip reloadBidiInfo when nothing changed since
bidi was computed

reloadBidiInfo runs on every commit (commitLastBlock). A
block that legitimately contains RTL used to recompute bidi,
COW-clone its whole character buffer, and advance its
content identity every commit even when nothing changed,
defeating the per-row draw cache for exactly the RTL
sessions doing bidi work and churning a full-buffer
allocation per frame.

Gate reloadBidiInfo on an O(1) mutation-counter watermark.
Bidi is a pure function of the raw content, and every
content mutation bumps _mutationCounter (both
iTermLineBlockDidChange and iTermLineBlockDidMutateInPlace
do), so if it has not moved since bidi was last brought up
to date the stored bidi and per-cell rtlStatus are still
valid and reloadBidiInfo returns immediately with no copy
and no bidi algorithm. The watermark is recorded after a
real reload and in the all-LTR skip branch, initialized to
-1 so fresh/decoded blocks recompute once, and propagated
across LineBlock copies (a copy shares the buffer and bidi,
so it is valid for the same counter).

Tests: reload is idempotent for unchanged RTL content,
re-annotates after RTL erasure, and clears a stale rtlFound
flag.

2026-07-09: Centralize content-identity generation
advancement

The per-row draw cache relies on "content changed =>
generation advanced", which was being re-established by hand
at each mutation site.  Consolidate the two paths that were
most prone to divergence.

LineBlock: add iTermLineBlockDidMutateInPlace, a central
hook for in-place mutations (cell/bidi/rtlStatus rewrites
that change what a row renders but not the block's
structure). It advances only the mutation counter, unlike
iTermLineBlockDidChange which also bumps _generation for
structural changes. reloadBidiInfo, setBidiForLastRawLine,
and eraseRTLStatusInAllCharacters now route through it
instead of each hand-writing _mutationCounter =
iTermAllocateGeneration().

Grid: generationForLine: now honors allDirty_. setAllDirty:
(the hot scroll path) only flips the flag without advancing
per-line generations, so a line whose content changed via
that path kept reporting a stale identity. On the NO->YES
transition setAllDirty: now reserves a block of `height`
never-reused generations in O(1) (one atomic, via the new
VT100LineInfoAllocateGenerationBlock), and
generationForLine: returns MAX(perLine, base + y) while
all-dirty: a distinct fresh identity per line,
collision-free, with a line individually dirtied mid-epoch
still winning. allDirty_ resets each frame after the sync,
so the synthesized identities do not persist; a resize
recreates every VT100LineInfo with fresh generations that
dominate the MAX, so a stale base is bypassed.

Tests: all-dirty gives every line a fresh distinct
generation, and a line dirtied after the all-dirty epoch
began still wins.

2026-07-09: Wire the per-row output cache into the Metal
build

Serve cached glyph/attribute/background blobs for rows whose
content identity and config generation are unchanged,
skipping the attributed string build. Behind the
metalRowOutputCacheEnabled advanced setting (default off).

Cacheability: a row is not stored when it has a per-row
overlay (selection, find match, semantic-history underline,
annotation), is suppressed, has an uncacheable content
identity, produced image or kitty runs, or contains a
blink-phase cell or kitty virtual placeholder (neither the
blink phase nor _kittyImageDraws is captured by the key, so
those rows must rebuild every frame). The box-cursor
text-color tweak is extracted so it runs after both a hit
and a fresh build. Offscreen command line and IME
marked-text rows are marked uncacheable where their content
diverges from the content identity.

Config generation: the tracker now also compares the source
colorMap by object identity (the stable textView.colorMap,
not the per-frame copy) to catch a map swap whose per-object
generation coincides with the old map's, alongside the
existing scalar colorMapGeneration for in-place palette
edits.

Content-identity generation holes fixed so the cache cannot
serve stale rows: terminalSetLineAttribute: same-width-class
transitions (DWL/DHL), erasePossibleSplitDwcAtLineNumber:
and the scrollRect: boundary EOL rewrites, and the
appendCharsAtCursor: DWC-wrap side-write past the
markCharsDirty rect. Each now marks the changed line dirty
so both the generation advances and copyDirtyFromGrid
re-syncs the content.

Cost when disabled (the default) is kept near zero: the
per-row content identity build and the per-cell volatile
scan are gated on the setting.  Cache capacity is 256 rows
per text view (a viewport multiple), and the Entry blobs use
std::string members. iTermRowOutputCache hand-rolls its LRU
over a POD key to avoid NSNumber/NSData boxing on the hot
path.

Tests: content identity + config tracker (incl. colorMap
object swap), LRU round-trip/eviction, and updated VT100Grid
dirty-tracking expectations for the split-DWC generation
fixes.

2026-07-09: Add iTermRowOutputCache: LRU of per-row build
blobs

An LRU cache of the expensive per-row build output (the
glyph-keys, per-cell-attributes, and background-color-RLE
blobs), keyed on a row's (configGeneration,
contentIdentity). ObjC++ over unordered_dense with malloc'd
buffers so there are no ObjC objects in the C++ container
and the hot-path caller (metalGetGlyphKeysData, ObjC++) pays
no bridging cost. Not wired into the build yet. Tested:
byte-exact round-trip, config generation as part of the key,
and LRU eviction.

2026-07-09: Complete the row-cache config key with builder
shaping settings

Add the eight attributed-string-builder settings that change
the glyph-keys blob (asciiLigatures,
asciiLigaturesAvailable, nonAsciiLigatures, zippy,
preferSpeedToFullLigatureSupport, lowFiCombiningMarks,
boldAllowed, italicAllowed) to iTermRowRenderInputs so the

antialiasing, cell metrics, scale, baseline, underline
