2026-07-09: Add collision-free per-row content identity for
the draw cache

Groundwork for a per-row Metal draw cache: give every
displayed row a content identity that is collision-free
(built from counters and exact comparisons, never a lossy
hash), so a later consumer can reuse the built
glyph/attribute data for unchanged rows.

Identity sources: - Grid: VT100LineInfo gains a
globally-unique generation advanced on
  every dirty mark (VT100Grid generationForLine:). Equal
  generations imply equal content.
- Scrollback: LineBlock exposes its existing generation plus
a new
  mutationCounter for in-place edits (bidi/RTL) that don't
  bump generation. Both are drawn from the global generation
  counter so two COW copies that diverge in place get
  distinct identities, and both are preserved across copies.
- VT100Screen contentIdentityForLine: forks grid vs
scrollback into a
  (source, generation, mutationCount, remainder) tuple,
  folding in the grid-top DWC state for the boundary
  scrollback row. Captured onto iTermMetalPerFrameStateRow
  during the snapshot.

Config identity: - iTermConfigGenerationTracker assigns a
per-textview config generation
  by exact comparison (memcmp of the render-inputs struct +
  isEqual: on color space + pointer compare on font table),
  not hashing, so distinct configs can never collide.
  iTermMetalGlue owns one and delegates.

Correctness of the generation across copy/sync/COW: -
VT100LineInfo copyWithZone: preserves the generation.  -
copyDirtyFromGrid: mirrors the source generation onto every
dest line
  (screenTop_ is not propagated, so
  scrolled/resized/bidi-only lines need it), keeping the
  immutable grid the renderer reads consistent.
- fastPathScrollLinesAtAndAboveCursor: advances the blanked
recycled
  lines' generation.
- Grid setBidiInfo:forLine: advances the line generation on
a bidi
  change (compared by content-based isEqual:).
- LineBlock's in-place rtlStatus writers
(eraseRTLStatusInAllCharacters,
  reloadBidiInfo) route through ModifyLineBlock so the
  possibly COW-shared character buffer is uniquified before
  writing; reloadBidiInfo is gated on anyLineNeedsBidiReload
  to avoid per-commit churn.

iTermRowContentIdentity is laid out hole-free for future
byte comparison; generation 0 is a reserved uncacheable
sentinel. Also removes three headers mistakenly added to the
Compile Sources build phase.

Tests: grid line generation
(advance/stable/distinct/copy/sync/fast-path scroll),
colorMap generation copy propagation, the config-generation
tracker, and the LineBlock mutation counter
(append/in-place/copy/ divergent COW siblings).

2026-07-09: Add a per-line content generation to the grid

VT100LineInfo now carries a globally-unique generation that
advances whenever the line is marked dirty, exposed via
VT100Grid generationForLine:. Equal generations imply equal
content, so a per-row draw cache can use it as a
collision-free content identity for grid lines. The counter
is global (not per-line) so a value identifies a line's
content uniquely across all lines. Not consumed yet.

2026-07-09: Derive a collision-free config generation for
the row cache

Add colorMapGeneration to the row-build input struct and
compute a per-textview config generation by exact comparison
against the previous frame rather than hashing:
iTermMetalGlue memcmps the input struct, compares the color
space with isEqual: and the font table by pointer (a stable
stored property), and advances a counter only on a real
change. Because config changes rarely this stays stable
frame to frame, and because it is a comparison rather than a
hash it cannot collide (a collision would serve a stale
cached row).

Also fix iTermColorMap copyWithZone: to propagate
_generation, which colorMapGeneration depends on and which
was a latent bug on its own.

2026-07-09: Snapshot preferSpeed and lowFiCombiningMarks in
the string builder

iTermMutableAttributedStringBuilder read
preferSpeedToFullLigatureSupport and lowFiCombiningMarks
live from iTermAdvancedSettingsModel during the build.
Snapshot both once at the drawing helper and thread them
through the builder settings
(setColorMap:/copySettingsFrom:) into a designated
initializer, so the build no longer reads advanced settings
live. This makes the builder's inputs fully captured, which
the per-row output cache will need to fingerprint. No
behavior change.

2026-07-09: Introduce iTermRowRenderInputs to consolidate
row-build config

Add a POD struct that gathers the frame-constant inputs the
Metal row build reads (per-cell colors,
ligature/powerline/thin-stroke/blink settings, transparency,
etc.). It replaces the individual pass-through ivars on
iTermMetalPerFrameStateConfiguration and is populated once
in loadSettings. The build now reads these values only from
the struct, which is groundwork for fingerprinting exactly
the inputs that affect a row's output so unchanged rows can
be cached.

Also folds the previously live-read underlineHyperlinks
advanced setting into the snapshot. No behavior change.

2026-07-09: Promote some DLog to RLog

2026-07-08: Add AI economy model, and fix manual-model
catalog fidelity

Manage Manual AI Models gains a leaf toggle to designate one
non-default manual model as the "economy" model, used for
cheap background judgements (command-safety checks,
screen-idle detection) in place of the main chat model. The
designation is stored in kPreferenceKeyAIEconomyModelName
and resolved by ScreenWatchPoller, which prefers it (used
verbatim, with its own url/api/key) over the catalog's
automatic same-vendor economy pointer.  The toggle is
disabled for the default row since a model cannot be both.

Manual models now win over a built-in of the same name
everywhere a model name is resolved (request routing,
capability gating, provider binding, and the pickers), so a
user can proxy a known model through a custom URL by reusing
its name.

Because a manual config could not express several per-model
catalog fields, cloning a preset silently dropped them. Fix
that class of bug:

- Picking a preset now fills in the model name too, not just
the other
  fields.
- Add "Supports temperature" and "Configurable thinking"
checkboxes,
  seeded from the preset. A stored value wins; when absent
  the value is inherited from the same-named built-in, so
  existing clones self-heal without being re-created.
  Same-named clones also inherit the built-in's
  reasoning-effort and service-tier options, which have no
  editor UI.
- Honor supportsTemperature in the OpenAI and DeepSeek
completion request
  builders, not just Anthropic. This is a no-op for every
  shipping model (all are true on those paths) but stops a
  temperature-rejecting manual or future model from sending
  a field the API 400s on.

Also swap the economy-row table marker from a colored emoji
to a monochrome leaf SF Symbol.

2026-07-08: Reconcile streamed tool calls against the full
response body

A streamed tool call could be dispatched with empty
arguments when the AI plugin delivered the final WebResponse
before the argument-bearing stream chunks were consumed.
AITermController.parseStreamingResponse finalized from the
incomplete incremental accumulator and discarded the
complete response body, so the tool received {} and the
orchestrator rejected it as malformed_args. The agent,
believing it had supplied the args, looped retrying the
identical call, and in the worst case (the completion
beating the tool_use block entirely) the turn finalized as
an empty text answer that ended the conversation.

The final branch now reparses the complete body and prefers
whichever message carries the more complete tool call, and
streams any un-surfaced text tail so a raced completion no
longer leaves a truncated bubble. The common non-raced path
is unchanged.

Adds AIStreamingToolArgLossTests covering both the
truncated-args and no-call-at-all races.

2026-07-06: Orchestration: Stop cancels the chat's watchers

Pressing Stop already cancels the in-flight turn and clears
the queued backlog, but the chat's registered watchers
survived, so the next time a watched session went idle it
fired a watcherEvent that restarted the orchestration loop
after the user asked everything to stop.

The dispatcher already subscribes to broker .userCommand
messages (it handled only permission responses); route .stop
to a new cancelAllWatchers that tears down every watcher's
escalation timer and screen poller, clears the list, and
persists. Silent, matching the "stop is stop" semantics;
watches the user still wants can be re-registered by asking.

2026-07-06: Companion: retry @-mentions that failed to
resolve

The phone resolved @-mentions to live session/workgroup
names via an async round-trip to the Mac, but a failed or
empty lookup was treated as final: a miss was cached (or
dropped) and the bubble showed a raw @UUID forever, with a
retry only if some later message happened to re-mention the
same id.

Now only a real hit (a live displayName) is cached. Misses
(nil displayName, no entry, or a whole-batch failure when
the Mac is unreachable) go to an unresolvedMentions retry
gate that stops a failed lookup from re-firing on every
incremental delivery but is cleared on reconnect and on chat
open, so a transient miss resolves on the next natural
trigger instead of staying raw.

2026-07-06: Orchestration chat: make Stop and @-mentions
behave

Three fixes for the AI orchestration chat surfaced by a
watch-the- code-reviews session that flailed:

- Prompt: forbid abbreviated or bare session/workgroup ids.
The model
  was writing @601ECE75 or a bare CB905D2B, which never
  linkify and render as dead text. Require the full
  @-prefixed id verbatim, or name the session by
  role+workgroup instead of truncating.

- Stop now clears the queued backlog (pending user messages
and
  watcher-event turns), not just the in-flight reply, so
  pressing Stop actually halts the pipeline. It clears
  indices 1... only and leaves the in-flight head for its
  own finishTurn to pop, avoiding the mis-pop hazard from
  the cancellation design doc.

- User-typed messages now jump ahead of queued watcher
events (but
  never past the in-flight head, and without soft-canceling
  the current turn) so a user can interject in a long
  orchestration loop. New pure helper
  ChatService.queueInsertionIndex, unit-tested in
  ChatServiceQueueTests.

2026-07-05: Refuse companion peers that predate the relay
move

Both companion relays moved to new addresses, and every
build before this one has the old relay hosts hardcoded, so
a cross-revision pairing cannot deliver pushes or, once the
old relay is retired, even connect.

Bump the companion protocol revision to 5 and raise
minimumPeer to 5 (lockstep) so the app-compatibility
handshake refuses any peer older than revision 5 with an
upgrade wall instead of letting it pair into a broken state.

2026-07-05: Prompt to re-pair companion device after relays
moved

Both the push relay and the main pairing relay moved to new
addresses. A device paired on an earlier build is still
registered with the old servers, so its phone cannot be
reached until it pairs again.

Record the push relay and main relay origins when a fresh
pairing completes, and at launch, if a device is paired and
the feature is enabled but those origins no longer match
where this build points (including the never-recorded case
of a pairing that predates the move), show an alert telling
the user to re-pair. OK opens Companion Device Settings;
Later defers to the next launch.

Also update the phone's canonical relay host to
relay.iterm2.com so the new relay is not disclosed as a
non-default host on the pairing confirmation screen.

2026-07-05: Companion: add debug-only AirDrop button for the
latest log file

Adds an AirDrop Latest Log button to the Settings
Diagnostics section, guarded by #if DEBUG so it only appears
in debug builds. It flushes the log writer and shares the
most recent log file (app or NSE) via the system share
sheet, or shows an alert when there are no logs yet.

2026-07-05: Fix iTermGitClientTests under malloc diagnostics

The fixture's NSTask-spawned git inherited the test host's
ASan/malloc env vars, which made git print
MallocStackLogging noise into the output the tests assert on
and sometimes exit nonzero outright (12 of 28 tests failed
when the suite ran under the diagnostic variant). The child
now gets a pristine environment with
Malloc*/DYLD_*/NSZombie*/ASAN_* stripped.

2026-07-05: AI live harness: make the chat-queue tests
survive a paired companion

The four chat-queue tests play the broker-side tool runner
themselves, which they "ensured" by assuming
ChatClient.instance had never been created - but the guard
was an unenforceable stub, and the assumption is false on
any machine with a paired companion device: the companion
bridge and agent-activity notifier touch the lazily-creating
ChatClient.instance at app launch, and its broker processor
then wraps the fixture's remoteCommandRequest (fabricated
session guid, no live PTYSession) in selectSessionRequest,
which the tests' recorders never match. The tests now
suspend broker processors for their duration and restore
them on teardown.

With the fixture unblocked, two latent test bugs surfaced.
Expectation fulfillment from recorder callbacks is now
one-shot: a later turn can legitimately call the tool again,
and a second fulfill() asserts, crashing the host. And
FakeToolResponder answers one response per REQUEST instead
of one total: a rebuilt history's interrupted-call filler
invites the model to re-issue the tool, and an unanswered
re-issue parked the turn in pendingRemoteCommands forever.
Orphan semantics are unchanged - a response whose dispatcher
was cleared by Stop is an orphan regardless - so the
production orphan-drop guard in ChatAgent.fetchCompletion is
still exercised.

No production changes; the orphan-response guard these tests
pin already behaves correctly.

2026-07-05: AI live harness: replay interleaved reasoning
turns with real items only

The encrypted reasoning payload binds the turn's ORIGINAL
structure: replaying [reasoning, text, function_call] is
accepted when the real turn had a text preamble, but
fabricating a text item inside a turn that had none is
rejected (function_call provided without its required
reasoning item). The interleaved leg of the reasoning-replay
test now makes its own setup call that asks for a preamble
and replays the real items verbatim, mirroring what the
production builder does with a multipart text+call turn, and
reports a skip when the model declines to emit the preamble.
The production builder needs no change: it already preserves
the parser's part order, which mirrors the response.

2026-07-05: AI chat: persist reasoning state, lock provider
per chat, replay tool history structured

Deletes the tool-transcript reload hack, which taught the
model to imitate transcripts and fabricate tool results
after a reload. On reload, tool calls now replay as real
function_call/functionOutput items (with
AIChatToolCallRepair healing orphans) instead of prose.

OpenAI reasoning state persists: every Responses request to
a reasoning-capable model asks for encrypted reasoning
(include: reasoning.encrypted_content; probe-verified to
work with store:true, so previous_response_id keeps serving
live turns). Captured items (streaming and not) ride the
persisted tool-call message's llmMessage, requiring no
schema migration, and replay ahead of their calls when
history is re-sent. Calls persisted without reasoning replay
with their item id stripped so the API treats them as
developer-provided context (live-verified: with the id and
no reasoning item, the API 400s). Gemini's analog, thought
signatures, replays verbatim when captured;
signature-requiring generations (3+, conservatively
including generation-less aliases) get Google's documented
bypass token for signature-less legacy calls. Gemini's
same-role coalescing no longer folds a user's follow-up text
into a functionResponse turn, which made the model return an
empty answer.

A chat is now bound to one provider at the model layer
(ChatProviderBinding, persisted via Chat.modelName), not
just in the UI: the first conversational turn binds,
same-vendor model switches stay allowed (and the model
picker stays enabled mid-chat now), cross-vendor turns are
rejected as a chat reply without reaching the API, and
configuration-less turns (the phone) run on the chat's bound
model instead of the global default. Unclassifiable models
are permissive so no chat can be bricked. Hosted-tool gating
(web search, code interpreter, file search) now follows the
turn's effective model rather than the global default.

Reasoning-only turns keep their captured items on the
non-streaming path, and the functionToolCall construction
has a single site so the id-gating rule cannot diverge
between replay paths.

Live coverage: a reasoning-replay contract test
(full-fidelity, interleaved-text, and legacy id-stripped
replays with real blobs, plus the negative control), a
store:true encrypted-content probe, and the chatReload
family restored to structured assertions.

2026-07-05: Companion: let the user mute a chat's push
notifications

The phone offers Mute/Unmute (swipe on the chat list, bell
button in the conversation) when the mac advertises protocol
revision 5. The mac persists the muted set (it decides
whether to push, possibly while the phone is unreachable)
and chat-list entries carry the flag back so the phone UI
stays authoritative.

A muted chat sends no pushes anywhere they originate: the
agent-activity notifier skips its turn-complete and
permission-request wakeups, syncSince omits its messages (a
wakeup fired by another chat must not surface them on the
lock screen; the floor still advances so they are consumed
silently), the orchestrator's notify tool returns an error
telling the model the user muted the chat, and watcher
notify_user pushes are suppressed. The muted set is pruned
on chat delete and cleared on unpair.

Also adds field logging along the whole path: the phone logs
the toggle round trip, the mac logs the persisted set on
every chat-list build, muted suppressions, the triggering
chat of each push, and muted drops in syncSince, and the NSE
logs the per-item sources it renders.

Companion/RelayMonitor/ was the Cloudflare GraphQL Analytics
monitor for the Cloudflare Worker relay, which is being
phased out. It is superseded by the push-based monitor in
the iterm2-companion-relay repo, which watches the
self-hosted VPS relay. Both deployed to the same Worker name
and KV namespace, so keeping the dead directory around was a
redeploy footgun. Recoverable from history if the Analytics
approach is ever wanted again.

2026-07-05: Companion: scale live-stream bitrate to
resolution, throttle FPS, and tune flow control

The Companion live video feed encoded the window at full
native resolution but pinned the HEVC encoder to a fixed 1
Mbps, so quality collapsed as the window grew (a 2756x2880
Retina window was ~31 Mpx crushed into 1 Mbps).

- Scale the encoder bitrate to the frame's pixel count
(constant
  bits-per-pixel-per-frame), clamped to [1 Mbps, ceiling].
  The phone's maxBitrate becomes an upper bound; nil means
  the host picks a resolution-aware rate.
- Throttle the effective frame rate for large frames toward
a
  minimum so total bandwidth stays bounded: a big window
  emits fewer frames of the same per-frame sharpness rather
  than a ballooning bitrate. The pacer's interval is retuned
  once the resolution is known.
- Implement updateStreamParams as FPS-only live adaptation
(retune the
  frame-rate cap; per-frame quality is never lowered so
  terminal text stays legible). maxBitrate is ignored on a
  running stream.
- Add advanced settings: companionStreamBitrateMultiplier
(quality
  knob), companionStreamMaxLeadMilliseconds and
  companionStreamMaxQueueDepth (in-flight limiter back-off
  thresholds).  Both limiter thresholds are floored at 0 so
  a negative value cannot wedge the stream into a permanent
  freeze.

Adds unit/behavioral tests for bitrate scaling, the
multiplier, FPS throttling, updateFrameRateCap, and pacer
retuning.

  classifies each request into register write/skip and push

2026-07-04: Fix false idle status during Claude Code
background work, add provenance and screen age to
orchestration, scrub keys from AI wire log

Debugging a night of confused AI chat watchers surfaced four
problems, all fixed here:

1. cc-status reported "idle" on every Stop hook even while
background agents/tasks kept running, so completion watchers
fired on every lull between subagents. cc-status now stays
"working" while the Stop, StopFailure, or SubagentStop
payload reports running background_tasks (Claude Code
2.1.198+; shapes verified live against 2.1.201). Since
idle_prompt payloads carry no task info, the last known
count is stored in iTerm2 RAM (never on disk, excluded from
arrangements) via set_status's new background_tasks argument
and read back with the new
iterm2.get_background_task_count() function and it2
subcommand. The hook installer now also registers
SubagentStop so the count updates between turns; the
existing health check prompts existing installs to
reinstall.

2. Agents seeing another window's workgroup had no way to
tell where it came from. Workgroups and sessions in the
snapshot now carry provenance ("A trigger in session X
entered this workgroup", "Created by the agent in chat Y")
recorded at entry/spawn time.

3. Screen text carries no recency cues, which led an agent
to mistake a stale transcript for fresh work. Session
snapshots, get_state, get_screen_contents, and watcher
status updates now report how long ago the screen last
changed, with a new PTYSession flag so a never-rendered
session reports unknown instead of a bogus age.

4. The AI chat wire log stored API keys verbatim. Credential
headers are now fingerprinted and request URLs, bodies,
responses, and stream chunks are scrubbed for key-shaped
tokens (sk-*, AIza*), keeping a short prefix/suffix so
wrong-key bugs stay diagnosable.

Also adds paranoid sandbox build targets for cc-status and
it2cli.

2026-07-04: Fix Companion build: share
reasoning/service-tier enums

Message.Configuration referenced
ResponsesRequestBody.ReasoningOptions.Effort and
ResponsesRequestBody.ServiceTier, but Message.swift is
compiled into the iOS Companion target while the heavy,
Mac-coupled ResponsesAPIRequest.swift is not, so the
Companion could not resolve those types.

Extract the two small enums into a new Foundation-only
AIReasoningTypes.swift shared by both targets, and typealias
the existing nested names to them so no Mac-side call sites
change.

2026-07-04: Point Companion push relay at push.iterm2.com

Move both the Mac app and the iOS Companion off the
workers.dev host to the custom domain.

2026-07-04: PushRelay: idempotent /register + self-hosted
Node deployment

The free Cloudflare KV plan caps writes at 1,000/day.
register() wrote KV unconditionally on every call and the
phone re-registers on every connection, so a reconnect storm
exhausted the daily cap  after which a rotated secret could
no longer be persisted and every push 403'd "bad secret".

Worker fix: register() reads-before-writing, skipping the
put unless secretHash/sandbox changed or the record is past
half its TTL (steady- state writes ~0, rotations still
always persisted). Adds an env.fetchImpl seam in
deliverToAPNs  undefined on Cloudflare (global fetch),
injected by the Node host below. No behavior change on
Cloudflare.

Self-hosted alternative that eliminates the KV cap entirely,
running the same src/worker.js off Cloudflare via a thin
platform shim:
  - host/kv.js       Workers KV -> better-sqlite3 (TTL
  expiry + sweep) - host/apns.js     APNs over node:http2
  (Node's global fetch is HTTP/1.1,
		     which APNs refuses); per-origin session
		     reuse
  - host/server.js   Node http -> worker.fetch + in-memory
  /register limiter - bin/push-relay.js  systemd entry (env
  config, .p8 via file/credential) - ops/
  hardened unit (LoadCredential .p8), idempotent deploy.sh,
		     push-relay-cf.conf (push.iterm2.com: CF
		     -> :8443 -> :8790), DEPLOY.md runbook
		     (Origin Rule port->8443, wrangler
		     Node>=22/auth caveat, 503 diagnostics)

Tests: 31 worker (workerd) + 17 host (better-sqlite3 KV, the
HTTP/2 APNs adapter, end-to-end /register->/push through a
real listener).

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context)
<noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-07-04: Surface the real connection error when companion
relay egress fails

Pointing the relay at a host that refuses the WebSocket
produced "Pairing failed: The operation couldn't be
completed.  (iTerm2SharedARC.PluginError error 1.)" because
the useful detail was lost twice. PluginError was not
LocalizedError, so bridging an Error existential to NSError
discarded its reason string entirely. And the reason itself
was nearly empty for connect failures: the host flattens the
underlying URLSession error into _onClosed(id, 1006,
reason), and the signed plugin JS rejects wsOpen with just
"closed <code>".

Make PluginError conform to LocalizedError so reason
survives bridging everywhere it is shown, and have
CompanionPluginClient track which sockets completed the
WebSocket upgrade so a receive failure on a never-opened
socket is recorded as the connect failure and attached to
the wsOpen rejection. The plugin JS is signature-verified
against an out-of-band key, so both fixes are host-side
only.

2026-07-04: Cap companion relay retry rate with exponential
backoff

A fresh-pairing relay park that died was re-advertised
(under a new pid) with no delay. When the connection failed
fast, e.g. a bad TLS certificate on the relay, this spun:
mint a pid, redraw the QR, and reconnect dozens of times per
second.

Route both retry lanes (paired re-park and fresh-pairing pid
regeneration) through a shared RelayRetryScheduler whose
delay doubles from 5s to a 60s cap while parks keep failing
and resets once a park succeeds. Also close a hole where
gate-change notifications could re-park the retiring pid
around the backoff, defer regeneration while a handshake or
SAS confirmation is in flight, surface connection errors in
the pairing window instead of failing silently, and emit the
waiting status only once the relay actually admits the park
so it cannot paper over an error.

2026-07-03: Fix infinite registration recursion crash in AI
chat

registrationProviderRequestRegistration(for:_:) was only in
a protocol extension, not a protocol requirement. Calls
through an AIRegistrationProvider existential dispatched
statically to the default implementation, dropping the
vendor and substituting LLMMetadata.effectiveVendor. When
the active model's vendor differed, the resulting
Registration failed isValid(for:), so
AITermController.registration stayed nil and handle(.begin)
requested registration forever, overflowing the stack.

Declare the (for:_:) overload as a protocol requirement so
dispatch reaches each conformer's implementation and
preserves the vendor. Also fail gracefully instead of
retrying when a stored registration still doesn't satisfy
the getter, so this can never overflow the stack again.

2026-07-03: Return focus to terminal when closing inline AI
chat panel

Closing the panel only cleared inlineChatVisible, leaving
first responder dangling on the removed chat view. Call
takeFocus() so keyboard focus returns to the session's text
view.

2026-07-03: Improve AI chat provider and model UX

Per-chat model persistence with provider, model,
reasoning-effort, and service-tier pickers; per-vendor API
keys; manual model configurations; chat-list multi-select
with delete and rename; and a reworked message renderer with
inline code blocks and terminal-command transcripts.

Also folds in review fixes on top of the original PR: -
Gemini tool schemas: sanitize a property whose name is
itself a JSON
  Schema keyword (type/items/additionalProperties) as a
  schema instead of reinterpreting the name as a keyword, so
  additionalProperties and type-unions no longer leak to
  Gemini.
- Per-vendor API keys: adopt the legacy shared-account key
only for the
  effective vendor, so an OpenAI key can no longer be handed
  to DeepSeek or Llama, and trust a vendor's own stored key
  verbatim so a proxied/gateway key is not discarded on
  upgrade.
- Manage AI API Keys only rewrites the vendors whose field
the user changed,
  so a failed keychain prefill (blank field) no longer
  overwrites a stored key on Save.
- A chat pinned to a model that has been retired from the
catalog falls back
  to a same-vendor model rather than silently switching
  providers, and a chat pinned to a manual/custom model now
  resolves that model at request time instead of falling
  through to the global default.
- Bound the tool-call failure retry loop, including the
unknown/hallucinated
  function-name branch, so a deterministically failing or
  unregistered tool cannot loop indefinitely, one request
  per failure.
- Route the Settings manual-model vendor label through the
same resolver used
  at request time, and drop manual-model entries whose
  name/url are not strings so a hand-edited or synced pref
  cannot crash Settings.

Adds unit tests for per-vendor key resolution, manual-model
vendor classification, and the Gemini keyword-named-property
case; keeps the AIChatToolCallRepair structured-translation
test seam.

2026-07-03: Add data-driven, runtime-refreshable AI model
catalog

Externalize the AI model list from hardcoded constants to a
bundled, signed JSON (OtherResources/ai-models.json) that
refreshes at runtime, so new models and the per-vendor
recommended pointer reach users without an app update.

- AIModelCatalog loads the higher-versioned of {bundled
snapshot,
  downloaded cache}; unrepresentable entries are skipped
  fail-safe.
- AIModelCatalogUpdater fetches a signed manifest, tries
compatible
  candidates highest-version-first, verifies each payload
  against the bundled rsa_pub.pem, validates catalog
  invariants, and atomically replaces the cache. Gated in
  order on AI being fully enabled, a non-empty
  aiModelCatalogURL, and one-time user consent (tri-state,
  with an in-flight guard against stacking modals).
- Manifest compatibility uses a monotonic integer
  appCatalogCompatibilityVersion instead of the app version
  string, so nightly/beta/adhoc builds gate correctly.
- Add an optional per-entry economyModel (a cheaper
same-vendor model)
  used by the orchestration screen-watch poller. Economy
  resolution preserves the configured model's transport
  (url/api), so a custom base URL is honored and the API key
  is not sent to the public vendor host.
- tools/copy_models.sh signs and publishes the catalog to
the website.  - Docs in docs/ai-model-catalog.md; unit
coverage in AIModelCatalogTest.

2026-07-02: Fix dead-key composition under the Kitty
keyboard protocol. Issue 12906

When "report all keys as escape codes" and "report
associated text" are both enabled, a dead-key composition
(for example US International apostrophe then e) lost its
accent: transformedText rebuilt the report from the physical
keyCode and discarded the composed character. Report the
committed text as the associated codepoints instead, keeping
the physical key as the unicode-key-code. Guard the same
path so a pre-Cocoa escape sequence routed back through
insertText (control combinations, etc.) is not mistaken for
composed text.

Also skip the main-menu key-equivalent forwarding in
-[PTYTextView performKeyEquivalent:] during key-event
replay, so a synthesized control keystroke can't match a
macOS window-tiling shortcut (replayed Control-C matched
"Center" and moved the window) instead of reaching the
terminal.

Test harness: add a Manual column to steps.csv for tests
that need a keyboard-layout switch or modifier remapping the
harness can't perform; skip them in a normal run (printing
the count and how to run them) and run only them with
--manual. Trim their segments from recorded-keys.json so
replay stays aligned, and fix a stale expected value.

2026-07-02: Companion: live terminal video streaming to the
iOS app

Stream rendered Mac terminal frames to the iTerm2 Buddy iOS
app as an HEVC video with a text side channel for selection,
copy, and scrollback browsing, rather than re-implementing
the terminal emulator on the phone.

- Binary media channel and streaming HEVC encoder/decoder,
change-driven
  variable frame rate with dedup, insurance keyframes, and
  flow control (token-bucket byte budget, in-flight limiter,
  pacer, priority outbox).
- Absolute-line addressing for scrollback; a
zoomable/scrollable live canvas
  with Safari-style gestures (long-press selects, one finger
  scrolls, pinch zooms), host-rendered selection highlight
  in history tiles, a magnifier, and draggable selection
  handles.
- Host-side selection/copy/paste driven from the phone,
geometry side channel
  for touch-to-cell mapping, and column reflow / resize
  handling mid-stream.
- Relay resilience: keepalive-driven recovery of half-open
sockets, parked
  accept unblocking on a hard drop, and teardown on unpair.
- Hardening from review: forward-compatible config decode,
off-main-thread
  race fixes, exclusive/inclusive selection reconciled by
  document order, side-margin-aware touch mapping, a bounded
  and validated history-tile cache, generation-aware config
  resend that preserves phone state, and assorted
  overflow/lock/lifecycle fixes.

Squashed from the companion-video branch, which retains the
full history.

2026-07-02: Fix dead space in tabs when tab numbers are
hidden (#686) 2026-07-02: Tint minimal-theme pane title bars
when a tab has differing tab colors

In the minimal theme the per-pane title bar normally blends
with the session background (issue 10177) because the shared
tab bar already conveys the tab color. When panes in a tab
have two or more distinct tab colors the tab bar can only
show one of them, so tint each pane's title bar with its own
tab color to tell them apart. Issue 12903.

2026-07-02: Draw the Tahoe tab progress indicator as a ring
and fix its height

On macOS Tahoe the OSC 9;4 / agent-activity progress bar was
a flush 2pt bar inside the pill-shaped tab, where it
overlapped the tab title and the working/idle status
subtitle in the fixed-height tab bar.

Render it instead as a rounded 2pt outline hugging the pill:
the tab progress-bar view is grown 2pt past the pill on
every side and an even-odd clip path (outer rounded rect
minus the pill-shaped hole) masks it into a ring.
PSMTabBarControl now applies the progress-bar clip mask with
kCAFillRuleEvenOdd so the nested subpath punches out the
center (a no-op for the existing single-subpath clip paths).

Fix the ring rendering short (top edge unfilled), most
visibly after switching between compact and regular themes
while progress was running. The indeterminate gradient
layers are center-anchored and were set up while the bar was
still 2pt tall, so their position.y stayed at 1.
layoutSublayers only refreshed their bounds, not their
position, so growing to the taller ring left them centered
too low and the top went unpainted. layoutSublayers now also
tracks position.y to the container height. The gradient
height follows the view frame (so it fills the ring), and
heightValue/frame changes now mark the sublayers for
relayout.

2026-07-02: Fix dashed underline leaking into box-drawing
lines

ShapeBuilder.stroke set a line dash on the shared CGContext
but never cleared it, so a dashed underline drawn via the
solid-underline path (iTermAdvancedSettingsModel
solidUnderlines, which strokes directly on the window
context without a save/restore) left the context in dashed
mode. Later box-drawing glyphs stroke without setting their
own dash and inherited it, rendering separators and TUI
input-box borders dashed while a dashed underline was on
screen. Reset the dash to solid after stroking so it no
longer leaks. Issue 12907.

2026-07-02: Run SSH integration through the user's login
shell

The SSH command type launched it2ssh via a bare
`/usr/bin/login -fpq <user> <shell> -c ...`, which is a
non-login, non-interactive shell and so sourced no dotfiles.
Any environment the user configures in their shell startup
(most notably a custom SSH_AUTH_SOCK pointing at an agent
that holds their keys) was therefore not applied, and ssh
fell back to the default agent. For agent-backed keys
(Secretive, 1Password, a manually run ssh-agent, etc.) this
produced "Load key ...pub: invalid format" followed by
"Permission denied (publickey)".

Wrap it2ssh the same way the custom-command "run in login
shell" path does, using ShellLauncher so the shell is exec'd
with argv[0] = "-<basename>" (login) and -i (interactive),
sourcing both profile and rc files uniformly across bash,
zsh, fish, tcsh, and xonsh. it2ssh and the ssh binary it
invokes are referenced by absolute path, so a dotfile that
rewrites $PATH cannot stop them from launching. Dotfile
output printed before it2ssh runs is handled as ordinary
terminal output; the conductor parser does not engage until
the token-gated it2ssh= OSC arrives, so it cannot desync the
protocol.

Fixes the SSH-command-type case of issue #12909.

2026-07-01: Update companion-relay submodule

Brings in the App Attest assertion off-by-one fix, the
nonDisplacing join mode (reject rather than displace an
occupied slot), and socket-lifetime logging (lived=Nms) for
diagnosing park flapping.

2026-07-01: Fix crash in Replace Selection when replacement
grows the buffer

"Replace with Pretty-Printed JSON" and the base64
encode/decode context-menu items go through the fold path
(replaceRange:withLines: promptLength:-1) with reason .fold
but create no FoldMark. When the replacement had more lines
than the selection, delta > 0 and
ResilientCoordinate.linesDidShift tripped it_assert(delta <
0), crashing in release builds.

Remove that assert and handle both signs: use the posted
replacedRange (deriving the range from delta is wrong when
the buffer grows), and for a plain replacement with no
FoldMark invalidate coords inside the replaced region
instead of drifting them onto unrelated lines. Coords below
the region shift by delta regardless of sign. Adds tests
covering growth/shrink for coords inside, at the first
replaced line, and below.

2026-07-01: Default the Python runtime manifest to
manifest-new.json on macOS 27+

On macOS 27 and later the Python API runtime now downloads
from manifest-new.json by default. Older OS versions keep
manifest.json.  Users who customized
PythonRuntimeDownloadURL are unaffected.
